## Module: j01-main-part1  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js

**Purpose:** The node-view (graph editor) home module: wires the entire game-profile editing SPA — window/item/field/tell/dataset/subset/producer/trigger/toast/sound/file-source node bodies, drag-wire connections, layout persistence (positions/sizes/groups/collapse), undo/redo hooks, and the coalesced live-refresh path for dataset/subset nodes. It is the top-level module index.html loads to boot the whole editor; deleting it would remove the entire teaching UI (profile authoring would have no front-end).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/index.html:255 (<script type="module" src="/js/graph/main.js">, the sole SPA entry point); Exports (`live`, `wire`, `selectedNodeId`, `setSelectedNodeId`) consumed by sibling modules such as src/oc/web/static/js/graph/routing.js, panels/datanodes.js, imaging.js (per repo-wide grep of `graph/main.js` imports across model.js, toast_node.js, trigger_node.js, sound_node.js, source_node.js, routing.js, node_parts.js, panels/*, node_lifecycle.js, camera.js, history.js, persist.js, groups.js, state.js, minimap.js, workers.js)

- **[HIGH] hard-rule**: startGroupResize hand-rolls a second mousedown/mousemove/mouseup drag loop instead of using the already-imported shared beginDrag primitive (CLAUDE.md hard rule 7). -- Lines 435-459: startGroupResize wires its own `document.addEventListener("mousemove", onMove)` / `mouseup` pair to resize a group's box, duplicating exactly the loop that `beginDrag` (imported at line 15 from `./dragresize.js`, and already used elsewhere in this very file for node-move drags, e.g. moveNodes around line 3347/3372) exists to provide. beginDrag's `(ev, {onStart, onMove, onSettle})` shape maps directly onto this function's `cancelPan()` (onStart), the dx/dy/snap/render/requestEdges/showSizeHud block (onMove), and the hideSizeHud/flushEdges/persist.layout/pushHistory block (onSettle) — there is no dragresize-incompatible requirement here (no threshold, no cursor override beyond default). This is precisely the anti-pattern rule 7 calls out: 'a new resize/drag loop NOT calling them [createFloatWin/dragresize.js] is a violation' — and unlike a brand-new feature, the primitive is already imported and used for an equivalent purpose two thousand lines later in the same module, making the omission harder to justify as an oversight of a different module's existence. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 1021): subsetLiveCols Map entries are never deleted on subset rename/remove, leaking one stale key per renamed/removed subset for the life of the session. -- Line 1021 declares `const subsetLiveCols = new Map()`; it is only ever `.set()` (line 1284, inside _refreshSubsetNode) and `.get()` (lines 1030, 1042), never `.delete()`. A subset rename (wireSubset's `.subrename` handler, line 1342) changes `s.id` without removing the old key's entry, and removeNode's `subset` plan (line 2035) calls `model.removeSubset` without clearing this cache either. Functionally harmless (a stale entry is simply never read again since lookups are keyed by the current, live subset id), but it is an unbounded per-session memory leak keyed by every subset name a user ever renames or deletes away from — worth a one-line delete alongside the existing rename/remove paths if noticed again.

- **[INFO] readability** (line 1606): wireTrigger's fire-button handler shadows the outer `n` (node) parameter with a local `n` (fired-sweep count), which is confusing though not a functional bug. -- wireTrigger(div, n) binds `n` to the node object at the top of the function (line 1571). Inside the `.tg-fire` click handler (around line 1606) it declares `const n = (r.started || []).length, sk = (r.skipped || []).length;`, shadowing the outer `n` within that callback's scope. Because the callback never needs the outer node object (everything it uses, `t`, was already destructured at the top), this doesn't cause a wrong value to be read anywhere — but it is a needless variable-name collision that would bite the next person who tries to reference the node inside that same callback.


_Module notes: Read lines 1-2047 in full detail (task range was ~1-1550; extended slightly to cover the natural function boundary of wireSource/openFindModal/renderPreview and to confirm hoisting resolution of functions referenced but defined later, e.g. buildNode/render/rebuildNode/moveNodes/armConfirm — all are top-level `function` declarations in the same module so forward-reference at event-bind time is safe, not a bug). Verified index.html:255 as the sole load site and confirmed beginDrag is genuinely already used elsewhere in this same file (lines ~3347/3372, outside my range) before flagging the startGroupResize duplication so it isn't a false "the primitive doesn't exist yet" positive. No dataset-rename repoint logic exists in this file's audited range (that lives in model.js's _datasetSites, and the wireDataset rename handler itself is at line ~2956, outside j01's range) so hard rule 5 was not applicable here. ## Module: j02-main-part2  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js

**Purpose:** The node-view SPA's central controller: owns the render()/rebuildNode() reconciliation loop that keeps graph-node DOM in sync with the profile model, wires every node type's per-field/per-control event handlers (window/item/field/tell/region/detect/scrollbar/dataset/subset/producer/trigger/toast/sound/filesource/dictionary), drives drag/resize/marquee/group/wire interactions on the canvas, coalesces live dataset-change refreshes into batched /flow/details fetches, and runs the app's boot sequence (kill stray OCR, load the last game, settle boot OCR, drop the veil). Deleting it removes the entire node-editor SPA - the page has nothing else to bootstrap the graph, wire any node, or drive pan/zoom/selection/undo-redo.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/index.html:255 (<script type=module src=/js/graph/main.js> - the actual entry point); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/history.js:13 (imports render, collectLayout, hydrateNodeLayout, reconcileOpenImages, reapplyNodeSizes); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js (imports several exports incl. showSatellite/setSelectedNodeId consumers); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_parts.js:22 (imports subsetParts); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/routing.js:13 (imports selectedNodeId, wire, startWire, CAN_DISABLE); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/datanodes.js:10 (imports refreshLive, refreshDatasetConsumers, setNodeBusy); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/nodemap.js:13, panels/dbstruct.js:14 (import focusNode); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js:15 (imports placeNewNode, render, autosave); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js:13, panels/livewin.js:12, panels/precap.js:18 (import autosave/refreshLive/etc.)

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 3015): The dataset node's 'clear data' button hand-rolls its own armed two-click confirm instead of calling the shared armConfirm() primitive that already exists in this same file. -- Lines 3015-3034 (`.dsclear` click handler) reimplement the exact 'first click arms + relabels, second click within a timeout commits, auto-disarms after 2.5s' pattern from scratch (dataset.armed flag, manual textContent swap, manual setTimeout). The file already extracts this precise pattern as `armConfirm(btn, run, opts)` (defined ~line 3770, itself used at lines 3799/3800/3839 and via `.rule-del` in wireFieldRules at line 533) specifically so a new confirm button is a CALL, not a copy (CLAUDE.md hard rule 7, using the exact 'delete = armed two-click' example). Because armConfirm is a hoisted function declaration, it was callable from line 3015 even before its textual definition. As it stands, a fix/behavior change to the shared confirm affordance (e.g. resetOnOutside dismissal, styling hook, timing) will silently miss this button, which is exactly the drift rule 7 exists to prevent. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 435): Three canvas interactions (group-box resize, out-port wire drag, marquee select) each hand-roll their own mousedown->mousemove->mouseup loop instead of building on the shared beginDrag() primitive already used elsewhere in this same file. -- dragresize.js's beginDrag(ev,{onMove,onSettle,threshold}) is explicitly 'the ONE mousedown->track-mousemove->mouseup loop' (its own header comment) and this file already uses it correctly for node dragging (moveNodes, line 3347) and click-vs-drag gating (dragFromHandle, line 3372, threshold 4). Three other spots duplicate the same document.addEventListener('mousemove'...)/('mouseup'...) wiring by hand instead: startGroupResize (lines 435-459, drags a group's resize grip), startWire (lines 3392-3431, drags a wire from a node's out-port to a drop target), and startMarquee (lines 4124-4169, rubber-band multi-select - its own `moved`/`Math.hypot(...)<4` gate re-implements exactly what beginDrag's `threshold` option already does). All three map cleanly onto beginDrag's onMove/onSettle shape with no technical obstacle (verified by reading dragresize.js); none of them needed a bespoke loop. This is the class of duplication CLAUDE.md flags as the most expensive recurring mistake, though here it looks like debt predating the beginDrag extraction rather than a fresh copy-paste. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 879): The window-rename handler's dataset-node repoint guard (`if (oldDs && newDs && newDs !== oldDs) movePos(...)`) can never be true, so a same-named dataset node is never carried to the new id despite node_lifecycle.js's comment saying this exact case is handled 'separately in the handler'. -- wireWindowControls' `.gi-id` change handler (lines 877-901) reads `oldDs = model.datasetOf(n.ref)` before `model.renameWindow(oldId, newId)` and `newDs = model.datasetOf(n.ref)` after, then only calls `movePos('ds:'+oldDs, 'ds:'+newDs)` when they differ (line 892-893). But `model.datasetOf(win)` (model.js:89) is defined as `return win.dataset || null` - it reads only the explicit `win.dataset` field, which `model.renameWindow()` (model.js:1215-1223) never touches (it only mutates `w.id` and `window_priority`). Since `n.ref` is the same object reference before and after the rename, `oldDs` and `newDs` are reading the identical unchanged field both times, so `newDs !== oldDs` is unreachable in every case - the branch is dead. node_lifecycle.js's `moveWindowPos` explicitly excludes the window's own dataset node from its remap and comments 'those carry separately in the handler, since their stored data moves too' (node_lifecycle.js:113-114), i.e. this exact line was supposed to be the mechanism - it structurally cannot fire. Net effect: today this is inert dead code with no observed symptom (nothing currently relies on it), but it means a window whose dataset id happens to track its own window id has no live path that keeps the dataset node's graph position in sync with a rename, contrary to the documented intent.

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 87): COLX.prod (and COLX.state) are unreachable default-column entries: node placement is keyed by `n.type`, but producer-preview satellite nodes carry type "vttable" (never "prod"), so their intended column (1080) is never read and they default-place at column 2300 (COLX.vttable) alongside every other vt-table instead. -- `ensurePositions()` (line 354) computes a brand-new node's default column via `COLX[n.type] ?? 300`. `COLX` (line 87) defines both `vttable: 2300` and `prod: 1080`, but model.js's node builder always tags a producer-preview satellite with `type: "vttable"` (model.js:339, `{ id: 'prod:'+pn.id, type: 'vttable', ref: { kind: 'producer', ... } }`) - the "prod" id PREFIX is only used for the node's *id*, never its `type`. Lookup is by `n.type`, so `COLX.prod` can never be selected; every vttable-kind node (dataset table, subset table, producer preview, source preview/dismissed) lands at the same column 2300 on first spawn instead of the producer preview landing near column 1080 as the entry implies. `COLX.state: 600` is similarly dead - no node type "state" exists anywhere in the graph model (grep across src/oc/web/static/js/graph confirms zero `type: "state"` sites), a leftover from a since-removed concept. Impact is limited to the one-time auto-placement of a freshly-shown satellite (the position is then remembered in `pos` and freely draggable), so this is cosmetic, not a data-correctness bug.


_Module notes: Read the entire file (4679 lines); deep line-by-line pass focused on 1550-3245 (slightly past the requested 3100 to close out the "detect"/"readout" node wiring block cleanly), skim pass over the rest for cross-refs. Cross-checked model.js (_datasetSites, datasetOf, renameWindow, setDataset) and dragresize.js (beginDrag/addResizeGrips) to verify two of the findings below aren't false positives from misreading contracts. Confirmed via grep that main.js is the SPA's actual <script type=module> entry (index.html:255) and that its exported bindings (focusNode, autosave, render, refreshLive, rebuildNode, subsetParts, etc.) are imported by history.js, imaging.js, node_parts.js, routing.js, and every panels/*.js file — none of the exports are unused. No occurrences of innerHTML, prompt()/confirm()/alert(), spellcheck="true", or locale-default date formatting were found in this file (rules 1-3, 9 hold here); the one `since(...)` call (line 1977) correctly uses the shared datefmt helper._

## Module: j03-main-part3  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js

**Purpose:** The node-graph SPA's central module: builds/reconciles every node's DOM (buildNode/fillNode/rebuildNode/render), wires every node type's per-field controls (window/item/field/tell/region/detect/scrollbar/readout/dataset/subset/producer/trigger/toast/sound/filesource/dictionary), owns node drag/resize/group/multi-select/clone/delete, the out-port drag-to-wire mechanism, the live dataset-refresh pipeline (refreshLive/queueNodeRefresh), game load/boot sequencing (loadGame, killStrayOcrThenBoot, boot veil), the settings modal (capture backend + OCR device wiring), and the top-level keyboard/mouse handlers (WASD nudge, marquee select, context-menu add-node, undo/redo). Deleting it removes the entire graph editor — every panel module (history, imaging, node_parts, routing, datanodes, activity, nodemap, dbstruct, toolbox, livewin, precap) imports state or functions back from it, and index.html's only module script would fail to load.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/index.html:255 (<script type="module" src="/js/graph/main.js">, the SPA's sole entry point); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/history.js:13 (render, collectLayout, hydrateNodeLayout, reconcileOpenImages, reapplyNodeSizes); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js (several imports from ./main.js); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_parts.js:22 (subsetParts); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/routing.js:13 (selectedNodeId, wire, startWire, CAN_DISABLE); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/datanodes.js:10, activity.js:13, nodemap.js:13, dbstruct.js:14, toolbox.js:15, livewin.js:12, precap.js:18 (refreshLive/autosave/focusNode/placeNewNode/render/etc.)

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 3015): Dataset node's "clear data" button hand-rolls its own two-click armed-confirm instead of using the shared armConfirm() primitive defined later in the same file. -- In the `dataset` node wiring (`n.type === "dataset"` branch), the `.dsclear` button implements its own arm/disarm state machine by hand: `clearBtn.dataset.armed`, a `setTimeout(...,2500)` auto-disarm, and manual `textContent` swaps between "clear data" and "confirm?" (lines 3015-3034). This is exactly the "armed two-click confirm" primitive CLAUDE.md rule 7 calls out — and the file already extracts that primitive as `armConfirm(btn, run, opts)` at line 3770, used for `selDeleteBtn`, `prettyRevertBtn`, `prettySaveBtn`, and every rule-row delete button. The clear-data button should call `armConfirm(clearBtn, run)` instead. It already shows the exact divergence rule 7 warns about: the shared helper's armed label is "confirm" while this hand-rolled copy uses "confirm?", and this copy has no Escape/click-outside disarm (`resetOnOutside`) that the shared helper supports — a future UX fix to the shared confirm behavior (e.g. dismiss-on-outside-click) silently won't apply to this button because it isn't built on the primitive. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 4259): The global WASD node-move/resize keydown handler lacks the Ctrl/Meta guard its sibling branches (Delete, Shift+R) have, so Ctrl+A/Cmd+A (or other ctrl+w/a/s/d combos not intercepted by the browser) silently nudges the selected node(s) and persists the layout change. -- In the top-level `keydown` listener (~line 4227 onward), the Delete branch explicitly requires `!ev.ctrlKey && !ev.metaKey && !ev.altKey`, and the Shift+R reset branch explicitly requires the same. But the fallthrough that computes `const dir = NUDGE[ev.key.toLowerCase()];` (line 4259) and the move/resize branches that follow it (lines 4264-4343) have no such guard — only the earlier `if (ev.ctrlKey || ev.metaKey)` block returns early, and only for `z`/`y` (undo/redo); any other ctrl/meta + {w,a,s,d} combination falls through untouched. Failure scenario: select one or more nodes, then press Ctrl+A (a very common "select all" muscle-memory keystroke, which the browser does not intercept/consume on a canvas-focused page with no editable target) — `dir = NUDGE['a'] = [-1, 0]` is truthy, so every selected node is nudged one grid step left and `persist.layout()` fires, silently corrupting the saved layout with no visual cue of why. The same applies to Cmd+A on macOS and to Ctrl/Cmd + w/s/d if a browser doesn't intercept them.

- **[LOW] shadowing** (line 3236): captureScrollCutout() declares a local `let pos` that shadows the module-level `pos` Map imported from state.js. -- `import { … pos, … } from "./state.js"` (line 30) brings in the canvas-wide node-position Map used pervasively throughout the file (e.g. `moveNodes`, `positionNode`, `ensurePositions`). Inside `captureScrollCutout` (line 3222), `let pos = null, conf = null, px = null;` (line 3236) locally shadows that binding for the rest of the function. Verified harmless today — nothing later in this function reads the module-level `pos` Map — but it is a foot-gun: any future edit inside this function that assumes it can read/write the node-position Map (as almost every other function in the file does) would silently operate on the local scroll-thumb-position variable instead, with no error, since JS lexical scoping just resolves to the nearer `pos`.

- **[INFO] duplication-review** (line 3870): carryClones() implements its own follow-the-cursor drag loop rather than the shared beginDrag()/makeDraggable() primitives from dragresize.js, though its interaction shape genuinely differs from a normal click-drag-release. -- Reviewed against hard rule 7 (a new drag loop should reuse dragresize.js's beginDrag). `carryClones` (lines 3870-3902) hand-wires `document.addEventListener("mousemove"/"mousedown"/"keydown", …)` directly instead of calling `beginDrag`. However this is a "carry until the next click or keypress" gesture (starts without a held mouse button, ends on the NEXT mousedown or ANY keydown) which is structurally different from beginDrag's mousedown-hold...mouseup-release contract (which has no way to terminate on a fresh mousedown or a keydown). Every other drag in the file that fits beginDrag's shape (moveNodes, dragFromHandle, resize grips) does use the shared primitive. Not flagging this as a rule violation since forcing it through beginDrag would require reshaping that primitive's contract, but noting it here in case a future refactor wants a second shared "carry-until-drop" primitive alongside beginDrag rather than leaving this the only hand-rolled instance.


_Module notes: Read the entire file (4679 lines) top to bottom; did line-by-line reasoning across the whole thing but gave the deepest scrutiny to the assigned 3100-4582 range (per-node wiring for region/detect/scrollbar/readout, node drag/resize/move machinery, wire-drag, live-refresh/loadGame, node-map/panel bootstrapping, Pretty-view switch, selection toolbar + group/clone/delete actions, settings modal, canvas mousedown/dblclick/marquee/contextmenu, WASD/keyboard handling, OCR/capture settings wiring, debug-launch/boot-veil sequence). No rule-1/2/3/4/6/8/9 violations found in this file (no innerHTML, no prompt/confirm/alert, no spellcheck, no DS_ROW_CAP-relevant tables, not a .ps1, no Claude/Anthropic mentions, no locale-default date formatting — the one date use goes through the shared `since()` helper). One clear rule-7 violation (a hand-rolled armed-confirm button that duplicates the `armConfirm()` primitive defined later in the same file) and one real correctness gap in the global WASD/keyboard handler (missing ctrl/meta guard that its sibling branches have). main.js is unambiguously the SPA's live entry point — loaded by `index.html` as the sole `<script type="module">`, and re-exported-from by history.js, imaging.js, node_parts.js, routing.js, and every panels/*.js module — so dead_code=false with high confidence._

## Module: j04-model-part1  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/model.js

**Purpose:** GraphModel is the single in-memory representation of a game's editable GameProfile plus graph-view-only state (satellite visibility). It normalizes the raw profile shape on load() (defaults every array field, migrates legacy toast hero/inline fields and title/message into styled text blocks, fans hoisted item_fields/item_tells back onto each item), derives the node/edge lists the canvas renders (nodes()/edges()), and hosts every mutation method the editor panels call (add/remove/rename/set-* for windows' datasets, producers+http spec, triggers, toasts, sounds, file sources, dictionaries, glyph atlas, satellites). It is also the canonical implementation of the two SSOT patterns CLAUDE.md calls out by name: _datasetSites() (every place a dataset id is stored/referenced, backing datasets()/renameDataset()/collision checks per hard rule 5) and _repointField() (every within-window place a FieldDef id is referenced, backing region/item-field renames). If this file were deleted, the entire node-graph editor UI (main.js, minimap.js, state.js, node_parts.js, toast_node.js) would have no data model to read or mutate against - profiles could not be loaded, edited, or saved from the graph view.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/minimap.js (imports GraphModel); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/state.js (imports GraphModel, central app store); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (drives nearly every mutator via `model.*`, wires model.setRenameHook -> api.repointPretty); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_parts.js (model.satelliteOn/toggleSatellite, model.producerColumns, etc for node rendering); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/toast_node.js (model.toastImage*/setToastImageProp/addToastImageText for the toast node's image editor)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 466): _repointField() repoints a dataset's single-field key_field on a window-field rename but never touches the dataset's concat-key key_fields list, so a rename silently breaks any dataset keyed on multiple fields. -- Lines 455-468 (_repointField) patch region.field, item.fields[].field, item.tells[].field, item.key.fields, win.key.fields, and `d.key_field` for the window's fed dataset - but only `d.key_field` (the *single*-field key mode, set via setDatasetKeyField at line 110). The concat-key mode (`datasetKeyMode` returns "concat" when `d.key_fields.length`, set via setDatasetKeyMode/toggleDatasetKeyField at lines 138-148) stores the key parts in the sibling array `d.key_fields`, which `_repointField` never reads or updates - confirmed by grepping the whole file for every `key_fields` occurrence (lines 110,116,128,139,141,145-147): none of them are touched during a rename. Concretely: create a dataset with `key_fields: ["name","level"]` (a two-part composite key, e.g. mirroring the docstring's own "Arcane Aegis" level-5-vs-level-3 example at CLAUDE.md), then rename the window's `level` field to `rank` via renameRegion/renameItemField (both call `_repointField`, lines 1267 and 1613-1617). The dataset's `key_fields` still lists the now-dead id `level`. Per the project's own documented contract ("a record with ANY key part unread is dropped, never guessed" - CLAUDE.md Datasets section, and the in-file comment at lines 450-454 warning exactly about this), every subsequent record for that dataset has an unread key part and is silently dropped - the dataset stops receiving updates with no error surfaced, until the user notices data has stopped and manually re-picks the key fields.

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 447): Field rename only repoints references *within the owning window* (regions/items/keys); it never repoints a renamed field's name in downstream subset filters/sort or a producer's source_field, which store the field name as a plain string and silently go stale. -- _repointField's own doc comment (lines 447-454) explicitly scopes itself to "every place a FIELD id is referenced within a window". But a dataset column name (which IS the FieldDef id, per datasetFields() at lines 256-271) is also referenced outside the window: subset row filters (`{field, op, value}`, added via addFilter at line 1146, edited directly in main.js:1392 `s.filters[+i].field = e.target.value`) and subset sort (`{field, desc}`, addSort at line 1149) store the raw field-name string with no repoint path anywhere in the file. Verified server-side impact in src/oc/enrich/subset.py:78 `raw = row.get(rule.field)` - a stale field name simply returns None for every row, so the filter's comparison silently stops matching (rows get filtered out, or a negative filter stops filtering anything) with no error. The same gap applies to a producer's `setProducerSourceField`/`source_field` (line 590) which names a column by string. Net effect: renaming a field via the region/item-field rename UI silently breaks any subset filter/sort built on that column, with no warning and no automatic fix-up - only the in-window structures (which the comment explicitly promises) are kept consistent.

## Module: j04b-model-part2  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/model.js

**Purpose:** GraphModel: the editable in-memory representation of one game's whole GameProfile (windows/regions/items/fields/tells/readouts/detect/states/scroll, datasets, subsets, producers, file_sources, triggers, toasts, sounds, dictionaries, glyphs) plus every edit operation the graph SPA UI calls (add/remove/rename/set* for each node kind), and the nodes()/edges() derivation that the canvas renders. It is the single source of truth the UI mutates; main.js/node_parts.js/panels never touch profile JSON directly. If deleted, the entire graph teaching UI (index.html's SPA) has no data layer to read/mutate/save, and profile authoring in the browser would be impossible.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/state.js:10,15 - `import { GraphModel } from "./model.js"; export const model = new GraphModel();` - the one singleton instance every graph module (main.js, node_parts.js, panels, imaging.js, routing.js, minimap.js) imports and drives; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/minimap.js:7,33 - separate GraphModel instance for minimap-only derivation; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js - hundreds of call sites into model.* (edit ops, nodes()/edges(), the CLONERS dispatch map at ~3841-3851 calling cloneWindow/cloneDataset/cloneSubset/cloneProducer/cloneTrigger/cloneFileSource/cloneDictionary, and the delete PLAN dispatch at ~2025-2032 calling removeWindow/removeItem/removeRegion/removeReadout/removeDetect/removeScrollbar/removeItemField); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_parts.js, producer_node.js, panels/*.js - render/edit individual node bodies by reading/writing through model accessors

- **[HIGH] correctness**: Deleting a region, item, or item-field (removeRegion/removeItem/removeItemField) drops the backing FieldDef but never cleans up dangling references to it in the window's default key (win.key.fields), the dataset's key_field/key_fields, or (for removeItemField) sibling tells in the same item - unlike the rename path, which routes through _repointField and explicitly covers all of these. -- _repointField (lines 455-468) is the documented SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH for 'every place a FIELD id is referenced within a window' - it fixes up region.field, item.fields[].field, item.tells[].field, it.key.fields, win.key.fields, AND datasetDef(...).key_field, and its own comment says a miss here 'leaves a key part pointing at a dead id ... every record silently vanishes / the ledger re-keys to NULL'. It is called ONLY from renameRegion (line 1267) and renameItemField (line 1617) - confirmed by grepping every call site in the file. The three delete functions in this range do NOT call it or anything equivalent:
- removeRegion (1251-1258): filters w.regions and drops the FieldDef from w.fields if unused, but never touches win.key.fields or datasetDef(datasetOf(win)).key_field/key_fields.
- removeItem (1552-1560): same gap for any field only that item contributed - win.key/dataset key_field survive with a dangling id.
- removeItemField (1594-1605): explicitly prunes it.key.fields (with a comment literally warning 'a key part pointing at a dead field would drop every record — prune it'), proving the author is aware of the danger, yet still misses win.key.fields, datasetDef(...).key_field/key_fields, AND any tell in the same item whose t.field pointed at the removed field id (tells pick their field from the same item's fields list per node_parts.js:465, so a tell referencing the just-deleted field is left dangling).

- **[MEDIUM] consistency**: cloneDataset(id) does not copy the source dataset's own config (aggregate/key_field/key_fields/key_norm/dedup/batch_mode/sync_mode), unlike every sibling clone* method which does a full structuredClone. -- cloneWindow/cloneSubset/cloneProducer/cloneTrigger/cloneFileSource/cloneDictionary (969-974) all route through _cloneById, which structuredClone()s the whole source object so the copy is (per its own comment) 'a full independent copy of the model object'. cloneDataset (949-954) instead hand-rolls: `(this.profile.datasets = this.profile.datasets || []).push({ id: newId });` - a bare stub carrying only the new id, dropping aggregate/key_field/key_fields/key_norm/dedup/batch_mode/sync_mode entirely. Both are wired identically into the same 'clone selection' feature (main.js CLONERS map, ~3845-3851), so a user cloning a dataset node reasonably expects the same 'full independent copy' behavior the other six node kinds give them, but instead silently gets a dataset with all settings reset to defaults (e.g. a dataset with dedup=false or a custom concat key loses that entirely on clone).


_Module notes: Reviewed lines ~700-1696 of model.js in detail (per instructions, focus 800-1558), with lines 1-700 skimmed/grepped for cross-references since j04-model-part1 covers that half. This file is pure data-model logic (no DOM), so hard rules 1/2/3/4/9 (poll reconciliation, dialogs, spellcheck, row caps, date formatting) don't apply here - none found, as expected. Rule 5 (dataset rename repoint via _datasetSites) is implemented correctly and used consistently by renameDataset/removeDataset/removeSubset/_unwireDataset in this range. Rule 8 (no Claude/Anthropic mentions) clean. The one significant bug found is a real, concretely-reachable gap in the delete paths for regions/items/item-fields not mirroring the rename path's (_repointField's) key-reference cleanup - confirmed by grep that _repointField (lines 455-468) is called ONLY from renameRegion/renameItemField, never from any remove* function, and confirmed via config/games/warframe.yaml that window-level `key.fields` is live, populated production data (e.g. equipment_window's key is [name]), and via main.js's node-delete PLAN dispatch (lines 2025-2032) that removeRegion/removeItem/removeItemField are called directly from the UI's delete button with no key-usage guard/warning._

## Module: j05a-imaging-part1  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js

**Purpose:** Owns every in-graph image/canvas surface for the teaching UI: a window's capture surface (draw region/data_area/item/detect/scrollbar/readout boxes via the shared Overlay), the glyph-atlas teaching flow (manual + auto-glypher proposal boxes), and an item's frozen cutout editor (cell/field/tell boxes) plus its debounced OCR-read preview pipeline. Deleting it would remove the ability to draw/edit any capture region in the graph UI, teach glyphs, or preview item-cutout OCR reads — the core of the 'teach everything in the UI' workflow the project is built around.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:74-82 (imports closeImage, openImage, openGlyphImage, armPreprocessPick, refreshDetect, nodeIdOf, closeItemImage, setItemCellKeepingChildren, openItemImage, refreshItemBoxes, scheduleItemRead, itemReadout, and more; calls e.g. openGlyphImage(div) at main.js:2913, closeImage/openImage in refreshOpenImages around main.js:265-267, armPreprocessPick from the '.pp-pick' button handler at main.js:938); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/history.js:14 (imports refreshImageBoxes, refreshDetect, refreshItemBoxes for undo/redo repaint); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_parts.js:16 (imports ITEM_KINDS to render the item node's draw-tool buttons); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/livewin.js:11 (imports prevHost, refreshDetect, refreshPreview); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/precap.js:14,830,867 (imports openImage/loadImage/updateImageLabel; calls openImage(winId) when precapture needs the canvas open)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 168): The colour eyedropper is completely non-functional: openImage()'s Overlay is built without an onPick callback, so applyPickedColor() can never fire. -- armColorPick() (line 219) and armPreprocessPick() (line 228) both arm picking via `imageCanvases.get(winId)?.overlay.setPick(true)` on the window overlay built in openImage() (lines 168-196), expecting the next canvas click to route through Overlay's `this.onPick?.(this._sampleColor(p))` (overlay.js:223) into applyPickedColor() (imaging.js:235), which either pushes a preprocess colour chip or sets a detector's colour. But the `new Overlay(canvas, {...})` call at imaging.js:168 only passes {onCreate, onChange, onSelect, canCreate} — no `onPick` — so `this.onPick` stays undefined and the optional-chained call in overlay.js:223 is a silent no-op. Confirmed via `git log -S onPick`: the only place that ever passed `onPick: (hex) => applyPickedColor("game", hex)` was `openGameImage()`, which commit 4dfed45 ("feat(collect): priority-order classify replaces the worthiness gate") deleted while rewriting the surrounding comments to claim this is now 'a shared sample flow for a window's detect-node colour and its preprocess colour list' — the wiring was never migrated to the surviving openImage(). Concrete repro: click the '.pp-pick' button in a window's preprocess panel (wired at main.js:938 to armPreprocessPick), get the 'click the image to sample the text color' status message, click the canvas — nothing happens, no colour is added, no error surfaces. Same for a detect node's colour eyedropper via armColorPick (see next finding — that path is additionally unreachable for a second reason).

- **[MEDIUM] dead-code** (line 219): armColorPick (the detect-node colour eyedropper arm function) has zero call sites anywhere in the repo. -- `export function armColorPick(winId, detId)` at line 219 is exported but never imported or called from any file in src/ (verified with a binary-safe recursive grep of the whole src tree). The detect node's colour-kind UI (node_parts.js ~line 707-714, the '.aset-swatch' `<input type="color">`) uses the native browser colour picker instead, so this canvas-eyedropper path for detector colours was superseded and left orphaned. Combined with the onPick-wiring gap above, this makes the whole detector-colour-eyedropper feature (armColorPick + its branch of applyPickedColor) unreachable dead code, distinct from armPreprocessPick which IS still called from the UI (but is equally broken due to the missing onPick wiring).

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 664): openItemImage's onCreate handler copy-pastes the field-spawn and tell-spawn sequences instead of extracting a shared primitive (hard rule 7). -- Lines 664-676 (the `k === "field"` branch) and lines 683-692 (the trailing tell branch) run the identical 9-step sequence — add the child to the model, placeNewNode, render(), add-to-item-group, rebuildNode(item), groups.renderGroups(), refreshItemBoxes+refreshImageBoxes, clearGrid+scheduleItemRead+autosave, panZoomTo — differing only in the node-id prefix ('fld'/'tell'), the node kind string ('itemfield'/'itemtell'), the add-model-object call (addItemField/addItemTell), and the add-to-group helper (addFieldToItemGroup/addTellToItemGroup). The tell branch's own comment ('a tell SPAWNS its own node too, grouped with the item (mirror the field path above)') acknowledges the duplication rather than hoisting it. This is the exact anti-pattern rule 7 calls out ('If you find yourself pasting a block and renaming identifiers, you're doing it wrong — hoist instead'), and the adjacent window-level onCreate handler (lines 172-188 in the same file) already demonstrates the better pattern — one flow branching only on what differs — making the item-level duplication stand out more as a miss. A future fix to one path (e.g. adding a step needed for both) can easily land on only one branch. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 534): refreshGlyphAtlas's focusLast parameter is never invoked with true, so its auto-focus-newest-glyph behaviour can never trigger. -- `export function refreshGlyphAtlas(focusLast = false, nodeEl = null)` (line 534) has a `focusLast` branch (line 564: `if (focusLast && glyphs.length) host.querySelector(...)?.focus();`) intended to focus the newly-added glyph's character input. Every call site in the repo (saveGlyph at line 375, openGlyphImage at 406/468, commitGlyphProposals at 490, and the atlas's own char/enabled/remove handlers at 555/559/562) passes no first argument or explicitly `false`; no caller anywhere passes `true` (checked via grep and git history). This is a small, harmless dead branch, but it means the apparent UX feature (auto-focus after saving a new glyph so the user can immediately refine the label) has silently never worked.


_Module notes: Audited src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js lines 1-820 (file is 1648 lines total; the trailing `export { ... }` block at line 1638 legitimately exports the non-`export`-keyword functions defined in this range, e.g. closeImage, openImage, closeItemImage, openItemImage, setItemCellKeepingChildren, refreshItemBoxes, scheduleItemRead, itemReadout — confirmed NOT dead despite lacking inline `export`). Used binary-safe `grep -a` in addition to the Grep tool because main.js contains a stray null byte that silently truncates ripgrep's matches in that file._

## Module: j05b-imaging-part2  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js

**Purpose:** Owns every in-graph image/canvas surface for the teaching UI: a window's capture canvas (draw region/detect/item/scrollbar/readout boxes, page nav, recapture, OCR preview table, live dashed-grid overlay), the standalone glyph-atlas surface (manual + auto glyph labelling), and item-template cutout canvases (cell/field/tell boxes plus a live per-item OCR readout). Every mouse/keyboard box edit funnels through one persist* writer per surface (persistWinBox/persistGlyphBox/persistItem, rule 7); every OCR/detect/preview round-trip (single-shot or coalesced/debounced via previewBusy/detectBusy/refreshOpen*) that drives the dashed grid, confidence-tinted reads, and detect-status badges lives here. Deleting it removes the entire visual authoring surface: no drawing fields, no teaching items/glyphs, no live OCR preview, no grid to check a window/item config actually reads real data.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/history.js (imports refreshImageBoxes, refreshDetect, refreshItemBoxes; replays them for every open window on undo/redo); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports the large majority of this module's exports: openImage/closeImage/openGlyphImage/armPreprocessPick/refreshDetect/refreshPreview/commitPreviewNode/previewTable/etc. — wires almost every button in window/item/glyph node bodies); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_parts.js (imports ITEM_KINDS to build the item node's draw-tool buttons); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/precap.js (imports openImage, loadImage, updateImageLabel for the precapture flow); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/livewin.js (imports prevHost, refreshDetect, refreshPreview — drives the self-paced live-tuning poll loop, liveTick)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 168): Colour eyedropper is silently broken: Overlay's onPick callback is never wired for the per-window image surface, so applyPickedColor() (and the still-live armPreprocessPick button) never fires. -- openImage()'s `new Overlay(canvas, {...})` (line 168) only passes onCreate/onChange/onSelect/canCreate — no `onPick`. Overlay._onDown (overlay.js:223) does `this.onPick?.(this._sampleColor(p))`, which is a silent no-op when onPick is undefined. `armColorPick`/`armPreprocessPick` (lines 219, 228) arm `overlay.setPick(true)` and set `_pickTarget`, and `applyPickedColor` (line 235) is the only place that reads `_pickTarget` back and applies the sampled hex to a detector's colour or the window's preprocess colour list — but with onPick never wired, that function is unreachable. git history confirms the regression: commit 4dfed45 ("priority-order classify replaces the worthiness gate") deleted `openGameImage()`, which used to be the ONLY caller passing `onPick: (hex) => applyPickedColor("game", hex)` to its Overlay; the surviving per-window `openImage()` never had onPick and nobody moved the wiring over. `armColorPick` now has zero callers anywhere in src/ (fully dead — consistent with detector/tell colours having moved to a native `<input type=color>` swatch in node_parts.js:713-714). But `armPreprocessPick` IS still wired to a live button: node_parts.js:189 renders `.pp-pick` ("sample the text color from the open image") and main.js:938 does `div.querySelector('.pp-pick')?.addEventListener('click', () => armPreprocessPick(n.ref.id))`. Failure scenario: open a window's image, set Text-appearance preprocess mode to "color", click "⊙ pick", see the status bar say "click the image to sample the text color", then click anywhere on the canvas — nothing happens: no colour is added to the preprocess chip list, no error is shown, `_pickTarget` is simply discarded on the next click of any other eyedropper. The only working way left to add a preprocess colour is the manual `.pp-hex` + `.pp-add` fallback a few lines below it in the same panel.

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 944): refreshPreview() unconditionally rebuilds the whole OCR-preview table via replaceChildren on every call, including live-tuning poll ticks — a full-list DOM rebuild in steady state (hard rule 1). -- Line 944, `host.replaceChildren(previewTable(res.cells));`, runs regardless of the `live` flag (only the earlier "reading…" placeholder at line 937 is gated on `!live`). `refreshPreview(winId, true)` is invoked every round of livewin.js's self-paced `liveTick()` (~200ms cadence while live-tuning mode is on) for any window whose preview satellite node has been opened once (`prevHost(w.id)?.dataset.ran === '1'`, livewin.js:472). `previewTable()` builds a brand-new `<table>`/`<tr>` set from scratch every time (for both the plain-fields view and the item-cutout diagnostics view), so even when the OCR read is byte-identical between two consecutive live ticks (e.g. the game screen is static while the author tweaks an unrelated box), the entire table is torn down and recreated — the exact anti-pattern hard rule 1 forbids ("NEVER el.innerHTML = ... the whole list each tick"; replaceChildren-of-a-freshly-built-table is functionally the same full-subtree replace). This causes visible flicker/layout thrash in the preview node precisely while the author is watching it live to tune field boxes — the scenario the rule exists to prevent. Contrast with the rest of this same file (setDetectStatus, setWindowDetectStatus, setWindowDrift, setWindowCollideStatus) which all reconcile existing elements' textContent/className in place, and setWindowCollideStatus additionally skips work entirely via a `dataset.sig` guard when nothing changed — refreshPreview has no equivalent guard. [Hard rule: 1]

- **[MEDIUM] dead-code** (line 1311): refreshOpenDetect()/refreshOpenPreviews()'s "refresh ALL open windows" branch (winId=null -> _detectAll/_previewAll) is never triggered by any current caller, and the comment in main.js's autosave() describing it is stale. -- `refreshOpenDetect(winId=null)` (line 1311) and `refreshOpenPreviews(winId=null)` (line 1328) both special-case a falsy `winId` into a `_detectAll`/`_previewAll` flag that refreshes every open window instead of just one. Repo-wide grep for `refreshOpenDetect(` / `refreshOpenPreviews(` finds exactly one call site each, both in main.js's `autosave()`, and both always pass a real, truthy `win` resolved via `model.windowOf(changed)` (guarded by `if (win) { ... }`, main.js:197-201) — the null/global path is therefore unreachable in the current codebase. main.js:192-193's comment even claims "A genuine all-windows refresh (image load/recapture/page nav) calls refreshOpen*(null) DIRECTLY (imaging.js)", but no such direct call exists anywhere in imaging.js: loadImage()/recapture instead call the scoped `refreshPreview(winId, ...)`/`refreshGridPreview(winId)`/`refreshDetect(winId)` per window directly (lines 1391-1396), bypassing the debounced refreshOpen* wrapper and its all-windows mode entirely. Not a crash risk (the branch is simply inert), but it is genuine dead logic plus a misleading comment that would mislead the next person reading autosave() into thinking a global-refresh path still exists.

- **[LOW] documentation** (line 1428): Stale/duplicated comment in refreshImageBoxes: both halves of the sentence describe "the live-detected grid", contradicting the actual fallback (author-time static preview). -- Lines 1428-1429: "prefer the live-detected grid (rows found in the actual capture); fall back to the live-detected grid (where items were actually located this capture)" — both clauses name the same thing. The code right below (line 1433, `gridPreviews.get(winId) || staticFieldPreview(winId) || []`) actually falls back to the geometry-only `staticFieldPreview()`, not to "the live-detected grid" a second time. Comment-only; no functional impact, but confusing to a future reader trying to understand the fallback chain.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 1610): Row-index overlay badge width falls back to a fixed 0.03 (instead of shrinking to 0) when a row's leftmost field box sits exactly at x=0, so the badge can overlap the field it's labelling. -- Line 1610: `const w = Math.min(0.045, box.x) || 0.03;` is meant to clamp the badge width so it never runs off the left edge of the data area. When `box.x` is exactly `0` (a field flush against the left edge), `Math.min(0.045, 0)` is `0`, which is falsy, so the `||` kicks in and produces `0.03` instead of `0` — the intended "no room, shrink to nothing" case instead draws a fixed-width badge starting at `Math.max(0, box.x - w)` = 0, sitting on top of / immediately beside the field's own box. Purely a cosmetic author-time diagnostic overlay (the `#N` row-index label drawn during scroll calibration); does not affect stored data or reads.

- **[INFO] convention** (line 1055): previewTable()'s two row caps (200 and 300) are separate hardcoded literals rather than one named/shared constant, and differ from each other and from the unrelated ROW_CAP=200 in pretty/widgets/table.js. -- Line 1055 (`kept.slice(0, 200)`) and line 1073 (`read.slice(0, 300)`) each hardcode a different cap for the two previewTable() branches (plain-fields vs item-cutout diagnostics). The true count is still shown above the table in both cases (`prev-count` div), so this satisfies the spirit of the row-cap rule (hard rule 4 is specifically about datasetDetailHTML/subTableHTML, which no longer exist under those names in this tree), but the two different magic numbers in the same function, uncoordinated with any shared constant, is a minor maintainability nit rather than a functional issue.

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 1398): loadImage()'s img.onerror handler clears the busy flag but never calls the timed() done() callback, leaving an orphaned "load image …" line in the log bar with no completion/duration line. -- Line 1398: `img.onerror = () => setNodeBusy(nodeIdOf(winId), false);` — on a failed image load (e.g. a stashed capture file that was deleted from disk), this never invokes the `done` returned by `timed()` (line 1353), unlike every other exit path in this function which does. Purely a log-bar cosmetic gap (the "load image winId…" line never gets its matching result line); no functional/resource impact since `done` only logs.

## Module: j06-groups-collisions  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/groups.js

**Purpose:** Implements the whole node-grouping overlay system: GROUP (box around nodes), SUB (box around a subset of one group) and SUPER (box around groups) as ONE parameterised tier engine (per the file's own rule-7 doc comment). Owns membership edits (create/add/detach/disband/absorb-on-drop), auto-hugging geometry, DOM reconciliation/rendering of the three box layers, the shared colour-scheme system + style options popover, ctrl-click multi-select for super-grouping, and persistence (collect*/hydrate*) of all three tiers into profile.layout. If deleted: all visual organisation (grouping/nesting nodes into titled boxes, colour schemes, super-groups) disappears, group geometry feeds into routing.js (line obstacle avoidance) and panels/nodemap.js (mini-map) would break, and main.js's many `groups.*` calls (drag-drop absorb, rename remap, node-delete cleanup) would throw on import.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (renderGroups, absorb, createGroup/createSubgroup/createSuperGroup, detachNode(s)/detachFromSub/detachGroups, disband/disbandSub/disbandSuper, groupOf/subgroupOf/superGroupOf, allGroups/allSubGroups/allSuperGroups, toggleGroupSelected/selectedGroupIds/clearGroupSelection, hydrate/hydrateSuper/hydrateSub/hydrateSchemes, collectSchemes, groupBoxes, reflowFollowers, initGroups, clear); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/routing.js (titleRects, groupBoxes, subGroupBoxes, superGroupBoxes as line-routing obstacles); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/nodemap.js (groupBoxes, superGroupBoxes, subGroupBoxes for the mini-map overlay); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js (group-related exports, per repo grep); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_lifecycle.js (remapNodes on node rename, forgetNodes on node delete)

- **[HIGH] correctness**: "Copy style" arm-then-click only actually works via the cog for the SUPER and SUB tiers; clicking the tier's own big label/rim (the natural target implied by the button's tooltip) silently cancels the copy instead of applying it. -- The copy-style button's tooltip (`renderSchemeGrid`, line 217: "arm, then click another group of this kind to copy its style") is shared by all three tiers, implying you click on ANY instance of that kind to copy into the armed target. The actual consumption path diverges by tier: GROUP checks `tryCopyFrom` on the TITLE band's mousedown (`onTitlePress`, line 610), while SUPER/SUB check it only inside their COG's click handler (`toggleSuperPopover` line 932, `toggleSubPopover` line 943) -- their label/rim mousedown handlers (`onSuperPress` line 633, `onSubPress` line 679) never call `tryCopyFrom`. This asymmetry is masked for GROUP only by DOM nesting luck: the group cog is a CHILD of the title element (both share class `ggroup-title`, `buildTitleEl` line 593-604), so `onOutside`'s `e.target.closest(openPopover.ownerSel)` check (`ownerSel: ".ggroup-title"`, line 924) matches no matter which part of the header was clicked, keeping the popover/copy-arm alive until the title's own mousedown (which does check `tryCopyFrom`) fires. For SUPER, the cog and label are SIBLINGS, not nested (`buildSuperEl`, lines 615-632); `ownerSel` is `".sgroup-cog"` (line 934), which does not match a click on the label. For SUB, the cog is a sibling of the rim strips/title (`buildSubEl`, lines 650-670); `ownerSel` is `".subgroup-cog"` (line 945), which does not match a click on a rim or the sub's title. Because `onOutside` is registered on `document` with useCapture=true (line 917), it runs in the CAPTURING phase -- strictly before any listener on the clicked descendant element fires. So: arm copy from a super-group's popover, then click ANOTHER super-group's label (not its tiny cog) -- `onOutside` sees a target that matches neither `.ggroup-pop` nor `.sgroup-cog`, calls `closePopover()` -> `disarmCopy()` (lines 744-748), clearing `copyArm` to null BEFORE `onSuperPress` (bound to that same label) ever runs; the click then just starts a normal drag instead of copying. Same failure for SUB via any rim/title click. Net effect: the documented gesture only works if the user happens to click the tiny cog icon on the OTHER super/sub group, and silently fails (with no error, no visual feedback) via the far more discoverable big label/rim click. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[MEDIUM] dead-code**: `groupAt`/`subGroupAt` (and the `innermostAt` helper they wrap) are exported but have zero call sites anywhere in the repo, while the drag-drop "absorb" path re-implements a strictly weaker version of the same point-in-box query. -- `export function groupAt(x, y) { return innermostAt(GROUP, x, y); }` and `subGroupAt` (lines 720-721) are the only references to `innermostAt` (line 722) in the whole repository -- confirmed by a repo-wide grep for `groupAt`, `subGroupAt`, and `innermostAt` that only matches this file's own definitions. Meanwhile `absorb()`/`absorbSub()` (lines 444-477) implement their own, separate "is point inside this box" test via local `boxContains`/`subBoxContains` (lines 478-479) plus a plain `Array.find` -- i.e. first-match-in-array-order, with NO innermost/smallest-area tie-break, unlike `innermostAt` which explicitly picks the smallest-area match (used for exactly this kind of ambiguity elsewhere, e.g. hit-testing). Since group boxes auto-hug their live member positions with no enforced non-overlap, two groups (or subgroups) CAN end up with overlapping bounding boxes; dropping a node into the overlap then joins whichever group happens to be first in the `groups`/`subGroups` array (creation order), not the visually smallest/topmost one a user would expect. This is both a plausible latent UX bug and, independent of that, straightforward dead code that should either be wired into `absorb`/`absorbSub` (fixing the tie-break) or removed. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[LOW] dead-code**: The `titleTextLabel` option of `openOptionsPopover` is accepted and given a per-tier value but never read anywhere in the function body. -- `openOptionsPopover(...)` destructures `titleTextLabel = "title text"` (line 777) and `toggleSuperPopover` explicitly overrides it to `"label"` (line 935), implying the title-colour row's caption should read differently for super groups ("label" vs "title"). Grepping the whole file shows only these two occurrences -- the popover markup hard-codes the caption as the literal string "title" (line 809: `h("span", { class: "gp-lab" }, "title")`) regardless of tier, so the intended per-tier wording was never actually wired up; the parameter is accepted, threaded through, and silently discarded.

- **[INFO] maintainability**: `applySchemeTo`'s super-tier background blend special-cases one built-in scheme's exact outline hex by string comparison. -- `t.bg = blendOnBg(s.outline === "#2c313c" ? "#aab4d8" : s.outline, 4);` (line 149) singles out the literal colour of the "slate" built-in scheme's outline and substitutes a lighter tint for the super-group fill, presumably because blending the very dark slate outline at 4% reads as almost-black. If that scheme's outline hex is ever edited (it's a `const` built-in today, so unlikely, but customs could coincidentally share the hex) this silent special-case stops applying with no comment explaining the magic value beyond the surrounding blend-math comments.

- **[INFO] efficiency**: `renderTier` re-queries the DOM via `querySelector` per record on every call, and `renderGroups()` is invoked on every `mousemove` during a node drag (main.js line 3356), rather than using a keyed `Map` of already-built elements. -- `renderTier` (lines 519-541) does `layer.querySelector(...)` per record to find its existing element instead of the keyed-`Map`-of-reused-elements pattern the codebase otherwise standardises on (e.g. `renderWorkers`/`renderActivity`). It does NOT rebuild via `innerHTML` and does NOT re-append already-attached elements (only new ones get `appendChild`), so it satisfies the letter of hard rule 1 (no thrash/no flash) -- this is purely a lookup-cost efficiency note for a function called every frame of a node drag, not a correctness or hard-rule violation.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/collisions.js

**Purpose:** Single shared source of truth for window-detection "collision" reporting: defines the 5 verdict states (ok/collision/misclassified/self_no_match/no_image) with their label/CSS-class/tooltip (`COLLIDE_VERDICTS`), a per-window badge builder (`verdictBadge`) and a full whole-profile report renderer (`collisionReportNode`) for the toolbox modal, plus a small `intersperse` helper for spacing detector chips. Exists so the per-node inline badge (imaging.js) and the toolbox's full report never drift in wording/colour/verdict logic (the exact rule-7 concern this module's own header comment calls out). If deleted, both the toolbox "check collisions" report modal and the graph's per-window-node collision badge would break (missing imports).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js (imports verdictBadge, used in setWindowCollideStatus to build the per-window-node collision badge); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js (imports collisionReportNode, used to render the whole-profile collision report body)

- No issues found.


_Module notes: Both files read in full (groups.js 1030 lines across two Read calls; collisions.js 62 lines, one call). Verified every export of groups.js against a repo-wide grep (not just main.js) to separate genuinely dead exports (groupAt/subGroupAt/innermostAt) from the large live surface. Traced the copy-style bug (finding 1) through actual DOM event-capture ordering (onOutside registered on document with useCapture=true fires before any descendant's own mousedown listener) and the concrete DOM nesting differences between buildTitleEl (group: cog nested inside title) vs buildSuperEl/buildSubEl (cog as a sibling of label/rim) to confirm it is a reproducible behavioral divergence, not a stylistic nitpick. No hard-rule 1/2/3/4/9 violations found in either file (renderTier reconciles in place with no innerHTML/no needless reordering; no blocking dialogs; no spellcheck overrides; no capped/uncapped tables here; no dates). No hardcoded game-specific logic in either file (pure generic node/group-id manipulation)._

## Module: j07-node-parts-icons-lifecycle  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_parts.js

**Purpose:** Pure DOM-builder functions that turn a node's model data into its header/body/ports markup for the graph SPA — windowControls, preprocessControls, scrollbarParts, windowDetects, the rule-pipeline editor (ruleRows/fieldConfigBody), itemFieldParts/itemTellParts/readoutParts, itemLists/keySection, gamePriority, and the master nodeParts() dispatcher used for every node type (game/window/region/detect/item/itemfield/itemtell/readout/scrollbar/preview/vttable/subset/producer/filesource/trigger/toast/sound/dictionary/dataset). If deleted, main.js's rebuildNode/fillNode would have no body content to render for any node type — the whole graph UI would be empty shells.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:44 (imports nodeParts, windowControls, gamePriority, itemLists, _colOpts, satToggleBtn, slideToggle, vtShowRemoved); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js:25 (imports keyPrevNode); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/source_node.js:7 (imports slideToggle); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/toast_node.js:9 (imports slideToggle); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/datanodes.js:11 (imports clockTime, vtShowRemoved); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:22 -> node_parts.js:22 imports subsetParts back from main.js (documented intentional circular import, deferred to call time inside nodeParts())

- **[HIGH] hard-rule**: clockTime() hand-rolls a second, non-localized time formatter instead of using datefmt.js, and displays raw UTC instead of the viewer's local time -- `export function clockTime(ts) { const m = /T(\d{2}:\d{2}:\d{2})/.exec(String(ts || "")); return m ? m[1] : ""; }` (line 950) regex-extracts the HH:MM:SS digits directly out of the ISO timestamp string, with no timezone conversion. Every batch timestamp it's fed (`b.ts`, used at panels/datanodes.js:233 `clockTime(b.ts)`) is produced server-side by `datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")` (src/oc/store/dataset_store.py:41), i.e. a UTC instant with a `+00:00` offset. Meanwhile datefmt.js's `fmtDateTime`/`fmtTimeSec`/`since` (the mandated 'ONE shared helper' per CLAUDE.md hard rule 9) all go through `new Date(iso)` and then `.getHours()/.getMinutes()`, which DOES convert to the browser's local timezone. Concretely: for any viewer whose browser is not set to UTC, a batch created at `2026-07-04T20:00:00+00:00` shows as '20:00' in the batches list via clockTime, while the exact same instant would show as a different local hour ('22:00' for UTC+2, etc.) anywhere else in the app that uses fmtDateTime/since/fmtTimeSec (backups.js, dbbackups.js, activity.js, livewin.js, precap.js, history_panel.js, ago.js all import from datefmt.js — clockTime in node_parts.js is the one outlier, confirmed by grepping every datefmt.js importer). This is both a genuine display bug (wrong wall-clock time shown) and a violation of the CLAUDE.md hard rule that date/time display must go through the one shared datefmt.js helper. [Hard rule: 9]

- **[LOW] dead-code**: fmtWhen() is exported but has zero call sites anywhere in the repo, and its non-today branch also violates the dd/mm/yy format rule -- `export function fmtWhen(ts)` (lines 943-947) formats a dataset's 'last change' as clock-time-if-today else a raw slice of the ISO string (`t.slice(0, 10)`, i.e. `YYYY-MM-DD`, not the mandated `dd/mm/yy`). A repo-wide grep for `fmtWhen` only turns up its own definition in this file — no import, no call site in any .js/.html. It appears to be superseded by datefmt.js's `since`/`fmtDate` (which already implement 'relative if recent, else absolute date' per that module's own doc comment) but was left behind unused. Low severity because it has no live effect today, but flagging since if it were ever wired up it would violate hard rule 9 (dd/mm/yy dates only, never ISO/locale slices). [Hard rule: 9]

- **[INFO] duplication**: clockTime duplicates functionality datefmt.js's fmtTimeSec already provides -- clockTime's stated purpose (HH:MM:SS from a timestamp) is exactly what `fmtTimeSec(iso)` in datefmt.js already does correctly (with proper local-time conversion). Per CLAUDE.md hard rule 7 (build a second instance of something on the shared primitive, not a copy), clockTime should have been `fmtTimeSec(ts)` (or `fmtDateTime`/`since` if a date is also needed for non-today batches) rather than a hand-rolled regex parser. Folded into the severity-high finding above since the duplication is what caused the timezone bug, but noting the reuse angle separately per the audit's duplication-detection remit. [Hard rule: 7]

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_icons.js

**Purpose:** Single source of truth for every node-type header glyph (rule 7): one INNER factory map keyed by node type, all built via dom.js's svg()/path helpers (zero innerHTML), rendered through one shared `icon()` wrapper that applies the common 24x24 viewBox + stroke style. Exposes nodeIcon(n) (icon for a live node instance, special-casing trigger's change-vs-interval look) and iconFor(type) (icon for a bare type name, e.g. in menus/legends/test buttons before a node exists). If deleted, every node header in the graph UI would render with no type glyph, and toast/sound node 'test'/'play' buttons would lose their icon.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:8 (imports nodeIcon, iconFor); main.js:2108 calls nodeIcon(n) when building a node header; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/toast_node.js:10 (imports iconFor); toast_node.js:175 calls iconFor("toast") on the test button; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/sound_node.js:8 (imports iconFor); sound_node.js:28 calls iconFor("sound") on the play button; src/oc/web/static/css/graph.css:57 (styles the .nicon class this module's icon() function sets on every returned <svg>)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_lifecycle.js

**Purpose:** The one place that carries or drops a node's per-id live UI state (canvas position, size, collapsed flag, busy/spinner count, dataset/window tab selection, table column widths, group membership, per-node timing-history file) across a rename or delete. Guarantees the CLAUDE.md-documented invariant that every per-node-id store is wired into BOTH remapNodeState (rename) and forgetNodeState (delete) so a rename can't silently reset a node's layout and a delete can't leak state. Exposes renameNode (the guard+move+post rename flow), movePos/moveWindowPos/moveItemPos (exact, window-subtree, and item-subtree state carriers) and forgetNodeState. If deleted, every node rename would reset that node's canvas position/size/collapsed state and orphan its satellites, and every delete would leave stale Map/Set entries keyed by the dead id.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:43 (imports movePos, moveWindowPos, moveItemPos, renameNode, forgetNodeState); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:600-602 (item rename -> moveItemPos); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:707-709, 776-778 (field/tell rename -> movePos); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:891-893 (window rename -> moveWindowPos, then movePos for the window's co-named dataset if renamed too); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:1344,1442,1575,1618,1741,1776,2921,2957,2971,3091,3125,3174 (subset/producer/trigger/toast/sound/filesource/dictionary/region/detect/readout renames -> movePos); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:2055 (node delete -> forgetNodeState(n.id))

- No issues found.
