## Module: j08-routing-route-hier  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/routing.js

**Purpose:** Builds the ONE unified link list for every node-to-node connection shown in the graph view (buildLinks), negotiates which face a shared out-port/trigger-fires/trigger-watch port leaves from (negotiateOutSide/computeTriggerSides/sideForPort), fans endpoints that share a node face apart, owns the persistent per-link <path> elements and morph animation (drawEdges/startMorph), and schedules + caches the A* routing pass (route.js/hierRoute.js) gated by a layout signature (scheduleRouting/runRouting), plus the drag-freeze and OCR-freeze behavior. If deleted, main.js's calls to drawEdges/requestEdges/flushEdges/setDraggingNodes/nodeRect/freezeRouting would throw, imaging.js and panels/nodemap.js would fail to import, flow.js would lose edgeGeometry, and the entire node-graph UI (the app's primary front-end) would render with zero edges.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports drawEdges, requestEdges, flushEdges, nodeRect, freezeRouting, setDraggingNodes at line 35-37; dozens of call sites e.g. lines 155, 316, 450, 455, 1418, 2396, 2422, 3343, 3361, 3878, 3895, 4341); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js (imports drawEdges at line 14; called after every box/preview mutation); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/nodemap.js (imports buildLinks, routeCache, ROUTE at line 12, builds the mini node-map preview from routed geometry); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/flow.js (imports edgeGeometry at line 13, animates data-flow blobs along the routed polyline); window.__route/__reroute/__routes/__nodeRects console + e2e hooks, read by tests/e2e/route-no-through-nodes.mjs

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 165): The `top` field built in buildLinks (line 165: `!!selClsFor(...)`) never affects rendering because it is always OR'd with a hardcoded `over = true`. -- `add(key, aId, bId, top, kind, ra, rb)` stores `top` on the link object (line 162: `links.push({ key, aId, bId, top, over, ... })`), but the only read site is `drawEdges()`'s `const el = edgeEl(l.key, (l.top || l.over) ? top : svg);` (line 446). Since `const over = true;` is hardcoded a few lines above (line 161, with a comment explaining every line now always draws on the top layer), `l.top || l.over` is always true regardless of `l.top`'s value — `l.top` is computed every buildLinks() call but is functionally inert. Likely a leftover from before `over` was hardcoded to always-true; harmless but confusing/dead.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 244): The `outDot` map used to nudge an arriving line clear of a node's out-port dot records the FIRST link found for a node id, not specifically its port line. -- Lines 244-260: `outDot` is built by iterating every link and, for each `l.aId` not yet seen, recording `{dir: l.d1, coord: ...}` from that link's `p1` — without checking `l.port`. If a node's first outgoing link in `model.edges()` iteration order happens to be a non-port (`kind==='own'`/tether) line rather than its actual data/control out-port line, the anti-overlap nudge for an arriving line on the same face will be computed against the wrong reference point. Purely cosmetic (spacing an arriving line's endpoint away from a dot it might not actually be near, or failing to nudge away from the real dot) — not a functional/data bug.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/route.js

**Purpose:** The core orthogonal connector router: builds a gridless visibility graph over inflated obstacle corners plus each node's 4 face ports, runs a per-connector A* (bends + soft group/title-band penalties, with face-choice built in as a super-source/sink), then nudges connectors sharing a corridor into separate parallel lanes and evicts any residual wall overlap, and renders the result to an SVG path (`polylinePath`). Exports `routeGraph`, `simplify`, `polylinePath`. If deleted, routing.js and hierRoute.js would fail to import (both depend on `routeGraph`), and panels/nodemap.js would lose `polylinePath` — there would be no A* routing engine at all, only the immediate elbow fallback in routing.js.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/routing.js (imports routeGraph, polylinePath at line 10; used in runRouting() and setRouted/setBezier); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/hierRoute.js (imports routeGraph, simplify at line 15; each outer/inner sub-pass is a routeGraph call); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/nodemap.js (imports polylinePath at line 5, renders the mini node-map from the same routed geometry)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 404): evictSegments computes a segment's coord/lo/hi once before the per-wall loop, so a second overlapping wall in the same pass is tested against a stale (pre-correction) position. -- In `evictSegments` (line 404), `const lo/hi/coord` (lines 421-423) are snapshotted from the segment's ORIGINAL `a`/`b` before entering `for (const r of walls)` (line 424). Inside that loop, a match mutates `a`/`b` in place (lines 437, 450) to evict the segment from wall A — but `coord`/`lo`/`hi` are `const` primitive snapshots, not re-derived from the now-mutated `a`/`b`. If a second wall (e.g. an overlapping title band, or two adjacent nodes) also originally covered the segment's coordinate, its check `if (coord <= e0+0.5 || coord >= e1-0.5) continue;` (line 428) still uses the stale `coord`, so it can wrongly skip a wall the segment is now actually inside (post wall-A correction), or apply a correction computed from numbers that no longer describe the segment's real position. Concrete scenario: wall A spans 90-110 (segment starts at coord=100, gets evicted to ~129 via CORNER_CLEAR), wall B spans 120-130 (segment now really overlaps B) — but B's stale-coord check (100 <= 120.5) short-circuits and B is never evaluated, leaving the line resting a few px inside wall B's rect. Effect is a small cosmetic overlap in the (narrow) case of two walls sharing a lane coordinate; this is a backstop pass after A* (which itself never crosses a hard obstacle), so severity is capped at medium.

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 518): clampEnds looks up byId.get(ln.from)/byId.get(ln.to) and clamps into that rect's face span without checking ln.fromGate/ln.toGate first, unlike every other consumer of ln.from/ln.to in this file. -- `clampEnds(pts, byId.get(ln.from), ln.srcSide)` / `clampEnds(pts, byId.get(ln.to), ln.dstSide, true)` are called unconditionally in `nudge()` (line 374) for every line. Every other place in this file that reads `ln.from`/`ln.to` explicitly special-cases a gated end first — `anchor()` (line 225: `gate ? gate.pt : center(byId.get(id))`), the `own` group-exemption set (line 241: `ln.fromGate ? null : groupOfNode.get(ln.from)`), `srcEntries`/`dstEntries` construction (lines 235-236, 238-239: `if (ln.fromGate) {...} else {...ln.from...}`), `endCenter()`, and `fanFaceEnds()` (`if (!ln.fromGate) push(...)`). `clampEnds` alone (lines 518-523) has no such guard. hierRoute.js's crossing pieces deliberately set BOTH a synthetic box-node id (`boxNodeId(gid)`, which IS present in that pass's `byId`) AND a gate on the SAME end for every group-crossing edge (e.g. hierRoute.js line 92: `{ from: boxNodeId(fromG), to: boxNodeId(toG), fromGate: ..., toGate: ... }`; line 95: `to: boxNodeId(toG), toGate: ...`; line 99: `from: boxNodeId(fromG), fromGate: ...`). So for every group-crossing line, `clampEnds` will clamp the gated endpoint into the (often much larger) group-BOX rect's face span instead of leaving it untouched. In the common case the gate point is already safely inside the box's own face span (gates.js keeps gates off corners), making the clamp a no-op — but if a group box is small or has several gates fanned near a corner, this can shift the endpoint (and, via its collinear-neighbour carry, the adjacent vertex too); the later unconditional `pinGate()` (line 383) re-pins the exact endpoint back onto the true gate point but only conditionally re-carries the neighbour (only if it's STILL collinear at that point), so the bogus clamp can leave a non-orthogonal kink near the gate that pinGate doesn't fully undo. Currently likely masked by gates.js's own margins, but it's a real, reproducible inconsistency with the rest of the file's gate-handling pattern.

- **[LOW] simplification** (line 201): routeGraph re-declares `const EMPTY = new Set();` (line 201), shadowing the module-level shared `EMPTY` (line 45) whose whole purpose (per its own comment) is to avoid allocating a Set per no-group edge. -- The module-level `EMPTY` (line 45) is documented as "Shared empty group-set so the (very common) no-group edge doesn't allocate a Set per option", and is used by the hot O(N^3) `mkSet`/`edgeOpts` path. Inside `routeGraph`, line 201 (`const FACES = ["L", "R", "T", "B"], EMPTY = new Set();`) creates a SECOND, function-scoped empty Set with the identical name, which shadows the outer one for the rest of `routeGraph`'s body (used at lines 244-245 for the S0/D0 hysteresis stub edges). Functionally harmless (both are empty, read-only-safe Sets, and this one is only allocated once per `routeGraph` call rather than per edgeOpts call), but the naming collision undermines the stated intent of the shared singleton and is confusing to read.

- **[LOW] code-smell** (line 138): routeGraph mutates the shared module-level constants object `C` in place via Object.assign (line 138) and never resets fields a later caller's config omits. -- `if (opts.config) Object.assign(C, opts.config);` permanently overwrites `C.clearance`/`C.laneGap` (the only two fields routing.js ever passes) on the single module-level `C` object; nothing restores previous values for callers that pass a different or partial config. Currently harmless because the sole caller (routing.js) always supplies the same `{clearance: ROUTE.cell*2, laneGap: ROUTE.cell}` shape every time, so results are consistent — but this is global mutable state, not reentrant: two independently-configured `routeGraph` callers running interleaved (e.g. future tests exercising different `bendCost`/`groupCross` tunables) would silently leak configuration into each other.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/hierRoute.js

**Purpose:** Decomposes graph routing into an OUTER pass (free nodes plus every group collapsed to one opaque hard box) and one INNER pass per group (that group's own members only, plus any foreign node whose rect happens to intersect the box as an obstacle), splitting every boundary-crossing edge at a fanned per-face gate (gates.js) into two/three pieces that terminate at the identical gate point, then stitching the routed pieces back into one seamless polyline (joinPts) — with a per-pass memoization cache (passSig/cachedRun) so a node move only re-routes the pass(es) it actually touches. If deleted, routing.js's `import { hierRoute } from "./hierRoute.js"` would fail outright, breaking the module; even if that import were removed, losing this file would drop hierarchical routing entirely (routing.js's default `ROUTE.hier = true` branch), forcing every route onto one flat global A* pass over the whole canvas (functionally different behavior, not just a perf regression, since groups would no longer act as opaque boxes).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/routing.js (imports hierRoute at line 11, calls it in runRouting() at line 634 whenever ROUTE.hier is true, which is the default)

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 99): Every crossing piece this file builds for an outer-pass gate end sets BOTH a synthetic box-node id and a `*Gate` field on the same end (lines 92, 95, 99), which is the data that triggers route.js's clampEnds missing-gate-guard finding (see route.js line 518). -- e.g. line 99: `outerEdges.push(piece(cr.key, { from: boxNodeId(fromG), to: cr.to, fromGate: gateTerm(cr.fromGate, "from", "outer") }, false, true));` sets `from` to a real, present-in-byId box-node id AND a `fromGate`. This dual-tagging appears deliberate (the box id feeds `passSig`'s cache signature and `okFrom`/`okTo` gating in route.js), and every OTHER route.js consumer of `ln.from`/`ln.to` already special-cases the gate — only `clampEnds` doesn't. No independent bug in this file's own logic was found; noting the interaction here since this is where the box-id-plus-gate combination originates.


_Module notes: No game-specific knowledge, no dates/dialogs/spellcheck/row-cap/dataset-rename surface in this module (pure geometry/graph code) — hard rules 2/3/4/5/6/8/9 are not applicable here. Rule 1 (poll/timer DOM reconciliation) is actually exemplified correctly: drawEdges() reuses a persistent Map<key,path> (edgeEls), never innerHTML, and only moves/repaints an element when its geometry actually changed — this module IS the canonical "reconcile in place" pattern other code should copy, not a violator. Rule 7 (extract-before-duplicating) is likewise satisfied: this is itself the one shared router (routeGraph) that both the flat and hierarchical modes build on. The two real findings worth attention are in route.js: a stale-snapshot bug in evictSegments's per-wall loop (coord/lo/hi computed once before the loop mutates the segment, so a second overlapping wall in the same pass is checked against outdated coordinates), and a missing gate-guard in clampEnds (every sibling function in the file special-cases ln.fromGate/ln.toGate before touching ln.from/ln.to, but clampEnds does not, and hierRoute.js's crossing pieces do set a synthetic box-node id alongside a gate on the same end). Both are narrow-trigger, cosmetic (a few-px overlap / a kinked stub), not data-correctness bugs — I would not bet money on either being currently visible in typical layouts, but they are genuine latent defects worth a small fix (add the same gate check clampEnds's neighbours use; snapshot-refresh coord after each wall correction in evictSegments)._

## Module: j09-floatwin-dragresize-camera  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/floatwin.js

**Purpose:** The one constructor (createFloatWin) for every floating panel in the graph UI - node map, node list, activity/tasks, precapture, live readout, stats, toolbox, testing, dbstruct, history, plus every pretty-view studio panel (theme/sources/palette/pages/inspector/elements) - built on dragresize.js's makeDraggable/addResizeGrips. Owns the header+body shell, viewport clamping, panel-to-panel snap (edges/centres/abut), a light docking model (chains of panels stacked under one another), collapse/expand, session-local geometry (position/size/collapsed/dock) with save hooks, and body re-parenting so a panel can be 'embedded' into a pretty-view widget. Deleting it removes every floating panel in the app (~15+ call sites) and the pretty Embed widget.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/history_panel.js:26 (createFloatWin); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js:51; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/dbstruct.js:28; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/precap.js:118; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js:126; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/nodemap.js:107,120; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/testing.js:61; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/livewin.js:73; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/stats.js:62; src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/theme.js:10, sources.js:36, palette.js:12, pages.js:13, inspector.js:21, elements.js:14; src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/panel.js:58,60,61 (floatWins().get(id), win.embed/reclaim/unembed); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/camera.js:6,60 (floatWins for usableViewport clipping); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:15(?)/256/3707-3727 (floatWins for hydrate-all, panel toggles, hide/show on pretty-view switch)

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 183): state.dock.dx/.ar are computed meaningfully when a dock link is created, then unconditionally overwritten and never read anywhere - dead sub-state inside the docking model. -- findDockParent (line ~199) and findDockChild (line ~215) compute dx: sl-al / dx: al-sl and ar: <alignment-tolerance-check> - real, position-derived values. But reflowDock (the only place a dock link is actually applied to layout) unconditionally does `d.ar = true; d.dx = 0;` (line 183) every time it runs, and the actual placement `w.place(pr - w.el.offsetWidth, pb + GAP, seen)` always right-aligns the child to the parent's right edge regardless of d.dx/d.ar. Grepping the whole static/ tree turns up no other reader of `.dock.dx` or `.dock.ar`. Likewise `place()`'s oldL/oldR parameters (and the prevL/prevR bookkeeping that feeds them) are threaded through to reflowDock, whose own doc comment says '(oldL/oldR accepted for call-site compat, unused.)'. None of this causes incorrect behaviour today (right-alignment is applied unconditionally and correctly), but it's meaningful-looking state that is actually inert - a future edit that tries to honour d.dx (e.g. to support left-anchored docking) would silently do nothing because reflowDock stomps it first.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/dragresize.js

**Purpose:** Shared drag (beginDrag/makeDraggable) and resize (addResizeGrips) primitives - the canonical 'one drag/resize loop' the project's own hard rule 7 points to. Also hosts the grid-snap helpers (GRID/snap/snapUp), the floating size HUD shown during a resize, and colResizeDrag (the one column-resize loop shared by every table). Used by graph nodes, floating panels (via floatwin.js), groups, and every resizable table (vtable.js, pretty table widget, graph table).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/vtable.js:19 (colResizeDrag); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js:13 (makeDraggable, addResizeGrips, GRID, snap); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/floatwin.js:7 (makeDraggable, addResizeGrips); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/groups.js:28,612,628,637 (beginDrag); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:15 (GRID, snap, snapUp, showSizeHud, hideSizeHud, addResizeGrips, beginDrag - used for node drag/resize); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:13 (GRID); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/table.js:9 (colResizeDrag); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/table.js:14 (colResizeDrag)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/camera.js

**Purpose:** The canvas camera: pan, wheel-zoom, animated pan/zoom-to-node/rect, right-drag panning, and the world CSS transform for the graph canvas (#gworld). Also computes the 'usable viewport' (the part of the canvas not covered by a visible floating panel) that every fit/centre operation frames against, and resizes the canvas/edge-SVG backing size to fit all node positions.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:40-42 (cancelPan, panTo, panZoomTo, panZoomToRect, zoomToNode, viewportCenterWorld, applyView, resizeCanvas, onWheel, startPan, consumePanSuppress) plus call sites at 409, 442(cancelPan), 625/659/958/974/990 (panZoomTo), 4089 (startPan), 4173 (consumePanSuppress); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js:23,675,692 (panZoomTo); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js:14 (panZoomTo); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/dbstruct.js:15,168 (panZoomTo); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/livewin.js:13 (panZoomTo); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/nodemap.js:14,94,96,101 (panZoomTo, panZoomToRect); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js:16,47,54,61,68,75,82,88,96,114,121 (panTo, used to auto-reveal newly created nodes when off-screen)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 147): startPan() (right-mouse-button canvas pan) never calls cancelPan(), so it can race an in-flight panTo/panZoomToRect animation and get silently overwritten by it. -- panTo (line 23) and panZoomToRect (line 92) both call cancelPan() before starting their own requestAnimationFrame loop, so they never run concurrently with each other. startPan (line 147, wired at main.js:4089 for ev.button===2 'right-drag pans ANYWHERE') does not call cancelPan() at all, and nothing else does on its behalf (grepped the whole static/ tree for cancelPan() - only 3 call sites total: panTo, panZoomToRect, and an unrelated group-resize in main.js). Concrete scenario: double-click a node (zoomToNode -> panZoomTo -> panZoomToRect, a 380ms eased rAF animation), then immediately right-click-drag to pan manually before that finishes. Both the animation's step() and startPan's mv() write view.panX/panY every frame with no coordination, so the camera visibly fights between the two; worse, the rAF loop keeps running independently of the mouse listeners, so it can overwrite the user's manual pan and snap the camera back toward the original double-click target even after the user has released the mouse and the drag has already ended.

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 13): panTo()'s pan animation never persists the resulting camera position (persist.local() is never called), unlike its sibling panZoomToRect(). -- panZoomToRect's step() calls `if (!panAnim) persist.local();` once the animation settles (line 99); onWheel calls it directly (line 191); startPan's up() calls it when a drag moved (line 159). panTo's step() (lines 24-31) has no equivalent call anywhere in its body or after `panAnim = requestAnimationFrame(step);` (line 31) - it is the only view-mutating animation in this file that never reaches persist.local(). panTo is used exclusively by toolbox.js to auto-scroll a freshly created node into view when it lands off-screen (e.g. toolbox.js:47,54,61,68,75,82,88,96,114,121). After that auto-reveal, the new pan offset is silently dropped from the per-device viewport sidecar; the next profile load / page reload snaps the camera back to whatever was last explicitly saved, discarding the reveal the user just saw.

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 147): startPan hand-rolls its own mousedown->mousemove->mouseup drag loop instead of reusing dragresize.js's beginDrag, the shared primitive hard rule 7 calls out by name. -- startPan (lines 147-163) reimplements exactly the pattern beginDrag already provides: a threshold-gated 'moved' flag via Math.hypot, onMove-style per-frame delta application, and cleanup on mouseup with a 'settled' callback. The one real difference is that startPan is wired for the RIGHT mouse button (main.js:4089, ev.button===2), while beginDrag hardcodes `if (ev.button != null && ev.button !== 0) return () => {};` (dragresize.js) - left button only - so beginDrag cannot be used here unmodified. That said, the fix is a one-line parameterization (an optional `button` option on beginDrag, default 0) rather than a genuine behavioural fork, which is exactly the case hard rule 7 says to hoist rather than copy: 'the second copy diverges, later features land on only one, behavior drifts.' As written, any future improvement to the shared drag loop (e.g. the finding above about cancelling a competing pan animation) has to be remembered and re-applied here separately, which is how the cancelPan() gap above happened. [Hard rule: 7]


_Module notes: Focused review of the 3 assigned files only; camera.js's persist.local()/cancelPan() findings are anchored on grepping ALL call sites of those two functions across the whole static/ tree (3 and 3 respectively, all inside camera.js) to confirm no other code path covers the gap. All exported symbols from all three files have live call sites elsewhere in the repo (confirmed via grep), so none of the three files are dead code, in whole or in part. No hard-rule 1/2/3/4/5/6/8/9 violations found in these three files (no polling-redrawn lists, no dialogs, no inputs, no row-capped tables, no dataset references, not .ps1, no Claude/Anthropic mentions, no date/time formatting). The one rule-7 finding (startPan) is flagged with its caveat (button-gating genuinely differs) so it isn't mistaken for a naive duplicate-code false positive._

## Module: j10-panels-precap-livewin  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/precap.js

**Purpose:** Implements the precapture floating panel/widget: record a session of game-window frames, process them through the OCR pipeline, stage results per dataset, and commit (save) them into the real datasets. Also owns the session list (new/load/rename/delete/clear, all via inline input or armed two-click, never a blocking dialog), the frame thumbnail grid + full-size lightbox frame viewer, and the 'choose stashed captures' picker modal (openCaptureModal) used to bind a window's image pages. If deleted: the precapture topbar panel and its pretty-view embed lose their mount point (buildPrecap/mountPrecap/showPrecap/hidePrecap all disappear), the capture-picker modal used from imaging.js breaks, and livewin.js's live/precapture mutual-exclusion checks (precapOpen/precapBusy) become undefined.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports pc, pcState, buildPrecap, fmtBytes; calls buildPrecap() at boot; wires precapBtn click to pc.setVisible); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/livewin.js (imports pc, precapOpen, precapBusy, fmtBytes for one-OCR-consumer-at-a-time mutual exclusion with live mode); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js (imports openCaptureModal for the window-binding image picker); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/panel.js (imports mountPrecap, showPrecap, hidePrecap as the pretty-view embed adapter for the 'precap' panel option)

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 313): renderPrecapData() unconditionally re-appends every dataset section on every poll tick instead of only moving it when it's out of place, contradicting both its own docstring and the correct pattern used elsewhere in this same file. -- Inside renderPrecapData's `for (const d of datasets)` loop: `dataEl.appendChild(e.section);   // (re)append in server order` runs every single call, unconditionally, even when e.section is already the correctly-positioned child of dataEl. Per DOM spec, appendChild on an already-attached node still executes a remove+insert, which can blur a focused descendant and forces a reflow — exactly the anti-pattern CLAUDE.md rule 1 calls out by name ('do NOT call appendChild/insertBefore on a reused, already-attached node every tick to set order... that detaches+reattaches it'). The function's own comment claims it is 'reconciled IN PLACE... so the 700ms poll doesn't churn the tables or reset scroll/search', but the reorder step doesn't gate on position at all, unlike the correctly-guarded sibling code in this very file: renderPrecapLeft (`const at = left._rowsBox.children[i]; if (at !== r.row) left._rowsBox.insertBefore(...)`, line 756-757). Concrete failure: while a precapture session is processing (st.datasets non-empty), renderPrecap -> renderPrecapData runs on every hub heartbeat; a user typing into the VTable's search/filter input for a staged dataset (VTable is described elsewhere as 'virtualized + searchable') would have that input's containing section detached/reattached on every tick, interrupting typing/losing focus, while datasets/rows update live. [Hard rule: 1]

- **[MEDIUM] dead-code** (line 516): renderPrecap's close-button tooltip logic targets `.modal`/`.modal-x`, but pcNode is never mounted inside an openModal()-built `.modal` — it only ever lives in a floatwin body or a pretty-widget host — so the block is unreachable and its documented behavior never happens. -- `const x = node.closest(\".modal\")?.querySelector(\".modal-x\"); if (x) x.title = precapBusy ? \"close & cancel the run (Esc)\" : \"close (Esc)\";` — `.modal`/`.modal-x` are classes created only by openModal() (modal.js:33-34), a separate component used elsewhere (e.g. openCaptureModal in this same file, and other dialogs). The precapture panel itself (pcNode/node here) is mounted via createFloatWin (floatwin body, no `.modal` ancestor) or via the pretty-widget NOOP_ADAPTER host (also no `.modal` ancestor) — confirmed by tracing mountPrecap's call sites in main.js and pretty/widgets/panel.js. `x` is therefore always undefined and the `if (x)` branch never executes. This is stale logic left over from a prior modal-based implementation (the file header comment even notes precapture is 'A floating panel... rather than a modal'); the intended UX ('closing always works now and cancels the run — say so on the button') silently never happens.

- **[LOW] hard-rule** (line 625): The `.pc-bar` stat rows and `.pc-recog` recognition chips are fully rebuilt via replaceChildren on every render tick regardless of whether their content actually changed, unlike the value-sig diffing used elsewhere in the same render pass. -- `bar.replaceChildren(...barRows)` (line 625) and `recogEl.replaceChildren(...rec.map(...))` (line 631-632) run on every renderPrecap call while the detail pane is busy, with no comparison against previous content (contrast with renderPrecapLeft's `if (e.label._meta !== meta)`/`if (r.meta._sig !== sig)` gating, or renderPrecapData's own `if (e._sig === sig) continue`). Low risk since these are non-interactive text chips (no focus to lose), but it is DOM churn on every poll tick in violation of the letter of rule 1 ('mutate the DOM ZERO times in steady state'). [Hard rule: 1]

- **[INFO] dead-code** (line 914): renderPrecap, renderPrecapData, renderPrecapLeft, fmtCaptureTime, and PC_PAGE are exported but not imported by any other module in the repo — only used internally within this file. -- Repo-wide grep for each identifier finds no import site outside precap.js itself (only internal recursive/self calls, e.g. _pcDraw -> renderPrecap, renderPrecapLeft -> renderPrecapLeft). Harmless (not literally dead — they're the file's own render entry points), but the exported surface is wider than any consumer needs; likely a leftover from before/around the 'extracted from main.js verbatim' refactor.

- **[INFO] dead-code** (line 642): Two small defensive checks target states that can no longer occur: disabling `.pc-opts input` while recording, and guarding `.pc-ctl` for a focused input. -- `right.querySelectorAll(\".pc-opts input\").forEach((i) => { i.disabled = recording; });` (line 642) — `.pc-opts` only exists in the `precapView === \"new\"` shape, but the \"record\" click handler (line 183) flips precapView to \"loaded\" immediately, so by the time `recording` can be true the `.pc-opts` inputs aren't even in the DOM; this line is effectively a no-op. Similarly the comment at line 669-671 ('don't clobber a live INPUT the user is editing (the clicks field)') guards `ctlEl.contains(document.activeElement) && ...matches(\"input\")`, but `.pc-ctl`'s only children are buttons (recBtn/proc/cancel/save) — no input ever lives there in the current markup, so the guard is vestigial. Neither causes incorrect behavior, just dead defensive code.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/livewin.js

**Purpose:** Implements the 'live' floating panel/widget: the live-mode master toggle and 'save to datasets' arm toggle, live stats (phase/saved/rate/rows), the polled+capped OCR debug log, the per-live-window detection-dot list, the server-side collector lifecycle (start/stop/adopt-on-reload/reflect-external-stop), the client-only read-only tuning loop (liveTick), and the shared `.ro-live` readout + `.live-wdot` painters reused by both this panel and the game node's window-priority rows. If deleted: live mode (the entire tuning/collection workflow, both the read-only client loop and the real server collector) and its panel/pretty-embed disappear; precap.js's mutual-exclusion call to setLiveMode would break; the game node's window-priority recognition dots (paintWdot/syncWpDots) would lose their data source.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports liveWin, liveWinState, buildLiveWindow, renderLiveWindow, syncLiveFromServer, applyLiveInterval, syncWpDots; calls buildLiveWindow()/syncLiveFromServer() at boot; wires liveBtn and the settings-modal frame-limiter field to applyLiveInterval); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js (imports renderLiveWindow, liveDetCount, liveRecog to drive the live detect loop's dot state); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/precap.js (imports setLiveMode to enforce one-OCR-consumer-at-a-time mutual exclusion when a recording/process run starts); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/panel.js (imports mountLive, activateLive, deactivateLive as the pretty-view embed adapter for the 'live' panel option)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 368): renderLiveReadouts() is called after the `if (!liveRoot || !active) return;` early-return in renderLiveWindow(), so `.ro-live` readout values on the graph only refresh while the live floating panel is open — they freeze whenever the panel is closed, even though the server collector keeps running and pushing new values via the heartbeat. -- renderLiveWindow(): `syncWpDots();   // game-node priority dots update even when the live PANEL is closed (collector runs via hub)\n if (!liveRoot || !active) return;\n ...\n renderLiveWinList();\n renderLiveReadouts();\n fitLivePanelHeight();`. syncWpDots() is deliberately hoisted ABOVE the early return, with a comment explaining exactly why: the collector runs server-side via the hub and its visuals must update independent of whether the panel is shown. renderLiveReadouts() needs the identical property (it also reads `liveColStatus?.readouts`, populated by the same subscribeCollector() heartbeat callback that fires 'even when the live PANEL is closed'), yet it sits below the early return and is skipped whenever `active` is false. This also contradicts node_parts.js's own comment on the readout node markup: `.ro-live` is 'updated in place from the heartbeat' (node_parts.js:508) with no caveat about needing the live panel open. Concrete failure: arm live collection (save-to-datasets on), close the live floating panel (or never open it — e.g. drive live purely from a pretty-view toast/trigger wired to a readout), and any readout-node display, or a toast/trigger depending on the same `.ro-live` value elsewhere on the graph, stays stuck at its last value ('—' if never opened) instead of tracking the running collector.

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 193): collapsible() is a second, independently hand-rolled 'chevron header toggles a hidden body' primitive, duplicating pretty/panels/inspector.js's section() (same shape: caret + click-header + body.hidden, remembered open/closed state) without any shared module between them. -- `function collapsible({ title, open = true, headExtra = null, headTitle = \"\", bodyClass = \"\" }) { const chev = h(\"span\", { class: \"clps-chev\" }, open ? \"▾\" : \"▸\"); const head = h(\"button\", { class: \"clps-head\", title: headTitle }, chev, h(\"span\", { class: \"clps-title\" }, title), headExtra); const body = h(\"div\", { class: \"clps-body\" + ... }); if (!open) body.hidden = true; return { section: ..., head, body, chev }; }` plus its sibling `setCollapsed(chev, body, on)`. pretty/panels/inspector.js's `section(key, label, onReset)` (inspector.js:62-74) builds the same conceptual widget — a caret span, a clickable header, a body toggled via `.hidden`, remembered open/closed state, click handler flips it — just with different class names (`pw-insp-*` vs `clps-*`) and an extra reset-button slot instead of livewin's headExtra slot. Per CLAUDE.md rule 7, a second instance of a UI primitive like this must be built by extracting the first into one shared parameterized primitive that both call, not hand-rolled again with renamed identifiers — the exact anti-pattern the rule names ('If you find yourself pasting a block and renaming identifiers, you're doing it wrong'). Neither is broken on its own, but the pattern will now drift further each time either is touched (e.g. inspector.js already supports a reset button that livewin.js's version lacks, and vice versa with headExtra). [Hard rule: 7]

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 635): setLiveSave(false) fires stopServerCollect() without awaiting it before starting the client-side liveTick() loop, unlike the arm-on path a few lines above and applyLiveInterval, which both explicitly await the teardown before restarting. -- `if (on) { stopServerCollect().then(startServerCollect); }   // await teardown, then (re)start\n else { stopServerCollect(); liveTick(); }                  // back to read-only tuning` — the disarm branch calls `stopServerCollect()` (a promise, per its own comment: 'the server stop joins the worker before responding -> await this before any restart so a re-arm... can't race the teardown') but does not await or .then() it before immediately invoking `liveTick()`, the client-side read-only detect loop. For a brief window both the still-tearing-down server collector and the newly-started client tuning loop can be issuing OCR/detect calls concurrently — the exact 'one OCR consumer at a time' invariant the rest of the file (and precap.js's precapBusy gate) is built around. Likely low-impact/self-correcting since the server side finishes shortly after, but it is inconsistent with the awaited-teardown discipline applied everywhere else in this same function and in applyLiveInterval.

- **[INFO] efficiency** (line 225): startDebugPoll()/pollDebug() can be re-entered while a fetch is mid-flight, briefly issuing two concurrent /api/live/debug requests, though the result is harmless since renderDebugEntries dedupes by seq. -- `pollDebug()` clears `dbgTimer = null` at its very start (before the fetch resolves) and only reschedules the next timer in its `.finally()`. If `syncDebugPoll()`/`startDebugPoll()` is invoked again during that in-flight window (e.g. the OCR-log section is toggled closed/open rapidly, or setLiveMode toggles quickly), `startDebugPoll`'s guard `if (dbgTimer || !dbgOpen || !active || !liveOn) return;` will pass again (dbgTimer reads null) and fire a second concurrent `pollDebug()`. Any duplicate/overlapping entries are silently absorbed by `renderDebugEntries`'s `if (dbgRows.has(e.seq)) continue;` check, so there is no visible defect — just an occasional redundant network call in a narrow, rare race.


_Module notes: Both files are actively wired at app boot (buildPrecap()/buildLiveWindow() in main.js) and consumed by imaging.js, pretty/widgets/panel.js, and each other (livewin.js <-> precap.js for one-OCR-consumer-at-a-time mutual exclusion) — neither is dead code. The strongest findings are: (1) precap.js's renderPrecapData does an unconditional appendChild-to-reorder every poll tick, a direct instance of the hard-rule-1 anti-pattern the codebase otherwise carefully avoids (contrast with the correctly-guarded renderPrecapLeft/renderLiveWinList in the same module pair); (2) livewin.js's renderLiveReadouts is misplaced after the early-return that syncWpDots was deliberately hoisted above for the identical reason, so readout-node displays go stale whenever the live panel is closed during an active collector run — a real, user-visible correctness gap in the recently-added readout/toast/trigger feature; (3) livewin.js's collapsible() duplicates pretty/panels/inspector.js's section() primitive (rule 7) with no shared implementation between them. No hard-rule 2/3/6 issues found (no blocking dialogs, no spellcheck, not .ps1), and rule 4 (row caps) and the core rule-1 pattern are correctly implemented in the majority of both files' poll-driven lists (session list, live window list, debug log) — the violations found are localized, not systemic.</notes>

## Module: j11-panels-nodemap-datanodes-stats  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/nodemap.js

**Purpose:** Implements the two floating overview panels ('node map': a scaled SVG of every node+edge with a draggable viewport indicator, and 'node list': a collapsible edge-tree outline grouped by super-group/group). Also builds the shared world-coordinate map model (nmMapModel) and the reusable SVG-building function (nmBuildMapSvg) used both by the live panel and by the standalone screenshot exporter (nodemapShot, consumed by toolbox.js). Deleting it would remove the node-map/node-list buttons' functionality entirely and break the graph screenshot feature (toolbox.js) and the live viewport indicator wiring in camera.js.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports nmState, nlState, buildNodeMap, buildNodeList, setNodeMapVisible, setNodeListVisible, nmSyncSelection, renderNodeViews); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/camera.js (imports nmUpdateViewport, called every pan frame); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js (imports nodemapShot for the screenshot export feature)

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule**: Sub-group boxes are drawn on the node-map but are neither labelled nor clickable, unlike groups/super-groups (incomplete extraction under hard rule 7's spirit of one shared, complete primitive). -- nmBuildMapSvg builds `subSvg` from `groups.subGroupBoxes()` (rects tagged `data-subid`, see line ~226), but `titleSvg` (line ~250-251) only maps over `superGroupBoxes()` and `groupBoxes()` -- subgroup boxes never get a title plate drawn, so a sub-grouped profile's map (and its screenshot export) shows unlabeled rectangles for every subgroup. Separately, `wireJump` (line ~78-103), shared by both the map and list panels, only checks `ev.target.closest('[data-sgid]')` and `'[data-gid]'`; it has no `'[data-subid]'` branch, so clicking a sub-group's rectangle on the node-map falls through to the plain `[data-id]` lookup, finds nothing, and silently does nothing (no zoom, no feedback) -- while the equivalent super-group/group rectangles correctly `panZoomToRect`. This looks like sub-groups were added to the geometry model (groups.js/subGroupBoxes) without updating the map's two other concerns (labelling + click-to-zoom) to match. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[MEDIUM] reuse**: nmFit + NM_COLOR are re-implemented, not shared, in minimap.js, and have already drifted between the two copies (hard rule 7: a second instance of a primitive must be extracted, not copy-pasted). -- src/oc/web/static/js/graph/minimap.js defines its own `fit()` (line 25-30) with the exact same formula/constants (CW=0.58, PAD=0.86, cap 11) as `nmFit` here (line 58-63), and its own `NM_COLOR` table (line 10-14) instead of importing nmColor/NM_COLOR from this module -- minimap.js's own comment even says 'matches the live minimap's nmFit so previews look the same', acknowledging the duplication. The two color tables have already diverged: minimap.js's NM_COLOR is missing the 'itemfield' key present here (so itemfield nodes in a backup preview silently fall back to the default gray instead of the intended color) and has an extra 'trigger' key this file doesn't define. This is exactly the drift hard rule 7 warns a copy-pasted-and-isolated primitive will accumulate. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[INFO] dead-code**: Several exported symbols (nmColor, nodeLabel, nodeShort, nmFit, nm, nl, nmTransform, nmRenderMap, nmFitPanelHeight, nmRenderList, graphBox) are not imported anywhere else in the repo. -- grep across src/oc/web/static shows none of these names are imported by any other module; they are used only internally within this file (e.g. nodeLabel is called from nmRenderList, nmRenderMap from renderNodeMap). Not dead code -- each is exercised via the internal call graph reachable from the genuinely-imported entry points (buildNodeMap/buildNodeList/renderNodeViews/nmUpdateViewport/nodemapShot) -- just an over-broad export list. No functional impact; flagging only for completeness.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/datanodes.js

**Purpose:** Renders the inline records table (VTable) and the batches ledger (list + per-batch event/preview detail) that live inside a dataset/subset node's 'vttable' satellite. Owns the observation and view-row drill-downs (expandObservations/expandSubsetRow), the batch revert/remove controls, and the single-flight-guarded fetch+render paths (refreshDataNode/refreshDatasetNode/loadBatchesNode) that keep those satellites in sync with the server on every live dataset change. Deleting it would remove all dataset-node data/batches functionality from the graph UI (no records table, no ledger, no revert/remove, no drill-down).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports vtables, vtableFor, refreshDataNode, refreshDatasetNode, expandSubsetRow, collapseVtablesExcept, batchesState, batEls, loadBatchesNode); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js (imports refreshDataNode, loadBatchesNode); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/precap.js (imports vtables, vtableFor, refreshAllDataNodes, refreshAllBatchesNodes)

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 353): The batch-detail 'preview' table (what applying a batch would change) has no row cap, unlike the events list next to it -- a bulk-import/replay batch can render thousands of raw <tr> elements inline in a graph node. -- renderBatchDetail (line ~340-370) builds `pvTable` via the plain `dataTable(cols, rows, ...)` helper over the ENTIRE `bd.preview` array with no slicing -- contrast with the events list right above it, which correctly goes through the virtualized VTable (`vtableFor('bat:'+ds, evHost)`). The project's own history shows bulk writes of tens of thousands of records in a single transaction (see 'record_many' bulk-write optimization), so a batch produced by a first full collection sweep or a ledger replay is exactly the case Hard Rule 4 exists for ('A node is a preview, not a data grid... 'Show all' => virtualize, never dump every row'). Opening such a batch's detail would build a many-thousand-row plain <table> inside the node's satellite panel. [Hard rule: 4]

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 113): The single-record observation drill-down and the subset-row source drill-down also render via the uncapped `dataTable` helper, with no cap on observation-history length. -- expandObservations (line 113-127) and srcBlock/expandSubsetRow (line 131-175) both feed arbitrarily long arrays (`obs`, matched rows) straight into `dataTable`, which emits one <tr> per row with no DS_ROW_CAP-style slice. A field that churns often (noisy OCR value bouncing between reads over a long multi-day collection run) can accumulate a large observation history for one key; opening that record's drill-down row would render the whole history inline. Same class of issue as the batch preview table above, just lower expected row counts in the common case. [Hard rule: 4]

- **[MEDIUM] resource-leak** (line 107): The error paths in `_refreshDataNode` and `_refreshDatasetNode` drop a VTable from the `vtables` map without calling `.destroy()`, unlike the normal host-switch path in `vtableFor`, leaking its ResizeObserver. -- `vtableFor` (line 34-42) is careful to call `vt.destroy()` before replacing a cached VTable when the host element changed. But both `_refreshDataNode`'s catch block (`vtables.delete(\`ds:${ds}\`); host.replaceChildren(...)`, line 107) and `_refreshDatasetNode`'s catch block (line 403) just delete the map entry and repaint the host, never calling `.destroy()` on the abandoned VTable instance. Every VTable unconditionally registers a ResizeObserver in its constructor (`this._roDispose = observeResize(this.scroll, ...)`, vtable.js line 199) that is only released by `destroy()`. A transient fetch/JSON error while a dataset's records or batches satellite is open (e.g. a flaky network blip, or the dataset briefly disappearing during a rename) leaks that observer and everything it closes over indefinitely.

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 179): `showRecordMany` (and the `openModal` import that exists solely for it) is unreachable from anywhere in the app. -- The function's own comment (line 177-178) says '(kept as an alternative to the inline drill-down above; not currently wired)'. A repo-wide grep for `showRecordMany` finds only its definition and its export -- no import, no call site anywhere in src/oc/web/static. Its sole reason for importing `openModal` (line 6) is this dead function.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/stats.js

**Purpose:** Implements the 'stats' floating panel: polls /api/stats/{game} every 2.5s for per-node/op timing rollups (count, last/avg/min/max ms, last-seen), grouping cards under per-window or per-op headings with collapse state and a per-window Sigma(last)/Sigma(avg) footer, flags slow replay/recompute ops, and lazily fetches+draws a duration-history line chart per card on first expand. Rows/headings/footers reconcile in place via keyed Maps (statsRows/statsHeads/statsFoots), matching the canonical poll-driven-DOM pattern. Deleting it would remove the entire stats panel and its topbar toggle button.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports statsWin, statsState, buildStats)

- **[LOW] error-handling** (line 116): pollStats() silently swallows fetch/parse failures with `.catch(() => {})`, leaving the panel stuck on its 'building...' spinner (or stale rows) with no visible error, unlike loadHistory's 'failed' placeholder for the same class of error. -- pollStats (line 108-117) does `fetch(...).then(...).then(...).catch(() => {})` -- any network error or non-JSON response is dropped with no user feedback. Contrast with `loadHistory` (line 371-390), which shows a 'failed' placeholder on the same kind of error. If the stats endpoint is down when the panel is first opened, the 'building...' spinner (showSpin, line 101-106) never resolves and never reports a failure; it just silently retries every 2.5s forever. Low impact since this is a diagnostic-only panel, not a data-integrity path.

## Module: j12-panels-activity-toolbox-dbstruct-testing  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js

**Purpose:** Floating "tasks" panel: lists every running background job for the current game (price sweeps, precapture worker, triggers) fed by the shared heartbeat hub (hub.js), with cancel/fire/enable controls; also independently drives every trigger node's 'last fired' label and the one-shot sound playback for a fresh trigger fire (regardless of whether the panel is open). If deleted: the tasks button/panel and its embed in the pretty-view widget disappear, trigger node last-fired labels stop updating, and trigger sound playback (funneled here) is lost entirely.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports act, actState, buildActivity; wires activityBtn click, _PANEL_TOGGLES/_NODE_PANELS entries "activity"); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/panel.js (imports mountActivity, activateActivity, deactivateActivity as the ADAPTERS.activity entry backing the pretty 'Embed: tasks' widget)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 246): triggerFireSeen (module-level Map) is never scoped to, or reset on, the current game, so switching game profiles via the dropdown can replay a stale fire as a fresh one and play an unwanted sound. -- loadGame() (main.js ~3513-3582) switches the active profile in place (no page reload) and calls hub.kick() at the end, but never clears activity.js's module-scope caches. detectFires() (lines 253-266) plays a trigger's sound target the moment `t.last_fired` differs from what triggerFireSeen last recorded for that trigger id — and this comparison runs unconditionally via the permanent `hub.subscribe((s) => updateTriggerNodes(s))` set up once in buildActivity() (line 65), independent of whether the tasks panel is even open. Default trigger ids are assigned by model.addTrigger() (model.js:604-609) as "trigger", then "trigger_2", etc. — the same default sequence for every game profile, so it's common for two different game profiles to each have a trigger literally named "trigger". If the user has two such profiles, each with that trigger having fired at some point (different last_fired timestamps), switching from game A to game B (dropdown, no reload) makes detectFires() see prev=A's timestamp, ts=B's timestamp, treat it as a brand-new fire, and play B's trigger's sound target immediately after load — even though nothing in game B actually just fired.

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 365): actCancelReq (optimistic 'cancelling…' set) is likewise never scoped to/reset for the current game, so a stale entry can permanently disable a same-keyed sweep's cancel button in a different profile. -- actCancelReq (line 45) is keyed by job key (e.g. `sweep:<dataset>`) and is only cleared when the backend confirms cancelling (line 364, `if (j.action?.cancelling) actCancelReq.delete(j.key)`) or when the row's key stops appearing (line 294). If the user cancels a sweep on dataset "mods" in game A, then switches (without reload) to game B which also has a dataset named "mods" and starts a fresh (non-cancelled) sweep on it, renderActivity's `busy = !!j.action?.cancelling || actCancelReq.has(j.key)` (line 365) stays true forever for that row — the cancel button is stuck disabled/"cancelling…" even though the new sweep was never cancelled, until the row's key happens to disappear.

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 148): stopActivityPoll / startActivityPoll back-compat aliases have zero callers anywhere in the repository. -- Lines 148 and 187 alias deactivateActivity/activateActivity "for back-compat with any external callers", and are re-exported at line 385-386, but a repo-wide grep for both names finds no import/usage outside this file's own export statement — every real caller (main.js, pretty/widgets/panel.js) already uses deactivateActivity/activateActivity directly. Harmless, but the aliases can be deleted.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js

**Purpose:** Floating "toolbox" panel: the create* functions here mint every top-level node type (window, producer, trigger, file source, toast, sound, dictionary, dataset, subset) and are wired to the canvas's right-click add-node menu; the panel itself also hosts the window-collision cross-check and three screenshot exporters (whole canvas / current viewport / node map). Deleting it removes every way to create a new node of these kinds plus the collision checker and screenshot tools.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports tb, tbState, buildToolbox, createWindowNode, createProducerNode, createTriggerNode, createDictionaryNode, createFileSourceNode, createToastNode, createSoundNode, createDatasetNode, createSubsetNode; wires createBtn and the right-click add-node menu at ~4194-4202, plus _PANEL_TOGGLES/_NODE_PANELS entries "toolbox")

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 49): Seven near-identical copy-pasted node-creation functions (createProducerNode, createTriggerNode, createFileSourceNode, createToastNode, createSoundNode, createDatasetNode, createSubsetNode) violate CLAUDE.md rule 7 instead of sharing one parameterized primitive. -- Lines 49-122: each of these seven functions is the same 5-line body — mint an id via a model.addX() call, `await placeNewNode(`<prefix>:${id}`, "<kind>", null, at); render();`, optionally `groups.addToGroup(group, [...])`, `autosave(null)`, optionally `panTo(...)` — differing only in the model method, id prefix and node-kind string. CLAUDE.md rule 7 is explicit: "If you find yourself pasting a block and renaming identifiers, you're doing it wrong — hoist instead" and requires the SECOND instance of something to be built on a shared primitive extracted from the first. None of these seven calls a shared helper; the next node type added will almost certainly paste an eighth copy. A `createSimpleNode(prefix, kind, addFn)` (or similar) called 7 times with different arguments would satisfy the rule and remove ~50 duplicated lines. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 188): CANVAS_MAX_AREA is hardcoded equal to CANVAS_MAX_SIDE**2, which makes the area-based scale clamp in safeShotScale() mathematically always dominated by the two side-based clamps — it can never bind tighter, contradicting the comment's stated intent of protecting against browsers with a smaller area cap than Chrome's. -- Lines 187-196: for any w,h, sqrt(CANVAS_MAX_AREA/(w*h)) with CANVAS_MAX_AREA == CANVAS_MAX_SIDE**2 is always >= min(CANVAS_MAX_SIDE/w, CANVAS_MAX_SIDE/h) (provable algebraically), so the third Math.min() argument in safeShotScale() never changes the result versus the first two. The comment above ("~268M px total area on Chrome; less on others") implies the intent is to also cap for browsers with a smaller real area limit (e.g. Safari's much smaller canvas area cap), but the constant used is Chrome's number, so a wide/tall graph on such a browser can still pass this clamp and fail at actual canvas creation. Impact is contained (stashShot's `blob.size < 100` check at line 235 catches the resulting corrupt/empty PNG and reports a clear error), so this is a comment/behavior mismatch rather than a silent-corruption bug.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/dbstruct.js

**Purpose:** Floating "database" panel: shows the per-game SQLite store's shape (file + size, each physical table's row count/columns/indexes, and a per-dataset events/present/batches breakdown), refreshed on open and on any dataset-change push, with per-table clear, per-dataset clear/remove, whole-store drop, and a link to the DB-backups modal. Deleting it removes the only in-app way to inspect or directly manage the raw store.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports dbWin, dbState, buildDBStruct; wires dbstructBtn click, _PANEL_TOGGLES/_NODE_PANELS entries "dbstruct")

- **[INFO] simplification** (line 124): Empty no-op if-branch left in render(). -- Lines 124-126: `if (!datasets.length && !dsRows.size) { // leave the empty section; ... }` has no body — it does nothing and can be deleted without changing behavior; the explanatory comment could move to a plain code comment instead of a conditional.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/testing.js

**Purpose:** Floating "testing" panel: uploads a recorded screen-capture clip and step/plays it through the server's live frame source (one server-side VideoSource) so live-mode detect+OCR can be exercised with no game running, plus a capture-backend rate benchmark (grabs/s, distinct frames/s) via /api/bench. Deleting it removes the only in-app way to replay a clip through the OCR pipeline offline and the capture-rate benchmarking tool.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (imports testWin, testState, buildTesting; wires testingBtn click, _PANEL_TOGGLES/_NODE_PANELS entries "testing")

- **[INFO] simplification** (line 174): testRender, testStopPlay, testStartPlay and wireCaptureBench are exported but have no importer anywhere else in the repo. -- Lines 174-177 export these four alongside testWin/testState/buildTesting, but a repo-wide search shows only main.js imports {testWin, testState, buildTesting} — the other four are only ever called from within this same file. Harmless over-export, not dead code (the functions are exercised via buildTesting's internal wiring), but the export surface could be trimmed to the three actually consumed elsewhere.


_Module notes: All four files confirmed alive via concrete imports in main.js (and activity.js additionally via pretty/widgets/panel.js's ADAPTERS.activity for the pretty-view \"Embed: tasks\" widget). No blocking dialogs, no innerHTML, no spellcheck=true, no toLocale*/locale-default date formatting, and no dataset-rename repoint logic found in any of the four files (rules 2/3/9/5 not applicable / compliant). Rule 1 (poll/timer DOM reconciliation) is followed correctly in both activity.js (renderActivity keyed Map + insertBefore-only-if-not-already-in-slot) and dbstruct.js (tableRows/dsRows keyed Maps, same reconcile pattern) — both explicitly modeled on the canonical renderWorkers/renderActivity pattern and both correctly implement the \"only move a row when not already at its slot\" rule. The two most substantive findings are: (1) activity.js's triggerFireSeen/actCancelReq module-level caches are never scoped to or reset for the current game, which loadGame()'s in-place profile switch (main.js) can expose as a spurious trigger-fire sound or a stuck cancel button when two profiles share a job/trigger id (very plausible given model.addTrigger()'s default \"trigger\"/\"trigger_2\" naming); and (2) toolbox.js's seven near-identical createXNode functions are a clear instance of the CLAUDE.md rule 7 anti-pattern (\"pasting a block and renaming identifiers\") that should be one parameterized helper._

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/producer_node.js

**Purpose:** Renders the 'producer' node's editor (an external HTTP-fetch data source: backend/method/URL/headers/query/timeout, per-item key-transform + source wiring OR list-mode 'explode' rows, response->column field mapping with array filter/pluck/aggregate) and wires its single fetch/cancel button + live sweep-status poll. This is the only UI for authoring producer nodes (e.g. warframe.market pricing, WFCD relic tables).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_parts.js:17 (import producerParts) and :819 (dispatched for n.type === "producer"); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:14 (import wireProducerNode) and :1432 (called when building a producer node's DOM)

- **[LOW] shadowing** (line 24): Local `const h` inside `elapsed()` shadows the imported hyperscript `h` from dom.js within that function's scope. -- Line 9 imports `h` (the DOM-builder) from dom.js; line 24 declares `const h = Math.floor(s / 3600)` inside the `elapsed` arrow function. Currently harmless because `elapsed()` only builds a template string and never calls the hyperscript `h(...)` internally, but it is a latent hazard: any future edit that adds DOM construction inside `elapsed()` (e.g. to render a richer duration widget) would silently get 'h is not a function' instead of building an element, since the local number shadows the import for the whole function body.

- **[INFO] style** (line 135): The 'name by' source-field `<option>` list omits an explicit `value` attribute, unlike the rest of the file's selects. -- `nfOpts` (line 135) builds options as `h("option", { selected: c === nf }, c)` with no `value:` key, relying on the browser default of using the option's text content as its value. Functionally equivalent to setting `value: c` explicitly (as every other `sel()`/typeSel/mapBlock option in this file does), so no bug, just a stylistic inconsistency worth normalizing if this block is touched again.

_Module notes: All five files are thin, declarative view-model 'Parts' renderers (state -> DOM via h()/frag()/svg() only, zero innerHTML) whose corresponding wiring lives in main.js; all are reached through node_parts.js's single nodeParts() dispatch table keyed by n.type, so none are dead code and none can be judged dead by a same-file-only grep. No hard-rule violations found: no prompt/confirm/alert anywhere; no explicit spellcheck=\"true\"; no locale-default date/time formatting (producer_node.js's `elapsed()` is a stopwatch-style running-duration format, mm:ss/h:mm:ss, not a calendar/clock display, so it's out of datefmt.js's remit rather than a violation); the one poll loop (wireProducerNode's status poll) is self-terminating on `document.contains(div)` and only ever touches specific elements/classes (.enr-sum/.enr-prog/button state), never rebuilding a list, so it is compliant with the poll/timer DOM-reconciliation rule; dataset/producer/source id repointing on rename is centralized in model.js's `_datasetSites()`/`_targetSites()` (verified pn.sources, s.sources, t.watch/dataset_targets/dataset_dest, t.targets, x.sources are all covered) so none of these files hand-roll a repoint loop; shared primitives (slideToggle, srcChip/srcInputs, labCell, TRASH) are reused rather than duplicated. Every domain-specific string constant in these files (producer AGG_OPS/FILTER_OPS, source SourceField method/anchor/stop/end/delim/index/path keys, toast STYLES/ALIGNS/DURATIONS, trigger KINDS/DS_ACTIONS) was cross-checked against its backend counterpart (oc/enrich/http_producer.py, oc/source/extract.py + oc/profile/models.py, the installed `toasted` package's enums via oc/notify/windows_toast.py, and oc/collect/triggers.py) and matches exactly — no silent-mismatch bugs found. All findings reported are low/info severity (a harmless variable-shadowing hazard and three stale/incomplete comments or tooltips); nothing rose to a correctness bug I'd flag as high or critical._

## Module: j14-graph-misc  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/backups.js

**Purpose:** Renders the PROFILE-backups browser (snapshot list + mini-map preview + armed restore) either standalone in a modal or embedded as a section of the settings modal. Deleting it would remove the ability to browse/restore prior profile YAML snapshots from the UI.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:22 (import { buildBackups }); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:4077 (buildBackups(bkHost, name, ...) inside the settings modal)

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule**: Restore button hand-rolls the armed two-click dance instead of using the shared armbtn.js primitive, and drops its auto-disarm safety net. -- Lines 65 and 90-106: `armed` is a plain closure variable and the click handler manually toggles textContent/class between 'restore this version' and 'click again to restore'. Unlike armbtn.js's armedButton (which this file's own twin, dbbackups.js, correctly uses), there is no timeout that auto-disarms the button after a few seconds. `armed` is only reset to null when the user selects a *different* backup row (line 69); if they stay on the same row after arming it, the button stays armed indefinitely. A user who single-clicks 'restore this version', gets distracted, and returns later intending to arm it (thinking it's their first click) will instead fire the actual restore on that click. Rule 7 says a second instance of a primitive (here, the armed-destructive-button pattern) must be built on the shared one -- armbtn.js exists specifically for this and dbbackups.js (committed alongside/after this file, per its own comment 'the twin of backups.js') already uses it; backups.js was never migrated to it. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 111): openBackupsModal is exported but never called anywhere in the repo. -- Lines 110-116 define and export `openBackupsModal`, described in the file's own top comment as being used 'both standalone (openBackupsModal) and as a settings-modal section'. A repo-wide grep for `openBackupsModal` only turns up its own definition and that comment -- main.js calls `buildBackups` directly inside the settings modal (line 4077) instead of this wrapper. The comment is now stale.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/dbbackups.js

**Purpose:** Renders the DATABASE-backups browser (SQLite store snapshots: list + meta + 'backup now' + armed restore), the data-side twin of backups.js. Deleting it removes the ability to snapshot/restore a game's collected-data store from the UI.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/dbstruct.js:13 (import { openDbBackupsModal }); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/dbstruct.js:51 (bkBtn click -> openDbBackupsModal(game(), refresh)); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/dbbackups.js:12 (imports armbtn.js's armedButton, the shared primitive -- correct usage, contrast with backups.js)

- **[INFO] clarity**: Post-restore status message reads ambiguously. -- Line 97: `statEl.textContent = \`restored ${since(m.iso)}\`;` uses the age of the *restored snapshot itself* (e.g. 'restored 3h ago'), not the time the restore action just happened (which is 'just now'). A user could misread this as saying the restore happened 3 hours ago rather than that they just restored the 3-hour-old version. Purely cosmetic, not a functional bug.

Deleting it would remove the '+ dictionary' picker UI (toolbox.js would have nothing to call).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js:11 (import { openDictionaryPicker }); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js:90 (openDictionaryPicker({...}) call)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/persist.js

**Purpose:** The single funnel for all graph persistence: debounced profile PUTs (content vs. pure layout), a debounced per-device viewport/minimap sidecar save, a one-time migration off old localStorage keys, and the scrub-hook seam Pretty Studio uses to keep transient override values out of the saved YAML. Deleting it would break autosave for every node edit, drag, resize, and viewport change.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:20 (import { initPersist, persist, setScrubHook }); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:295 (initPersist({...})); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/camera.js:7 (import { persist }, viewport pan/zoom saves); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/history.js:12 (import { persist }, undo/redo re-save); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js:8 (import { persist }); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/{activity,stats,nodemap,livewin,toolbox,precap,dbstruct,testing}.js (persist import for their own edits)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 133): Legacy `octbl:` (per-table column width) localStorage keys are not scoped per game, but the unconditional wipe at the end of migrateLegacy() runs on every game load -- so only the first game opened after upgrading actually keeps its migrated table widths; every other game's are silently lost. -- Lines 103-137: `octbl:<id>` keys (table.js's/vtable.js's old localStorage-era column-width store) were never namespaced by game -- only by table/dataset id. The import loop (lines 122-124, `for (const lk of localStorageKeys("octbl:")) ...`) only runs inside `if (rawStr && !hasLayout)`, i.e. only for the specific game currently being migrated. But the cleanup block at the bottom (lines 133-136, 'wipe the legacy keys regardless') deletes *every* `octbl:` key unconditionally on *every* call to migrateLegacy, regardless of whether this call's import branch ran. Concretely: a user with games A and B, both predating `profile.layout`, opens A first -- A's migration scoops up ALL `octbl:*` keys (including ones that really belong to B's tables) into A's `profile.layout.tables`, then wipes the whole `octbl:` prefix from localStorage. When B is opened afterward, `hasLayout` is still false for B, but there are no `octbl:` keys left to import -- B's old column widths are gone for good, and were never even written into B's own profile (they went into A's, unused since the table ids don't match A's own tables).

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 46): flushProfile()'s early-return when no profile name is loaded doesn't reset the pendingContent flag it was gated on. -- Lines 44-46: if `scheduleProfile(true)` set `pendingContent = true` before `M?.profile?.name` exists, the subsequent `flushProfile()` call (fired by the debounce timer) bails out at line 46 without clearing `pendingContent`. The flag would only be consumed/reset on a later successful flush, at which point `wasContent` could reflect a stale scheduling intent from before a profile even existed. In practice this looks unreachable, since content edits that call `persist.content()` are all gated behind the graph already being built from a loaded profile, so no concrete failure path was found -- flagged for completeness only.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/sound.js

**Purpose:** The one shared helper that plays a trigger sound (`/sounds/<name>`) in the browser, best-effort (missing file / blocked autoplay both silently no-op). Deleting it would break both the sound node's preview button and the toast/sound heartbeat fire-detector, which both call it as the single play funnel (rule 7).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:17 (import { playSound }), main.js:1757 (preview button); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js:15 (import { playSound }), activity.js:263 (heartbeat fire-detector)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/graph/table.js

**Purpose:** A `<table class="grid-table">` enhancer adding click-to-sort headers and drag-to-resize columns, with per-table state persisted through an injected store. Historically the mechanism behind sortable/resizable inline tables in the graph UI.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:18 (import { setTableStore }), main.js:293 (setTableStore(_tableStore) call -- wired but see dead_code_reason)

- **[HIGH] dead-code** (line 58): enhanceTable (and everything it exclusively calls: cellCmp, applySort, cycleSort, renderIcons, startColResize, measureColWidth) is unreachable -- no caller anywhere in the repo. -- Confirmed via a repo-wide grep for `enhanceTable`: the only match is its own definition (line 58). The module's persisted-store plumbing (setTableStore/loadState/saveState) is only ever read inside enhanceTable, so main.js's `setTableStore(_tableStore)` call (line 293) currently has no effect. vtable.js's VTable class has since taken over sortable/resizable table rendering for the same `<table class="grid-table">` styling.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 51): cellCmp's 'blanks always sink' rule is applied before the sort-direction multiplier, so blanks float to the TOP in a descending sort instead of sinking, contradicting its own comment -- but currently unreachable (see dead-code finding above). -- Line 51: `if (sa === "") return 1;` (a sorts after b) is computed direction-agnostically, but `applySort` (line 132) does `cellCmp(...) * s.dir`. For a descending sort (`dir = -1`, which is the very first click per cycleSort), a blank-vs-value comparison of `cellCmp("", "5") = 1` becomes `1 * -1 = -1`, meaning the blank row sorts BEFORE '5' -- i.e. blanks land at the top of a descending sort, not the bottom as the comment '// blanks always sink' (line 51) promises. Would be a real, visible bug on any column mixing blank and non-blank values, but the function is currently dead code per the finding above, so there is no live impact today.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 33): Re-deriving (winId, itemId) from an itemCanvases key by splitting on the FIRST colon breaks if a window id itself contains a colon. -- Line 33: `const i = key.indexOf(":"); refreshItemBoxes(key.slice(0, i), key.slice(i + 1));` assumes the key's format `${winId}:${itemId}` (set in imaging.js:697) has no colon inside winId. Window/item ids are plain `id: str` in the pydantic models (src/oc/profile/models.py) with no character restriction enforced, so a window id containing a colon would make this split misattribute characters to itemId instead of winId, causing `model.item(wrongWinId, wrongItemId)` (inside refreshItemBoxes) to fail to find the item and silently skip redrawing its boxes after an undo/redo restore. The same fragile first-colon-split idiom is used elsewhere in the codebase (model.js:197/686, panels/stats.js:127), suggesting colon-free ids is an accepted repo convention rather than an isolated mistake here, which is why this is flagged low rather than higher.

Deleting it would break essentially every other graph module, which all import their shared mutable state from here.

- No issues found.


_Module notes: Read all 15 files in full plus cross-referenced every symbol they export against the whole src/oc/web/static tree (and Python side for any hardcoded game/window-name logic -- none found; these are all generic graph-editor infrastructure with zero game-specific knowledge). No hard-rule violations found for spellcheck (rule 3), dates (rule 9), row caps (rule 4), dataset-rename repointing (rule 5), or Claude/Anthropic mentions (rule 8) in this module. Two real findings stand out: (1) table.js's core function (enhanceTable) is dead -- superseded by vtable.js's VTable class -- with only the incidental setTableStore export still wired up; (2) backups.js hand-rolls the armed-restore-button dance instead of reusing armbtn.js (which its own twin dbbackups.js correctly uses), dropping the auto-disarm timeout safety net -- a clear rule-7 case. persist.js's legacy octbl: migration has a genuine (if bounded, one-time) multi-game data-loss bug. Also noticed (outside this module's file list, so not reported as a finding here) that main.js contains a literal NUL byte at offset 171387 inside a string literal (`[...model.datasets(), "\x00", ...]` used as an array-join sentinel) -- worth a look by whoever owns main.js/dataset-key code, since it made grep treat the file as binary._
