## Module: j15-pretty-canvas-chart  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js

**Purpose:** Renders one 'pretty' page's widgets into the studio surface: resolves each widget's box from its anchor chain (canvas or another widget, at one of nine points, plus optional size-match/scale), places/resizes the DOM frames every frame after a build/drag/resize/surface-resize, and in edit mode wires per-widget drag/resize/select plus draws the anchor-relationship cue overlay (SVG lines + labels showing anchor offsets, own dimensions, and width/height matches). Deleting it would remove the entire pretty-page rendering/editing surface - pretty.js has no other way to turn a page's widget list into DOM.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js (imports renderPage; GRID re-export at canvas.js:867 is NOT the source pretty.js uses - it imports GRID directly from ../graph/dragresize.js instead); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/inspector.js (imports POS_UNITS for the per-field unit <select>)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 280): reanchor() silently corrupts a widget's x/y field to NaN (and eventually JSON null) when that field's unit is "calc" -- reanchor(id, anchor) (lines 269-284) unconditionally computes `w.x = before && base ? r2(pxToLen(before.left - base.left, unitOf(w, "x"), ref.w)) : sx;` (line 280, mirrored for y on 281). pxToLen's "calc" branch (line 71) returns a STRING ("42.50px", meant to seed a fresh calc source), not a number. r2 = (v) => Math.round(v * 100) / 100 (line 36) then does string arithmetic on that string: Number-coercing "42.50px" fails (trailing non-numeric suffix) -> NaN. So w.x/w.y is overwritten with the literal number NaN, discarding the user's hand-authored calc expression (e.g. "100% - 20px"). Every OTHER geometry mutator in this same file explicitly guards against this: storeLen (lines 77-80, 'never overwrite a "calc" field'), setGeom (line 813, `if (unitOf(w,k)==="calc") w[k] = String(v)`), setUnit (line 838), and even the one caller of offsetsForBox in onResize (lines 751-752, `if (unitOf(widget,"x") !== "calc") widget.x = off.x;`) - reanchor is the single path that omits this guard despite computing the exact same kind of value via the exact same pxToLen. Reachable from ordinary use: the inspector's per-field unit picker (POS_UNITS, includes "calc") applies to x and y (panels/inspector.js geomGrid, GEOM_FIELDS includes x/y), and every control in appendAnchorRows (anchor-to select, "this" corner grid, "target" corner grid) calls ctx.setAnchor -> canvasCtrl.reanchor with no calc guard anywhere upstream (panels/inspector.js:353, pretty.js:93). ctx.requestSave() is called immediately after (line 283), and JSON.stringify(NaN) is "null", so the authored calc expression is permanently lost from the persisted page document, not just transiently wrong on screen.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 408): distLabel renders "NaN<unit>" for the anchor-cue distance label of a widget whose x/y unit is "calc" -- distLabel (line 408) is `${r2(Math.abs(w[k] ?? 0))}${unitOf(w, k)}`. When the field's unit is "calc", w[k] holds a raw CSS calc string (e.g. "100% - 20px"), and Math.abs(string) coerces via Number(), which fails on the non-numeric text -> NaN, so the cue chip shows e.g. "NaNcalc" instead of a usable distance. Cosmetic only (the anchor cue is a visual aid in edit mode), but it's the same unhandled-calc gap as the reanchor bug above, just non-destructive here since nothing is written back to the model.

- **[INFO] dead-code** (line 867): GRID re-export (and the unitOf export) are unused outside this file -- `export { GRID };` (line 867) re-exports graph/dragresize.js's GRID, but the only consumer of GRID in the repo (pretty.js:13) imports it directly from ../graph/dragresize.js, not from canvas.js - so this re-export has no importer. Similarly `export const unitOf` (line 37) is only ever referenced from within canvas.js itself (verified via repo-wide grep); no other module imports it. Neither is a functional bug (harmless unused export surface), just dead public surface worth trimming or intentionally leaving as a documented convenience.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/chart_draw.js

**Purpose:** Dependency-free SVG chart renderer: drawChart(host, spec) builds one <svg> (line/area/bar/scatter/pie/donut) from row data and replaces host's content. Used as the rendering core for the pretty studio's 'chart' widget and for the OCR run-duration sparkline in the stats panel. Deleting it would remove both of those chart displays entirely (no other charting code exists in the front-end).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/chart.js (imports drawChart, calls it from a keySubscription/observeResize-driven render()); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/stats.js (imports drawChart, calls it from renderChart(), itself gated behind a samples-signature check in loadHistory so a live poll never rebuilds an unchanged chart - satisfies hard rule 1 at the call site)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 42): A pie/donut chart with exactly one 100%-share slice renders as an empty circle (no visible slice) due to a degenerate SVG arc -- For pie/donut (lines 30-49), each slice's SVG arc runs from angle a0 to a1 = a0 + (v/total)*2*PI. When there is exactly one slice with v>0 (so v/total == 1), a1 == a0 + 2*PI, whose cos/sin coordinates are numerically identical to a0's (2*PI-periodic) - i.e. the arc's start point p(R,a0) and end point p(R,a1) are the same (x,y). Per the SVG arc-command spec, an elliptical arc whose endpoints coincide is treated as omitted entirely (no rendering), not as a full circle. So a pie/donut chart bound to data where only one category has a nonzero value (a very reachable case - a single-row dataset/subset, or a pie grouped by a field where only one bucket is populated) silently draws nothing instead of a solid circle/ring. big/large-arc-flag logic (line 39) doesn't help since the issue is coincident endpoints, not sweep direction.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 11): num() coerces an empty-string cell to the numeric value 0 instead of treating it as missing -- num = (v) => { const n = Number(v); return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : null; } (line 11). Number("") === 0 in JS, so a row whose bound field is an empty string (a plausible representation of an unset/blank value from a dataset or subset row) is coerced to a real 0 data point rather than skipped like null/undefined are. For line/area/scatter charts this plots a false zero (and stretches the y-axis lo/hi range to include 0) instead of leaving a gap, which can visibly misrepresent a trend when some rows are genuinely blank rather than legitimately zero.

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 21): Pie/donut with no y column configured bypasses the "no data" placeholder and silently falls back to charting the label column -- The initial empty-state gate (line 21) only fires `!ys.length && spec.type !== "pie" && spec.type !== "donut"`, i.e. it's skipped for pie/donut even when ys is empty. Line 31 then falls back to `const field = ys[0] || spec.x`, i.e. uses the x/label column as the value column. For a typical text label column this yields v<=0 for every row (Number(text label) is NaN), so `slices` ends up empty and nothing is drawn - but the user sees a blank chart rather than the "no data" message shown in every other empty-data case. Minor UX inconsistency rather than a functional break.


_Module notes: Both files are actively wired in: canvas.js's renderPage/POS_UNITS are imported by pretty.js and panels/inspector.js; chart_draw.js's drawChart is imported by pretty/widgets/chart.js and graph/panels/stats.js (the latter properly gates redraws behind a samples-signature check before calling drawChart, so no hard-rule-1 poll/DOM-thrash violation traces back into these two files - drawChart's full-SVG-rebuild-per-call design is fine given how every caller invokes it only on data/size change, matching its own header comment). No blocking dialogs, no spellcheck fields, no dataset-rename surface, no row-capped tables, no date/time formatting, and no Claude/Anthropic mentions in either file - none of hard rules 2/3/4/5/6/8/9 apply here. Rule 7 (shared primitives) is honored: canvas.js's drag/resize wiring calls the shared makeDraggable/addResizeGrips from graph/dragresize.js rather than rolling its own.

The standout finding is a real, traced data-corruption bug in canvas.js's reanchor() (used by every anchor-target/corner change in the inspector UI): it writes r2(pxToLen(..., unitOf(w,'x'), ...)) into w.x/w.y unconditionally, but every OTHER geometry mutator in the file (storeLen, setGeom, setUnit, onResize) explicitly special-cases the "calc" unit because pxToLen returns a STRING for that unit ("42.5px", meant to seed a fresh calc expression) rather than a number - reanchor is the one path that forgot the guard, so r2() does string arithmetic on a non-numeric-suffixed string, yields NaN, and overwrites the user's hand-authored calc expression with the literal number NaN (which then serializes to JSON null on the following ctx.requestSave(), permanently destroying the authored expression). Confirmed reachable: the inspector's per-field unit picker (POS_UNITS, includes "calc") applies to x/y, and every control in appendAnchorRows (anchor-to select, this-corner grid, target-corner grid) routes through ctx.setAnchor -> canvasCtrl.reanchor with no calc guard upstream either.

Chart_draw.js's pie/donut path has a classic single-slice SVG arc bug: when exactly one category holds 100% of the total, the arc's start and end angles are 2*PI apart, which map to the identical (x,y) coordinate (cos/sin are 2*PI-periodic) - per the SVG arc spec, a coincident start/end point arc is treated as an omitted (no-op) segment, so the "full circle" renders as nothing rather than a solid slice. Reachable any time a pretty chart widget is configured as pie/donut over data with only one non-zero category._

## Module: j16-pretty-core  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js

**Purpose:** Pretty Studio controller: mounts the canvas surface + studio panels into #pretty, owns transient state (edit/view mode, current page, selection, marquee, style/config preview) and wires model+canvas+panels+data through one shared `ctx` object every panel/widget reads. Deleting it would remove the entire Pretty Studio feature (nothing else builds the canvas surface, wires WASD-nudge/undo-redo keybinds, or drives page/widget selection).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:3742-3748 (dynamic import '../pretty/pretty.js'; calls isMounted/mountPretty/setPrettyGame/activatePretty/deactivatePretty); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:3561 (_pretty.setPrettyGame(name) on every game load while pretty view is active)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 182): setPrettyGame() never clears the inspector panel, unlike switchPage(), leaving it wired to a stale widget object from the previous game -- switchPage() (lines 395-401) resets selection AND calls `panels.inspector.clear()` before re-rendering. setPrettyGame() (lines 182-192), which does the same job across a GAME switch, only clears `selection`/`selectedId` - it never calls `panels.inspector.clear()`. panels/inspector.js keeps its own `current` widget reference (set by show(widget), inspector.js:28) captured in closures on its DOM field handlers, which call ctx.applyGeom(w.id,...)/ctx.restyle(w.id)/ctx.reconfig(w.id) (inspector.js:92,109,290,645,657) against that stale object, not a fresh lookup. After setPrettyGame() swaps in a new game's `pretty.doc`, `current` still points at a widget detached from that doc. Since widget ids are simple per-document auto-increment strings (model.js uid(), 'w1','w2',...), the first widget of almost any game is 'w1', so if the inspector was open when a game switch fires (e.g. graph/main.js:3561's un-awaited `_pretty.setPrettyGame(name)` while pretty view is active), it keeps showing the OLD game's widget and any edit made through it can silently apply to an unrelated, coincidentally-same-id widget in the NEW game.

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 315): Rubber-band marquee multi-select is a from-scratch reimplementation of the node graph's existing marquee, violating rule 7 (extract a shared primitive on the second instance) -- startMarquee() (lines 315-344, wired at surface.addEventListener('mousedown', startMarquee) line 165) attaches mousedown/mousemove/mouseup, draws a `.pw-marquee` div, computes AABB intersection against `canvasCtrl.boxes()`, and commits the selection on mouseup with shift for additive. graph/main.js already implements the identical primitive for the node graph: its own startMarquee() (around line 4124, wired at main.js:4091) does the same mousedown/mousemove/mouseup + rectangle div + AABB-intersect + commit-on-mouseup + additive-modifier dance (ctrl instead of shift, world-space transform instead of scroll offsets, but otherwise the same mechanics). Per CLAUDE.md rule 7 this second box-select implementation should have been extracted into one parameterized primitive (parallel to how dragresize.js/createFloatWin are already shared) instead of copy-pasted and adapted. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[MEDIUM] race-condition** (line 182): setPrettyGame() has no re-entrancy guard against overlapping calls for different games -- setPrettyGame() (182-192) is invoked un-awaited from graph/main.js:3561 on every game load while Pretty view is active. If it fires twice before the first `await papi.getPretty(g)` resolves (two fast game switches), whichever network response resolves last wins and calls pretty.load() with its own closured game/doc - not necessarily the most-recently-requested game. The module-level `game` variable is set synchronously at the top of each call, so `game` can end up naming one game while the doc actually loaded into `pretty.doc` (and rendered) belongs to an earlier-requested one, until the user switches again.

- **[LOW] consistency** (line 412): setMode('view') hides every studio panel but 'edit' never restores which ones were open -- setMode() (lines 412-420) force-hides every panel via p.win.setVisible(false) when entering view mode, but records nothing about which were visible; switching back to edit mode does not reopen any of them. Contrast with activatePretty()/deactivatePretty() (198-218) which explicitly track `_hiddenPretty` and restore exact prior visibility on return from the node-graph view. A user with e.g. the inspector and sources panels open who clicks 'view' then 'edit' gets both panels gone until manually reopened from the toolbar.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/model.js

**Purpose:** PrettyModel: the editable Pretty Studio document (theme + pages + widgets), its CRUD operations (add/remove/clone/rehome/rename widgets and pages, sharing a widget across pages), debounced persistence to <game>.pretty.yaml, and the serialize/restore hooks the undo/redo history snapshots against. Deleting it removes the entire data layer the Pretty Studio UI edits.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:9 (import), :29 (instantiated as module-level `pretty`)

- **[HIGH] data-loss** (line 12): A pending debounced save can be silently dropped if the game is switched within the 400ms window -- save() (26-30) starts a 400ms timer that calls flush() (31-35), which persists `this.doc` under `this.game` at fire time. load(game, doc) (12-16) - called by pretty.js's setPrettyGame() on every game switch - immediately reassigns `this.game` and replaces `this.doc` with the new game's document, but never cancels or fast-flushes any debounce timer left running from an edit made in the previous game. If a game switch happens within 400ms of an edit (setPrettyGame is invoked un-awaited from graph/main.js:3561 on every game load while pretty view is active), the pending flush() fires AFTER load() has already swapped in the new doc/game: it harmlessly re-saves the NEW game's own (already-current) doc, while the previous game's actual edit - already applied only to the in-memory object load() just discarded - is never sent to the backend. The edit is silently lost with no error surfaced to the user. The node-graph model already has the fix for this exact class of bug: graph/main.js:3516 does `await persist.flush();  // commit any pending save before switching games` before switching; PrettyModel has no equivalent flush-before-switch, and load() doesn't flush before overwriting `this.doc`/`this.game`.

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 158): removeWidget() never cleans up other widgets' anchor/matchW/matchH/{{widget:id}} references to the deleted id, and uid() immediately recycles the freed id -- renameWidget() (189-212) is careful to repoint every reference to a widget id (other widgets' anchor.to, matchW, matchH, and every {{widget:<id>}} token via walk()). removeWidget() (158-164) does none of that: it only splices the widget out of its home page's widgets array (or un-shares it) and saves - it never clears any OTHER widget's anchor.to/matchW/matchH pointing at the deleted id, nor any {{widget:<deletedId>}} text token. canvas.js does guard matchW/matchH resolution with `recs.has(...)` so a dangling reference is silently ignored while the id stays unused, but uid() (219-224) always hands out the LOWEST free `w<n>` slot, so deleting a widget frees its id for immediate reuse by the very next addWidget() call (e.g. delete the last widget 'w5', add a new one -> it is assigned 'w5' again). Any surviving anchor/match/token reference to the deleted id then silently re-attaches to the brand-new, unrelated widget with no warning - a widget can end up anchored to, or size-matched with, a completely different element than the one the user originally picked, purely because of id recycling.

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 214): PrettyModel.touch() has no call site anywhere in the repository -- touch() (line 214), documented as a 'mutate a widget then persist' helper, is never called from pretty.js, any panels/*.js, any widgets/*.js, or anywhere else in the repo (checked the whole static/js tree and the wider repo) - every actual mutation path already calls this.save() itself directly.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/binding.js

**Purpose:** Resolves the binding/token mini-language used throughout Pretty Studio: dataset:/subset:/node:/widget:/status/activity/page sources (with optional python-style row slices, aggregates, join, and decimal-format suffixes) to scalars for {{token}} dynamic text and conditions, and to row arrays for table/chart widget bindings; also enumerates every bindable source for the UI's source-picker dropdowns and reports which live subscribe-key a token depends on so pages re-evaluate reactively.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js:18 (resolveToken, subKeyForToken); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/inspector.js:10 (sourceTokenList, resolveToken, subKeyForToken); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/sources.js:13 (resolveToken); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/chart.js:4 (resolveRows, dataKeyForBinding); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/table.js:6 (resolveRows, columnsOf, dataKeyForBinding); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/util.js:7 (subKeyForToken, dataKeyForBinding); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/label.js:5 (renderDynamicText); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/container.js:4 (renderDynamicText)

- **[LOW] completeness** (line 29): sourceTokenList() omits the 'status:windows' token even though resolveToken() implements it -- sourceTokenList() (21-37) adds 'status:datasets' to the picker list but never adds 'status:windows', even though resolveToken()'s status branch (131-140) explicitly handles `rest === 'windows'` (returns `(st.windows || []).length`). The token still works if authored by hand or carried over from an older doc, but the UI's source picker never offers it, so a user building a binding through the UI can't discover it.


_Module notes: Core of the Pretty Studio dashboard builder: model.js is the editable document (theme/pages/widgets) + debounced persistence + undo/redo serialize hooks; pretty.js is the controller wiring model+canvas+panels and owning transient UI state (mode/page/selection/marquee); binding.js resolves {{token}} / binding sources (dataset, subset, node, widget, status, activity, page) to scalars/rows for dynamic text, conditions and table/chart widgets. All three are actively used (imported from graph/main.js and pretty/widgets|panels/*.js) - none are dead files. Found three high-confidence correctness bugs (a save silently dropped across a fast game switch, a stale inspector reference that can misapply edits to the wrong game's widget after a switch, and dangling anchor/matchW/matchH/{{widget:id}} references that can silently re-wire onto an unrelated widget because deleted widget ids get recycled by uid()), plus one clear hard-rule-7 violation (pretty.js reimplements the graph view's rubber-band marquee-select from scratch instead of extracting/sharing one primitive). Remaining findings are minor completeness/consistency/dead-code items._

## Module: j17-pretty-panels  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/inspector.js

**Purpose:** Builds the floating 'inspector' panel for Pretty Studio edit mode: the central widget-editing UI. Given the selected widget it renders layout (position/size/anchor), page-sharing, show/enable conditions, and profile-tabbed config + style editors, wiring every control to commit through ctx.pretty.save()/ctx.refresh()/ctx.reconfig()/ctx.restyle(). If deleted, a user could select a widget on the canvas but have no way to change its binding, type-specific config, conditions, layout or style — Pretty would be add/move-only.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:15 (import { buildInspector }); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:126 (inspector: buildInspector(ctx) in mountPretty); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js: panels.inspector.show/clear/syncGeom/refresh called from selectWidget/syncSelection/deselect/removeWidget/rehomeWidget/renameWidget/switchPage/restorePretty/setMode

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 450): Conditions section's header reset button uses the destructive full-refresh path that every other condition edit in the same function deliberately avoids -- Line 450: `section("conditions", ..., () => { w.conditions = { rules: [], visible_when: "", enabled_when: "" }; save(); })` calls the generic `save` defined at line 38 (`ctx.pretty.save(); ctx.refresh(); render();`). Every OTHER mutation inside conditionsEditor (add/remove a rule, change effect/source/op/value at lines 519-537) instead calls the local `recompile` (line 464: `compileConditions(w); ctx.pretty.save(); ctx.recondition(); render();`), and the comment directly above `recompile` (lines 460-463) explains why: `ctx.refresh()` destroys and recreates every widget on the page, which tears down any embedded panel (e.g. the live panel) -> unembed -> onHide -> stops the live collector. The reset-all-conditions button is the one path in this section that was never updated to use `recompile`, so clicking it (e.g. to clear a widget's conditions) can silently stop a live collection running elsewhere on the same Pretty page, exactly the regression the surrounding code was written to avoid for every other conditions edit.

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 22): Inspector keeps editing an orphaned widget object after the active game changes mid-session, silently discarding edits -- `current` (line 22) is a closure-local reference to the widget object passed to `show(widget)` (line 28); `clear()` (line 29, called elsewhere via ctx from removeWidget/rehomeWidget/switchPage) is the only way to null it out and blank the panel. `pretty.js`'s `setPrettyGame(g)` (called whenever the game changes while Pretty is the active view — see graph/main.js:3561 `if (prettyActive && _pretty) _pretty.setPrettyGame(name)`) clears `selection`/`selectedId` and reloads an entirely new `pretty` doc via `pretty.load(g, ...)`, but never calls `panels.inspector.clear()`. If a widget was selected (inspector open/showing it) at the moment the game is switched, `current` keeps pointing at the now-orphaned widget object from the PREVIOUS game's doc — it is no longer part of any page in the freshly loaded doc. The inspector DOM still shows its old fields; editing any of them (e.g. typing new label text, which calls the local `save()` at line 38) mutates the orphaned object and calls `ctx.pretty.save()`, which serializes the CURRENT (new-game) doc — the edit is silently lost with no error or visual indication that the panel is stale.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 388): The merged 'layout' section's reset button only clears the anchor, not geometry or size-matching, despite being labelled generically -- layoutEditor (line 387-392) groups the geometry grid (position/size/z), the anchor controls, AND the match-w/match-h rows under one collapsible section titled 'layout' with a single reset icon whose onReset (line 388) only does `ctx.setAnchor(w.id, { to: "", corner: "tl", target: "tl" })`. A user clicking 'reset layout' expecting position/size/match to also reset would see matchW/matchH and any custom x/y/w/h/z untouched. Minor UX mismatch between the section's label and its reset scope, not a functional break.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/sources.js

**Purpose:** Builds the floating 'sources' panel: a live, searchable catalogue of every bindable {{token}} — globals, dataset/subset fields, node inputs, and widget-published values — each shown with its current resolved value so users can discover/copy binding syntax without hunting through docs. If deleted, there would be no in-UI way to discover which tokens exist or what they currently resolve to; users would have to guess/hand-author binding strings.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:16 (import { buildSources }); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:127 (sources: buildSources(ctx) in mountPretty); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js: panels.sources.win visibility toggled by activatePretty/deactivatePretty/setMode

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 35): Sources panel is never refreshed after a mid-session game switch, so it can show a stale (previous-game) catalogue and values while open -- `render()`/`refresh()` (lines 103-165) is only invoked from the search input, the 'only with value' checkbox, a section's onToggle, or a data-key subscription firing (wireValueData, lines 60-68). `pretty.js`'s `setPrettyGame(g)` (invoked while Pretty is the active view on a game switch — graph/main.js:3561) does not call `panels.sources.refresh()`. If the sources panel is open at the moment the game changes, `m.datasets()`/`m.profile.subsets`/node inputs keep rendering the OLD game's names until the user manually toggles a section or the panel's onHide/onShow cycle (closing and reopening it) triggers a fresh render — a real, easily-reproduced staleness bug distinct from the elements panel, which IS refreshed automatically via renderCurrent().

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 103): render() fully tears down and rebuilds the entire sources tree on any single tracked key's change instead of reconciling per-row -- render() (line 103) always does `list.textContent = ""` then rebuilds every open section from scratch via fresh el()/makeRow() calls, and it is wired as the subscriber for EVERY required key (globals/datasets/subsets/status/activity — wireValueData, lines 60-68) via one shared `refresh` callback. A change to a single dataset (or the activity heartbeat ticking over) tears down and recreates rows for every OTHER open section too, not just the one whose data changed — the full-innerHTML-equivalent rebuild rule 1 targets, just implemented via textContent="" + rebuild rather than innerHTML=. It's mitigated by data.js only notifying on a real value change (so true steady state causes zero renders), but any single relevant change still discards/recreates unrelated rows (dropping in-flight visual state like the 600ms 'copied' flash) and does more DOM churn than necessary with many datasets/fields open. [Hard rule: 1]

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 91): Row click always flashes 'copied' even if the clipboard write actually failed -- Lines 91-95: `try { navigator.clipboard.writeText(token); } catch { /* */ } row.classList.add("pw-src-copied"); ...` — writeText returns a Promise that can reject asynchronously (e.g. clipboard permission denied, insecure context) without throwing synchronously, so the synchronous try/catch won't catch it; the 'copied' success flash fires unconditionally regardless of outcome, and a rejected promise with no attached .catch produces an unhandled-rejection console warning. Minor: the user can be told 'copied' when nothing was actually copied.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/theme.js

**Purpose:** Builds the floating 'theme' panel: exposes the Pretty document's theme object (the style every widget inherits by default) through the one shared styleEditor, so users set page-wide font/color/spacing defaults in one place. If deleted, there would be no UI to edit document-level style defaults; every widget would need its per-widget style set individually.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:17 (import { buildTheme }); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:128 (theme: buildTheme(ctx) in mountPretty); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js: panels.theme.win visibility toggled by activatePretty/deactivatePretty/setMode

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 12): Theme editor keeps editing the previous game's (now orphaned) theme object if the panel is open across a mid-session game switch -- refresh() (lines 12-14) only runs on the panel's own onShow hook (line 11) or whatever explicitly calls `panels.theme.refresh()`; `pretty.js`'s `setPrettyGame(g)` (fired on a game switch while Pretty is active — graph/main.js:3561) loads a brand-new doc via `pretty.load()` but never calls `panels.theme.refresh()`. If the theme panel is visible at the moment the game changes, the styleEditor instance mounted in `win.body` was bound to the OLD game's theme object reference (`ctx.pretty.theme()` captured at the time refresh() last ran); editing a control after the switch mutates that orphaned object and calls `ctx.pretty.save()`, which persists the current (new) doc unaffected — the edit is silently lost with no indication anything went wrong, same failure class as the inspector's stale-widget bug.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/palette.js

**Purpose:** Builds the floating 'widgets' palette: a fixed list of every registered widget type (WIDGET_LIST); clicking one drops a new instance of that type onto the current page via ctx.addWidget. Provides the persistent, always-available way to add elements in Pretty edit mode (alongside the right-click add-widget context menu built independently in pretty.js). If deleted, there would be no panel-based way to add a new widget type to a page.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:14 (import { buildPalette }); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:125 (palette: buildPalette(ctx) in mountPretty); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js: panels.palette.win visibility toggled by activatePretty/deactivatePretty/setMode

- **[INFO] simplification** (line 15): File mixes two different hyperscript helpers (el from widgets/util.js and h from dom.js) for adjacent elements -- Line 6 builds the button via `el("button", "pw-pal-item")` (widgets/util.js) then appends children built with `h("span", ...)` (dom.js, line 16-17) instead of using one consistently. Both are innerHTML-free and functionally fine (no hard-rule violation), just a stylistic inconsistency worth normalizing if touched again.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/elements.js

**Purpose:** Builds the floating 'elements' panel: a flat outliner listing every widget on the current page, letting the user select (click), highlight (hover -> canvas cue), or remove (armed two-click) an element without hunting for it on the canvas. If deleted, users would have no page-wide element list and would have to locate/select every widget directly on the canvas.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:19 (import { buildElements }); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:130 (elements: buildElements(ctx) in mountPretty); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js: panels.elements.refresh() called from renderCurrent/syncSelection/deselect/rehomeWidget (so, unlike sources/theme/pages, it IS correctly refreshed on a game switch via renderCurrent())

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 11): nameOf() duplicates inspector.js's widgetName() label-derivation logic instead of sharing one helper -- Line 11: `const nameOf = (w) => { const c = w.config || {}; return c.title || c.text || c.label || (w.binding && w.binding.id) || w.type; };` is byte-for-byte the same fallback chain as `widgetName` in panels/inspector.js:345 (`const c = x.config || {}; const lab = c.title || c.text || c.label || (x.binding && x.binding.id) || x.type;`), just without the trailing ` · id` suffix. This is exactly the 'second instance of a primitive must be extracted, not copy-pasted' case rule 7 calls out — both files already import `el` from the same `widgets/util.js`, which would be the natural home for one shared `widgetLabel(w)` helper. Currently harmless (both copies agree), but any future change to how a widget's display name is derived (e.g. adding subset-binding support) risks updating one copy and not the other. [Hard rule: 7]

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/pages.js

**Purpose:** Builds the floating 'pages' panel: the page manager for Pretty — lists every page, switches the current page on click, drag-reorders, renames in place (pencil button), and deletes (armed two-click); replaces the old topbar page dropdown. If deleted, there would be no way to add/rename/reorder/delete Pretty pages at all (only the ctx.addPage() call remains reachable, with no UI to manage existing pages).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:18 (import { buildPages }); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:129 (pages: buildPages(ctx) in mountPretty); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js: panels.pages.refresh() called from switchPage/removePage/restorePretty; panels.pages.win visibility toggled by activatePretty/deactivatePretty/setMode

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 30): Hand-rolled HTML5 drag-and-drop reimplements the shared drag-reorder primitive instead of calling it -- Lines 30-35 wire native `draggable=true` + dragstart/dragend/dragover/dragleave/drop handlers to reorder page rows. `src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/reorder.js` already exists specifically for this: its header comment states 'ONE drag-to-reorder primitive, shared by every reorderable list in the pretty UI ... rule 7. Mouse-based to match the dragresize convention (no HTML5 DnD)', and it IS reused correctly by both `panels/inspector.js:212` (column list) and `widgets/table.js:99` (column headers) via `makeReorderable`. pages.js is a third reorderable list in the same UI that was built as a fresh, independent HTML5-DnD implementation instead of a `makeReorderable(list, { itemSel: ".pw-pages-row", handleSel: ".pw-pages-grip", axis: "y", onReorder })` call — exactly the copy-paste-and-isolate pattern rule 7 exists to prevent. It also draws `draggable=true` on the whole row rather than scoping the drag start to the `.pw-pages-grip` handle the UI visually implies is the drag affordance. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 13): Page list is never refreshed after a mid-session game switch, so it can show/act on the previous game's stale page ids -- refresh() (lines 21-25) only runs from the panel's own onShow hook or explicit callers (switchPage/removePage/restorePretty in pretty.js); `setPrettyGame(g)` (fired on a game switch while Pretty is active — graph/main.js:3561) loads a new doc but doesn't call `panels.pages.refresh()`. If the pages panel is open when the game changes, its rows keep showing the OLD game's page titles/ids. Clicking a stale row calls `ctx.switchPage(p.id)` with an id that no longer exists in the new doc; `pretty.js`'s `switchPage`/`renderCurrent` falls back to `pretty.pages()[0]` so this doesn't crash, but the user is silently redirected to the new game's first page instead of the one they clicked — a smaller blast radius than the inspector/theme staleness bugs (no data loss) but still a real, visible inconsistency.


_Module notes: All six files are actively wired into src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js (imported + instantiated in mountPretty's `panels = {...}` object); none are dead code. No violations found of hard rules 2 (blocking dialogs), 3 (spellcheck), 4 (row caps - none of these files render capped dataset/subset row tables, that lives in graph/node_parts.js), 5 (dataset rename repoint - these files reference datasets by name but don't implement renaming), 6 (n/a, no .ps1 files), 8 (no Claude/Anthropic mentions), 9 (24h/dd-mm-yy dates - no date/time formatting in this module) or the zero-innerHTML convention (grepped, no innerHTML anywhere in the six files).

Two systemic, cross-file findings worth the orchestrating agent's attention as a themed group rather than six isolated bugs:
1. Game-switch staleness: pretty.js's setPrettyGame() (called on a mid-session game change while Pretty is the active view, per graph/main.js:3561) only refreshes panels.elements (via renderCurrent) - it never calls clear()/refresh() on inspector, sources, theme, or pages. This produces a spectrum of bugs from cosmetic (pages/sources show stale names until manually toggled) to silent data loss (inspector/theme keep editing an orphaned widget/theme object from the previous game, and the edit is dropped on save with no error). Since pretty.js itself is outside this module's assigned file list, each affected panel file has its own finding above, but the actual fix likely belongs in pretty.js's setPrettyGame.
2. Two clear-cut rule-7 (no copy-paste duplication) violations: pages.js hand-rolls HTML5 drag-and-drop instead of calling the existing `makeReorderable` primitive (reorder.js) that table.js and inspector.js already share; and elements.js's nameOf() duplicates inspector.js's widgetName() label-derivation logic verbatim.

By contrast, inspector.js's own live-badge/read-out mechanism (currentVal/truthBadge/markActive, lines ~469-509 and ~558-623) and its geometry live-sync (syncGeom, lines 114-123) are good examples of hard-rule-1-compliant in-place reconciliation (update only textContent/class on a keyed reference, never rebuild), which made the sources.js full-rebuild-on-any-change pattern (finding above) stand out by comparison._

## Module: j18-pretty-widgets  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/button.js

**Purpose:** Widget definition for the pretty-page 'Button' element: a small glue action (fire a trigger, run a price sweep, set a transient node-input override, switch page, or toggle live collection). Deleting it would remove the 'button' entry from the widget registry (index.js) so authored pages referencing type 'button' would render nothing for that widget (widgetDef() returns null, canvas.js's makeInstance leaves the default no-op instance).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/index.js:11-14 (imported and registered into WIDGETS/WIDGET_LIST); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js (instantiates via widgetDef(widget.type).create(...)); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/inspector.js:271-281 (edits config.action/target/value/page/label for type==='button')

- **[MEDIUM] duplication** (line 9): Exported ACTIONS list is never imported anywhere; inspector.js hardcodes an identical copy instead. -- button.js:9-10 defines and exports `ACTIONS = ["fire_trigger", "run_sweep", "set_value", "switch_page", "live_on", "live_off", "live_toggle"]`, presumably meant to be the single source of truth for valid button actions. Nothing imports it (grepped the whole static tree). Instead, src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/inspector.js:272 hardcodes the exact same literal array for the action <select>. If a future change adds/removes/renames an action in one place and not the other, the inspector's dropdown and the button's runtime switch silently drift out of sync (e.g. a new action offered in the dropdown that create() doesn't handle, or vice versa). Either delete the unused export or have inspector.js import and use it.

**Purpose:** Widget definition for the pretty-page 'Chart' element: draws a bar/line/etc. chart from a bound dataset/subset, redrawing only when the bound data key changes or the host resizes. Deleting it removes the 'chart' widget type from the registry.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/index.js:7,14 (imported and registered); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js (instantiated via widgetDef)

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/control.js

**Purpose:** Widget definition for the pretty-page 'Control' element: binds to one node-input path (trigger interval, price throttle, field confidence, etc.) and writes a transient override via a presentation the author picks (slider/number/select/toggle/radio/stepper), clamped to the input's real constraint from constraints.js. Deleting it removes the 'control' widget type and the only UI mechanism for live-tuning node inputs from a pretty page.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/index.js:8,14 (imported and registered); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js (instantiated via widgetDef)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/form.js

**Purpose:** Widget definition for the pretty-page 'Form' element: renders one input per configured dataset column and POSTs a new record on submit, keyed exactly as the collector would. Deleting it removes the only widget that lets a pretty page write data back into a dataset.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/index.js:10,14 (imported and registered); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js (instantiated via widgetDef); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/inspector.js:247-270 (edits config.dataset/submit/fields[])

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 25): A form field configured as input type "select" always renders as an empty, non-functional dropdown because there is no way to author its options anywhere in the UI. -- form.js:25-27 builds a <select> and populates it strictly from `f.options`: `for (const o of (f.options || [])) { ... inp.appendChild(op); }`. The only place `f` (an entry of `widget.config.fields`) is authored is src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/inspector.js:247-270, whose per-field row only exposes column name, input-type picker (text/number/select), a required checkbox, and delete (lines 253-258) — there is no control that ever sets `f.options`. So choosing "select" as a field's input type in the inspector produces a <select> with zero <option> children in the rendered form: permanently empty, nothing selectable, and (per the missing-value bug below) it will also always read back as an empty string on submit. This makes the advertised "select" field type entirely non-functional today.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 41): Required-field validation reads a manually-tracked `values` object that is only updated by the 'input' event, not seeded from the control's actual current value, so a field with a non-empty default (once select options can be authored) would be wrongly flagged as missing. -- form.js:31-33: for every field, `values[f.column]` is force-set to `""` right after the input/select element is created, and is only refreshed by the 'input' listener (line 31) when the user interacts with the control. Submission's required check (line 41) reads `values[f.column]`, not `inputs.get(col).inp.value`. Today this is masked because select fields always render with no options (see the finding above) so their real value is also "" — but the moment select options become configurable, a required select whose first <option> has a non-empty value would be rejected as "missing" until the user manually re-touches it, even though the payload actually submitted (built from `inp.value` at line 45) would have been correct.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/index.js

**Purpose:** The widget registry: maps a widget `type` string to its module (mirrors node_icons/panel pattern noted in the header comment) and provides `newWidget(type)` to build a fresh widget data object from a type's defaults. This is the single place that assembles the whole pretty-page widget catalogue; deleting it breaks every widget lookup (palette, canvas render, newWidget) across the pretty studio.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js:16 (widgetDef); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js:11 (newWidget, WIDGET_LIST); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/palette.js:5 (WIDGET_LIST)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/panel.js

**Purpose:** Widget definition for the pretty-page 'Embed' element: re-hosts an existing graph-view floating panel (live/precapture/tasks share a mount/activate/deactivate adapter; stats/nodemap/nodelist/toolbox/testing embed generically via floatwin's shared embed/reclaim/unembed) into the pretty surface, so panel logic isn't duplicated per view (rule 7 compliant). Deleting it removes the 'panel' widget type and the only way to surface those floating panels inside an authored page.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/index.js:12,14 (imported and registered); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js (instantiated via widgetDef); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/inspector.js:14,179 (PANEL_OPTIONS drives the config.panel <select>)

- **[INFO] design-limitation** (line 49): A given embeddable panel (live/precap/activity, or any floatwin-backed one) can only be visibly hosted in one place at a time. -- panel.js re-parents a single shared content root (mount()/embed()) into whichever widget host last called update(). If two 'Embed' widgets on the same or different pages are both bound to the same panel id, the later one to update() reclaims the DOM subtree, leaving the other's host empty. This is a pre-existing, explicitly documented tradeoff of the shared primitive (see graph/floatwin.js:638-639: "a panel can be embedded in one place at a time"), not something introduced by this file, so it is not a new bug — flagged only as a known limitation worth being aware of if a user reports a blank embedded panel widget.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/table.js

**Purpose:** Widget definition for the pretty-page 'Table' element: renders a dataset/subset's rows with per-column show/rename/width/reorder config, optional sort, and optional paging; rows reconcile against a reused <tr> pool (rule 1) and are capped at ROW_CAP. Deleting it removes the only tabular data view available to authored pages.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/index.js:6,14 (imported and registered); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js (instantiated via widgetDef)

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 11): Rows beyond ROW_CAP (200) are silently dropped with no "showing N of TOTAL" indicator anywhere, unlike the graph view's own convention for the same cap pattern. -- table.js:11 sets `ROW_CAP = 200` and the file's header comment explicitly invokes "capped (rule 4)". Rule 4 requires the count still show the true total. When paging is off, the footer is simply hidden (line 125: `foot.hidden = true`) with no total-row indicator at all. When paging is on, the footer only shows `${page+1}/${pages}` (line 121) — a page counter, not a row count — so a user has no way to tell that some rows were truncated by ROW_CAP versus genuinely absent. Contrast with the established convention elsewhere in this codebase for the exact same situation: src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js:1540 renders `showing ${rows.length} of ${p.total}` whenever a cap trims the list. table.js has no equivalent, so a table bound to a >200-row dataset looks like it contains all of the data when it does not. [Hard rule: 4]

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 119): When paging is enabled with a configured page size greater than ROW_CAP (200, e.g. author sets pageSize=500), a slice of rows within every page is permanently unreachable via prev/next, not just hidden until the next page. -- table.js:116-122: `pages` is computed from `ps` (the author-configured pageSize, unbounded — panels/inspector.js:184-185 has no min/max on it) while the rendered `view` is `rows.slice(page*ps, page*ps+ps).slice(0, ROW_CAP)`. If `ps > ROW_CAP`, only the first ROW_CAP rows of each `ps`-sized bucket are ever rendered; the remaining `ps - ROW_CAP` rows of that bucket are skipped on that page and never revisited by any other page, since `next`/`prev` (lines 38-39) step by whole `ps` chunks. Concretely: 1000 rows, pageSize=300 -> pages=4; page 0 shows rows 0-199 (of its 0-299 bucket), rows 200-299 are never shown on page 0 OR page 1 (which starts at row 300). This silently discards data from the table, not merely defers it, which goes beyond the intended "cap what's rendered, not what's real" behavior rule 4 aims for.

### src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/util.js

**Purpose:** Shared widget plumbing used by every widget module: `keySubscription` gives each widget a uniform require/subscribe/release lifecycle for the reactive data layer (rule 7 — no widget hand-rolls this bookkeeping); `textKeys` maps {{token}}s to subscribe keys; `el` is a tiny DOM-element helper (class + text); `humanize` prettifies a raw column key into a label; `dataKeyForBinding` is re-exported from binding.js for convenience. Deleting it breaks every widget file and canvas.js (all import from it).

**Used by:** keySubscription: src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js:19, widgets/chart.js:6, widgets/container.js:5, widgets/label.js:6, widgets/table.js:7; el: src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/canvas.js:19, pretty.js:21, style_editor.js:7, topbar.js:6, panels/{sources,inspector,palette,elements,pages}.js, and every widgets/*.js file; humanize: src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/inspector.js:12, widgets/table.js:7; textKeys / dataKeyForBinding: widgets/{chart,container,label,table}.js, canvas.js:19

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 25): `el()` is a second, independently hand-rolled DOM-element builder alongside dom.js's `h()`, which dom.js's own header comment names as "the ONE element builder every site uses (CLAUDE.md rule 7)". -- util.js:25-30 defines `el(tag, cls, text)` (createElement + className + textContent) and it is used pervasively across the entire pretty/ tree instead of dom.js's `h()`. dom.js:9 states in its own comment: "the ONE element builder every site uses (CLAUDE.md rule 7)", which by the codebase's own stated intent makes `el()` the kind of second primitive rule 7 warns against introducing. Concrete evidence they are used side-by-side for no clear technical reason in the same file: src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/panels/palette.js imports both (`el` from widgets/util.js line 6, `h` from dom.js line 7) and mixes them within one function (line 14 builds the button via `el()`, lines 16-17 build its children via `h()`). That said, `el()` only supports class+text (a narrow subset of `h()`'s attrs/children/event-handler support) and this is a long-standing, uniformly-applied pattern across all of pretty/ rather than a newly copy-pasted duplicate introduced by these files, which tempers how squarely it matches rule 7's canonical examples (floating panels / drag loops / poll-rendered lists). Flagged as medium rather than high given that nuance. [Hard rule: 7]


_Module notes: Overall this module is clean and well-built: every widget correctly implements the {type, title, icon, defaults(), create(host, widget, ctx) -> {update, destroy}} contract that canvas.js expects, all subscribe/require/release plumbing is centralized in util.js's keySubscription and used uniformly (no widget hand-rolls it), table.js's row reconciliation is an exemplary rule-1-compliant implementation (keyed <tr>/<td> reuse, textContent-only diffs, no innerHTML anywhere in the module), and there is no game-specific/hardcoded-name logic anywhere (all bindings resolve through binding.js/constraints.js against the live profile). No blocking dialogs, no spellcheck, no locale-default date formatting, and no 12h time formatting appear in this module.

The two findings worth prioritizing: (1) form.js's "select" field input type is currently dead-on-arrival because there is no options editor for form fields anywhere in inspector.js, so choosing it always renders an unusable empty dropdown; (2) table.js's ROW_CAP (200) truncation is never surfaced to the user as a "showing N of TOTAL" count (unlike the equivalent convention already established in graph/main.js:1540), and interacts badly with a user-configured pageSize > 200 to make a range of rows permanently unreachable through paging, not just deferred.

Also worth a follow-up decision (not urgent): whether pretty/'s standalone `el()` helper (widgets/util.js) should be folded into dom.js's `h()` given dom.js explicitly claims to be "the ONE element builder every site uses" — palette.js already imports and uses both in the same file. This is pre-existing across the whole pretty/ directory, not something newly introduced by these 10 files, so it reads more as a design decision made when pretty/ was first built than a fresh violation._

## Module: j20-static-top-a  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/api.js

**Purpose:** Thin, defensive wrapper around every backend HTTP endpoint the SPA calls. Monkey-patches window.fetch once to give ALL /api/* calls a per-request abort deadline, GET coalescing (concurrent identical GETs share one network request), and a per-path circuit breaker (fail-fast after repeated timeouts, auto-recovering trial after a cooldown) so a wedged --reload server degrades gracefully instead of silently stalling the whole UI. Below that, one small typed helper per endpoint (profiles, preview/detect/rule_trace, captures, OCR device tuning, capture backend, video harness, bench, precapture, live collector, dataset batch/event edit+revert, prices, triggers, toasts, file sources, activity, dictionaries, sounds, subsets/flow-details, capture bindings). If this file were deleted essentially every graph/pretty panel loses its only path to the backend -- nothing else in the tree calls fetch('/api/...') directly except this module's own window.fetch patch.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (import * as api); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/producer_node.js; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/dict_picker.js; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/persist.js; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/backups.js; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/dbbackups.js; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js, livewin.js, toolbox.js, precap.js, testing.js, datanodes.js, dbstruct.js; src/oc/web/static/js/hub.js; src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js, model.js, data.js, overrides.js, widgets/form.js, widgets/button.js

- **[MEDIUM] reliability** (line 39): Circuit breaker requires 3 fresh consecutive failures to re-open after a cooldown trial fails, defeating fail-fast for a persistently-dead endpoint. -- `_breakerFail` resets `b.fails = 0` at the moment it trips (line ~44). After `BREAKER_COOLDOWN_MS` elapses, `_breakerOpen` lets the next call through as a 'live trial' per the file's own doc comment (lines 17-19: 'the first call after the cooldown is a live trial; success clears the breaker'). But the comment never states what a FAILED trial does, and the code answer is: one failure just sets fails=1 (not >= BREAKER_FAILS), so the breaker stays closed and the NEXT 2 calls also go out live and each must run the full per-call deadline (LIGHT_MS=30s, or OCR_MS=60s) before failing, before the breaker trips again. For a genuinely wedged backend (the exact scenario this file's top comment is built to survive -- 'a dead --reload worker whose parent still holds the port'), this means after every 10s cooldown window the guard spends up to ~90-180s issuing slow doomed requests before it fail-fasts again, instead of re-opening on the first failed trial -- largely defeating the stated purpose of the breaker for its worst-case target.

### src/oc/web/static/js/overlay.js

**Purpose:** The one canvas primitive for drawing/editing labelled fraction-boxes over a captured image (window regions/detectors/scrollbar, item cell/fields/tells, and the glyph-teaching canvas) -- create/move/resize boxes, grid/cell/read-out/raw-OCR overlays, colour-coded validation pills, zoom with device-resolution supersampling. If deleted, the entire teaching UI (window node canvas, item node canvas, glyph atlas canvas in graph/imaging.js) loses its only way to draw or edit a region.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js (import { Overlay }; 3 call sites: window canvas openImage ~L168, glyph canvas openGlyphImage ~L435, item canvas openItemImage ~L658)

- **[HIGH] resource-leak** (line 205): Overlay registers a permanent `window` 'mouseup' listener in the constructor that `destroy()` never removes, leaking every Overlay instance (and its retained decoded capture image) for the life of the page. -- `_bind()` (called once from the constructor at line 88) does `window.addEventListener("mouseup", () => this._onUp());` (line 205). The class's own doc comment on the constructor explicitly warns 'A NEW Overlay is built on every image open, so this MUST be disposed on close (see destroy()) or it leaks unbounded' (lines 90-93), but `destroy()` (lines 96-98) only disposes the ResizeObserver: `destroy() { this._roDispose && this._roDispose(); this._roDispose = null; }` -- there is no `removeEventListener` anywhere in the file (verified via grep: zero matches). Every open/close cycle of a window node, item node, or the glyph-teaching canvas (graph/imaging.js's openImage/openItemImage/openGlyphImage + closeImage/closeItemImage, which call `.destroy()` believing it fully tears the instance down) constructs a fresh Overlay whose closure keeps `this` (and everything it references -- `this.canvas`, `this.boxes`, and critically `this.img`, a decoded capture image that can be several MB at 4K) alive forever via the un-removable window listener. In the normal authoring workflow (opening/closing many item images per session, switching window pages, rebuilding nodes) this accumulates: a long teaching session leaks one Image + full box/read-out state per image ever opened, and every leaked instance's `_onUp` still runs on every mouseup anywhere on the page (cheap per-call, but growing linearly with leaked count). Fix is a one-line addition: store the bound handler and call `window.removeEventListener('mouseup', this._onUpBound)` in `destroy()`.

### src/oc/web/static/js/vtable.js

**Purpose:** Virtualized, searchable/sortable data table (RecyclerView-style row pooling) used for large dataset/subset views in graph nodes -- renders only visible rows regardless of dataset size, with resizable/reorderable/persisted columns, a custom scrollbar, an AND/OR/NOT/wildcard query language, and an inline per-row drill-down. This is the codebase's answer to hard rule 4 (graph node tables must cap rendered rows / virtualize rather than dump every row) for the actual data-browsing surfaces (as opposed to the small inline preview tables in datasetDetailHTML/subTableHTML that just cap at DS_ROW_CAP).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/datanodes.js (new VTable(host, key) for dataset/subset detail panels; new VTable(node) for a plain listing; vt.collapse() on focus-out); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (setVTableStore wiring the profile-backed column-state store; liveVTables()/vtableById() exposed on an e2e test hook for width/sort/column assertions); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/history.js (reapplyPersistedVTables() called after an undo/redo restore so already-open tables re-snap to the restored column widths/order/sort)

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 58): The per-table column-state store bootstrap (in-memory Map fallback + setXStore hook) is copy-pasted near-verbatim from graph/table.js instead of being extracted into one shared module. -- vtable.js lines 58-62:
```
let _store = (() => {
    const mem = new Map();
    return { load: (id) => mem.get(id) || {}, save: (id, st) => mem.set(id, st) };
})();
export function setVTableStore(store) { _store = store; }
```
is byte-for-byte the same pattern as graph/table.js lines 16-20 (`setTableStore`, same mem-Map fallback, same load/save shape), and the file's own comment at line 56 even says 'mirroring table.js'. Per rule 7 ('adding a SECOND of something must refactor the first into a shared primitive, then build both on it'), vtable.js is the second implementation of this exact per-id column-state store and should have hoisted a shared `colstate.js` (load/save + setStore) that both table.js (plain HTML `<table>` enhancer) and vtable.js (virtualized rows) call, rather than re-declaring the identical bootstrap independently. Low blast radius (4 lines, cosmetic/maintenance only -- a change to the store contract now has to be made in two places) but it is the exact anti-pattern the rule calls out. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[INFO] convention** (line 118): vtable.js builds its DOM scaffold with raw document.createElement/className/append instead of the project's h()/dom.js hyperscript convention used elsewhere in the tree. -- The file imports `h` from `./dom.js` but uses it only once (the `_toggleExpand` loading placeholder at line 468); the entire `_build()` scaffold (lines 118-200) and every dynamically created row/cell/marker element elsewhere in the file use `document.createElement(...)` + property assignment instead of `h(...)`. This is not a hard-rule violation (no innerHTML is used anywhere -- grep confirms zero matches outside a comment) and is functionally identical to h()-built DOM, just stylistically inconsistent with the rest of the SPA's 'zero-innerHTML hyperscript' authoring style. No concrete failure scenario, purely a style note.


_Module notes: Focused, high-confidence findings only. The standout is the overlay.js window-'mouseup' listener leak (high) -- it directly contradicts the class's own documented disposal contract and is trivially confirmed by grep (zero removeEventListener calls in the file) plus reading every call site in graph/imaging.js that relies on destroy() to fully tear an Overlay down. The api.js breaker re-trip finding (medium) is a real logic gap but softer -- it degrades recovery speed rather than corrupting anything. The vtable.js/table.js duplicated store-bootstrap (medium, rule 7) is small in scope; flagged because the instructions explicitly want near-duplicate primitives surfaced and the file's own comment admits the mirroring. Did not find any hard-rule 1-6/8/9 violations in these three files (no DOM polling/reconciliation code, no blocking dialogs, no spellcheck, no dataset-rename repoint logic, no .ps1, no Claude/Anthropic mentions, no locale-default date/time formatting) and did not find any game-specific/hardcoded-item logic (all three files are fully generic infrastructure). All three files are confirmed live and heavily used; none are dead code._
