## Module: j21-static-top-b  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/ago.js

**Purpose:** Shared 'X ago' ticker primitive: one 1s setInterval repaints every registered element's relative-time label so it advances second-by-second instead of jumping on the next poll. Auto-drops elements that leave the DOM and stops the interval when nothing is tracked. Deleting it would force every caller (activity/stats/backup panels) to hand-roll its own ticking interval, likely diverging (the exact anti-pattern rule 7 forbids).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js (liveAgo/stopAgo, trigger last-fired labels); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/stats.js (liveAgo/stopAgo, dataset last-seen); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/backups.js (liveAgo); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/dbbackups.js (liveAgo)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/bgtimer.js

**Purpose:** Drop-in setTimeout/clearTimeout that fires on schedule even while the tab is backgrounded, by delegating the actual wait to a Web Worker (bgtimer.worker.js) whose timers aren't throttled, and running the callback on the main thread when the worker posts back. The one unthrottled-timer primitive so a background-safe loop isn't reinvented per caller.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/livewin.js (bgSetTimeout/bgClearTimeout for the live-tuning loop, per its own comment the sole caller)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/bgtimer.worker.js

**Purpose:** The actual Worker script bgtimer.js spins up (`new Worker(new URL('./bgtimer.worker.js', import.meta.url))`). DOM-free timer service: arms/cancels setTimeout by id and posts {fire:id} back. Its unthrottled timers are the whole reason bgtimer.js works while the tab is hidden.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/bgtimer.js (dynamic Worker(new URL(...)) construction, line 14) — not a static import, so a plain grep for the filename inside other modules would miss this; it is the worker entry point instantiated at runtime

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/conn.js

**Purpose:** Single source of truth for 'can we reach the backend': api.js's tfetch() reports every request outcome here, a full-screen retry overlay shows while down, an auto-probe against a cheap GET dismisses it, and isOnline() lets every poller across the app halt instead of hammering a dead server. Deleting it would mean every poller re-implements its own reachability tracking (rule-7 anti-pattern) and the app would silently spin/error instead of showing the offline overlay.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/api.js (reportReachable/reportUnreachable on every fetch outcome); src/oc/web/static/js/hub.js (isOnline gate before kick()); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/dsevents.js (reportReachable/reportUnreachable from the SSE stream); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (onChange -> reload once back online, line 4665); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/data.js, src/oc/web/static/js/graph/producer_node.js (isOnline gates); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js, panels/testing.js (isOnline gates on countdowns/playback)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/ctxmenu.js

**Purpose:** The one shared floating right-click context menu (position at click, clamp on-screen, dismiss on outside click/Escape). Both the graph node view's 'add node' menu and the Pretty studio's 'add widget' menu are built on this per its own header comment — the canonical rule-7 primitive for this UI element.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (right-click add-node menu); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js (right-click add-widget menu)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/datefmt.js

**Purpose:** The ONE shared date/time formatter mandated by hard rule 9: dd/mm/yy dates, 24-hour times, plus relative 'since'/'sinceShort' age labels. Every date/time surface in the UI is required to route through here instead of locale-default JS formatting. Deleting it would force every caller back onto ad-hoc (likely locale-varying) formatting.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js, panels/activity.js, panels/livewin.js, panels/precap.js, graph/backups.js, graph/dbbackups.js (fmtDateTime/fmtDate/etc.); src/oc/web/static/js/history_panel.js (sinceShort); src/oc/web/static/js/ago.js (since)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/defaults.js

**Purpose:** Single source of truth for detect-node confidence defaults (mirrors DEFAULT_DETECT_THRESHOLD in src/oc/profile/models.py) and the two profile<->box converters for a detect entry, so the classic and graph editors don't each hard-code their own 0.8 literal / knob set.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/model.js (imports DEFAULT_DETECT_THRESHOLD, used when seeding new detect boxes at lines 1382 and 1483)

- **[INFO] coverage** (line 19): detectToProfile/detectToBox exported but not currently called from graph/model.js -- Only DEFAULT_DETECT_THRESHOLD is imported by graph/model.js today; detectToProfile/detectToBox (the on-disk<->box converters) have no current caller found in the static tree. Not flagged as dead code because they are plausibly the intended single-source-of-truth converters for a caller not covered by this grep pass (e.g. a save/serialize path), and removing them on a false-negative would just re-invite the exact per-editor divergence the file's header comment warns against.

### src/oc/web/static/js/desktop_resize.js

**Purpose:** Draws the 8 edge/corner resize grips for the frameless pywebview desktop window (no OS sizing border exists since the window is chromeless) and drives native resize via the pywebview js_api set_bounds bridge. Deleting it would make the desktop build's window un-resizable by dragging its edges.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/titlebar.js (initWindowResize(), called only when window.pywebview is present)

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 71): No pointercancel handling in the drag loop -- beginResize() wires pointerdown -> pointermove/pointerup on the grip and relies on setPointerCapture, but only pointerup ever calls onUp() to remove the listeners and cancel the pending rAF. If the browser/OS revokes pointer capture without delivering pointerup (e.g. an interrupting system dialog, alt-tab during drag) the pointermove/pointerup listeners from that drag are never removed; a subsequent pointerdown on the same grip would add a second set of listeners (addEventListener with different closures, so not deduped), causing two onMove handlers to run concurrently for the next drag on that grip. Low likelihood in normal use, but a real leak/duplication path since no 'pointercancel' listener exists.

### src/oc/web/static/js/dom.js

**Purpose:** The ONE hyperscript element builder (h()/frag()/svg()) that replaces innerHTML string-building across the entire front-end (hard rule/CLAUDE.md rule 7's canonical example), plus the shared collapsible-panel (fieldset), ResizeObserver wrapper (observeResize), and a set of shared monochrome icon factories (TRASH/CAMERA/WARN/...). Deleting it would break essentially the whole UI (40+ importing files) and reintroduce innerHTML everywhere.

**Used by:** 40 files across src/oc/web/static/js and js/graph and js/pretty (h/frag/svg/fieldset/observeResize/TRASH/etc.) — e.g. graph/main.js, overlay.js, vtable.js, floatwin.js, all graph/panels/*.js, pretty/panels/*.js, modal.js, ctxmenu.js, titlebar.js, log.js, conn.js

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/hub.js

**Purpose:** The single backend heartbeat fan-out: subscribes once to the pushed SSE 'activity' channel (via graph/dsevents.js) and replays the latest snapshot to every subscriber (worker/device/trigger status panels), replacing what used to be several independent per-panel pollers. kick() forces an immediate refresh after a user action that changed server state.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (init/start); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js, panels/livewin.js, panels/precap.js (subscribe); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/producer_node.js (subscribe); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/widgets/button.js, pretty/data.js (subscribe/kick)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/keys.js

**Purpose:** Client-side mirror of the server's KeySpec (src/oc/store/keys.py): builds the same dedup key from field values + a KeyDef, purely for instant client-side preview while a user edits key config in the UI. The server's own computed key is always the source of truth; a mismatch here would only cause a stale UI preview, never a wrong write (the real write-key computation lives server-side per the Producer-keys-via-key_map_for architecture).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_parts.js (imports buildKey, used at line 600 to preview the record key)

- **[INFO] coverage** (line 7): keyParts and DEFAULT_KEY exported but only consumed internally by buildKey -- keyParts() and DEFAULT_KEY are exported (public API) but the only external consumer (node_parts.js) imports just buildKey; keyParts/DEFAULT_KEY are only used inside keys.js itself today. Not a bug — harmless — just noting for awareness in case a future audit expects an external caller.

### src/oc/web/static/js/log.js

**Purpose:** The bottom terminal-style log bar: shows the latest message, expands to a capped (MAX=500) scrollable history, mirrors uncaught errors/console.error/warn so problems are visible without devtools, and provides timed(label) for logging an operation's elapsed duration. Deleting it would remove the only visible-without-devtools error/activity trail in the app (used by boot, logstream.js, and every long OCR/capture operation).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (setLogOpen, log, timed, mirrorConsole at boot); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js, graph/producer_node.js, graph/persist.js, graph/state.js (log/timed); src/oc/web/static/js/logstream.js (log, for server-pushed activity lines)

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 57): Log timestamp built with toLocaleTimeString instead of the shared datefmt helper -- Line 57: `ts: new Date().toLocaleTimeString("en-GB", { hour12: false })`. CLAUDE.md hard rule 9 explicitly bans toLocaleTimeString/toLocaleDateString/toLocaleString anywhere in the front-end ("it varies by machine and silently breaks this rule") and requires every date/time display to go through the ONE shared helper in static/js/datefmt.js. datefmt.js already exports `fmtTimeSec(iso)` which produces exactly the HH:MM:SS 24-hour format this log row needs (see log.js's own row rendering: `h("span",{class:"log-ts"}, ts)`), so this should be `fmtTimeSec(new Date())` (or store an ISO/ms timestamp and format it with fmtTimeSec at render time) instead of a locale call. This is the exact repeat-offense pattern the rule was written to catch. [Hard rule: 9]

### src/oc/web/static/js/logstream.js

**Purpose:** Pipes the backend's per-trigger/per-fetch activity-log lines into the bottom log bar (log.js) by subscribing to the shared dsevents SSE bus's 'log' channel, instead of opening its own EventSource (which would permanently consume one of the browser's ~6 per-host connections). openLogStream(game)/closeLogStream() are the only two entry points.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (openLogStream on game switch)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/modal.js

**Purpose:** Shared stackable modal manager (title bar, close button, backdrop-click/Escape dismiss, per-modal AbortController wired to `handle.signal` so fetches/timers a modal started are cancelled the instant it closes). The one primitive every dialog in the app is built on.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js, graph/panels/toolbox.js, graph/panels/precap.js, graph/dict_picker.js, graph/panels/datanodes.js, graph/backups.js, graph/dbbackups.js (openModal)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/singleflight.js

**Purpose:** singleFlight(key, fn): runs an async fn per key at most once concurrently, but guarantees a call that arrives mid-flight is retried exactly once after the current run finishes (never silently dropped) — the hoisted '...Again' idiom used by push-driven refreshes so the final event of a burst is never stranded.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js, graph/panels/datanodes.js, pretty/data.js (singleFlight for push-driven refresh coalescing)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/static/js/titlebar.js

**Purpose:** Custom window chrome (app title + minimize/maximize/close) for the frameless pywebview desktop build, built only when window.pywebview is detected so a plain browser tab is untouched. Also wires up desktop_resize.js's edge grips. Deleting it would leave the desktop app window with no way to move/minimize/maximize/close (no native title bar exists, it's frameless).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (initTitlebar(), line 27)

- **[INFO] stale-comment** (line 10): Header comment claims to be shared by 'graph/main.js + main.js' but a standalone main.js no longer exists -- The file's top comment says "Shared by BOTH entry points (graph/main.js + main.js) so the chrome is authored once, never copied per page." The plain js/main.js entry point (and the old ui.html teach page) was deleted when the front-end consolidated to a single SPA (index.html -> js/graph/main.js only, per project history). A repo-wide grep found only graph/main.js importing titlebar.js today. Harmless (doesn't affect behavior), but the comment misdescribes the current architecture and could mislead a future reader into thinking a second entry point exists.

### src/oc/web/static/js/history_core.js

**Purpose:** View-agnostic undo/redo engine: a linear stack of full-state JSON snapshots with per-entry labels, arbitrary jump-to-index, a 200-entry cap, and a subscribe hook for a history panel. Both the node graph and the Pretty studio each instantiate one independent copy of this (createHistory) rather than forking their own stack/index/truncate logic (rule 7). Deleting it would remove undo/redo entirely from both editors.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/history.js (createHistory({snapshot, restore, label: diffLabel})); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js (createHistory({...}), same pattern)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 44): applyAt() skips emit() if restore() rejects, leaving index and subscribers out of sync -- applyAt(i) sets `index = i` and `restoring = true` before `await restore(...)`; the `finally` clears `restoring`, but `emit()` on the line after the try/finally only runs if restore() resolved — if it throws, the exception propagates past `emit()` uncaught. None of the three callers (history_panel.js's undo/redo button onClick and row-click jumpTo, graph/history.js's undo()/redo() wrappers, pretty/pretty.js's keybind handlers) attach a .catch() to the promise, so this becomes an unhandled promise rejection. This is not just theoretical: graph/history.js's own `restore()` ends in `await reconcileOpenImages()` -> `openImage()` -> `await loadImage(...)`, a network fetch of a captured/stashed image that can plausibly fail (offline backend, aborted request). On such a failure, `hist.index()` already points at the target entry (mutated before the await) but the history panel's counter/highlighted row and any status line are never told to re-render (emit() never ran), so the visible UI state can silently drift from the internal index until an unrelated later push/undo/redo happens to call emit() again. The failure itself is not silent (log.js's global `unhandledrejection` listener logs it), but the state/UI desync is a real, reachable correctness gap.

### src/oc/web/static/js/history_label.js

**Purpose:** Derives a short human-readable label for a history entry by depth-first diffing the previous JSON snapshot against the new one ("add window 'arcanes'", "windows > eq > id: equipment -> gear"), shared by both histories so the edit list reads identically in the graph and Pretty panels. Explicitly approximate by design (a scannable log, not an audit trail).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/history.js (diffLabel, passed as label to createHistory); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js (diffLabel, same pattern)

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 37): Array length-mismatch diff can misattribute add/remove when an element is inserted/removed mid-array rather than appended -- walk()'s array branch finds the first index where JSON.stringify(a[i]) !== JSON.stringify(b[i]) and reports that element as the one 'added'/'removed'; if the actual edit inserted or removed an element in the middle of the array (shifting all subsequent elements), the reported element name will be wrong (it names whatever now sits at the first differing index, not necessarily the truly new/removed one). This matches the file's own "Approximate by design" comment and only affects a cosmetic label string in the history panel, never the actual snapshot/restore data, so not flagged as a real bug — noting for completeness only.

### src/oc/web/static/js/history_panel.js

**Purpose:** The shared floating history panel UI (built on graph/floatwin.js's createFloatWin, per rule 7) listing every recorded edit with the current point highlighted, click-to-jump, and header undo/redo arrows. One builder used by both the node graph and Pretty studio, each handing it its own createHistory instance.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (createHistoryPanel, line 3636); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/pretty.js (createHistoryPanel, line 243)

- No issues found.


_Module notes: All 18 files are reachable and in active use (no dead code). Two things worth the parent's attention: (1) log.js:57 uses `toLocaleTimeString("en-GB", {hour12:false})` for the log-bar timestamp — this is a direct, mechanically-detectable violation of hard rule 9 (no toLocaleTimeString anywhere; the codebase already has `fmtTimeSec` in datefmt.js built for exactly this HH:MM:SS use case and log.js should call it instead). (2) history_core.js's `applyAt()` sets `index = i` and `restoring = true` before `await restore(...)`; if `restore()` throws (plausible — graph/history.js's restore chain ends in `await reconcileOpenImages()` -> `openImage()` -> `await loadImage()`, a network fetch), the `finally` clears `restoring` but the following `emit()` is skipped because the exception keeps propagating past it, and none of undo()/redo()/jumpTo()'s callers (history_panel.js, graph/history.js, pretty/pretty.js) catch the rejection — so a transient restore failure leaves `hist.index()` already advanced to the target entry while subscribers (the history panel counter/highlight, any status line) never re-render to match, until the next unrelated push/undo/redo. Not silent (log.js's global unhandledrejection handler logs it), but the state/UI can drift out of sync. Minor/cosmetic notes: titlebar.js's header comment still says it's "shared by BOTH entry points (graph/main.js + main.js)" — the plain `main.js` entry point was deleted when the UI consolidated to a single SPA (per project memory), so the comment is stale (only graph/main.js uses it now); desktop_resize.js's pointer-drag loop has no `pointercancel` handler, so if the browser revokes pointer capture without a pointerup (rare), the grip's move/up listeners are never torn down and a later drag on the same grip could double-fire. Everything else (ago.js, bgtimer.js/.worker.js, conn.js, ctxmenu.js, datefmt.js, defaults.js, dom.js, hub.js, keys.js, logstream.js, modal.js, singleflight.js, history_label.js, history_panel.js) read as solid, on-contract implementations of the shared primitives they claim to be, with no hard-rule violations, no blocking dialogs, no innerHTML, no dataset/rename logic, and (where they poll/tick — ago.js, history_panel.js) correct keyed/positional in-place DOM reconciliation._

## Module: j22-vendor  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/static/js/vendor/dom-to-image.js

**Purpose:** Vendored single-file ESM bundle of the modern-screenshot library (v4.6.0, per its header comment; historically forked from/named after dom-to-image). Provides DOM-to-image serialization: clones a live DOM subtree (including <canvas>/<img>/<video> elements) into an inert clone with inlined computed styles, embeds external images/fonts as data URLs, wraps the clone in an SVG foreignObject, and rasterizes that to a canvas/PNG/JPEG/WebP/blob. Exists so the toolbox 'screenshot canvas' feature can capture the graph's live #gworld view (including canvas contents that a plain html2canvas-style DOM copy would render blank) without a build step or CDN fetch (CSP blocks external scripts, so it must be vendored inline).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js:8 - `import { domToBlob } from "../../vendor/dom-to-image.js";`; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/toolbox.js:176 - explanatory comment referencing the vendored dom-to-image usage for the screenshot tool

- **[INFO] vendor-hygiene**: Stock vendored library, unmodified since import except a repo-wide reindent -- Per instructions this is a third-party vendored file (modern-screenshot v4.6.0, MIT) and is exempt from exhaustive line-by-line correctness review. Confirmed it is genuinely used (single import site in graph/panels/toolbox.js calling domToBlob) and confirmed via git log that the file has only ever been touched by the initial vendoring commit (1f66b7a) and a whole-repo indentation-style commit (0cbbe6b) - no local forking/patching to audit or flag. No hardcoded game/window-title logic present (pure generic DOM-serialization utility), so no CLAUDE.md 'zero game knowledge in Python/JS' concern applies here.


_Module notes: Single-file module, single external call site. No hard-rule violations found (not a poll/timer-redrawn list, no dialogs, no dataset logic, no date formatting, not a duplicated UI primitive - it's a generic DOM->image serialization library used once by the toolbox screenshot feature)._

## Module: p01-core  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/interfaces.py

**Purpose:** Defines every pluggable backend contract as an ABC (ProcessDetector, WindowProvider, CaptureBackend, OcrEngine, Corrector, ProducerSource, SourceParser, WindowClassifier, Notifier) plus the shared value objects that ride with them (ProducerCtx, ToastText, ToastSpec). This is the seam the whole 'backends behind ABCs, chosen by name' architecture depends on: code that uses a backend imports only these classes, never a concrete implementation. Deleting it would break every module in capture/, window/, process/, ocr/, detect/, learn/, enrich/, source/, notify/ and registry.py/engine.py, which all subclass or type-hint against these ABCs.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (imports all ABC classes to type the registry tables and build_* return types); src/oc/engine.py (Engine dataclass fields typed against these ABCs); src/oc/capture/*.py, src/oc/window/win32_provider.py, src/oc/process/psutil_detector.py, src/oc/ocr/rapidocr3_engine.py, src/oc/detect/classifier.py, src/oc/learn/*_corrector.py, src/oc/enrich/http_producer.py, src/oc/source/parsers/*.py, src/oc/notify/*.py (subclass the relevant ABC); src/oc/collect/reader.py, src/oc/collect/triggers.py, src/oc/detect/matcher.py, src/oc/cli/sweep_job.py, src/oc/ocr/serialize.py, src/oc/profile/models.py (type-hint/duck-type against ProducerCtx/ToastSpec/OcrEngine etc.); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/{main,model,toast_node}.js (ToastSpec-shaped payloads consumed via the toast node UI); tests/test_variables.py, tests/test_reader.py, tests/test_http_producer.py (import OcrEngine / ProducerCtx directly)

- **[INFO] design** (line 91): OcrEngine.read_line's default line-ordering sort key rounds y/h, an approximation that can misorder near-boundary lines -- `round(ln.box.y / max(1, ln.box.h))` buckets lines into 'rows' by dividing y by height and rounding. For two lines whose y sits close to a half-row boundary relative to their own height, rounding could occasionally group/order them unexpectedly. This is only the DEFAULT fallback for backends without a native rec-only path (rapidocr3 overrides read_line per grep), so it's low-impact, and no concrete misbehavior was observed in current callers.

- **[INFO] consistency** (line 256): ToastSpec.hero_image / inline_images duplicate paths already validated as strings on ToastDef in profile/models.py with no format contract stated here -- The dataclass docstring says these are 'filesystem paths to already-rendered generated images' but doesn't state whether relative paths are resolved against captures_dir or an absolute path is required. Not a bug (no caller misuse found), just worth tightening the contract if a second Notifier backend is ever added.

### src/oc/types.py

**Purpose:** Backend-agnostic value types (PixelBox, FractionBox, ProcessInfo, WindowInfo, Frame, OcrLine) that every backend and the collection pipeline pass across module boundaries instead of talking in a concrete backend's own types. FractionBox/PixelBox conversion is the core of the profile coordinate convention (fractions of window client area, resolved to pixels per capture).

**Used by:** src/oc/interfaces.py (ABC method signatures); src/oc/capture/*.py, src/oc/window/win32_provider.py, src/oc/collect/reader.py, src/oc/collect/grid.py, src/oc/detect/*.py, src/oc/ocr/*.py, src/oc/profile/models.py (FractionBox for region/detect coordinates); src/oc/web/routes/*.py (frame/box conversions for preview and capture endpoints); tests/test_geometry.py, tests/test_grid.py, tests/test_locate.py, tests/test_locator2.py and many others

- No issues found.

### src/oc/registry.py

**Purpose:** Name -> implementation lookup tables for every backend kind, populated by @register_* decorators in each backend module, with lazy discovery (_ensure_loaded importing _IMPL_MODULES) so settings.yaml can select a backend purely by string name. This is what makes 'swap a backend = change one name' true; deleting it breaks every build_* call in engine.py and the web routes that build ad-hoc backends (capture_backend.py, bench.py, sources.py, prices via price_runner).

**Used by:** src/oc/engine.py (build_capture/build_window/build_process/build_ocr/build_classifier/build_corrector/build_notifier); src/oc/capture/{mss,printwindow,wgc,adaptive}_backend.py, src/oc/window/win32_provider.py, src/oc/process/psutil_detector.py, src/oc/ocr/rapidocr3_engine.py, src/oc/detect/classifier.py, src/oc/learn/{rapidfuzz,difflib}_corrector.py, src/oc/enrich/http_producer.py, src/oc/source/parsers/*.py, src/oc/notify/{null,windows_toast}.py (register_* decorators); src/oc/web/routes/{capture_backend,bench,sources}.py (capture_names/parser_names for UI pickers), src/oc/web/deps.py (build_notifier), src/oc/cli/{sweep_job,bench}.py (build_producer/build_capture); tests/test_sources.py (build_parser, parser_names)

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 188): producer_names() has no callers anywhere in the repo -- Its docstring says it's 'for the node's type picker', mirroring parser_names()/capture_names() which ARE consumed by web/routes/sources.py and web/routes/capture_backend.py respectively, but grepping the whole repo for `producer_names` finds only its own definition at line 188-191. The producer-node type picker in the web UI (routes handling ProducerDef) doesn't call it, so either that UI wiring was never finished or was removed. Not harmful (small, self-contained function) but it's unused code that should either be wired into the producer-node UI or removed.

### src/oc/settings.py

**Purpose:** Parses config/settings.yaml into a typed Settings dataclass (per-kind BackendChoice name+options, Tuning knobs, profiles_dir/data_dir/captures_dir). Settings.load() is the one place that turns the YAML file into the object Engine.build() consumes; deleting it removes the only path from disk config to runtime backend selection, forcing every kind back to its hardcoded dataclass default.

**Used by:** src/oc/engine.py (Engine.build calls Settings.load() when none passed); src/oc/web/deps.py get_settings() and most src/oc/web/routes/*.py (settings.captures_dir, settings.profiles_dir, settings.tuning, settings.corrector, etc.); src/oc/collect/collector.py, src/oc/collect/precapture.py, src/oc/collect/live.py (engine.settings.captures_dir / tuning fields); tests/test_precapture.py, tests/test_gate.py, tests/test_sources_routes.py, tests/test_flow_routes.py (import Settings/Tuning directly)

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 84): choice() helper doesn't validate BackendChoice.name is non-empty, and treats a YAML dict form missing 'name' as a KeyError only at registry lookup time -- `choice()` does `BackendChoice(node['name'], node.get('options', {}))` for the dict form — if a user authors `capture: {options: {...}}` without a `name` key, this raises a raw `KeyError: 'name'` from settings.yaml parsing rather than a clear config-validation error. Low severity since it still fails loudly (not silently), just with a less friendly message than e.g. the registry's own `No {kind} backend named ...` error.

### src/oc/engine.py

**Purpose:** The single place that turns Settings (names + options) into a live Engine bundle of constructed backend objects (capture/window/process/ocr/classifier/corrector/notifier), plus the one-time DPI-awareness and stats_store configuration side effects needed before any capture/window work happens. Deleting it removes the only wiring point between settings.yaml and running backends; every collector/web-route entry point that needs 'the current backends' calls Engine.build().

**Used by:** src/oc/web/deps.py get_engine() (module-level cached Engine used by nearly all web routes); src/oc/cli/*.py (detect/capture/collect/price commands each build an Engine); src/oc/collect/collector.py, src/oc/collect/precapture.py, src/oc/collect/live.py (consume engine.settings / engine.capture / engine.ocr / engine.classifier / engine.notifier)

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 61): classifier_opts dict-merge order lets settings.classifier.options silently shadow the injected 'ocr' and 'profile_dir' keys -- `classifier_opts = {"ocr": ocr, "profile_dir": str(Path(settings.profiles_dir)), **settings.classifier.options}` places the spread of user-authored options AFTER the two wired-in values, so if a user's settings.yaml ever put an `ocr:` or `profile_dir:` key under `classifier.options` (a plausible name collision since those happen to be the exact constructor parameter names of DetectClassifier), it would silently replace the live OcrEngine object with whatever raw value came from YAML, causing a TypeError deep inside DetectMatcher rather than at Engine.build() call time. Currently harmless because config/settings.yaml's classifier is a bare string (no options), but it's a latent footgun with no guard/warning.

### src/oc/runtime/overrides.py

**Purpose:** Holds transient, in-process-memory overrides that Pretty Studio applies to a freshly loaded profile without ever touching the authored YAML (a path like `producers[p1].throttle` -> value, keyed by game). load_live_profile() is the function every runtime consumer (collection, pricing, triggers, preview, live/precapture sessions) must call instead of the raw profile loader so a user's in-UI tweak takes effect immediately. Deleting it would make Pretty Studio's live edits either no-ops or force-write to disk, breaking the 'transient by design' guarantee documented in the module docstring.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/pretty.py (get_overrides/set_override/clear_override/apply_overrides — the actual CRUD endpoints for Pretty Studio); src/oc/web/routes/{capture,precapture,live,flow,prices,bench}.py, src/oc/web/trigger_sched.py, src/oc/web/source_sched.py, src/oc/web/app.py (all call load_live_profile instead of oc.profile.load_profile); tests/test_pretty.py (imports oc.runtime.overrides as ov directly)

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 67): all_games() has no callers in the repo -- Defined at line 67 to list every game with at least one active override, but nothing in web/routes or elsewhere calls it, and it isn't re-exported from runtime/__init__.py's __all__ either. Small, self-contained, harmless, but unused.

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 87): _coerce() silently passes through non-scalar (list/dict) target types unchanged, bypassing any type coercion or validation -- The coercion ladder handles bool/int/float/str but falls through to `return value` unchanged for any other attribute type (e.g. a list field). The documented path grammar examples (interval_s, throttle, enabled, min_confidence) are all scalars, and no current caller sets a list-typed path, so this isn't triggering a live bug — but if Pretty Studio UI ever grows a control that overrides a list/dict-typed profile attribute, an override value from JSON (e.g. a plain list of strings) would be assigned verbatim without becoming the pydantic model instances the rest of the code expects, risking an AttributeError downstream when that field is later accessed as if it held model objects.

### src/oc/runtime/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package-level re-export surface for the runtime overrides API (apply_overrides, clear_override, get_overrides, load_live_profile, set_override), so the rest of the codebase can `from ..runtime import load_live_profile` etc. without knowing the submodule is overrides.py. Deleting it wouldn't break anything functionally IF every caller were updated to import from oc.runtime.overrides directly, but as-is every current caller imports from oc.runtime, so removing this file breaks the whole codebase's import path.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/trigger_sched.py, src/oc/web/source_sched.py, src/oc/web/app.py, src/oc/web/routes/{prices,preview,pretty,precapture,live,flow,capture,bench}.py (all do `from ..runtime import load_live_profile` or similar)

- No issues found.


_Module notes: Reviewed all 7 files in full against current on-disk content (confirmed via git log that interfaces.py/registry.py include the recent toast-notification and adaptive-capture-backend commits, per the cache-bust note). No hardcoded game-specific knowledge found anywhere in this module (clean per project rule). No mutable-default-argument bugs (dataclasses.field(default_factory=...) used correctly throughout). Bare/broad except blocks (registry._ensure_loaded's per-module import guard, overrides.apply_overrides' per-path guard) are intentional and match their docstrings (an optional backend or a stale override must not break the whole load) - not flagged as violations. No hard-rule (CLAUDE.md numbered rules 1-9) violations found in this module; rules 1-8 mostly target web/static/js and .ps1 files, none of which live here, and rule 9 (24h time/dd-mm-yy dates) doesn't apply since this module does no date/time formatting. Two low-severity unused-function findings (registry.producer_names, overrides.all_games) and two low/info design footguns (engine.py's classifier_opts merge order, settings.py's KeyError-on-missing-name) are the only things worth a second look; nothing rises to high/critical confidence._

## Module: p02-capture  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/capture/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package marker for oc.capture with a one-line docstring ('Screen capture backends.'). No code. Its existence is what makes `oc.capture.*` importable as a package; deleting it would break every `from ..capture.xxx import` / `importlib.import_module('oc.capture.xxx')` call in the codebase (registry._IMPL_MODULES imports oc.capture.mss_backend/printwindow_backend/wgc_backend/adaptive_backend by dotted path).

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES dotted imports of oc.capture.*); every module under src/oc/capture/ (implicit package membership)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/capture/adaptive_backend.py

**Purpose:** Composite CaptureBackend that switches per grab between a cheap 'foreground' sub-backend (mss BitBlt while the game is on top, no GPU cost) and an occlusion-safe 'background' sub-backend (printwindow or wgc) when the target window isn't foreground. This is the DEFAULT capture backend selected in config/settings.yaml (capture.name: adaptive). Deleting it would remove the ability to pay the background-capture GPU/re-render cost only when actually backgrounded — settings.yaml and the web UI's capture-backend picker would break (both name 'adaptive' explicitly).

**Used by:** config/settings.yaml (capture.name: adaptive, options.foreground/background); src/oc/registry.py _IMPL_MODULES ("oc.capture.adaptive_backend") for self-registration discovery; src/oc/web/routes/capture_backend.py (_COMPOSITE = "adaptive"; build_capture("adaptive", foreground=.., background=..) in _build()); src/oc/engine.py Engine.build() -> build_capture(settings.capture.name, **settings.capture.options)

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 54): Foreground/black-frame heuristic is a coarse single-pixel-max check -- grab_window() treats a foreground grab as usable only if `int(f.image.max()) > 8`. This correctly catches the documented case (exclusive-fullscreen returns pure black to a desktop BitBlt) but would also silently discard a foreground grab of a legitimately near-black scene (e.g. a loading screen, a black equipment-window background) and fall through to the background grab instead — functionally harmless (background grab is still correct pixels) but means the 'skip the GPU-cost path while foregrounded' optimization silently stops applying on dark screens. Not a data-correctness bug, just a perf-heuristic edge case worth knowing about.

### src/oc/capture/fastcap.py

**Purpose:** Standalone (explicitly 'not wired into the collector or registry yet') high-FPS ring-buffer burst capturer: spawns a dedicated thread that grabs frames as fast as a backend allows (built by name via the registry, e.g. 'wgc'), does zero per-frame processing, and stores them in a preallocated numpy ring for a consumer to drain/process afterwards. Built for the floating damage-number reading pipeline (paired with oc.numbers.process.NumberProcessor via CapturedFrame/feed_captured). If deleted, nothing currently importing it would break (see used_by), but the documented pairing with oc.numbers would lose its intended capture source.

**DEAD CODE CLAIM (unverified):** No module anywhere (src/, tests/, scripts/) actually imports FastCapturer/FastCaptureConfig/CapturedFrame/burst/iter_drained — grepped repo-wide, zero import sites. This matches the module's own docstring ('Nothing here is wired into the collector or registry yet — it's a standalone tool') and the project memory note describing the damage-numbers pipeline as standalone/not-yet-wired, so this looks like deliberate, documented scaffolding rather than an accidental orphan. Flagging dead_code=true only in the literal 'no current callers' sense, not as a recommendation to delete.

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 187): _Ring reallocation on a frame-shape change corrupts already-buffered frames instead of clearing them -- `_Ring.write()` calls `self._alloc(image.shape)` whenever the incoming frame's shape differs from `self._shape` (e.g. the captured window resizes mid-run). `_alloc` replaces every slot with a fresh `np.empty` (uninitialized memory) but does NOT reset `_count`, `_head`, `_seq`, `_t`, or `_src`. So immediately after a shape change, `drain()`/`snapshot()` will report the same number of 'live' frames as before, with their old timestamps/seq numbers intact, but their backing arrays are now uninitialized garbage (only the just-written slot holds a real image) until the ring wraps all the way around again. A consumer draining right after a resize gets frames whose `.image` is uninitialized memory stamped with stale-but-plausible metadata, silently, with no error.

- **[LOW] race-condition** (line 398): stop() may tear down the backend while the capture thread is still running -- `stop()` does `self._stop.set(); t.join(timeout)` and then unconditionally calls `self._close_backend()` regardless of whether the join actually completed. If the loop thread is wedged in a slow `self._grab()` call past the 2s default timeout, `_close_backend()` (e.g. WgcCaptureBackend.close() -> session.stop()) runs concurrently with that still-in-flight grab on the same backend object.

### src/oc/capture/mss_backend.py

**Purpose:** Registered 'mss' CaptureBackend: a plain cross-platform desktop BitBlt of an absolute-screen rectangle (or a window's client rect via grab_window). This is the default 'foreground' sub-backend used by AdaptiveCapture, and the fallback engine printwindow_backend.py/wgc_backend.py both reach for when asked to grab an arbitrary screen region (which PrintWindow/WGC can't do directly, being window-targeted APIs).

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES "oc.capture.mss_backend", @register_capture("mss")); config/settings.yaml (capture.options.foreground: mss); src/oc/settings.py (default BackendChoice("mss")); src/oc/capture/adaptive_backend.py (default foreground="mss", and its grab() always delegates absolute-screen boxes to the foreground backend); src/oc/capture/printwindow_backend.py and src/oc/capture/wgc_backend.py (lazy `from .mss_backend import MssCaptureBackend` fallback for grab()); src/oc/web/routes/capture_backend.py (read_foreground default "mss"); tests/test_precapture.py (mocks a foreground-grab dispatch keyed on "mss")

- **[LOW] robustness** (line 19): grab() has no guard for a zero-size box -- If `box.w`/`box.h` is 0 (e.g. a minimized window's client rect fed straight through), `mss.mss().grab(region)` raises `mss.exception.ScreenShotError` uncaught here. Every current caller (AdaptiveCapture's try/except around the foreground grab, FastCapturer's try/except around `_grab()`) happens to tolerate this, so it isn't currently reachable as an unhandled crash, but this backend itself performs no defensive check the way printwindow_backend.py and wgc_backend.py do (both explicitly special-case w<=0/h<=0 and return an empty frame).

### src/oc/capture/printwindow_backend.py

**Purpose:** Registered 'printwindow' CaptureBackend: grabs a specific window's own GDI/DirectX surface via `PrintWindow(hwnd, hdc, PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT)`, working even when the window is unfocused/backgrounded/occluded. This is the default 'background' sub-backend behind AdaptiveCapture and the capture method CLAUDE.md calls out as the default for reading borderless games while backgrounded.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES "oc.capture.printwindow_backend", @register_capture("printwindow")); config/settings.yaml (capture.options.background: printwindow); src/oc/capture/adaptive_backend.py (default background="printwindow"); src/oc/web/routes/capture_backend.py (read_background default "printwindow"); src/oc/capture/fastcap.py (docstring lists it as a selectable backend name)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 33): PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT is passed without PW_CLIENTONLY, but the target bitmap is only client-rect sized — no border/titlebar offset crop is applied -- Per the documented Win32 contract, `PrintWindow` copies the ENTIRE window (border + titlebar + client) into hdcBlt unless `PW_CLIENTONLY` (0x1) is also set; only `PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT` (0x2) is passed here (`self._flags = PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT`). The destination bitmap, however, is sized via `GetClientRect` only (`w, h = right-left, bottom-top` of the CLIENT rect), with no offset for where the client area sits inside the full window. Contrast this with wgc_backend.py's `_client_crop`, which explicitly computes and applies that offset for the same situation. Net effect: for any window that has a visible title bar/border (i.e. window rect != client rect — ordinary windowed-mode, not the borderless-fullscreen case this module's docstring targets), the captured/cropped image will show the window's top-left chrome shifted into the client-sized canvas instead of the true client content, so every OCR region drawn against that profile will silently read the wrong pixels with no error raised. Only harmless when client rect == window rect (borderless/frameless windows), which appears to be the assumed target per the module and CLAUDE.md docstrings, but nothing enforces or warns about that assumption for a generically 'teachable, game-agnostic' capture path.

- **[MEDIUM] resource-leak** (line 41): GetWindowDC handle acquired before the try/finally can leak on an early exception -- `hwnd_dc = win32gui.GetWindowDC(hwnd)` runs BEFORE the `try:` block starts; if `win32ui.CreateDCFromHandle(hwnd_dc)` or `mfc_dc.CreateCompatibleDC()` raises (plausible since this runs in a polling loop against a live, externally-controlled window that can close/become invalid between the earlier `GetClientRect` call and here), `hwnd_dc` is never released via `ReleaseDC` — a real per-occurrence GDI/USER handle leak. Additionally, if `bmp.CreateCompatibleBitmap(...)` itself fails, the `finally` block still calls `win32gui.DeleteObject(bmp.GetHandle())` on a bitmap wrapper that was never actually created, which is at best a no-op and at worst could itself raise and mask the original exception.

- **[LOW] duplication** (line 64): Lazy mss-fallback-for-region-grabs block duplicated verbatim in wgc_backend.py -- The `if self._mss is None: from .mss_backend import MssCaptureBackend; self._mss = MssCaptureBackend(); return self._mss.grab(box)` pattern in grab() is repeated identically in wgc_backend.py's grab(). Per CLAUDE.md hard rule 7 ('a second instance of something must be extracted into a shared primitive, not copy-pasted'), this small but genuine duplication should be a shared helper (e.g. a mixin method or module-level function) both backends call. [Hard rule: 7]

### src/oc/capture/wgc_backend.py

**Purpose:** Registered 'wgc' CaptureBackend using Windows.Graphics.Capture (via the optional windows-capture wheel) to read DWM's already-composited frame for a window without forcing it to re-render — background/occlusion-safe with near-zero cost to the captured game. Streaming=True lets the live collector poll it tight-loop for free. Recommended/default source for fastcap.py's high-FPS burst capture. If the optional dependency is missing, this module fails to import and the registry silently skips registering 'wgc', so the name simply won't resolve (by design).

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES "oc.capture.wgc_backend", @register_capture("wgc")); config/settings.yaml (documented as an alternative background backend name); src/oc/web/routes/capture_backend.py (selectable via _sub_names()/set_backend as foreground or background); src/oc/capture/fastcap.py (FastCaptureConfig.backend default "wgc", burst() default backend="wgc")

- **[LOW] resource-leak** (line 214): atexit.register(self.close) permanently pins every constructed instance for the process lifetime -- Each `WgcCaptureBackend()` construction registers its own bound `close` method via `atexit.register`, and atexit callbacks are never unregistered. Any code path that builds more than one instance over a process's life (repeated foreground/background swaps through the web UI's /api/capture/backend route, or a script/benchmark that constructs FastCapturer(backend="wgc") in a loop) leaves every prior (already explicitly closed) instance — plus its `_Session` — strongly referenced by the atexit registry forever, an unbounded slow leak. Comment calls this 'belt-and-suspenders', which is reasonable for a single long-lived engine instance, but doesn't hold once the backend is rebuilt repeatedly at runtime.

- **[LOW] duplication** (line 262): Lazy mss-fallback-for-region-grabs block duplicated verbatim in printwindow_backend.py -- Same 4-line lazy-mss-fallback pattern as printwindow_backend.py's grab() (see that file's finding) — a candidate for extraction into one shared helper per CLAUDE.md hard rule 7. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 242): Session is targeted by window TITLE while the crop math uses the caller's window HANDLE -- `_ensure_session(window.title)` starts/targets the native WGC capture purely by title string, but `_client_crop(window.handle, window, ...)` uses the specific hwnd passed into `grab_window`. If two live windows share an identical title, the WGC session could be capturing a different window than the one whose geometry is used to compute the client-area crop offset. Narrow (requires duplicate window titles) and not something the current single-game-instance use case would hit.


_Module notes: Cross-file architecture check: all six files comply with the zero-game-knowledge rule (no hardcoded item/window/game names anywhere in this module). All three plain backends (mss/printwindow/wgc) and the adaptive composite self-register via @register_capture and are discoverable through registry._IMPL_MODULES, and are genuinely wired end-to-end through config/settings.yaml -> Engine.build() and through the web UI's src/oc/web/routes/capture_backend.py picker (that route file itself is outside this module's scope so no findings were filed against it, but it was read to establish used_by evidence). fastcap.py (plus the paired src/oc/numbers package) is confirmed standalone/not-yet-wired by its own docstring and by a repo-wide grep finding zero actual import sites — treated as intentional scaffolding, not an accidental orphan, per project memory notes on the damage-numbers pipeline. The most consequential finding is the printwindow_backend.py PrintWindow flag/crop mismatch (high severity): confidence is based on documented Win32 PrintWindow semantics (default paints the full window unless PW_CLIENTONLY is set) contrasted with wgc_backend.py's own explicit _client_crop, which strongly suggests this is a real, not merely theoretical, gap for any non-borderless target window._

## Module: p03-window  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/window/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package marker for the oc.window backend package; holds only a one-line docstring identifying the package as 'Window-location backends.' Its existence lets Python treat window/ as an importable package so window.dpi, window.input, and window.win32_provider can be imported.

**Used by:** Implicitly loaded whenever anything imports a oc.window.* submodule, e.g. src/oc/registry.py:55 (_IMPL_MODULES entry 'oc.window.win32_provider'), src/oc/engine.py:52 ('from .window.dpi import set_process_dpi_aware'), src/oc/collect/precapture.py:48 ('from ..window.input import scroll_window'). Not directly referenced by name anywhere, which is expected for a package __init__.

- No issues found.

### src/oc/window/dpi.py

**Purpose:** Best-effort, one-time enabling of per-monitor DPI awareness (v2 context, then shcore, then legacy SetProcessDPIAware fallback) so win32 window geometry and screen/window captures agree on true physical pixels instead of OS-virtualized logical pixels under display scaling. Called once at Engine build time before any window/capture backend is constructed.

**Used by:** src/oc/engine.py:52-54 - 'from .window.dpi import set_process_dpi_aware' then set_process_dpi_aware() inside Engine.build(), the only call site.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 41): Fallback branches declare success without checking the underlying Win32 return value, unlike the first branch. -- The first attempt correctly gates on truthiness: `if ctypes.windll.user32.SetProcessDpiAwarenessContext(...):`. The two fallback branches do not check their call's result at all - `ctypes.windll.shcore.SetProcessDpiAwareness(2)` (an HRESULT, non-zero = failure e.g. E_ACCESSDENIED if awareness was already set another way) and `ctypes.windll.user32.SetProcessDPIAware()` (a BOOL) are both called and then unconditionally followed by `_done = True; return True`, regardless of what the API actually returned. So the documented contract 'Returns True if applied' can be violated: the function can report success when the underlying Windows call reported failure. Currently harmless because the only caller (engine.py:54) discards the return value and no test asserts on it, but it is a latent correctness gap if that changes, or if 'per-monitor aware' silently isn't achieved (matching the exact class of scaling/skew bug this module exists to prevent).

### src/oc/window/input.py

**Purpose:** Post a synthetic WM_MOUSEWHEEL message directly to a target window's handle to scroll it programmatically without moving the real mouse cursor, so the collection pipeline can page through a scrollable grid (e.g. mod/equipment lists) in a game window that isn't under the pointer or even foregrounded. win32 imports are deferred inside the function so the module still loads cleanly off Windows (the scroll then simply no-ops).

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/precapture.py:48 'from ..window.input import scroll_window'; called at precapture.py:696, 708, 832, 851, 903 to advance the scroll grid during teach-time scan/capture.

- **[INFO] error-handling** (line 20): Both the win32 import and PostMessage call are wrapped in bare `except Exception`, broader than the documented 'win32 isn't available' case. -- The docstring justifies this as making the module load/no-op on any platform, and the function's contract is explicitly best-effort (returns True/False, never raises). This is a deliberate, documented design rather than an accidental swallowed error, so it does not rise above informational - but it would also silently mask an unrelated bug inside win32api/win32con (e.g. a real TypeError from a future edit) as an ordinary 'scroll unavailable' False.

### src/oc/window/win32_provider.py

**Purpose:** The 'win32' WindowProvider backend (selected via settings.yaml `window: win32`, registered under registry._WINDOW['win32']): resolves a target game window from a PID or title substring and reports its client area (title bar/borders excluded) mapped into absolute screen coordinates, which is exactly the coordinate space FractionBox.to_pixels() and the capture backends expect. Deleting it would leave the 'win32' backend name unresolved, so build_window('win32', ...) in Engine.build() would raise a KeyError and no window could ever be located on Windows (the only supported window backend in this repo).

**Used by:** config/settings.yaml:13 'window: win32'; src/oc/registry.py:55 _IMPL_MODULES entry 'oc.window.win32_provider' (imported for its @register_window("win32") side effect); src/oc/registry.py:154 build_window(...) looks it up by name at Engine.build() time; Indirectly exercised through every caller of oc.locate (collector.py, precapture.py, cli/capture.py, cli/detect.py, cli/bench.py, web/deps.py) via engine.window.*

- **[MEDIUM] error-handling** (line 44): No exception handling around raw win32gui/win32process calls; a window destroyed in the gap between enumeration and query propagates an uncaught pywintypes.error out of the WindowProvider, breaking the pipeline's designed 'no window -> return None, never raise' contract. -- `_enum()` collects a hwnd list, then `find_for_pid`/`find_by_title`/`_to_info`/`_client_box`/`_pid_for_hwnd` each make further win32gui/win32process calls (GetClientRect, ClientToScreen, GetWindowThreadProcessId, GetWindowText) against those same hwnds with no try/except anywhere in the file. If the target window (or any enumerated window) closes between `_enum()` returning and the follow-up call - a real race during alt-tab/quit - these raise pywintypes.error instead of returning None. Collector.run()'s own loop (src/oc/collect/collector.py:610-644) only catches KeyboardInterrupt, so the exception would propagate out of that call. The live web worker (src/oc/collect/live.py:121-132) does wrap the whole collector.run() in `except Exception`, so in practice a web-driven session would stop collecting and surface `self._error` rather than crash the server or keep polling - but this is a silent full-session stop from what the architecture intends to be a routine 'window not present -> skip a tick' case (see CLAUDE.md pipeline stage 1), not a data-integrity issue.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 56): Comment says the loop prefers 'a non-minimized window with real client area' but only IsIconic is checked - a restored, zero-size client rect is not filtered. -- In find_for_pid, once IsIconic(hwnd) is false, best=hwnd is accepted unconditionally; nothing checks that GetClientRect would return a non-zero w/h (e.g. a window mid-restore-animation or genuinely 0x0). Narrow edge case, likely self-corrects on the next tick's revalidation.

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 71): from_handle() re-validates only IsWindow/IsWindowVisible/IsIconic, not that the handle still identifies the same window (pid/title) originally located. -- Windows can, in principle, recycle a destroyed HWND value for an unrelated window. WindowLocator (locate.py) caches only the raw handle and calls from_handle() to 'cheaply revalidate' each tick; if the value were reused within one poll interval it would silently report a different window's geometry/title for that cycle. In this codebase the risk is muted because the next stage, WindowClassifier.classify(), rejects any frame that doesn't match a taught anchor (architecture doc: 'no match -> skip'), so a stray wrong-window frame is very unlikely to produce a saved record. Noting only because it's the kind of assumption worth being aware of if the classifier gate is ever loosened.

### src/oc/locate.py

**Purpose:** Resolves the on-screen window for a game profile: process-name-authoritative lookup (falling back to a title-substring hint only when the profile declares no process names, so a loose title match can't grab an unrelated window such as a File Explorer folder named after the game) plus WindowLocator, a per-profile handle cache that avoids the (slow, seconds-scale) full process enumeration on every frame by cheaply revalidating the cached handle via WindowProvider.from_handle. Shared by the collector loop, the precapture scanner, and the web teaching UI.

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/detect.py:6,24 (locate_window one-shot); src/oc/cli/bench.py:27,46 (locate_window one-shot); src/oc/cli/capture.py:10,24 (locate_window one-shot); src/oc/web/deps.py:8,42-43 (WindowLocator singleton via get_locator()); src/oc/collect/collector.py:23,123,293 (WindowLocator per Collector instance, called every tick); src/oc/collect/precapture.py:40,201 (WindowLocator for teach-time scanning); tests/test_locate.py (direct unit tests of _scan and WindowLocator)

- No issues found.


_Module notes: All five files are clean of hardcoded game-specific knowledge (process_names/window_title_hint are profile-authored fields, not literals) - compliant with the zero-game-knowledge rule. No hard-rule violations from the CLAUDE.md list (poll/timer DOM, blocking dialogs, spellcheck, dataset-table row caps, dataset rename repointing, .ps1 ASCII, second-instance-of-a-primitive, Claude/Anthropic mentions, 24h/dd-mm-yy dates) apply to this module - it is backend/window-location Python with no UI or date-formatting surface. No mutable-default-argument bugs, no SQL/path injection surface, no key_map_for producer-key concerns (this module doesn't touch dataset records). The two most substantive findings are: (1) win32_provider.py has no exception handling around raw win32gui/win32process calls, so a window-teardown race can propagate an uncaught pywintypes.error instead of the intended 'no window -> None' contract (medium, mitigated at the web layer by live.py's broad except but still a silent full-session stop); (2) dpi.py's two fallback DPI-awareness attempts don't check their Win32 return codes before declaring success, unlike the first attempt (low, currently inert since the return value is unused by its only caller)._

## Module: p04-detect  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/detect/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package marker + one-line docstring for the oc.detect subpackage (window/state detection backends and helpers). Makes oc.detect.classifier/matcher/template importable as a regular package. Deleting it (or its docstring) doesn't remove any behavior, but the package layout convention used throughout this repo relies on explicit __init__.py files, so removing it risks packaging/tooling assumptions even though Python 3's implicit namespace packages would still resolve the imports.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES entry 'oc.detect.classifier' imports through this package); src/oc/detect/classifier.py, matcher.py, template.py (siblings in the same package); src/oc/web/routes/preview.py ('from ...detect.matcher import ...'); src/oc/collect/items.py ('from ..detect.matcher import text_match_score'); tests/test_detect.py, tests/test_gate.py

- No issues found.

### src/oc/detect/classifier.py

**Purpose:** Implements DetectClassifier, the 'detect' WindowClassifier backend: given a captured Frame and a GameProfile, decides which window (and optional state) is on screen by combining each window's DetectDef probes (cheap template/colour first, OCR text last), either via priority-order early-return or best-fit aggregate scoring. This is the concrete implementation Collector.tick() step 2 depends on. If deleted, config/settings.yaml's `classifier: detect` would fail to resolve in registry.build_classifier(), so Engine.build() would raise and nothing in collect/ or the web app could start.

**Used by:** config/settings.yaml ('classifier: detect'); src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES + register_classifier('detect')); src/oc/engine.py (Engine.build() -> build_classifier(settings.classifier.name, **classifier_opts)); tests/test_gate.py, tests/test_detect.py (direct import + construction via DetectClassifier.__new__)

- **[LOW] efficiency** (line 65): _window_score recomputes each detector's score a second time for windows that already passed _window_matches, and only OCR text reads are memoized -- In classify()'s best-fit branch (empty window_priority), candidates are first filtered by _window_matches (line 47-53), which calls self._matcher.matches(d, frame) -> matcher.score(d, frame) once per active detector. Every window that passes is then re-scored from scratch by _window_score (lines 55-66, especially line 64-65: `self._matcher.score(d, frame) ... for d in active`) to compute the tie-break. DetectMatcher memoizes OCR text reads per-frame by box (matcher.py's _memo dict), so a text detector's second score() call is cheap, but template (cv2.matchTemplate) and colour (border_score/color_score) scoring have no such memo — those run their full pixel computation twice for every passing window whenever more than one window matches on a given frame. Not a correctness bug, just doubled compute for the cheap-detector class on multi-match frames.

### src/oc/detect/matcher.py

**Purpose:** The evaluation core for one DetectDef against a frame: DetectMatcher.score()/matches()/evaluate() dispatch to template match, colour/border presence, or OCR text match (with a per-frame OCR memo and a GPU-only batched 'prewarm' pass), plus the free helper functions text_match_score/match_detail/detector_passes/combine_passes used to combine and explain per-detector verdicts. classifier.py's window/state recognition, and the web teaching UI's live /api/detect + /api/detect/collisions endpoints, and collect/items.py's item-tell text scoring all sit directly on top of this file.

**Used by:** src/oc/detect/classifier.py (DetectMatcher, combine_passes, detector_passes); src/oc/web/routes/preview.py (DetectMatcher, combine_passes — /api/detect, /api/detect/collisions/{game}); src/oc/collect/items.py (text_match_score, line ~476, for item-tell text scoring); tests/test_detect.py (text_match_score, DetectClassifier), tests/test_gate.py (DetectMatcher, DetectDef.is_cheap)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 231): score()/evaluate() unconditionally prefer template, then color, over text — silently ignoring det.text whenever template or color is also set, contradicting the documented/tested is_cheap invariant -- score() (lines 226-240) and evaluate() (lines 245-269) both branch `if det.template: ... elif/if det.color: ... else: (text path)`. DetectDef has no validator forbidding template/color and text from being set simultaneously, and DetectDef.is_cheap (profile/models.py) explicitly documents the opposite priority for the purpose of classifying cost: 'A detector with text set is never cheap' — and tests/test_gate.py:43 encodes this exact scenario with the comment 'text wins even if a colour is also set (text means an OCR read is needed)' for `DetectDef(id='m', text='R', color='#fff', threshold=0.8)`. That detector is correctly flagged is_cheap=False (implying its text match matters and OCR is required), and classifier.py's _cheap_first sorts it after cheap detectors while _text_detect_boxes/prewarm includes its box for OCR batching — yet DetectMatcher.score() takes the `if det.color:` branch at line 231 and returns border_score/color_score WITHOUT EVER calling _text_score, so the OCR read (even though it was just prewarmed) and the author's `text` criterion have zero effect on the verdict. Concrete failure: an author sets a detector with both a colour hint and a text landmark (e.g. hoping the colour narrows a region and the text confirms it), the window/state will pass or fail purely on the colour presence — a coincidental colour match elsewhere on screen with no text present would still 'pass' silently, while a correct colour absent (say a themed variant) with the right text present would 'fail' even though the intended text check would have passed. Same precedence issue applies to template vs. text (template checked first at line 228, before color and before text).

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 126): match_detail's early-return ordering differs from text_match_score's, so its editor-only 'reasoning' output can disagree with the actual score's basis for rejection on min_chars vs. empty-target cases -- text_match_score checks emptiness of the normalised read (`if not ng: return 0.0`), then the min_chars floor, then emptiness of the normalised target (`if not nw: return 1.0`) — in that order (lines 81-86). match_detail instead checks `if not nw or not ng: return out` (line 126) BEFORE checking min_chars (line 128). For an empty-target ('any text present') detector whose read is shorter than min_chars, text_match_score correctly reports 0.0 because it reaches the min_chars check first; match_detail short-circuits on `not nw` before ever reporting the min_chars comparison context in a way consistent with that path. This is explicitly documented as 'NOT on the runtime path — text_match_score stays the single source of the score' (docstring, lines 110/120), so it never changes what actually matches — it only means the editor's per-character 'why' highlight can be less informative than it could be for this one edge case. Cosmetic, not a scoring bug.

### src/oc/detect/template.py

**Purpose:** Small OpenCV helper backing 'template'-kind DetectDef probes: load_template() reads and caches a landmark PNG from disk, best_match() returns the peak normalized cross-correlation of that template within a captured crop. Invoked from DetectMatcher.score()/evaluate() whenever a detector has `template` set (checked first, ahead of color/text — see matcher.py finding above). Deleting this module breaks the import at the top of matcher.py (`from .template import best_match, load_template`), which is unconditional, so ANY use of the detect module — including plain text/colour detectors — would fail at import time.

**Used by:** src/oc/detect/matcher.py (unconditional module import; load_template/best_match called from DetectMatcher.score()/evaluate() when det.template is set)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 10): _TEMPLATE_CACHE is a module-level dict with no invalidation — a template PNG replaced on disk at the same path is never re-read for the life of the server process -- load_template() (lines 13-21) caches every loaded template image forever in the module-global `_TEMPLATE_CACHE` dict keyed by string path, with no TTL, mtime check, or any exposed way to bust an entry (confirmed via a repo-wide grep: `_TEMPLATE_CACHE` and `load_template` are referenced nowhere else). DetectMatcher instances are created fresh per-request in web/routes/preview.py and once at startup inside DetectClassifier for the whole life of `data-occultist serve` (engine.py builds the classifier once in Engine.build()), but the cache they share is process-global regardless. Concrete scenario: a user re-authors a template-kind detector's landmark image (re-captures the same cutout to the same on-disk filename) while the server is running; every subsequent /api/detect preview call and every live classify() tick keeps matching against the STALE cached bytes until the process is restarted, silently showing/using outdated match results with no visible indication that the cache is stale. This project has hit exactly this class of bug before with OCR result caching (which required an explicit prefer_cache/signature invalidation scheme) and template.py has no analogous mechanism at all.


_Module notes: Scope: the four files implementing the "detect" window/state classifier backend (registered via @register_classifier("detect"), selected by config/settings.yaml's `classifier: detect`). All four are live, reachable code with confirmed callers (engine.py -> registry.py -> classifier.py; web/routes/preview.py; collect/items.py; several tests) — none are dead. No hardcoded game-specific knowledge found (only doc-comment examples like "INVENTORY"/"relic rewards", never a branch keyed on a literal item/window name). No hard-rule (CLAUDE.md items 1-9) violations found — this module has no UI polling/dialogs/spellcheck/tables/dataset-rename/.ps1/Claude-mention/date-format surface. The one high-confidence finding is a real precedence bug in DetectMatcher.score()/evaluate() (template > color > text, unconditionally) that contradicts the documented and unit-tested invariant on DetectDef.is_cheap ("text wins even if a colour is also set... text means an OCR read is needed", tests/test_gate.py:43) — a detector with both `color`/`template` and `text` set silently never runs the text check at all. A secondary medium finding is an unbounded, never-invalidated module-level template-image cache in template.py (stale-cache risk in the long-running `serve` process, mirroring a class of bug this project has hit before with OCR caching). Everything else is low/info-level nuance (double-scoring efficiency in best-fit mode, a cosmetic ordering difference in the editor-only `match_detail` helper, and template.py's `template`-kind detector currently having no authoring path in the shipped UI though the backend fully supports it)._

## Module: p05-collector  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/collect/collector.py

**Purpose:** The Collector class that wires the whole per-tick pipeline together: locate window -> capture frame -> settle-gate (skip blurred/mid-animation grabs) -> classify (window_id,state_id) with a tolerant detector-region cache -> state-validity gate -> live-readout read (bypasses motion/state gates) -> region OCR read with a per-window frame cache (skip re-OCR of a pixel-identical grid) -> confidence-floor filter -> per-dataset temporal Confirmer (stability+dedup) -> write to the dataset's DatasetStore (or an explicit RecordSink) -> mirror-mode scroll-slice sync (soft-remove keys scrolled out of a visible slice) -> per-tick timing telemetry. Also implements the two-rate run() loop (fast gate_interval poll, throttled collect_interval OCR) and per-detection batching for `batch_mode: detection` datasets. If deleted, nothing drives actual collection: `data-occultist collect`, the web UI's live-collection worker (LiveSession), and dataset population from OCR would all have no engine to run.

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/collect.py (imports Collector, TickResult, TickStatus; constructs Collector(engine, profile) and calls collector.tick() in the CLI collect command); src/oc/collect/live.py (imports Collector, TickStatus; LiveSession._loop constructs Collector(self._engine, self._profile), sets collector.on_frame, and drives collector.run(self._interval, on_tick=self._on_tick, should_stop=self._stop.is_set) for the web UI's live-collection worker); tests/test_gate.py (imports Collector, TickResult, TickStatus directly; unit-tests the two-rate throttle accounting, the per-detection-batch tick-gap counter, and LiveSession phase handling against TickStatus values); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/livewin.js (consumes the JSON that LiveSession.status()/debug() derive from TickStatus/TickResult over the live-collection web API, e.g. phase_status strings like "throttled"/"moving"/"saved")

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 585): For a profile with live readouts, a sustained-motion frame runs the full classify (+ read_readouts) OCR pass every gate_interval tick, silently defeating the collect_interval OCR throttle. -- In tick(), when `self._any_readouts` is true the settle-gate early return is skipped, so a moving frame still runs `self._classify(frame)` and (if the matched window declares readouts) `self._reader.read_readouts(...)`, then returns `TickResult(TickStatus.moving, ...)`. `TickStatus.moving` is unconditionally a member of `_HEAVY_SKIPPED` (line 585-586), so in `run()` `last_ocr` is never advanced for these ticks (`if ocr_due and result.status not in self._HEAVY_SKIPPED: last_ocr = now`). If the screen stays in continuous motion (e.g. combat/particle animation, which is exactly the case this feature exists for per the comment "must surface even mid-combat"), `last_ocr` can remain stuck (e.g. at the initial `-inf`), so `ocr_due = (now - last_ocr) >= interval` is true on every fast `gate_interval` poll, not just every `collect_interval`. With the shipped defaults (config/settings.yaml: collect_interval=1.0, gate_interval=0.25) that is a 4x-more-frequent execution of the classify pass than the two-rate design promises in run()'s own docstring ("runs the classify/OCR path at most once per interval"). The current warframe.yaml profile happens to give its readouts window a cheap color detector (config/games/warframe.yaml lines 83-91), so classify() itself stays cheap there and the practical cost today is limited to the small read_readouts boxes — but nothing in the code enforces that; a profile author (everything here is meant to be UI-authored, zero game logic in Python) could put readouts on a window whose top-priority detect requires OCR text matching, at which point every fast poll during motion would pay a full OCR-heavy classify, not just the throttled rate.

- **[LOW] type-hint** (line 169): `_frame_cache` is annotated as a 2-tuple but is populated and read as a 3-tuple at runtime. -- Line 169 declares `self._frame_cache: dict[str, tuple[int, list[Record]]] = {}`, but line 389 stores `(sig, records, sentinel_ypos)` (a 3-tuple) and line 379 destructures `cached[1], cached[2]` accordingly. Python doesn't enforce the annotation so this isn't a runtime bug, but it's misleading to a reader or a static type checker and should read `tuple[int, list[Record], float | None]`.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 401): The explicit-`RecordSink` code path, if ever exercised with a dataset-less window, resolves a key map/confirmer for `dataset=None`, an untested edge case. -- When `self._explicit_sink is not None` and the matched `window.dataset_id` is `None`, the early "nothing storable" return at line 401 is skipped (it requires `self._explicit_sink is None`), and execution falls through to `confirmer = self._confirmer_for(window)` (line 431), which calls `self._key_map(dataset)` with `dataset=None`, in turn calling `self._profile.key_conflict(None)` / `self._profile.key_map_for(None)`. Whether the profile model handles a `None` dataset id gracefully is unverified. In practice this is currently unreachable: a repo-wide grep shows both real callers (`src/oc/cli/collect.py:36` and `src/oc/collect/live.py:124`) construct `Collector(engine, profile)` with no `sink` argument, and `JsonlSink` (the only concrete `RecordSink` in the codebase, in `src/oc/collect/sink.py`) is never instantiated anywhere, so the whole `sink`-provided branch is a cold/dead path today, just not one the collector.py file itself can be blamed for exercising incorrectly — flagging in case a future caller starts passing a sink.

- **[INFO] dead-code** (line 89): TickStatus.idle is a vestigial enum member kept only for back-compat and is never emitted. -- The class docstring for `TickStatus.idle` (line 89) says "legacy: kept for status back-compat; no longer emitted", and a repo-wide grep confirms no code constructs `TickResult(TickStatus.idle, ...)` anywhere. It remains harmlessly listed in `_HEAVY_SKIPPED` (line 585). This matches its own documentation and is not a defect, just noted for completeness.
