## Module: p06-reader  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/collect/reader.py

**Purpose:** The single OCR-read engine for a window: locates the grid's cells (static template, content-detected dynamic rows, or content-located item templates), runs ONE batched OCR pass over the union of every target box (plus a batched focus-read of whatever the first pass missed), runs each field's rule pipeline / FieldResolver dictionary correction on the raw text, and packages the result either as Record objects for live collection (read()) or as rich debug/preview dicts for the teaching UI (read_preview(), representative_raws(), read_cutout()) and live triggers (read_readouts()). If deleted, both Collector.tick() (live collection) and every web preview/rule-trace/cutout-test endpoint lose their entire OCR-to-record pipeline - nothing else in the codebase performs region OCR + field extraction.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py (imports Record, RegionReader; constructs RegionReader(engine.ocr, resolver, templates, glyphs); calls .read_readouts, .region_signature, .read every tick); src/oc/collect/precapture.py (imports RegionReader; constructs it; calls .region_signature and .read in _read_cached for boot/replay OCR); src/oc/web/routes/preview.py (imports RegionReader; constructs it in two handlers; calls .read_preview, .read_readouts, .representative_raws, .read, .read_cutout - the teaching UI's live preview, rule-trace, and cutout-test endpoints); src/oc/collect/stability.py, src/oc/collect/sink.py, src/oc/collect/commit.py (import the Record dataclass defined here as the shared record type through the rest of the collection pipeline); src/oc/collect/items.py (docstring on locate_item_cells/read_cutout describes the self-match invariant RegionReader.read_cutout is relied on to preserve)

- **[HIGH] caching-bug** (line 601): region_signature always returns None for item-template windows, permanently disabling the 'skip re-OCR on an unchanged frame' cache for the app's primary grid windows. -- region_signature (lines 594-607) hashes the union produced by _pixel_targets (lines 255-256), which is _targets_from_cells(expand_cells(window), frame) - i.e. built purely from window.regions, never the data_area/item-content region _resolve_cells actually OCRs for item-template windows. grid.py's expand_cells builds every cell's boxes dict from `{r.field: r.box.to_fraction() for r in window.regions if r.enabled}`; per the WindowDef docstring, item-template windows are meant to leave regions empty ('items ... preferred over regions+scroll'). I verified this empirically against config/games/warframe.yaml: every window with items configured (equipment_window, mod_window, relic_select, relic_refinement) has regions=[], so expand_cells returns cells with boxes={} for all of them, _pixel_targets is always [], and region_signature hits 'if not targets: return None' (line 601) unconditionally. In collector.py's tick() (~line 373-389) and precapture.py's _read_cached (~line 1062-1070), `sig is not None` gates both the cache-hit check and the cache write, so with sig always None the OCR-skip optimization this function's own docstring describes ('lets the collector skip re-OCR while the user isn't scrolling/changing the screen') never engages for any item-template window - which, per the real profile, is every scrollable grid window in the game (equipment, mods, relics). On a perfectly static equipment screen, Collector.tick() re-runs the full OCR+resolve pipeline every single tick instead of reusing the previous tick's records.

- **[MEDIUM] consistency-bug** (line 570): representative_raws doesn't special-case pips/diamonds readout fields the way read_readouts does, so a pip-type readout would report an empty raw/conf pair in the rule-trace debug panel. -- representative_raws (lines 556-592) builds the readout raw/conf map by running every enabled readout box through _detect_reads (a text-OCR call, line 570) regardless of the linked field's type. read_readouts (lines 516-554), in contrast, explicitly branches on `self._is_pip(fdef)` (lines 533-535) and reports the pixel-counted value instead of attempting to OCR text. ReadoutDef.field (profile/models.py) has no type restriction, so a pips/diamonds field can legally back a readout. For such a readout, representative_raws would OCR its icon area as text (almost certainly finding nothing) and default to ("", 0.0) at line 573 for the field-node rule-trace panel, while read_readouts correctly reports the count elsewhere - two supposedly-equivalent 'what did this field read' code paths disagreeing. Not currently exercised (no pips/diamonds readout exists in config/games/warframe.yaml), so this is latent rather than actively manifesting.

- **[LOW] docs-mismatch** (line 46): Record.raw's docstring claims pip fields are excluded, but the dict it documents always includes their synthetic count string. -- Record.raw's comment (lines 45-46) states the dict is 'Empty for pip fields / fallback-fired fields', but read() builds it via a pure truthiness filter: `raw={fid: fr.raw for fid, fr in c.fields.items() if fr.raw}` (line 467). For a pips/diamonds field, _FieldRead.raw is always the synthetic string f'{cnt} {unit}' (e.g. '3 pips', set at line 408) - never falsy - so it IS included, contradicting the docstring. collector.py's _debug_reads() (lines 54-66) forwards `dict(r.raw)` straight into the live debug log the web UI surfaces, so a pip field shows up under 'raw' as if OCR literally read the text '3 pips', which can mislead someone debugging genuine OCR/text quality via that log.

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 326): _resolve_cells's static-grid branch computes text_boxes that _ocr_union never actually uses. -- In the plain static-grid branch of _resolve_cells (no items, no dynamic scroll), text_boxes is built (line 326) and passed to `_ocr_union(frame, text_boxes, window.preprocess, clip)` (line 333). But _ocr_union (lines 121-146) only consults its boxes argument when clip is None ('u = clip if clip is not None else _union(boxes)', line 130); by the time of this call, clip is guaranteed non-None - either the data_area clip from self._clip(...), or the explicit full-canvas override at lines 330-332 ('if clip is None: ... clip = PixelBox(0,0,iw,ih)'). So text_boxes never influences what actually gets cropped/OCR'd; it's inert. The other two branches of _resolve_cells (items, dynamic scroll) already reflect this by passing [] instead of real boxes (lines 305, 320) - this branch is the inconsistent one and could drop the text_boxes computation with zero behavior change.

- **[LOW] efficiency** (line 497): _read_tell_boxes is invoked once per candidate cell (its own small OCR batch each time) instead of once across every cell, undermining the batching the rest of the module is built around. -- read()'s validity filter (line 497) and read_preview()'s per-cell loop (line 650) each call self._read_tell_boxes(frame, window, ics[ci]) separately for every cell. Any cell whose item has a fieldless text tell triggers its own _focus_reads call there, which genuinely batches via self._ocr.read_lines(crops) (one engine call per invocation, per the OcrEngine contract) - but across N cells that means N separate small read_lines batches instead of ONE batch covering all cells' tell crops. This is exactly the per-call overhead the module's top docstring says the whole batched-OCR design exists to avoid; the main field-read pass in _read_cells already does this correctly (one pending/focus pass spanning every cell, lines 387-397). No fieldless text tell exists in config/games/warframe.yaml today, so this is currently a no-op, but it will silently regress read/preview latency the moment one is authored through the UI.

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 261): _anchor_region can select a disabled region as the row-detection anchor, unlike expand_cells/cells_for_rows which filter on region.enabled. -- _anchor_region (lines 258-265) returns the first region whose field type is FieldType.text without checking region.enabled, whereas grid.py's expand_cells/cells_for_rows both filter `if r.enabled` when building actual cell boxes. A disabled text-type region could therefore be chosen as the geometric row-detection anchor in _detect_rows even though its field never appears in the cells built downstream. Since the anchor is used purely for geometry (which x-range/y-centre defines a 'row'), not to read/store a value, this is likely benign by design; flagging only as an asymmetry with the enabled-filtering used everywhere else cells are built, not a reproduced failure.


_Module notes: reader.py is the single OCR-read core the whole system is built on (module docstring: batch the union of target boxes into ONE OCR pass, then assign lines to boxes, so N-cell grids cost one OCR call instead of N). _read_cells is explicitly the one function both collection (read()) and the teaching preview (read_preview()) ride, per CLAUDE.md rule 7 ("the two can never drift apart") - confirmed they do share it. No hardcoded game/item knowledge found (fully FieldDef/WindowDef/ItemDef driven, satisfies the zero-game-knowledge rule). No bare except, no mutable default args, no blocking dialogs/innerHTML/spellcheck/date-format issues (this is pure backend Python with no UI surface). The most substantive finding is a caching bug: region_signature's hash region is built from expand_cells(window), which only reflects window.regions - and I verified against the live config/games/warframe.yaml that every item-template window (equipment_window, mod_window, relic_select, relic_refinement) has an EMPTY regions list (items supersede regions+scroll per the WindowDef docstring), so region_signature always returns None for them, permanently disabling the "skip OCR on an unchanged frame" optimization for the app's primary grid windows. Everything else is lower-severity consistency/documentation/latent-efficiency items; call-signature cross-checks against items.py (valid_cell/tell_report/cell_occluded/resolve_overlaps/grid_drift/locate_item_cells) and rows.py/grid.py (fit_row_lattice/cells_for_rows/expand_cells) all lined up correctly with no argument-order mismatches._

## Module: p07-precapture  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/collect/precapture.py

**Purpose:** Implements PrecaptureSession: the 'record fast, batch-OCR later' pipeline used by the web UI's precapture modal. A worker thread grabs+JPEG-stashes frames (to RAM and to captures/<game>/precapture/<session>/*.jpg) while the user scrolls a game window (optionally auto-scrolling it via wheel input and a scrollbar-thumb-driven timed step-and-shoot loop); a second worker thread later decodes each stashed frame, classifies/reads it through the same RegionReader used by live collection, stages deduped records per dataset, checkpoints progress to ocr_state.json, and on 'save' fuzzy-consolidates OCR-noise near-duplicate keys before committing into the real per-dataset DatasetStore. If deleted, the whole precapture feature (routes/precapture.py's PrecaptureSession import) breaks at import time, taking down record/process/save/session-management endpoints and the precapture UI panel with it.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/precapture.py:18 - `from ...collect.precapture import PrecaptureSession`; instantiates one session per game (line 60) and calls start_recording/stop_recording/start_processing/pause/cancel/reset/save/status/list_sessions/load_session/rename_session/delete_session/delete_all_sessions/is_running/kill/update_profile across its endpoints; src/oc/web/routes/activity.py:20 - `from .precapture import _sessions` (re-exported dict of live PrecaptureSession instances, for GPU-activity/is_running checks); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/precap.js - front-end panel driving the /api/precapture/* HTTP endpoints backed by this module (indirect, via routes/precapture.py)

- **[HIGH] concurrency** (line 586): start_recording()'s 'already running' guard is not atomic with the phase transition, so two overlapping calls can both start a recording thread against the same shared session state. -- start_recording() (lines 584-604) checks `if self._phase in (Phase.recording, Phase.processing): return` under `self._lock`, then releases the lock and does `_join_prev()` plus `_apply_window_autoscroll()` (which calls `self._locator.locate(self._profile)` -- documented elsewhere in this codebase as 'process enumeration is slow on Windows, first scan ~seconds') BEFORE re-acquiring the lock to actually set `self._phase = Phase.recording` and spawn `self._thread`. During that multi-second window `self._phase` is still not `recording`, so a second overlapping call (browser double-click before UI feedback updates, or a retried HTTP request) passes the same guard check and also proceeds to create its own session id, reset shared state, and start its own thread. Whichever call finishes second silently overwrites `self._thread` and `self._session`, so the FIRST thread keeps running _record_loop but is no longer reachable via `_join_prev`/`stop_recording`/`kill` (they only ever see the latest `self._thread`), while both threads concurrently append to the shared `self._frames` list and write into whatever `self._dir` (a property driven by the now-overwritten `self._session`) currently resolves to -- producing an interleaved/corrupted recording session that the normal stop/kill/reset controls can no longer fully tear down.

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 1061): Precapture processing throws away the reader's terminator (sentinel_ypos) cutoff, so it can stage rows past a list-ending terminator marker that live collection actively purges. -- RegionReader.read() returns `(records, sentinel_ypos)`, where `sentinel_ypos` is the topmost kept terminator item's position -- collector.py's live tick (collector.py ~line 545-546) uses it to call `store.remove_after(...)`, actively removing stale/misread keys parked past a terminator (e.g. a 'no relic selected' placeholder marking real list end). PrecaptureSession._read_cached (lines 1061-1070) calls the same `self._reader.read(frame, window, fields)` but discards the second return value entirely (`records, _sentinel = self._reader.read(...)`), and _process_batch never looks at it. So a window whose profile defines a terminator item (config/games/warframe.yaml currently has one item with `terminator: true`) gets no equivalent cutoff applied when data is collected via precapture: any misread/placeholder row that scrolled past the terminator during recording stays staged and, because _consolidate()'s fuzzy merge only folds keys that are textually SIMILAR to a frequent canonical key (not structurally-unrelated garbage), it is not caught there either -- it can be committed straight into the real dataset store on save(), producing data a live-collection run of the same window would never have kept.

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 1177): _reset_locked() doesn't clear the recognition tally or perf-timing accumulators, so status() can show stale per-window recognition counts and bogus timing figures from a previous run during a brand-new recording. -- _reset_locked() (lines 1177-1188) is called by reset(), load_session(), delete_session()/delete_all_sessions(), and start_recording() to blank per-run state, and it does zero `_frames`/`_staged`/`_processed`/`_read`/`_no_key`/`_gaps`/`_rec`/`_phase`/`_error` -- but NOT `self._recog`, `self._cur_window`, `self._cur_state`, or the perf accumulators `self._t_decode`/`self._t_classify`/`self._t_read` (those are only reset inside `_launch_processing`, lines 951-970). Concretely: run a precapture session through to 'done' (populating `_recog` with e.g. `{'equipment/browsing': 42}` and `_t_decode` etc with real totals), save or reset, then start a brand-new recording. During the ENTIRE new recording phase, status()'s `recognized` list still reports the previous session's stale window/state tallies (nothing in _record_loop updates `_recog`), and status()'s `timing()` divides the still-nonzero `_t_decode+_t_classify+_t_read` totals by `max(1, self._processed)` which is now 1 (processed==0), reporting the OLD run's total batch time as if it were a per-frame duration -- until the user actually starts processing the new recording, which is the only path that clears these fields.

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 872): The fast auto-scroll loop's 'did we leave the window' check treats an unclassifiable detect-signature comparison as 'unchanged', the opposite of the convention used two lines earlier in the same loop and of detsig's own documented contract. -- Line 798-800 in the outer record loop does `delta = detsig.changed(feat, scroll_feat); if delta is None or delta >= detsig.MIN_CELLS:` -- i.e. treats a None comparison (frames can't be compared) as 'changed, reclassify', matching detsig.py's own docstring ('None ... the caller should treat that as changed'). But inside the TIMED step-and-shoot inner loop, line 872-874 computes `left = (not fg) or (detsig.changed(detsig.features(f, self._detect_fracs), scroll_feat) or 0) >= detsig.MIN_CELLS` -- here `None or 0` collapses to `0`, and `0 >= MIN_CELLS` is False, so an incomparable signature (e.g. a transient empty/black grab from a capture glitch, which this same file's _grab_frame docstring explicitly anticipates as a real failure mode) is silently treated as 'still the same window', letting the timed scroll/grab cadence continue against a window that was never actually re-verified, instead of breaking out to re-classify like the outer loop would.

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 1090): _state_allows_save and _key_map duplicate collector.py's identically-named, identically-purposed methods verbatim instead of sharing one primitive, and the duplication has already visibly drifted. -- PrecaptureSession._state_allows_save (lines 1082-1088) is a byte-for-byte copy of Collector._state_allows_save (collector.py lines 189-195). PrecaptureSession._key_map (lines 1090-1093) is a near-copy of Collector._key_map (collector.py lines 197-205) but is missing the `if self._profile.key_conflict(dataset): logging.getLogger(__name__).warning(...)` check the collector version has -- meaning the exact same 'windows feeding this dataset disagree on the record key' condition (a real, documented risk class in this codebase: mismatched read/write keys have previously re-keyed a ledger to NULL) is silently undiagnosed when data is collected via precapture instead of live collection. This is the concrete 'second copy diverges, later features land on only one' anti-pattern CLAUDE.md rule 7 calls out by name; both helpers should be hoisted into one shared function both Collector and PrecaptureSession call. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 1181): self._consolidated is assigned but never read anywhere in the file or repo. -- _reset_locked() sets `self._consolidated = None` (line 1181); grepping the whole file and the whole src tree finds no other reference to `_consolidated` (it is never initialized in __init__ either, and never read in save()/status()). It looks like a leftover attribute from a removed caching feature for the consolidated preview -- harmless, but dead state that should be removed or wired up.

## Module: p08-items-rows  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/collect/items.py

**Purpose:** Locates item-template cells dynamically (no fixed grid): back-projects each item's locator OCR text to a cell origin honoring authored align/align_x, gap-clusters those origins per axis into the grid's real rows/columns, resolves overlapping template claims by priority (resolve_overlaps), and scores/validates each candidate cell's tells (visual + text, including pip/diamond mark counts) to confirm it is a genuine item instance rather than a gap/tooltip/occluded row. If deleted, every profile window that defines item templates (WindowDef.items, e.g. Warframe's mod/arcane/equipment windows) loses its entire location+validation path; collector.py, reader.py, precapture.py and the preview/teaching routes all import from it directly, so collection, live preview, and precapture would all break for any item-template window.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py (imports item_templates, calls it before the per-tick read loop); src/oc/collect/reader.py (imports ItemCell, cell_cover, cell_occluded, grid_drift, locate_item_cells, resolve_overlaps, tell_report, valid_cell — the core of _resolve_cells/_read_cells); src/oc/collect/precapture.py (imports item_templates for the offline/precapture pass); src/oc/web/routes/preview.py (imports item_templates for the teaching-UI live preview endpoints); src/oc/web/captures_store.py (docstring references item_templates as the template-tell reference builder); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/imaging.js (comment: its tile-origin JS mirrors static_grid_origins() here so the drawn grid preview matches exactly)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 517): A diamonds-type field marked as its own tell always normalizes mark count by /5, unlike the dedicated diamonds Tell kind which normalizes by /3 specifically to handle 3-mark strips. -- In _field_tell_result (~line 517-519), `score = min(1.0, cnt / 5.0)` is used for both FieldType.pips and FieldType.diamonds when a field doubles as its own tell. But src/oc/collect/tells.py's diamonds_score (used by the dedicated TellKind.diamonds path) deliberately normalizes by /3 instead, with its own docstring stating exactly why: "arcanes cap at different ranks (3-mark and 5-mark strips both exist)... Normalising by 5 would score a 3-mark arcane 0.6 and miss it." _field_tell_result does precisely what that sibling comment warns against. It is not currently triggering a failure — config/games/warframe.yaml's arcane_level field (type diamonds, tell: true) sets tell_conf: 0.3, comfortably under the 0.6 ceiling a fully-visible 3-mark strip produces — but any future re-tune of that threshold above 0.6 (e.g. to 0.7 for stricter detection) would make a fully-visible 3-mark arcane's field-tell permanently fail, since count_diamonds can never exceed 3/5=0.6 for a 3-mark strip.

- **[LOW] robustness** (line 88): _crop() resolves a FractionBox to pixels and slices frame.image without clamping to the frame's actual bounds. -- _crop (line 88-90) does `frame.image[pb.y:pb.y+pb.h, pb.x:pb.x+pb.w]` where pb comes from FractionBox.to_pixels (types.py), which does not clamp negative/over-range coordinates. Python/numpy slice semantics can silently return an empty or differently-shaped array instead of raising, for boxes that stray outside the frame. In the current call graph this is defused: every consumer (count_diamonds/count_pips in pips.py, telldet.visual_score's kind handlers in tells.py) explicitly checks `.size == 0` before doing anything with the crop, and upstream box math (_cell_in_bounds, _expand_box's [0,1] clamp) already keeps boxes sane in practice. Flagging as a latent gap, not an observed failure.

- **[LOW] clarity** (line 356): A Tell-kind row locator's confidence floor and a field-kind row locator's confidence floor are derived inconsistently, by reusing RegionDef.tell_conf (a tell-validation knob) as the row-locator OCR-garbage floor. -- In locate_item_cells (~line 356), `tc = getattr(loc, "tell_conf", 0.0) or 0.0; conf = tc if tc > 0 else _LOC_MIN_CONF`. RegionDef.tell_conf genuinely exists and is documented as "min OCR confidence the read must reach" for TELL validation, not for locator-line filtering; reusing it here means a field authored with a low tell_conf (for tell-leniency reasons) also lowers the bar for admitting OCR noise as a row anchor if that same field is chosen as the locator. Tell (the tell-object class) has no tell_conf attribute at all, so getattr's default silently makes every Tell-based locator fall back to _LOC_MIN_CONF regardless — the two locator kinds can't be tuned the same way. No dedicated "locator confidence" knob exists, so this looks like an intentional (if overloaded) reuse rather than an oversight, but it's worth confirming with the author.

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 558): valid_cell()/cell_results() return True by vacuous `all([])` for an item template with zero tells and zero field-tells. -- cell_results concatenates per-tell and per-field-tell results; if an ItemDef has neither, the list is empty and `all(r["pass"] for r in [])` is True, so such a template validates every candidate cell unconditionally. This matches the codebase's documented concept of a fieldless "guard" template (see grid_drift's comment about a fieldless guard like a "no relic" placeholder tiling every row with its own locator, never stored) so it reads as intentional rather than a bug, but is worth a second look if a template is ever misconfigured with no tells by accident.

### src/oc/collect/rows.py

**Purpose:** Pure client-fraction row-position math for a scrolled/dynamic grid: detect_row_centers clusters OCR line y-centres into row bands (a new band when the gap exceeds ~0.6x the expected pitch), and fit_row_lattice fits a regular pitch+phase lattice over those bands so missing/occluded rows are filled in and phantom noise rows are rejected. This is what lets a scrolled list (parked at an arbitrary sub-row offset) still produce evenly-spaced, content-anchored row positions. If deleted, reader.py's dynamic-grid path (_detect_rows, used for any scroll-enabled window with >1 row and no item templates) has no implementation to import — the whole reader module would fail to import, breaking collection entirely, not just this one feature.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/reader.py (imports fit_row_lattice; RegionReader._detect_rows calls it to place cells for scroll-enabled, region-based grid windows); tests/test_rows.py (direct unit tests of both detect_row_centers and fit_row_lattice, including the anchor='top'/'bottom'/'center' modes and pitch_tol clamping/gap-filling behavior)

- **[INFO] clarity** (line 45): anchor="bottom" is only exercised by direct unit tests, never by the production call site, and isn't documented in the module's top docstring. -- reader.py's one call to fit_row_lattice never passes `anchor` (so it always defaults to "center"); anchor="top"/"bottom" are only reached via tests/test_rows.py::test_anchor_top_and_bottom calling detect_row_centers directly. This isn't dead code (it has genuine callers, and is a small, deliberately generic capability of a pure-logic helper module) but it is currently unreachable from the actual collection pipeline. The module's top docstring documents "center" and "top" semantics but omits "bottom" (whose contract — bottommost LINE CENTRE, not bottom edge — is only spelled out in the test's inline comment).


_Module notes: Both files are pure-logic, backend-agnostic geometry/scoring modules with no game-specific knowledge (satisfies the zero-game-knowledge rule) and no mutable-default-argument, bare-except, or hardcoded-name issues. Neither file is dead code — both have multiple real, verified call sites (collector.py, reader.py, precapture.py, web/routes/preview.py for items.py; reader.py + tests/test_rows.py for rows.py). No hard-rule (CLAUDE.md numbered) violations apply since these are pure Python collection-pipeline modules with no web UI, dates, or .ps1 surface. The one finding worth a second look with the user is the diamonds-field-tell /5-vs-/3 normalization inconsistency in items.py's _field_tell_result — it doesn't fail against the current warframe.yaml config (tell_conf 0.3 is safely under the 0.6 ceiling a 3-mark strip produces) but is a real latent trap if that threshold is ever tuned higher, and the exact rationale for why /5 is wrong already exists verbatim in the sibling tells.py module.</notes>
_

## Module: p09-triggers-tells  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/collect/triggers.py

**Purpose:** Evaluates every kind of TriggerDef declared in a game profile (interval, on_change, on_any_change, on_app_start, on_capture, on_live_start, on_live_stop, on_readout, manual) and dispatches its targets (price-node sweep, file-source read, toast notification, dataset clear/clone/move action) through single shared funnel functions (fire_target, fire_toast, read_source_target) that both the collector loop and the web 'fire now' routes call, so an automatic fire and a manual test fire never drift. Also persists small sidecar JSON state (.trigger_fires.json last-fire timestamps, .subset_sigs.json watched-subset output hashes) so on_change subset-watch dedup and restart behaviour are correct. If deleted: no trigger would ever fire automatically (interval price refreshes, on_change relic-reward pricing, toasts, sounds, dataset clear/clone actions) - only the manual 'fire now' UI button paths would remain, and even those import fire_target/fire_toast/read_source_target/record_fire from this module (web/routes/triggers.py), so they would break too.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py:579-581 (_build_triggers constructs TriggerRunner, called from run()); src/oc/cli/collect.py:6 (CLI collect command); src/oc/web/app.py:205 (TriggerRunner import, likely app-boot on_app_start firing); src/oc/web/trigger_sched.py:23,96 (TriggerRunner + read_fires, drives lifecycle/interval trigger scheduling for the web app); src/oc/web/source_sched.py:21 (TriggerRunner for file-source scheduling); src/oc/web/routes/toasts.py:14 (toast_spec, test-toast preview route); src/oc/web/routes/triggers.py:13 (fire_target, fire_toast, read_source_target, record_fire - the manual 'fire now' route); src/oc/store/changes.py:64,121-123 (OnChangeFirer.__call__/_flush calls runner.on_change(dataset, recs) - the dataset change-bus subscriber); tests/test_triggers.py, tests/test_dataset_ops.py, tests/test_variables.py, tests/test_sources.py

- **[INFO] clarity** (line 442): Broad `except Exception` swallowing is pervasive by design across every fire path (fire_target, fire_toast, read_source_target, _subset_sig) -- Every external-effect call (start_sweep, notifier.notify, read_source, subset compute) is wrapped in a bare `except Exception` with an explicit `noqa: BLE001` and a comment justifying it ('must never crash the collector loop / a request'). This is consistent with the project's documented tolerance for best-effort side effects and is exercised by tests (test_on_change_ignores_disabled etc.), so it is intentional, not an oversight. Flagging only as a reminder that any future refactor should preserve the same never-crash guarantee for new target kinds.

- **[INFO] design-observation** (line 144): TriggerRunner.tick()'s per-trigger last-fire seeding only covers interval triggers present at __init__ time; a trigger added to the live profile object after construction would never accumulate elapsed time (self._last.get(t.id, now) always yields a 0 delta) -- In `tick()`, `now - self._last.get(t.id, now) >= t.interval_s` - for a trigger id absent from `self._last`, the fallback default is the freshly-computed `now` itself, so the delta is always exactly 0 every tick, and self._last is only ever populated inside the firing branch. This looked like a bug where a newly-authored interval trigger could get permanently stuck never firing, but checked against src/oc/web/routes/live.py: `_refresh_profile` (docstring: 'no-op mid-run') only pushes a reloaded profile into a LiveSession before a run starts; collector.run() calls `_build_triggers()` (a fresh TriggerRunner, freshly seeding self._last for every interval trigger in the profile at that moment) once per run(), and profile edits mid-run are explicitly documented as not taking effect until restart. So the scenario that would trigger this defect (mutating profile.triggers in place while the same TriggerRunner instance keeps running) does not occur via any current call site. Recording as info only in case a future change (e.g. hot-reloading triggers mid-session) reintroduces the precondition.

### src/oc/collect/tells.py

**Purpose:** Scores 'tells' - the per-cell visual/textual signals (filled/color/border/template/diamonds/text) that confirm a grid cell really holds an item versus an empty slot, popup, or gap - used both to validate a located cell (every tell must pass) and, for visual tells, to locate rows in the first place without OCR. Pure numeric/OpenCV scoring functions with no game-specific content (colors/templates are all profile-authored). If deleted: item-cell validation (valid_cell/cell_results/tell_report in collect/items.py) and a detector's own color/border check (detect/matcher.py) would have no scoring implementation, breaking template/color/border/diamonds/filled tell evaluation across the whole detection pipeline.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/items.py:58 (telldet.is_visual), :349 (_is_visual_loc), :495 (telldet.visual_score in _tell_result, used by cell_results/tell_report/valid_cell); src/oc/detect/matcher.py:234-237 (border_score/color_score imported directly for a detector's own colour-presence check)

- **[INFO] design-observation** (line 65): border_score falls back to whole-crop color_score behaviour when the perimeter band would swallow the entire box (band*2 >= min(h,w)), including the degenerate case width=0 still yielding a 1px minimum band via max(1, ...) -- `band = max(1, int(round((width or 0.0) * min(h, w))))` enforces a minimum 1px ring even when a profile author sets `width` to 0, rather than treating 0 as 'no perimeter test'. In practice Tell.width defaults to 0.2 (models.py) so this edge is unlikely to be hit by authored profiles, and the fallback (`band*2 >= min(h,w)` -> plain mean) degrades gracefully rather than crashing. Not a functional bug, just a minor surprise if a UI author explicitly zeros the width expecting a no-op ring.

- **[INFO] robustness** (line 48): _color_mask assumes crop is always a 3-channel (H,W,3) array when computing `crop.astype(float32) - bgr` with axis=2 norm; a genuinely 2D grayscale crop would raise instead of degrading to 0 -- Unlike filled_score, which explicitly branches on `crop.ndim == 3` vs 2D, `_color_mask` (used by color_score/border_score) unconditionally treats crop as 3-channel BGR. Every capture backend in this codebase (PrintWindow/WGC/mss) returns 3-channel BGR frames, so this path is not reachable with current capture backends, but there is no defensive ndim check the way filled_score has one for the same input shape.


_Module notes: Both files are heavily used, well-documented, and closely cross-checked against tests (tests/test_triggers.py, tests/test_dataset_ops.py, tests/test_variables.py, tests/test_sources.py) and the higher-level callers (collect/collector.py, web/trigger_sched.py, web/source_sched.py, web/routes/triggers.py, web/routes/toasts.py, store/changes.py, store/dataset_ops.py). No hardcoded game-specific knowledge in either file (satisfies the zero-game-knowledge rule) - all identifiers (dataset/subset/producer/toast/sound ids) are profile-authored strings. No mutable-default-argument bugs, no blocking dialogs (N/A, Python not JS), no date/time formatting (record_fire stores a machine ISO timestamp, not a user-facing display format, so hard-rule 9 does not apply). I verified two things that looked suspicious on first pass but turned out NOT to be bugs after checking the surrounding architecture (recording here so a future audit doesn't re-flag them):
1. TriggerRunner.tick()'s `self._last.get(t.id, now)` fallback would never let a NEW interval trigger fire if it were added to `profile.triggers` after the runner was constructed (the fallback recomputes to the current `now` every tick, so the elapsed delta is always exactly 0). This looked like a real bug, but web/routes/live.py's `_refresh_profile` explicitly documents "no-op mid-run" - the live session only reloads/rebuilds the profile+TriggerRunner between runs (via collector.run()'s `_build_triggers()`), never while the loop is executing, so `profile.triggers` never gains a live member the running runner wasn't seeded for. Not reachable given current call sites.
2. `_fire_targets` dispatches `fire_dataset_target` (for `dataset_action`/`dataset_targets`) without a local try/except, unlike every other target kind in the same function which is wrapped defensively. Checked store/dataset_ops.py: `fire_dataset_target` itself wraps `run_dataset_action` in try/except and documents "must never crash the collector loop / a request" - the safety net exists, just one call-frame deeper. Not a bug.
Sound-node targets (`SoundDef`, tested in test_sound_node_defaults_and_roundtrips) are intentionally NOT dispatched by `_fire_targets` - sound playback is entirely client-side, driven off `last_fired` timestamp changes observed in web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js (playSound), so triggers.py has nothing to do for a sound target beyond the `record_fire` stamp it already does. Confirmed by reading sound.js/activity.js; not a gap.
tells.py has no dedicated unit test file (no tests/test_tells.py), but is thoroughly exercised indirectly via src/oc/collect/items.py (cell_results/tell_report/valid_cell call visual_score/is_visual) and src/oc/detect/matcher.py (border_score/color_score for a detector's own colour check) - confirmed live call sites, not dead code._

## Module: p10-fields-glyph  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/collect/fields.py

**Purpose:** Implements the field value RULE PIPELINE: turns one raw OCR string into a typed value by running the field's ordered FieldRule list top-to-bottom (extract/case-fold/round/dictionary/set/drop). This is the single execution engine for FieldDef.rules referenced throughout the collection + teaching-UI stack; deleting it would break turning any OCR read into a stored value anywhere in the app (collection, live readouts, cutout preview, the rule-trace debug panel).

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/reader.py (imports run_rules; calls it directly for the no-resolver path in _read_cells/read_readouts/read_cutout, and indirectly via FieldResolver.resolve in the resolver-present path); src/oc/learn/resolver.py (imports DictOutcome, run_rules; FieldResolver.resolve()/rule_trace() wrap run_rules with dict_hook=apply_dictionary); src/oc/web/routes/preview.py (imports run_rules directly for the POST /api/rule_trace debug endpoint); tests/test_fields.py (imports coerce, run_rules directly; the primary unit-test surface for the pipeline); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/node_parts.js RULE_WHEN/RULE_THEN/okRuleType/ruleValidForType mirror this file's _WHEN_TYPES/_THEN_TYPES/_type_ok/rule_applies (comment explicitly references oc.collect.fields.rule_applies to keep them in sync)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 107): Rules authored on pips/diamonds fields are type-table-eligible but never actually execute during real collection, only in the debug trace panel. -- _WHEN_TYPES marks below/above as valid for type code "nc" (= number OR pips/diamonds, see _type_ok), and rule_applies()/node_parts.js's mirrored ruleValidForType() therefore present a below/above (or any) rule on a pips/diamonds FieldDef as valid/actionable in the teach UI. But every real read path in src/oc/collect/reader.py (read/_read_cells, read_readouts, read_cutout) checks _is_pip(fdef) and `continue`s straight to assigning the raw pixel count to the record BEFORE ever calling run_rules or FieldResolver.resolve — so a rule authored on such a field is silently inert in production. The only place that actually executes it is POST /api/rule_trace (src/oc/web/routes/preview.py), which calls run_rules unconditionally on every field's representative_raws() text regardless of type, so the debug trace panel can show the rule apparently "working" while it never touches the stored value.

- **[INFO] edge-case** (line 239): A field pipeline with two or more `dictionary` rules loses the earlier rule's `verified` classification instead of taking the weakest across all of them. -- Within one dictionary rule, `_correct_words` already picks the weakest mechanism (`_VERIFY_RANK`) across its own words. But at the run_rules level (line ~239-240), `if out.verified is not None: result.verified = out.verified` simply overwrites result.verified on every dictionary rule that fires, so if a field authored two dictionary rules (e.g. one pinned to dict A then one to dict B), a later exact-dict hit would overwrite an earlier fuzzy hit's 'fuzzy' marker with 'dict', reporting a stronger verification than the value actually received end-to-end.

### src/oc/collect/glyph_match.py

**Purpose:** Post-OCR PIXEL-level glyph refinement: builds a GlyphMatcher from a taught glyph atlas (GameProfile.glyphs, authored per-game in the UI) and corrects same-vocabulary single-character confusions (e.g. relic code Q3 vs G3) that word-level dictionary/fuzzy correction can't disambiguate, by sliding each taught glyph template across the OCR'd text band and requiring a clear NCC margin before substituting. Also supplies segment_boxes()/segment_word(), the character-splitting helpers behind the teach UI's 'auto-glypher' tool that carves a captured word crop into individual glyph training samples. Deleting this file would remove the glyph_check refinement step entirely (FieldDef.glyph_check would become a dead flag) and break the auto-glypher capture endpoint.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/reader.py:420 (self._glyphs.refine(text, gcrop) inside _read_cells, gated on FieldDef.glyph_check); src/oc/collect/collector.py:131 (glyph_atlas(profile.glyphs, ...) built once per collector session); src/oc/collect/precapture.py:271 (glyph_atlas(...) for the precapture recorder); src/oc/web/routes/preview.py:264,435 (glyph_atlas(...) feeding the /api/preview and /api/item/read RegionReader instances); src/oc/web/routes/capture.py:217,244 (segment_boxes(...) powering the auto-glypher capture endpoint); tests/test_glyph_match.py (imports GlyphMatcher directly; synthetic deterministic tests pin the sliding-match/margin/min-score logic)

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 109): segment_word() has zero callers anywhere in the repository. -- segment_word (lines 109-115) is a thin wrapper that materialises segment_boxes()'s boxes into crops. A repo-wide grep for `segment_word` finds only its own definition/docstring in glyph_match.py — no caller in src/oc/web/routes/capture.py (which calls segment_boxes directly and slices the crops itself), src/oc/collect/*, or tests/. segment_boxes itself is very much alive (used by the auto-glypher endpoint), so this is a narrow, single-function dead-code pocket, not a dead module.


_Module notes: Both files are pure-logic, backend-agnostic modules with zero hardcoded game knowledge (consistent with CLAUDE.md's core principle) — no violations of the "everything is teachable" rule found. No mutable-default-argument bugs, no bare excepts, no blocking-dialog/DOM/date-format hard-rule violations (these are pure Python value-processing modules, not web UI code, so hard rules 1-6/9 don't directly apply; rule 7's shared-primitive concern is honored — the rule-pipeline type table in fields.py is deliberately mirrored, not copy-pasted, into node_parts.js's RULE_WHEN/RULE_THEN/okRuleType with an explicit comment pointing back to oc.collect.fields.rule_applies). Rule 8 (no Claude/Anthropic mentions) — clean.

Cross-checked fields.py's RuleWhen/RuleThen/Extract enums and FieldRule/FieldDef against src/oc/profile/models.py: the _WHEN_TYPES/_THEN_TYPES dictionaries exactly cover every enum member (13 whens, 10 thens) with no drift.

Main substantive finding: FieldType.pips/diamonds fields are declared rule-eligible ("nc" type code lets below/above conditions target them) by fields.py's own type table, and the web UI (node_parts.js) mirrors that eligibility so such rules are never greyed out — but every real collection code path in src/oc/collect/reader.py (read/read_readouts/read_cutout, all outside this module's file set) unconditionally `continue`s past run_rules/FieldResolver.resolve for pip/diamonds fields, assigning the raw pixel count straight to the record. Only the /api/rule_trace debug endpoint (src/oc/web/routes/preview.py) actually executes such a rule (since it calls run_rules unconditionally on every field's representative raw text), which is misleading: an author can watch a rule "work" in the debug trace panel while it never touches the real stored value. I flagged this at medium severity in fields.py since the type table is the visible surface of the gap, though the root cause (the `continue` in reader.py) lives outside my assigned files.

segment_word() in glyph_match.py is a thin wrapper over segment_boxes with zero callers anywhere in the repo (grepped web/, collect/, tests/) — segment_boxes itself IS used (web/routes/capture.py's auto-glypher endpoint), so the module overall is very much alive; only this one helper looks unused._

## Module: p11-live-pips-slice  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/collect/live.py

**Purpose:** Runs the REAL collection pipeline (capture -> classify -> valid_for_save -> read -> confirm -> DatasetStore -> triggers) in a background thread, one LiveSession per game, so the web UI's 'live' mode actually writes data (not just a read-only OCR preview). Exposes thread-safe start/stop/status/debug for the FastAPI routes to poll via the activity heartbeat. If deleted, the web UI would lose its only way to run the real Collector interactively (no server-side live collection, no reward-only datasets like relics_offered, no live readouts feeding toast/sound triggers).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/live.py:12 `from ...collect.live import LiveSession`; :20 `_sessions: dict[str, LiveSession]`; :60-69 `_session()` constructs/caches one per game; :93-105 start route calls s.start(); :123-130 stop route calls s.stop(); :133-138 status route calls s.status(); :141-148 debug route calls s.debug(); src/oc/web/routes/activity.py:18 `from .live import _sessions as _live_sessions` (reads LiveSession.status() into the heartbeat's `live` field); src/oc/web/routes/triggers.py:17,85 `from .live import live_readouts` (feeds `{{ro_n}}` token interpolation from the running session's readouts); src/oc/web/routes/toasts.py:18,43,59 same `live_readouts` usage for toast firing; src/oc/web/gpu_watch.py:62 `from .routes.live import any_running as live_running` (GPU watchdog must not release VRAM while a live session runs); src/oc/web/app.py:152,263 `from .routes.live import kill_all_sessions as kill_live` (startup/shutdown lifespan safety net); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/livewin.js:520 `api.live.start(...)` and friends drive the routes above from the client; tests/test_gate.py:16 `from oc.collect.live import LiveSession`; :190-215 builds a bare instance via `LiveSession.__new__` and exercises `_on_tick` directly

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 166): _on_tick treats TickStatus.moving as 'not in a phase', undoing the anti-flicker design it sits right next to -- In `_on_tick` (lines 150-173), only `TickStatus.saved` and `TickStatus.throttled` are special-cased to keep `_phase=True`/`_cur` populated; every other status (including `moving`) falls into the final `else` at lines 169-172, which sets `_phase=False` and resets `_cur=(None, None)`. But `TickStatus.moving` is emitted by the collector while a RECOGNIZED window is merely mid-animation/scroll (collector.py lines 358-360 build it with `window_id`/`state_id` populated, exactly like `throttled`) — OCR is skipped for that tick, but the window itself is still valid. Concrete scenario: a user scrolls the live Equipment grid; every mid-scroll tick returns `moving` -> `status()`'s `phase`/`window`/`state` flash to unset each time, then flash back once scrolling settles and OCR resumes — the very flicker the comment two lines above ("both count as 'in a phase', so the live view stays steady instead of flickering to idle every slot") says was fixed for `throttled`. Corroborating evidence: the `_recog` bucketing right below (line 223, `"miss": k in ("", "idle", "unrecognised", "no_window")`) does NOT list `moving` as a miss, i.e. the code already considers a moving tick 'recognized' for that purpose, contradicting the phase/`_cur` reset for the same tick. No test (`tests/test_gate.py`) covers a `moving` tick through `_on_tick`, consistent with this being an overlooked case rather than deliberate.

- **[LOW] race-condition** (line 79): start() is a check-then-act without holding the lock across the whole operation, so two near-simultaneous /start calls could spin up two Collector threads -- `start()` (lines 79-102) checks `if self.is_running(): return` before acquiring `self._lock`, does its state reset under the lock, then creates and starts `self._thread` OUTSIDE the lock. FastAPI/Starlette can run sync def routes like `POST /api/live/{game}/start` on different threadpool workers, so two overlapping requests could both observe `is_running() == False` and each create/start their own Collector thread against the same profile/store, doubling OCR/GPU work (writes themselves are probably idempotent via key dedup in commit_records, so this looks like wasted work rather than data corruption). In practice the only caller, `livewin.js`'s `startServerCollect()`/`setLiveMode()`, is gated by a client-side `liveOn` boolean that prevents a normal single click from double-firing, so this is a latent gap rather than an observed live bug.

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 154): _readouts values are never cleared when the window stops matching, so they can go stale but keep being served as 'live' -- `_on_tick` (line 154-155) merges `result.readouts` into `self._readouts` whenever present but there is no code path that ever removes a key from `_readouts` when the window later becomes `unrecognised`/`no_window`/etc. `status()` (line 222) and `live_readouts()` (routes/live.py:47-57) both serve `self._readouts` as-is, so once a readout is seen it is reported indefinitely for the life of the running session, even after the corresponding on-screen HUD element is gone (e.g. the player left that screen entirely). This may be the intended 'last known HUD value' behavior (the docstring just says 'ephemeral'), so flagging as informational rather than a defect.

### src/oc/collect/pips.py

**Purpose:** Converts a non-text visual count into an integer for FieldType.pips/FieldType.diamonds fields — some equipment values (e.g. a mod's rank, an arcane's rank) are shown as glowing dots or filled/hollow diamond marks rather than digits, which OCR cannot read. Provides count_pips (generic bright-blob counter), count_filled_diamonds (filled marks in a rank strip = the field's value) and count_diamonds (total marks present, filled or not = a presence/tell signal). Without this file, 'visual count' fields and diamond-strip item detection would have no implementation, forcing such data to be hand-typed or unreadable, or forcing game-specific pixel logic into the reader itself.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/reader.py:33 `from .pips import count_filled_diamonds, count_pips`; :343-347 `_pip_value` dispatches by `FieldType.diamonds`/other to one or the other for grid-cell reads; src/oc/collect/items.py:27 `from .pips import count_diamonds, count_pips`; :505-516 dispatches the same way for item-template reads (count_diamonds used here deliberately for presence, not the filled value — see items.py:505 comment); src/oc/collect/tells.py:21 `from .pips import count_diamonds`; :98 uses it as a normalized 'tell' score (`count/3.0`) to help classify a template as arcane-like; tests/test_pips.py (count_pips), tests/test_diamonds.py (count_diamonds, count_filled_diamonds) — dedicated unit coverage for every helper including the private `_is_diamond_contour`/`_rank_row`/`_diamond_boxes` behaviors via their public entry points

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 96): _rank_row's 'median' pick is only exact for odd-length rows -- `med = sorted(b[3] for b in row)[len(row) // 2]` (line 96) selects the upper-middle element for even-length `row`, not a true median. Harmless here since `med` only seeds a loose `0.4x..2.5x` sizing band (line 97), but the comment describing it as "the row's median height" is imprecise.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 104): _rank_row divides by min(gaps) with no zero-guard -- `if max(gaps) > 2.2 * min(gaps): return []` (line 104) would raise ZeroDivisionError-free (it's a multiplication, not division, so no crash) but if `min(gaps)` is 0 (two diamonds detected at coincident x-centres) the condition becomes `max(gaps) > 0`, rejecting the row unless every gap is exactly 0 too — a false negative rather than a crash. Re-reading the code: this is actually safe (no division happens), so downgrading to informational; the earlier centre-based de-dup in `_diamond_boxes` (lines 136-139) makes coincident centres unlikely in practice anyway.

- **[INFO] robustness** (line 19): count_pips/_diamond_boxes assume a 3-channel BGR crop with no defensive check beyond None/empty -- `image.max(axis=2)` (lines 19, 44, 121) requires a 3-D array; a 2-D (already-grayscale) or malformed crop would raise `numpy.AxisError`/`IndexError` rather than returning 0 like the `image is None or image.size == 0` guard does. Every caller passes a slice of `frame.image`, which is a BGR capture buffer throughout the codebase, so this is a documented precondition rather than an observed bug — flagged only as a fragility note in case a future capture backend or preprocessing step ever hands this a single-channel crop.

### src/oc/collect/slice_sync.py

**Purpose:** Pure-logic decision of which stored keys a 'mirror' dataset (a continuously scrolling, reflow-on-removal grid — e.g. a relic-choice list where consuming one shifts every later one up) should soft-remove as the collector scrolls. Removal keys off REPLACEMENT (a different item now read in a key's last-known exact grid slot) rather than mere absence, so a flaky OCR miss or a reflow-after-consumption never causes a false removal. Without this file, mirror-sync datasets would have no safe way to detect 'this row's item is gone' and would have to fall back to naive absence-based removal, which the module's docstring shows is wrong twice over (false removal on OCR miss, false removal on every item after a consumption).

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py:34 `from .slice_sync import SliceSync`; :145 `self._slice_sync: dict[str, SliceSync]`; :523-528 per-dataset instantiation (`SliceSync(self._tuning.confirm_frames)`), seeded via `syncer.seed(store.positions())`, driven every mirror-sync tick via `syncer.observe(vlo, vhi, read_cells, store.present_keys(), fresh)`; :529-530 `store.remove_keys(gone)` applies the result; :538 `store.set_positions(read_cells)` persists the slot map slice_sync will re-seed from next run; tests/test_slice_sync.py — full dedicated unit suite: replacement-after-confirm, flaky-miss-not-removed, same-row/different-column rejected, sub-row tolerance vs. full-row rejection, present-relic-at-shifted-slot safe, out-of-view slot not judged, unclean frame contributes no evidence, absence counter resets on reappearance, pos_map/seed round-trip, legacy no-column seed stays non-removable

- **[INFO] maintainability** (line 36): _cell/_absence entries for keys removed via a path other than SliceSync itself are never pruned -- If a key disappears from the store through some route other than this class's own `gone` return (e.g. an explicit user delete, or DatasetStore.reconcile()'s separate removal path — not observed calling into slice_sync from this module's own code), its entries in `self._cell`/`self._absence` are simply never iterated again (since `present_keys` no longer contains it) but stay resident for the life of the `SliceSync` instance. Bounded by the size of the game's item catalogue, so not a practical leak — noted only for completeness.

- **[INFO] documentation** (line 69): Docstring says removal needs 'confirm_frames consecutive clean frames'; the implementation actually needs confirm_frames replaced-observations that persist until the ORIGINAL key is re-read (not literally consecutive) -- `_absence[k]` is only cleared when `k` itself appears again in `read_cells` (lines 50-52); a clean frame where `k`'s slot is neither re-read nor replaced (i.e. the `if not replaced: continue` branch at line 67-68) leaves the counter untouched rather than resetting it, and a non-clean (occluded) frame is skipped entirely without touching the counter either (line 48-49). So the counter can reach `confirm_frames` across non-adjacent 'replaced' observations spanning intervening ambiguous/occluded frames, not strictly consecutive ones. This is confirmed intentional/tested behavior for the reappearance-reset case (`test_confirm_resets_when_relic_reappears`), and the deviation from 'consecutive' only makes removal slower to trigger, never faster/wrong — a safe-direction wording mismatch, not a logic bug.

## Module: p12-collect-rest  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/collect/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package marker + one-line docstring for oc.collect. Its only job is making the directory an importable package so every sibling module (grid, reader, collector, stability, ...) can be imported via `oc.collect.X`; deleting it would break every `from oc.collect import ...` / `from .X import ...` in the codebase.

**Used by:** implicit: required by every `oc.collect.*` submodule import across the repo; tests/test_settle.py: `from oc.collect import settle`

- No issues found.

### src/oc/collect/grid.py

**Purpose:** Turns a WindowDef's authored region boxes into concrete per-cell FractionBox dicts. `expand_cells` tiles the static authored grid (scroll rows/cols/strides taken at face value, or a single cell for a non-grid window); `cells_for_rows` instead tiles the authored per-row layout onto row positions actually detected live in the frame (columns still use the configured stride). Both are consumed by reader.py's cell resolution for every grid/scroll window - deleting this file breaks OCR targeting for any multi-row/column window.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/reader.py: `from .grid import Cell, cells_for_rows, expand_cells` (_pixel_targets, _detect_rows, _resolve_cells); tests/test_grid.py: expand_cells; tests/test_items.py: Cell

- **[LOW] robustness** (line 39): expand_cells has no floor on scroll.cols/rows, unlike its sibling cells_for_rows -- expand_cells only early-returns a single cell when `scroll.rows <= 1 and scroll.cols <= 1`; if a profile has rows>1 but cols==0 (ScrollDef.cols has no ge=1 constraint in profile/models.py), `for col in range(scroll.cols)` silently produces zero cells for every row - the window reads nothing, with no error surfaced. cells_for_rows guards the analogous case with `cols = max(1, scroll.cols if scroll else 1)`. Not crash-prone and likely rare (a misconfigured cols=0 would be immediately visible as an empty grid preview in the teaching UI), so kept at low severity, but the two functions should share the same guard per CLAUDE.md's "extract the shared primitive" convention.

### src/oc/collect/preprocess.py

**Purpose:** Applies a window's teachable OCR-cleanup (color mask / Otsu threshold / invert / upscale) to a crop before recognition. The `color` mode is the primary mechanism the UI's eyedropper teaches to turn stylised colored game text into clean black-on-white for reliable OCR - this is the whole reason the preprocessing pipeline exists.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/reader.py: `from .preprocess import apply as apply_preprocess`, called from _ocr_union, _box_crop, _detect_reads, and read_cutout

- **[CRITICAL] correctness** (line 27): _color_mask overflows int16 when squaring per-channel color differences, silently corrupting the color-distance test -- `img = image.astype(np.int16)` then `dist = np.sqrt(((img - bgr) ** 2).sum(axis=2))`. A per-channel diff can be up to 255, and 255**2 = 65025 exceeds int16's max of 32767, so numpy's int16 power silently wraps (verified: `np.array([255],dtype=np.int16)**2` -> -511, not 65025). This is NOT a corner case: Preprocess.tolerance is documented as valid over "0..441" (the full possible 3-channel Euclidean distance), i.e. the field is explicitly designed to be used at magnitudes that trigger this overflow. Confirmed by direct execution to produce both false negatives (the corrupted sum goes negative -> sqrt gives NaN -> `NaN <= tolerance` is always False, so a RuntimeWarning fires and the pixel is silently treated as non-matching) and false positives (a pixel with true Euclidean distance 361.3 from the taught color, at tolerance=300, is incorrectly masked as a MATCH because the overflowed sum computes to ~255). This directly degrades the "color" preprocess mode's accuracy - exactly the feature meant to make text OCR-readable - for any taught color/tolerance combination where per-channel diffs exceed ~181. Fix: accumulate in a wider dtype (np.int32 or float) before squaring, e.g. `img = image.astype(np.int32)` and `bgr = np.array(..., dtype=np.int32)`. Note: contrast with settle.py/detsig.py/scrollbar.py in the same module, which all correctly upcast to a wide-enough dtype before arithmetic - this file is the outlier.

### src/oc/collect/templating.py

**Purpose:** Server-side `{{token}}` renderer for toast notification title/message/attribution/image text: resolves `{{readout:id}}`, `{{dataset:id.field|agg}}`, `{{subset:id[a:b]|join:","}}` etc. against live readouts plus lazily-read dataset/subset rows. Deliberately mirrors the pretty UI's binding.js so a toast fired server-side renders identically to the live page. Re-audited fresh from disk per the cache-bust note (current on-disk content, not a stale read).

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/triggers.py: `toast_spec()` -> `from .templating import TokenContext, render` (the fire path + test-toast route); src/oc/web/routes/toasts.py: `from ...collect.templating import TokenContext` (test-toast preview endpoint); src/oc/notify/toast_image.py: `render_png()` -> `from ..collect.templating import render` (toast image text rendering)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/collect/stability.py

**Purpose:** The Confirmer: requires `confirm_frames` consecutive IDENTICAL reads (keyed by the dataset's resolved key) before a record is treated as confirmed and allowed to reach a DatasetStore - the core defence against transient/occluded/flickering reads (tooltips, fade animations) ever being saved.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py: `Confirmer(self._key_map(dataset).build, self._tuning.confirm_frames)` in `_confirmer_for`, fed via `confirmer.observe(kept)` in tick(); tests/test_stability.py

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 41): observe() can satisfy confirm_frames from duplicate keys within a SINGLE tick, not just across separate ticks -- observe() iterates every record of the current call in one pass and increments the same key's pending `count` for each matching occurrence it sees - it does not distinguish "this frame" from "a later frame". If a single tick's kept-records list contains two rows that resolve to the identical dedup key and identical values (e.g. two on-screen items with the same name+rank), the second row's iteration alone can push `count` from 1 to `confirm_frames` (commonly 2) and confirm the record on its very FIRST tick - bypassing the intended "stable across several separate ticks" guarantee entirely. Likely low real-world impact since most game UIs (the only example in-repo, Warframe, included) collapse duplicate identical inventory rows into one tile with a count badge rather than showing them as separate grid cells, but the code has no explicit per-tick dedup guard against it, and CLAUDE.md's zero-game-knowledge principle means other, as-yet-untaught games could plausibly show literal duplicate rows.

### src/oc/collect/settle.py

**Purpose:** Perceptual "has the screen stopped moving" gate shared by the live collector and precapture: builds a tiny grayscale thumbnail (bottom FPS-overlay strip cropped off first) and compares consecutive grabs within a noise tolerance, so OCR only ever runs on a settled frame instead of a blurred mid-scroll/fade one.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py: `settle.thumb`, `settle.is_settled`, `settle.CROP_PX` in the tick's motion gate; src/oc/collect/precapture.py: same, in the record loop's settle gate; tests/test_settle.py

- No issues found.

### src/oc/collect/sink.py

**Purpose:** Declares `RecordSink` (ABC: write/close) and a concrete `JsonlSink` (append-only `data/<game>/<window>.jsonl`) as an alternate, pluggable write path a Collector can be constructed with instead of routing confirmed records into a DatasetStore.

**DEAD CODE CLAIM (unverified):** JsonlSink is never imported or instantiated anywhere in src/ or tests/, and no caller anywhere (cli/collect.py, collect/live.py, web routes, tests) ever constructs `Collector(engine, profile, sink=...)` - every real construction site (`Collector(engine, profile)` in cli/collect.py and live.py) leaves `sink=None`. RecordSink is therefore imported purely for an always-None type annotation, and the `self._explicit_sink is not None` branches in collector.py are unreachable in the current app. This looks like a leftover write-path abstraction from before records were routed through DatasetStore/commit_records; worth confirming with the user before deleting in case it's an intentional embedding API for external callers.

- **[INFO] dead-code** (line 20): JsonlSink concrete class and the Collector `sink=` injection point have zero live callers -- Grepped the whole repo for `JsonlSink`, `RecordSink`, `sink=`, and `Collector(` - the only Collector construction sites pass no sink argument. If this is meant to stay as a public extension point for programmatic embedding (not exercised by the CLI/web app), it's worth a one-line test exercising it so it doesn't silently bit-rot; otherwise it's a candidate for removal.

### src/oc/collect/commit.py

**Purpose:** The single `commit_records()` helper that folds a list of confirmed Records into a DatasetStore via `record_seen`, shared verbatim by live collection (collector.py) and the teaching UI's one-shot manual "commit" action (routes/preview.py) - guarantees both write paths can never diverge (CLAUDE.md rule 7).

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py: `commit_records(store, confirmed)` - "the ONE write path"; src/oc/web/routes/preview.py: `commit_records(store, gated)` - "the SAME write path collection uses"

- No issues found.

### src/oc/collect/detsig.py

**Purpose:** Cheap, noise-tolerant feature signature over just the detector search regions, used to decide whether a frame has changed enough to warrant re-running window classification (which otherwise runs an OCR text read per detector, per window, every frame). Shared primitive behind both Collector._classify's cache and precapture's scroll-config gate.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py: `detsig.features`, `detsig.changed`, `detsig.TOL`, `detsig.MIN_CELLS` in the classify cache; src/oc/collect/precapture.py: same functions in the scroll-config/detection loop

- No issues found.

### src/oc/collect/scrollbar.py

**Purpose:** Heuristic, threshold-free scrollbar-thumb-position reader: along the scroll axis, locates the segment whose brightness deviates most from the track background and returns its position/length/confidence. Used both to track a live mirror-dataset's visible slice and by the teaching UI's scrollbar calibration tool.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py: `from .scrollbar import scroll_detail`, used in the mirror-dataset scroll-position read; src/oc/web/routes/preview.py: 3 call sites (live preview scrollbar readout, the calibration/cutout endpoint, and the OCR-debug endpoint); tests/test_scrollbar.py

- No issues found.


_Module notes: Standout finding is a confirmed, reproducible numeric-overflow bug in preprocess.py's _color_mask (verified by executing the function directly - see failure_scenario). Everything else in this module is small, single-purpose, and matches its docstring contract; the only other notable item is sink.py's JsonlSink/RecordSink being unreachable in the current app (no caller ever passes Collector(sink=...))._
