## Module: p13-store  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/store/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package facade for oc.store: re-exports ChangeEvent/ChangeOp, DatasetStore, KeyMap/KeySpec, the inspect submodule, rows_at, and store_for as the module's public surface. Deleting it would break every `from ..store import store_for` / `from ...store import inspect, rows_at, store_for` style import used across collect/, enrich/, web/routes/, learn/ and source/.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/flow.py:10 (from ...store import inspect, rows_at, store_for); src/oc/collect/collector.py; src/oc/collect/triggers.py; src/oc/collect/precapture.py; src/oc/store/db_backup.py (from . import changes); src/oc/store/dataset_ops.py (from . import store_for)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/store/change.py

**Purpose:** Defines ChangeOp (add/update/remove) and the ChangeEvent dataclass that is the ledger's row shape, plus its JSON (de)serialization. This is the type every DatasetStore read/write path passes around; deleting it removes the ledger's event representation entirely.

**Used by:** src/oc/store/dataset_store.py (ChangeEvent/ChangeOp used throughout _insert_core, replay, history, batch_events, preview_batch); src/oc/store/__init__.py re-export; tests/test_ledger.py, tests/test_store.py, tests/test_dataset_store_announces.py

- **[INFO] aliasing** (line 17): ChangeEvent.values is stored by reference, not copied -- `_insert_core` builds `ChangeEvent(ts, op, key, values, ...)` with the same dict object that was just json.dumps'd, and `from_dict`/`to_dict` likewise pass dicts through without copying. If a caller mutates a dict after handing it to record_seen/record_many and also holds onto the returned ChangeEvent, the in-memory event's `.values` would reflect the later mutation even though the DB row was already serialized from the pre-mutation state. No current caller appears to do this (values are built fresh per call), so this is a latent footgun rather than an observed bug.

### src/oc/store/changes.py

**Purpose:** The dataset change bus: publish()/subscribe() so any DatasetStore write announces itself once, decoupled from its consumers (SSE push to the Pretty UI, and OnChangeFirer which coalesces bursts into one on_change trigger fire per dataset). Deleting it would sever live UI refresh and on_change/on_any_change triggers from every write path.

**Used by:** src/oc/store/dataset_store.py:493 (_announce -> changes.publish); src/oc/store/db_backup.py (restore_db_backup / AutoBackup); src/oc/web/routes/dbschema.py, src/oc/web/routes/flow.py (changes.publish); src/oc/web/app.py (wires OnChangeFirer/AutoBackup as subscribers); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/flow.js, pretty/data.js, graph/panels/dbstruct.js (SSE-side subscribe consumers of the resulting stream)

- **[LOW] consistency** (line 100): OnChangeFirer's two-condition skip guard is subtle but was verified correct -- `if not records and not data_changed: return` only skips when BOTH are true (metadata-only ping with nothing to price); a real change with empty records (data_changed=True, e.g. a clear) still enqueues and fires on_change with empty items, matching the docstring. Verified all current data_changed=False call sites (only DatasetStore.set_positions) already pass empty records too, so behavior is currently safe, but the guard's correctness depends on that invariant holding for any future data_changed=False caller with non-empty records — worth a comment noting the coupling.

### src/oc/store/dataset_ops.py

**Purpose:** Implements the dataset-level actions a trigger (or the web 'fire now' route) can perform on a dataset: clear, or clone/move its rows (batch-preserving or resolved-collapsed) into another dataset, snapshotting the DB first for destructive ops. This is the ONE funnel both the collector's automatic trigger dispatch and the manual web fire-now route call, so behavior can't drift between the two paths.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/triggers.py:406 (from ..store.dataset_ops import fire_dataset_target); src/oc/web/routes/triggers.py:91 (from ...store.dataset_ops import fire_dataset_target)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/store/dataset_store.py

**Purpose:** The core per-dataset store: an append-only SQLite event ledger (`events`) plus a maintained `current` materialisation, batches, revert/restore, reconcile-based removal, scroll-position mirroring, and every read/write API (record_seen, record_many, reconcile, revert_batch, summary, history, batches, preview_batch, etc). This is the single most load-bearing file in the module; deleting it removes all dataset persistence.

**Used by:** src/oc/store/factory.py:19 (from .dataset_store import DatasetStore); src/oc/store/inspect.py, dataset_ops.py, db_backup.py (_connect/_db_path); src/oc/collect/collector.py, precapture.py, templating.py, triggers.py; src/oc/web/routes/flow.py, pretty.py, preview.py; src/oc/enrich/http_producer.py, src/oc/source/runner.py, src/oc/learn/dict_feed.py; tests/test_datasets.py, test_store.py, test_ledger.py, test_dataset_store_announces.py, test_stats_store.py

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 701): revert_batch(reverted=False) announces a restored batch's remove-op values as if they were live records to price -- Reproduced directly: build a batch containing add('a'), add('b'), remove('a'); call revert_batch(b1, True) then revert_batch(b1, False). present_keys() correctly ends up {'b'} (a stays removed), but the change-bus announce for the restore carries [{'name':'a',...}, {'name':'b',...}, {'name':'a',...}] — i.e. it re-announces 'a' twice, once from its add event and once from the remove event's carried-forward values, even though 'a' is not present after the restore. `revert_batch`'s SELECT for the announce (`WHERE dataset=? AND batch=? AND reverted=0`) does not filter `op != 'remove'`, so it contradicts its own docstring ("restoring re-applies this batch's live rows -> announce them so on_change prices them") and TriggerRunner._items_for (src/oc/collect/triggers.py) has no presence check, so an on_change/on_any_change trigger watching this dataset would be handed a since-removed item name to price/act on after any batch restore that included a remove.

- **[INFO] unused-validation** (line 46): AGGREGATES tuple is not validated/enforced anywhere -- The module defines AGGREGATES = ("latest","first","sum","mean","max","min","all") but nothing in Python checks a dataset's configured aggregate string against it (profile/models.py's aggregate_for() returns whatever string is in the YAML, or "latest" if empty, with no validation). An unrecognised aggregate value silently falls back to latest-like behaviour in aggregate_records() (`if policy not in (...): return dict(latest)`), so this is safe by default rather than crashing, but the constant's only real consumer today is the JS mirror in graph/main.js — worth noting if a future YAML typo (e.g. "maen") should surface as a warning instead of silently behaving like "latest".

- **[INFO] concurrency** (line 368): DatasetStore has no internal lock around its own read-modify-write state (self._next_id/_batch) despite check_same_thread=False -- The connection is opened with check_same_thread=False, implying cross-thread reuse is intended, but _insert()/_insert_core() read/mutate self._next_id and self._batch as plain Python attributes with no threading.Lock. Concurrent record_seen() calls on the SAME DatasetStore instance from two threads could interleave incompatibly with SQLite's single-open-transaction-per-connection semantics (a second BEGIN IMMEDIATE on the same connection while one is already open raises OperationalError). In practice every caller found in the repo (sweep_engine.py explicitly documents 'write_one runs on this single consumer thread ... stores aren't thread-safe') already serializes writes to one thread per store instance, so this is a documented-elsewhere contract rather than an observed bug, but the contract isn't stated in dataset_store.py itself.

### src/oc/store/db_backup.py

**Purpose:** Whole-game SQLite store snapshot/restore: consistent online-backup copies to data/<game>/.backups/<stamp>.gz (+ a JSON metadata sidecar), GFS-style pruning, restore-in-place, and an AutoBackup change-bus subscriber implementing the daily-on-change backup. Deleting it removes all DB backup/restore capability including the pre-clear/pre-move safety snapshots dataset_ops.py relies on.

**Used by:** src/oc/store/dataset_ops.py:27 (from .db_backup import snapshot_db); src/oc/web/routes/dbbackup.py (list_db_backups/snapshot_db/restore_db_backup, presumably the backup panel's HTTP endpoints); src/oc/web/routes/dbschema.py, src/oc/web/routes/flow.py (snapshot_db/backup_dir); src/oc/web/app.py (wires AutoBackup as a changes.subscribe() consumer)

- **[MEDIUM] documentation** (line 14): Module docstring contradicts the actual (and correctly implemented) retention policy -- The top-of-file docstring states "Retention is keep-last-N (DB files are far bigger than a profile YAML, so a flat count cap beats time-thinning)", but the code three lines later implements grandfather-father-son retention (`# Retention is grandfather-father-son ... keep the few freshest, then weekly for recent weeks, then monthly`) via `backup.gfs_keep(recent=DB_BACKUP_RECENT, weekly_weeks=DB_BACKUP_WEEKLY_WEEKS)`. The GFS comment and the actual call match each other and are correct; only the module-level docstring is stale, presumably left over from an earlier flat-cap design that was replaced with weekly/monthly thinning without updating the header.

- **[LOW] resource-leak** (line 125): Uncompressed .raw temp file is not cleaned up if src.backup(dst) fails before _finalize() runs -- snapshot_db() creates `raw` via `sqlite3.connect(str(raw))` and only unlinks it inside `_finalize()`'s `finally: raw.unlink(missing_ok=True)`. If `src.backup(dst)` (or `_counts(dst)`) raises before `_finalize` is ever invoked, the partially-written `<stamp>.gz.<pid>.<tid>.raw` file is left on disk permanently (nothing else in this module or db_backup's list/prune functions looks for or cleans `*.raw` orphans). Low impact — it's just disk clutter on an already-rare failure path (backup() failing on a WAL-mode source), not data loss.

### src/oc/store/factory.py

**Purpose:** The single funnel that opens a DatasetStore with its key spec and aggregate policy resolved ONCE from the game profile (or explicit overrides), so the same dataset can never be keyed/aggregated differently by different callers — the specific drift bug (mismatched read/write keys re-keying the ledger to NULL) this project has hit before.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py, precapture.py, templating.py, triggers.py; src/oc/enrich/http_producer.py, src/oc/source/runner.py, src/oc/learn/dict_feed.py; src/oc/store/dataset_ops.py, src/oc/store/inspect.py; src/oc/web/routes/flow.py, pretty.py, preview.py

- No issues found.

### src/oc/store/flow_events.py

**Purpose:** A directed, counted 'data moved along this graph edge' event bus, distinct from the dataset change bus — it feeds the graph canvas's blob-flow animation (which node wrote how many items to which node, and what edge kind). Deleting it removes the flow animation's data source but not correctness of storage itself.

**Used by:** src/oc/store/dataset_ops.py:124 (publish_flow(...) on a successful dataset action); src/oc/collect/triggers.py, collector.py, precapture.py; src/oc/enrich/price_runner.py, src/oc/source/runner.py, src/oc/jobs/subprocess_job.py; src/oc/web/routes/flow.py (subscribe side, feeding the SSE flow-event stream to graph/main.js)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/store/inspect.py

**Purpose:** Thin read-only view layer over DatasetStore for the dashboard/graph: list_datasets, records, history, batches, summarize — all delegating straight to store_for()+DatasetStore so the dashboard sees exactly the replayed ledger, never a separate cache.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/flow.py:10,48-49 (inspect.list_datasets, inspect.summarize); src/oc/store/__init__.py re-export

- No issues found.

### src/oc/store/keys.py

**Purpose:** Defines KeySpec (an ordered, teachable list of field ids + separator + normalisation knobs) and KeyMap (which spec keys a given record, plus the dedup on/off switch) — the generic, game-agnostic 'what makes two reads the same row' recipe every dataset store, producer and reader must resolve identically via GameProfile.key_map_for.

**Used by:** src/oc/store/dataset_store.py (KeyMap/KeySpec used as the store's key parameter throughout); src/oc/profile/models.py (key_map_for/_item_default_spec/_window_default_spec build KeySpec/KeyMap from YAML); src/oc/collect/triggers.py, templating.py, collector.py, precapture.py, stability.py; src/oc/enrich/http_producer.py, src/oc/source/runner.py; src/oc/web/static/js/keys.js (client-side mirror for the teaching UI)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 35): collapse_ws=False is silently overridden whenever strip_punct or strip_words is also set, collapsing distinct-whitespace values into the same key -- Reproduced directly: `KeySpec(fields=('name',), strip_punct=True, collapse_ws=False).build({'name':'Foo  Bar'})` and the same spec against `'Foo Bar'` (one space) both return `'foo_bar'` — identical keys — whereas the same spec with strip_punct=False correctly distinguishes them ('foo__bar' vs 'foo_bar'), matching the docstring's claim that 'collapse_ws off keeps each space its own underscore'. Root cause: `_norm_part` only takes the collapse_ws-respecting path (`s.strip().replace(' ', '_')`) in the final step, but when `strip_punct or strip_words` is true it first runs the value through `norm_text(s, ..., collapse_ws=True, ...)` — hardcoding collapse_ws=True regardless of the caller's collapse_ws argument — so any internal multi-space run is already collapsed to a single space before the final step ever sees it. A dataset key configured with strip_punct/strip_words AND collapse_ws=False (a 'concat' key wanting near-match punctuation stripping but exact whitespace-sensitivity) would merge two OCR reads that differ only in space count into one record instead of keeping them distinct. No test exercises this specific combination (tests/test_subset.py only covers strip_punct+strip_words with the default collapse_ws=True).

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 28): Numeric key parts stringify differently for int vs float of the same value (0 vs 0.0), which would split what should be one record if upstream field coercion is ever inconsistent -- `_norm_part`'s emptiness check `value in (None, "")` correctly treats 0 as a valid part per the docstring, but the resulting key text comes from `str(value)`, so an int 0 yields "0" while a float 0.0 yields "0.0" — two different key strings for the conceptually same value. This is speculative (I did not find a concrete upstream path in this module's files that produces both types for the same field), but flagging since a composite key like name+level would silently fork into two records if a field's numeric type ever varies between reads (e.g. one path emits int, another emits float via a different coercion rule).

### src/oc/store/stats_store.py

**Purpose:** Per-graph-node execution-duration store: records timing samples (tick/ocr/capture/replay/sweep/etc) keyed by a 2-char op code into one CSV per node under data/<game>/stats/, keeps an in-memory rolling aggregate for the stats panel to poll cheaply, and supports rename/remove/prune-stale lifecycle following the graph. Deleting it removes the performance/stats panel entirely.

**Used by:** src/oc/store/dataset_store.py:877 (stats_store.time_block wrapping the current-materialisation rebuild); src/oc/engine.py, collect/collector.py, collect/precapture.py, cli/sweep_job.py (record_timing/time_block call sites); src/oc/web/routes/stats.py, src/oc/web/app.py (configure()/aggregate()/history()/prune_stale() wiring); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/stats.js (consumes the aggregate()/history() HTTP responses; WINDOW_N mirrored as STAT_LAST_N=20, verified in sync)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/store/textnorm.py

**Purpose:** One small, fully parameterised text canonicaliser (lowercase -> drop punctuation -> drop whole words -> collapse whitespace) used to bridge near-match values in subset joins and (via keys.py) composite dataset keys, with every knob (word list, case, punctuation) supplied by the caller so no game-specific vocabulary is baked in here.

**Used by:** src/oc/store/keys.py:36 (_norm_part delegates to norm_text for the strip_punct/strip_words path); src/oc/enrich/subset.py (near-match join normalisation); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/main.js (client-side mirror, per its own comment referencing the same canonicalisation contract); tests/test_subset.py:162-163 (direct norm_text assertions)

- No issues found.


_Module notes: All 11 files are reachable and none are dead code — verified via repo-wide grep: dataset_ops.fire_dataset_target is called from both src/oc/collect/triggers.py:406 and src/oc/web/routes/triggers.py:91; db_backup's snapshot_db/restore_db_backup/list_db_backups/AutoBackup are wired from src/oc/web/app.py, src/oc/web/routes/dbbackup.py, src/oc/web/routes/dbschema.py, src/oc/web/routes/flow.py, and src/oc/store/dataset_ops.py; store_for (factory.py) is the funnel used by collector.py, precapture.py, templating.py, triggers.py, http_producer.py, source/runner.py, dict_feed.py, and every web route under routes/; inspect.py backs routes/flow.py's dataset listing; keys.py/textnorm.py back subset joins and key building across collect/templating.py and enrich/subset.py; stats_store backs the stats panel (web/routes/stats.py, graph/panels/stats.js) and is called from collector.py/precapture.py/engine.py.

Two findings were CONFIRMED by direct execution (not just static reading):
1. store/keys.py `_norm_part`: ran `KeySpec(fields=('name',), strip_punct=True, collapse_ws=False).build()` against "Foo  Bar" (2 spaces) and "Foo Bar" (1 space) — both produced the identical key "foo_bar", even though the same KeySpec with strip_punct=False correctly produces "foo__bar" vs "foo_bar" for the same inputs. Root cause: when strip_punct/strip_words is set, `_norm_part` delegates to `norm_text(..., collapse_ws=True)` — hardcoding collapse_ws=True regardless of the caller's `collapse_ws=False`, so the "keep each space as its own underscore" contract silently breaks whenever punctuation/word-stripping is combined with collapse_ws=False. No existing test (tests/test_subset.py, tests/test_datasets.py) exercises this combination.
2. store/dataset_store.py `revert_batch(batch, reverted=False)`: built a store with a batch containing add("a"), add("b"), remove("a"), then reverted the whole batch and restored it while spying on the change-bus. The restore announced THREE records ([a,b,a]) including "a" — the item that is actually absent (removed) in the resulting state (`present_keys()` correctly showed only {"b"}). This contradicts the method's own docstring ("restoring re-applies this batch's live rows -> announce them so on_change prices them") and would cause an on_change/on_any_change trigger (via TriggerRunner._items_for, which blindly reads `r.get("name")` with no presence check) to treat a just-re-removed item as something to price after a batch restore.

Both were run with the repo's .venv python; commands available on request. No hardcoded game/item names or window-title logic found anywhere in this module — the only Warframe-flavoured text is illustrative prose inside docstrings (keys.py, textnorm.py, dataset_store.py comment), never a code branch. No mutable-default-argument bugs (KeySpec/KeyMap are frozen dataclasses used as defaults; ChangeEvent.changed uses field(default_factory=dict)). No bare `except:`; all broad `except Exception` are annotated `# noqa: BLE001` with an explicit "must never break a write/collector" rationale matching the architecture doc. No SQL injection (the only dynamic table-name interpolation in clear_table/drop_database is checked against a live sqlite_master allow-list first)._

## Module: p14-learn  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/learn/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package entry point; re-exports FieldResolver and ResolvedField as the module's public surface. Deleting it would break `import oc.learn` (though every current caller imports the submodules directly, e.g. `oc.learn.resolver`, `oc.learn.dictionary`).

**Used by:** No current caller does `from oc.learn import ...` (grepped repo-wide) — all call sites import submodules directly (tests/test_learn.py, src/oc/collect/collector.py, src/oc/collect/precapture.py, src/oc/web/routes/preview.py, scripts/bench_preprocess.py). Still executes automatically whenever any `oc.learn.*` submodule is imported (Python always runs a package's __init__ first), so it is not inert.

- No issues found.

### src/oc/learn/dict_feed.py

**Purpose:** Derives a DictionaryDef's vocabulary from stored dataset columns instead of a hand-typed list (the `feeds` config). `pull_terms`/`apply_feeds` do the one-shot pull (used on profile save); `DictFeeder` is a change-bus subscriber that re-pulls+rewrites the dictionary's term file when any dataset it feeds from changes. Deleting it would break dictionary auto-refresh entirely — any game using DictionaryDef.feeds (e.g. warframe.yaml's relic/item dictionaries fed from relic_contents) would need hand-typed term lists again and would silently go stale as new items are collected.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py:235-237 - `from ..learn.dict_feed import DictFeeder; _sub_feed(DictFeeder(...))` registers the live subscriber on the change bus at startup; src/oc/web/routes/profiles.py:69-70 - `from ...learn.dict_feed import apply_feeds; apply_feeds(settings.data_dir, name, profile)` on profile save; src/oc/profile/models.py:733 - DictionaryDef docstring cross-references this module; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/model.js:922 - comment documents that the feed pull runs server-side via oc.learn.dict_feed

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 73): DictFeeder ignores the change bus's `data_changed` flag, unlike the sibling OnChangeFirer subscriber that explicitly guards on it, causing redundant re-pulls on metadata-only pings. -- `DictFeeder.__call__(self, game, dataset, records, data_changed=True)` (lines 73-82) never reads `data_changed` — it unconditionally does `self._pending.add((game, dataset))` and (re)arms the debounce timer. Compare `OnChangeFirer.__call__` in src/oc/store/changes.py:91-104, which does `if not records and not data_changed: return` before enqueuing, per the bus's own documented contract (changes.py:20-23: '`data_changed` is False only for a metadata-only ping ... subscribers that fire on data changes ... must ignore it'). DictFeeder's own docstring says it 're-pulls fed dictionaries when their source data changes' — exactly the class of subscriber the contract says must check the flag. Concretely, `DatasetStore.set_positions` (src/oc/store/dataset_store.py:679-690) publishes with `data_changed=False` on every learned scroll-position write, and `collector.py:534-538` calls `set_positions` whenever the read grid's slot map changes during normal collection. Any game with a feed-derived dictionary will re-derive its full term list (`pull_terms` re-queries the whole fed dataset via `store_for(...).records()`) and re-diff it against the on-disk file on every such ping, debounced to at most once per 0.5s while collection keeps producing position updates. Impact is bounded — `write_dictionary` (profile/loader.py:251-262) no-ops the actual file write when content is unchanged, and the trailing-timer coalesces bursts — so this doesn't corrupt data, but it is avoidable, contract-inconsistent busy-work that fires continuously during ordinary collection. [Hard rule: 7 (duplicated primitive instead of shared one) / documented data_changed contract in store/changes.py]

### src/oc/learn/dictionary.py

**Purpose:** Wraps a game's authored (or feed-derived) word list into fast exact/word lookup structures (`Dictionary`), and `build_dictionaries(profile, corrector)` builds the pooled + per-id Dictionary objects the resolver consults for a `dictionary` field rule. This is the non-self-learning vocabulary layer the project deliberately kept after ripping out the old lexicon (see memory note 'No self-learning').

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/collector.py:21,125 - `build_dictionaries(profile, engine.corrector)` per collection tick setup; src/oc/collect/precapture.py:38,265 - same, for precapture preview OCR; src/oc/web/routes/preview.py:21,259,431 - same, for the teaching-UI live preview and rule-trace endpoints; src/oc/learn/resolver.py:24 - imports `_norm` for word-key normalisation; tests/test_learn.py:1,10 - `Dictionary` used directly to build a resolver's dictionary in every test; scripts/bench_preprocess.py:32,66 - `build_dictionaries`/`_norm` for the OCR preprocessing benchmark script

- **[INFO] encapsulation** (line 42): `Dictionary.word_map` returns the internal `_words` dict by reference rather than a copy. -- `resolver.py`'s `_words()` caches and reads this dict directly (`cached = (dmap, ...)`), but every current consumer only reads it (`.get`, `in` checks) — no mutation path exists today, so this is not an active bug, just a soft encapsulation gap that would let a future caller corrupt a Dictionary's canonical word map for every FieldResolver sharing it (Dictionary objects are shared/cached by object identity across resolvers via `build_dictionaries`).

### src/oc/learn/difflib_corrector.py

**Purpose:** Zero-dependency stdlib fallback Corrector backend (registered as "difflib") used by every unit test in tests/test_learn.py and as the non-default corrector option a game could pick in settings.yaml instead of rapidfuzz.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py:60,123 - listed in `_IMPL_MODULES` so `@register_corrector("difflib")` runs at startup discovery; config/settings.yaml:31 - the alternate value `corrector: <difflib>` could select this backend (current value is `rapidfuzz`); tests/test_learn.py:2,9,21,71,86,220 - directly instantiated as the corrector in nearly every resolver test

- No issues found.

### src/oc/learn/rapidfuzz_corrector.py

**Purpose:** The default, fast Corrector backend (registered as "rapidfuzz") — used in production per config/settings.yaml's `corrector: rapidfuzz`. Uses plain `ratio` rather than rapidfuzz's `WRatio` specifically to avoid token-subset matches collapsing distinct records (documented in the class comment, e.g. "Akarius Prime" vs "Akarius Prime Link").

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py:59,123 - listed in `_IMPL_MODULES` for discovery; config/settings.yaml:31 - `corrector: rapidfuzz` selects this backend by name via `Engine.build()` -> `build_corrector`; src/oc/engine.py:73 - `build_corrector(settings.corrector.name, **settings.corrector.options)` instantiates it at runtime

- No issues found.

### src/oc/learn/resolver.py

**Purpose:** The `dictionary` rule's implementation for the field rule pipeline: FieldResolver.apply_dictionary is the `dict_hook` callback that `oc.collect.fields.run_rules` calls for a `dictionary` action, handling all four DictMode variants (off/correct/drop/correct_drop) via exact-term short-circuit then per-word correction/unmerge/fuzzy-snap, and reporting which mechanism (dict/split/fuzzy) verified the value. Deleting it would make every `dictionary` rule a silent pass-through (per fields.py's own docstring, 'Without one, a dictionary rule is a pass-through') — OCR text fields would never snap to the authored vocabulary at all.

**Used by:** src/oc/learn/__init__.py:8 - re-exported as the package's public API; src/oc/collect/collector.py:22,126 - `FieldResolver(engine.corrector, tuning.accept_confidence, pooled, dict_map)` built once per collection setup and used per read; src/oc/collect/precapture.py:39,266 - same, for precapture preview; src/oc/web/routes/preview.py:22,260,338,432 - same, for the teaching-UI live preview and the `/rule_trace` debug endpoint (`rule_trace` method used at preview.py:338+ via `resolver.rule_trace`); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js:220-227 - front-end calls `/api/rule_trace`, which is backed by `FieldResolver.rule_trace`; scripts/bench_preprocess.py:33,67 - benchmark script; tests/test_learn.py:3,10,71,86,220 - the bulk of this module's behavior is exercised directly here (27 passing tests)

- No issues found.


_Module notes: All 6 files pass tests/test_learn.py (27 passed) as currently on disk. No hardcoded game-specific knowledge found anywhere in this module (all logic is generic text/vocabulary manipulation driven by profile-authored DictionaryDef/FieldRule data) — consistent with the zero-game-knowledge-in-Python rule. No mutable-default-argument bugs, no bare `except:` (all broad excepts are `except Exception` with a `# noqa: BLE001` comment explaining the best-effort intent). Correctors are registered via `@register_corrector` in `registry._IMPL_MODULES` (`oc.learn.rapidfuzz_corrector`, `oc.learn.difflib_corrector`) and selected by name from `config/settings.yaml` (`corrector: rapidfuzz`), confirming both are live, not dead. Traced the full FieldResolver/run_rules contract against `oc/collect/fields.py`'s `DictOutcome`/`RuleResult` and it matches exactly (mode semantics, verified-rank logic, drop propagation) — cross-checked against tests/test_learn.py's 27 cases including the documented Q3/G3 glyph-confusion non-regression test.

Main finding worth a second look: `DictFeeder.__call__` (dict_feed.py) ignores the change-bus's `data_changed` flag, unlike its sibling `OnChangeFirer` (store/changes.py) which explicitly skips metadata-only pings (`if not records and not data_changed: return`). Since `collector.py` fires a `data_changed=False` ping on every learned-scroll-position write (`store.set_positions`), any game with a feed-derived dictionary re-derives+re-compares its whole term list on every such ping (debounced to at most once per 0.5s while writes continue). Impact is bounded (write_dictionary no-ops when content is unchanged, and the debounce coalesces bursts) so I rated it medium rather than high — but it's a clear, provable divergence from the sibling implementation's documented contract, and the two debounce/coalesce implementations (identical trailing-timer-with-lock shape; `threading.Timer` appears in exactly these two places in the whole repo) look like a hard-rule-7 "second copy instead of shared primitive" case.

Everything else I looked at (Dictionary exact/word-map construction, _unmerge ambiguity gating, corrector cutoff semantics in both backends, FieldResolver's per-word correction/verified-rank logic, rule_trace) held up under line-by-line reading and matched its docstring/contract; I found no other high-confidence bugs._

## Module: p15-ocr  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/ocr/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package marker/docstring for the `oc.ocr` namespace package. Contains no code beyond the module docstring; its only role is making the directory an importable Python package.

**Used by:** Implicitly required by every `oc.ocr.*` import (registry._IMPL_MODULES entry 'oc.ocr.rapidocr3_engine', plus direct imports of oc.ocr.cuda/device_switch/gpu_mem/serialize from collect/live.py, collect/precapture.py, web/routes/ocr.py, web/routes/preview.py, web/routes/capture.py, web/gpu_watch.py).

- No issues found.

### src/oc/ocr/cuda.py

**Purpose:** Shared CUDA plumbing used by the OCR backend: register_cuda_dlls() puts the pip nvidia-*-cu12 wheel's DLL folders on the search path/PATH so onnxruntime's CUDA execution provider can load; cuda_available() reports whether onnxruntime exposes the CUDA provider; patch_arena_shrinkage() monkeypatches an inference-session class so ORT frees its CUDA BFC arena at the end of each run (otherwise VRAM only ever grows across OCR bursts of varying crop sizes).

**Used by:** src/oc/ocr/rapidocr3_engine.py (imports register_cuda_dlls, patch_arena_shrinkage; calls both when building/using a GPU session); src/oc/web/routes/ocr.py:10 (cuda_available, for gpu_available/default_mode in the OCR settings API); src/oc/ocr/device_switch.py:27 (lazy import of cuda_available inside enter_device)

- **[LOW] robustness** (line 22): register_cuda_dlls() is one-shot via a module-level flag, so a transient failure on the first call is never retried. -- `_CUDA_DLLS_REGISTERED` is set to True unconditionally before the try/except that does the actual work, and the function returns immediately on any later call regardless of whether the first attempt actually found/registered anything. If the first call raced something (e.g. the `nvidia` package not yet fully importable) and hit the blanket `except Exception: pass`, every later call in the process is a silent no-op even though a retry might have succeeded. In practice this is called exactly once at `Rapid3OcrEngine.__init__` when `use_gpu` is set, so the observable impact is limited, but the flag conflates 'attempted' with 'succeeded'.

### src/oc/ocr/device_switch.py

**Purpose:** The single shared implementation of 'flip the OCR engine to GPU for a high-throughput batch, then restore the CPU baseline afterwards' used by both the precapture processing worker and the live collector so 'auto' device mode has exactly one code path (per CLAUDE.md rule 7: second use of a primitive must share, not copy).

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/precapture.py:46,979,983 (_process_loop wraps _process_batch); src/oc/collect/live.py:22,122,132 (_loop wraps the Collector.run call); batch_device is set from src/oc/web/routes/precapture.py:80,236 and src/oc/web/routes/live.py:99 ("gpu" when the persisted OCR mode is "auto", else None)

- **[LOW] efficiency** (line 26): enter_device() gates GPU-burst mode on the global cuda_available() check, not the active engine's own cuda_capable flag added specifically for this purpose in a recent commit. -- Rapid3OcrEngine.cuda_capable (added in commit 699819d) is False for the OpenVINO engine_type, and the web UI is documented to grey out GPU/Auto when it's False — but enter_device() only calls `cuda.cuda_available()` (whether onnxruntime's CUDA provider is installed at all) before flipping `ocr.set_device(True)`. If a user's persisted device mode is "auto" while engine_type is "openvino" (e.g. they picked auto under onnxruntime, then switched engine later, and nothing resets the persisted mode), every precapture/live batch would still call set_device(True) then set_device(False), forcing two needless engine rebuilds per batch with zero behavioral benefit (OpenVINO ignores the CUDA flag). Confidence is moderate rather than high since this depends on front-end gating I did not audit (out of this module) actually allowing that combination to persist.

### src/oc/ocr/gpu_mem.py

**Purpose:** Reads this process's dedicated GPU VRAM usage (the same number Task Manager shows) via the Windows 'GPU Process Memory' PDH performance counter, since NVML/nvidia-smi cannot report per-process VRAM on WDDM. Feeds the VRAM readout shown in the web UI.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/ocr.py:11,127 (process_gpu_mem() feeds the `gpu_mem` field of `_state()`, served by GET /api/ocr/device and the activity heartbeat via ocr_state())

- No issues found.

### src/oc/ocr/rapidocr3_engine.py

**Purpose:** The sole registered OCR backend (`@register_ocr("ppocr5")`), wrapping the rapidocr v3 package. Owns lazy construction/rebuild of the underlying RapidOCR engine from flat settings.yaml options, exposes the runtime knobs the web settings modal drives (device, detection scale, thread caps, CUDA arena cap, engine_type), and implements read_image (full det+rec, with a detect-on-downscaled-frame/recognize-on-original-crop fast path) and read_line (rec-only, skips detection).

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py:57 (_IMPL_MODULES lists 'oc.ocr.rapidocr3_engine' for discovery); config/settings.yaml `ocr.name: ppocr5` (Engine.build() resolves the registered class by this name); tests/test_rapidocr3_engine.py (direct instantiation/behavior tests); Consumed everywhere `Engine.build().ocr` is used: src/oc/collect/reader.py, src/oc/collect/live.py, src/oc/collect/precapture.py, src/oc/detect/matcher.py, src/oc/numbers/process.py, src/oc/web/routes/preview.py, capture.py, ocr.py

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 279): Rapid3OcrEngine never overrides read_lines(), so every 'grid' read (dozens of crops) runs as N separate single-crop model calls instead of one batched recognition pass, contradicting the codebase's own documented contract for that method. -- `OcrEngine.read_lines` (src/oc/interfaces.py:94-98) defaults to `[self.read_line(im) for im in images]` — a per-item loop — with an explicit docstring inviting backends that 'can batch the recogniser' to override it 'to run them in one pass (far fewer GPU launches)'. Rapid3OcrEngine defines `read_image` and `read_line` but never overrides `read_lines`, even though this very module already knows how to batch rec-only calls: `read_image`'s downscale branch calls `engine.text_rec(TextRecInput(img=crops))` (lines 306-309) to recognize many crops in ONE call. Every caller of read_lines assumes the batched behavior: src/oc/collect/reader.py:199 (`self._ocr.read_lines(crops)` for a grid's 'dozens of crops'), src/oc/detect/matcher.py:165-189 (docstring: 'instead of a read_line per box... read_lines'), src/oc/numbers/process.py:15 ('OCR for all ripe tracks in a frame is batched through OcrEngine.read_lines (one GPU pass...)'), config/settings.yaml's `rec_batch_num: 16` comment ('grid reads recognise dozens of crops; fewer, bigger launches'), and src/oc/web/app.py:86-91's warmup comment ('grid reads use read_lines/text_rec'). None of that is true for the current ppocr5 backend: each crop pays its own `_ensure_engine()`/lock/session.run() overhead, and `Rec.rec_batch_num` never gets a batch bigger than 1 to apply to. This is a silent regression from the deleted v1 backend (`rapidocr_engine.py`, removed in commit 11c6570), whose `read_lines` explicitly batched through a shared `_text_rec` helper; no test (tests/test_rapidocr3_engine.py) exercises read_lines at all, only a mocked stub in tests/test_gate.py's fake engine.

- **[LOW] robustness** (line 129): set_scale() accepts any positive integer with no upper bound, so an extreme scale on a small crop can drive cv2.resize's target size to zero and raise. -- `set_scale` only clamps to `max(1, int(n))` — no ceiling. In `read_image`'s downscale branch, `small = cv2.resize(image, (w // f, h // f), ...)`; if `f` exceeds the crop's shorter side (e.g. a narrow field crop with scale set far higher than realistic 1-4 values), `w // f` or `h // f` truncates to 0 and cv2.resize raises on a zero-sized destination. Low severity because the practical scale range (UI/settings) is small integers and typical crops are hundreds of pixels wide, so this needs a misconfiguration to trigger.

- **[INFO] precision** (line 304): Downscaled detection boxes are rescaled by the nominal factor `f`, not the ratio cv2.resize actually applied, so boxes can drift by up to f-1 px when the crop's dimensions aren't evenly divisible by f. -- `small = cv2.resize(image, (w // f, h // f), ...)` then `boxes = [np.clip(np.asarray(b) * f, ...) ...]` multiplies by the integer `f`, whereas the true applied scale is `w / (w // f)` (and separately `h / (h // f)`) whenever `w`/`h` aren't multiples of `f`. This is inherited unchanged from the removed v1 backend (same `b * f` pattern), so it's a pre-existing, presumably-accepted characteristic rather than a new regression — flagged for completeness only, not as a new defect.

### src/oc/ocr/rapidocr3_map.py

**Purpose:** Dependency-free translation layer between (a) flat settings.yaml/backend options and rapidocr v3's dotted-key params dict, and (b) v3's RapidOCROutput/rec-only output shapes and this codebase's OcrLine/PixelBox types. Kept free of any import-guard on the optional `rapidocr` package so it stays unit-testable without the dependency installed.

**Used by:** src/oc/ocr/rapidocr3_engine.py:37 (imports join_rec, to_lines, to_params); tests/test_rapidocr3_map.py (direct unit tests of all three functions)

- **[LOW] defensive-completeness** (line 82): to_lines()'s None-guard checks only boxes/txts, not scores; it relies on zip()'s implicit shortest-iterable truncation to handle a None scores rather than an explicit check. -- `if boxes is None or txts is None: return []` doesn't cover `scores is None`; the function then does `zip(boxes, txts, scores or ())`. If `scores` were `None` while `boxes`/`txts` held real entries, `scores or ()` evaluates to an empty tuple and `zip` would silently produce zero results, discarding valid detections rather than erroring or defaulting a per-line score. Verified this can't currently trigger against the installed rapidocr 3.9.0: `RapidOCROutput`/`build_final_output` (site-packages/rapidocr/main.py) only ever sets `boxes`+`txts` together with `scores` from the same recognition pass, so `scores` is never None alongside populated `txts` in the actual call sites (`to_lines(out.boxes, out.txts, out.scores)` and `to_lines(*zip(*keep))`). Still worth an explicit `or scores is None` for defensiveness against a future/different upstream shape.

### src/oc/ocr/serialize.py

**Purpose:** The one global, process-wide OCR serialization point: `OCR_LOCK` (an RLock wrapper tracking a last-used timestamp) ensures at most one OCR inference runs at a time (concurrent GPU/CPU OCR jobs thrash each other and end up slower, not faster); `ocr_job()` is a context manager that builds the engine's model outside the timed/lock region, then holds the lock for a whole logical unit of work (a preview read, a detect pass, one precapture frame) so jobs don't interleave; `ocr_idle_for()` lets the GPU watchdog ask 'has OCR run recently?' without instrumenting every call site.

**Used by:** src/oc/ocr/rapidocr3_engine.py:38 (`_INFER_LOCK = OCR_LOCK`, wraps every read_image/read_line inference as the per-call safety net); src/oc/web/gpu_watch.py:60 (OCR_LOCK, ocr_idle_for — gates auto-release of the GPU session); src/oc/web/routes/capture.py:218, src/oc/web/routes/preview.py:23 (ocr_job for preview/one-shot reads); src/oc/collect/precapture.py:47,1018 (ocr_job wraps a batch frame's OCR work)

- No issues found.


_Module notes: Overall this module is clean, well-documented, and free of hardcoded game knowledge (matches the project's core principle). Every file is reachable via the registry/self-registration pattern or a direct import chain; none are dead code. The standout finding is a genuine, well-evidenced regression: Rapid3OcrEngine (the sole OCR backend, ppocr5/rapidocr v3) never overrides read_lines(), so it falls back to the ABC's naive per-crop loop even though the module itself already knows how to batch rec-only calls (used inside read_image's own downscale branch) and multiple other modules' docstrings/comments (numbers/process.py, detect/matcher.py, web/app.py, settings.yaml's rec_batch_num comment) assert read_lines does exactly one batched GPU/CPU pass over all crops. This silently defeats rec_batch_num for every grid read (the primary collection workload — dozens of crops per tick) and was not introduced deliberately: the deleted v1 backend (rapidocr_engine.py, removed in commit 11c6570) had an explicit batched implementation that never got ported. No test covers read_lines for this backend. Remaining findings are low/info: a one-shot CUDA-DLL registration flag that never retries, an auto-mode device-burst check that doesn't consult the engine's own cuda_capable flag, an unbounded set_scale that could zero out cv2.resize's target on an extreme value against a tiny crop, an inherited (not new) sub-pixel box-rescale imprecision in the downscale path, and an incomplete None-guard in to_lines() that currently can't trigger given the installed rapidocr package's actual output contract._

## Module: p16-profile-models  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/profile/models.py

**Purpose:** Defines the entire per-game profile schema as pydantic models: boxes/coordinates (Box, to_fraction), the field value-rule pipeline (FieldRule/RuleWhen/RuleThen/Extract/DictMode), region/item/tell/detect/state definitions consumed by the collector's classify+read pipeline, scroll/preprocess config, dataset/subset/producer/file-source/trigger/toast/sound/dictionary/glyph definitions, graph-layout persistence, and the top-level GameProfile with key-resolution helpers (key_map_for, key_conflict, stat_node_ids, fields_for, dictionary_terms*). This is the single source of truth for 'what a profile looks like' - the YAML loader validates against it and the collector/reader/web UI all read these types. If deleted, nothing could parse config/games/*.yaml, the collector could not build FieldResolver/Detect/Tell/Item logic, and the 'everything is teachable via YAML' architecture collapses.

**Used by:** src/oc/profile/loader.py - GameProfile.model_validate on YAML load; loader's _migrate_detect_thresholds/_migrate_keys/_migrate_dictionary_ids/_migrate_readout_ids exist specifically to backfill fields this module declares required (e.g. DetectDef.threshold); src/oc/collect/reader.py, items.py, tells.py, grid.py, collector.py, live.py, precapture.py, fields.py, glyph_match.py, preprocess.py - consume FieldDef/RegionDef/ItemDef/Tell/DetectDef/ScrollDef/Preprocess/FieldRule; src/oc/detect/matcher.py, classifier.py - DetectDef/StateDef/MatchMode/StripMode/TellKind; src/oc/learn/resolver.py, dict_feed.py - FieldRule/DictMode/DictionaryDef; resolver.py's run_rules(dict_hook=self.apply_dictionary) is the exact contract FieldRule.dict_id/dict_mode document; src/oc/enrich/subset.py, http_producer.py, price_runner.py - SubsetDef/JoinSource/HttpSpec/ProducerDef; src/oc/web/routes/preview.py, toasts.py, sources.py - ToastDef/ToastImageDef/FileSourceDef; preview.py also calls run_rules for the node debug trace; src/oc/source/extract.py - SourceField/SourceMatch; src/oc/notify/toast_image.py - ToastImageDef; src/oc/locate.py, runtime/overrides.py, interfaces.py, cli/sweep_job.py; src/oc/web/app.py:174 - GameProfile.stat_node_ids() feeds stats_store.prune_stale() at startup; ~25 test files (test_fields.py, test_learn.py, test_reader.py, test_datasets.py, test_variables.py, test_keys.py, test_items.py, test_detect.py, test_grid.py, test_subset.py, test_triggers.py, test_http_producer.py, test_http_mapping.py, test_dataset_ops.py, test_sources*.py, test_merge.py, test_pretty.py, test_locate*.py, test_gate.py, test_precapture.py, test_prices_live.py, test_profile_http_guard.py, test_profile_backup.py) construct/validate these models directly; config/games/warframe.yaml - validated end-to-end via load_profile in this session (loads cleanly, 6 windows)

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 1308): GameProfile.stat_node_ids() omits datasets fed only by a producer or file_source that has no matching DatasetDef and no window feeding it, so prune_stale() (run once per boot) can delete that dataset's accumulated timing-stat CSV every restart. -- stat_node_ids() builds the 'live node' set as {ds:<id> for id in self.datasets} union {ds:<w.dataset_id> for w in self.windows if w.dataset_id} (lines 1315-1321). ProducerDef.dataset and FileSourceDef.dataset are free-typed strings with no requirement that a corresponding DatasetDef exist in self.datasets - grep across profile/ and store/ found no server-side (Python) code that auto-creates a DatasetDef when a producer/file_source's dataset is set; that invariant is maintained only client-side by graph/model.js's ensureDatasetDef (called from setProducerDataset/setSourceDataset/addProducer). dataset_store.py:877 calls stats_store.time_block(game, f'ds:{self._dataset}', ...) for every DatasetStore regardless of how it's fed. If a profile is hand-edited or PUT via the API bypassing the graph UI with a producer/file_source pointing at a dataset name never separately declared, prune_stale (stats_store.py:392, invoked from web/app.py on every boot) will delete that dataset's stat CSV next boot since its 'ds:<id>' is absent from live_nodes but matches the prunable 'ds:' prefix. Impact is limited to losing a timing-telemetry rollup (self-heals as new samples accumulate), not data loss, so this is a soft gap rather than a confirmed live incident; not covered by any existing test (test_stats_store.py exercises prune_stale only with hand-built live sets, never via GameProfile.stat_node_ids()).

- **[LOW] maintainability** (line 145): FieldRule._ARG_WHENS is an unannotated underscore class attribute that pydantic v2 silently turns into a ModelPrivateAttr; it resolves correctly via self._ARG_WHENS on an instance but FieldRule._ARG_WHENS (class-level access) returns the ModelPrivateAttr descriptor, not the tuple. -- Verified by direct import: `FieldRule._ARG_WHENS` prints `default=(...)` (a ModelPrivateAttr repr) while an instance's `r._ARG_WHENS` correctly resolves to the plain tuple. The only current use (in _ser, line 153) is instance-level (self._ARG_WHENS), so today's behavior is correct - but the pattern is a footgun for any future maintainer referencing FieldRule._ARG_WHENS statically (e.g. from a test or another module) expecting a plain tuple; they'd get pydantic's private-attribute wrapper instead. An explicit `ClassVar[tuple[...]]` annotation would remove the ambiguity.

- **[LOW] style** (line 515): ScrollDef.calib_samples uses a bare mutable default (`= []`) instead of the `Field(default_factory=list)` pattern used by every other list field in this file. -- Confirmed this is NOT a functional bug: pydantic v2 deep-copies mutable field defaults per instance (verified empirically - two ScrollDef() instances do not share the same calib_samples list; mutating one leaves the other untouched), so the classic Python mutable-default-argument hazard does not apply. It is purely a stylistic inconsistency versus every other list field in this file (FieldDef.rules, WindowDef.fields, GameProfile.windows, etc., all `Field(default_factory=list)`) - worth a drive-by cleanup for consistency, not a defect.


_Module notes: Read the full 1469-line file in two passes (offset 0 and 1088). Confirmed zero hardcoded game-specific knowledge (pure schema/data model). Confirmed via `.venv` pytest run (90 passed across test_fields/test_learn/test_reader/test_datasets/test_variables/test_stats_store) and a live `load_profile(config/games, 'warframe')` call that the current on-disk warframe.yaml validates cleanly against this schema post field-rule-pipeline refactor. No bare except, no mutable-default-argument bugs (verified pydantic v2 deep-copies bare list defaults), no path traversal/injection surface (pure declarative data, no I/O in this file). RuleWhen/RuleThen enums cross-checked 1:1 against src/oc/collect/fields.py's run_rules implementation - no missing branches. KeyDef.spec()'s `or (\"name\",)` fallback for an emptied fields list was investigated as a possible 'silently un-drops an unkeyable key' issue but is intentionally mirrored by graph/model.js (line ~1602-1603) which also refuses to leave key.fields empty - not a bug. Also investigated whether item-template renames could leave stale 'item' backrefs in the hoisted item_fields/item_tells flat lists (a rename-repoint hard-rule-5-shaped concern) but item_fields/item_tells are @computed_field properties recomputed fresh from self.items[].fields/.tells at every serialization, so a backref can never go stale within a save/load cycle - not reachable as a bug in this file._

## Module: p17-profile-rest  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/profile/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package facade for oc.profile: re-exports the pydantic model classes (GameProfile, WindowDef, FieldDef, DatasetDef, KeyDef, RegionDef, ScrollDef, StateDef, DetectDef) and the loader IO functions (load_profile/save_profile/backups/dictionaries/graph-local) so the rest of the codebase can do `from ...profile import X` without knowing the internal module split. Deleting it would break every `from ..profile import ...` / `from ...profile import ...` import across cli/, web/routes/, web/app.py, runtime/overrides.py, and several tests.

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/profiles.py, capture.py, collect.py, detect.py, bench.py (list_profiles/load_profile); src/oc/web/app.py, trigger_sched.py, source_sched.py (list_profiles/load_profile); src/oc/web/routes/profiles.py, pretty.py, dictionaries.py, capture.py, flow.py, live.py, precapture.py, preview.py, prices.py, sources.py, toasts.py, triggers.py (list_profiles/load_profile/save_profile/GameProfile/KeyDef/list_dictionaries/read_dictionary); src/oc/runtime/overrides.py (load_profile); tests/test_flow_routes.py, test_datasets.py, test_sources_routes.py, test_profile_backup.py

- **[INFO] api-surface** (line 27): merge_profiles / pretty helpers / repoint_pretty are not re-exported here -- Every current call site imports these via their submodule path directly (`from ...profile.merge import merge_profiles`, `from ...profile.pretty import load_pretty, save_pretty`, `from ...profile.pretty_repoint import repoint_pretty`), so this is consistent, not a bug — just noting the __all__ list is a deliberate subset, not the full module surface.

### src/oc/profile/loader.py

**Purpose:** The single IO boundary for a game profile: atomic YAML read/write with Windows sharing-violation retry, a byte-signature (mtime_ns,size) parse cache, versioned backup snapshots with a structural-vs-cosmetic diff (so node-drag/box-resize churn doesn't spam backups) plus time-based retention thinning, dictionary-term externalization to config/dictionaries/*.txt, forward-migration of older profile YAML shapes, and the gitignored per-device graph-local (viewport) sidecar. Deleting it removes the only way profiles are read/written from disk.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/profiles.py (load_profile/save_profile/backups/graph_local); src/oc/web/routes/pretty.py (load_profile/save_profile via commit_overrides); src/oc/cli/capture.py, collect.py, detect.py, bench.py (load_profile); src/oc/runtime/overrides.py (load_profile); tests/test_profile_backup.py, tests/test_keys.py (_migrate_keys/_migrate_detect_thresholds directly), tests/test_datasets.py

- **[CRITICAL] data-loss** (line 110): _migrate_keys unconditionally strips DatasetDef.key_field on every load, destroying a live, currently-authored dataset key setting -- `_migrate_keys` (lines 101-127) runs on EVERY `load_profile`/`read_backup` call (no version gate exists anywhere in the profile format) and does `kf = d.pop("key_field", None)` for every dataset dict, treating it purely as an ancient shape to synthesize a window-level `key` from. But `DatasetDef.key_field`/`key_fields` were reintroduced later (see models.py `DatasetDef`, `key_map_for`) as a live, currently-teachable 'dataset-level key override' — the graph UI's dataset node has a real '1->many' key-mode dropdown (`static/js/graph/model.js` `setDatasetKeyField`/`datasetKeyMode`, modes auto/single/concat/nodedup) that writes exactly this field, and `GameProfile.key_map_for` consults it. Because the old and new YAML shapes are byte-identical, the migration cannot tell them apart and silently deletes the value on every parse. Confirmed by direct repro: `save_profile` a `DatasetDef(key_field="sku")` -> the YAML on disk correctly has `key_field: sku` -> `load_profile` returns `key_field == ""` -> a subsequent `save_profile` (as happens in `restore_backup`, `commit_overrides`, or any read-modify-write route) permanently overwrites the file, erasing `key_field: sku` for good. It's even worse when the dataset is fed only by a producer/file_source rather than a window: the migration only ever transplants the popped value onto `raw['windows']` entries, so for a producer/file_source-fed dataset the setting is deleted with no substitute at all. `key_fields` (concat mode) is untouched by this bug, only the single-field override.

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 348): The non-structural key set used to decide whether a save creates a backup snapshot matches bare field names (x/y/w/h) anywhere in the tree, not just node-layout/region-box geometry -- `_NONSTRUCTURAL_KEYS = {"layout","x","y","w","h","config_collapsed","hidden_columns"}` and `_strip_nonstructural` (lines 348-359) recursively drop any dict key with these exact names anywhere in the whole profile, intending to cover `NodeLayout.x/y/w/h` and `FractionBox.x/y/w/h` (both documented at the top of the file). But `ToastImageTextDef.x`/`.y` (models.py) are unrelated, genuinely-structural content: the pixel position of a text line painted onto a generated toast image. Confirmed by repro: changing a toast image text's position from (10,10) to (200,150) makes `_structural(old) == _structural(new)` -> `save_profile` persists the new position to the live file but silently skips creating a backup snapshot, even though it's a real authored edit, not UI chrome. Any other current or future model field that happens to share one of these four short names would fall into the same crack.

- **[LOW] path-safety** (line 44): profile/backup file paths are built directly from caller-supplied name/stamp with no traversal guard -- `profile_path` (line 44) and `backup_path` (line 408) do `Path(profiles_dir) / f"{name}.yaml"` / `.../name/f"{stamp}.yaml"` with no rejection of `..`/absolute-path components; `name`/`stamp` ultimately come from URL path segments in web/routes/profiles.py. A crafted name could in principle resolve outside `profiles_dir`. Low severity given this is a local single-user dev tool and the UI only ever offers existing profile names, but noted since it's an explicit review checklist item.

- **[INFO] duplication** (line 440): backup_meta hardcodes the stamp format string instead of reusing the module's own _STAMP_FMT alias -- `backup_meta` (line 440) does `datetime.strptime(stamp, "%Y%m%d-%H%M%S-%f")` even though the file already aliases this exact string as `_STAMP_FMT = backup.STAMP_FMT` at the top. Harmless today (values are identical), but if `oc.backup.STAMP_FMT` ever changes, this call site silently stops matching and `backup_meta` falls back to showing the raw stamp string instead of a formatted date, with no error.

### src/oc/profile/merge.py

**Purpose:** Upsert-merges an incoming (possibly partial, single-window) profile edit into the existing on-disk profile by id, so a game can accumulate several windows/datasets/etc. authored one at a time without one save clobbering another window's config.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/profiles.py put_profile (invoked when the `merge` query param, default True, is truthy); tests/test_merge.py, tests/test_gate.py, tests/test_variables.py

- **[LOW] doc-staleness / latent-bug** (line 28): Docstring describes the old single-window teach-page save flow, which the current graph SPA no longer uses (it always full-replaces with merge=false), so this function's upsert-only (never-delete) semantics are effectively dormant in the live app -- The module docstring (lines 1-7) says 'The teaching UI edits one window at a time and sends a single-window profile' — true of the deleted teach.html page, but the current SPA's only save call is `static/js/graph/persist.js:52`: `await api.saveProfile(M.profile, false); // full replace — the graph is complete`, which passes `merge=false` and bypasses `merge_profiles` entirely. Every collection this function merges (`windows`, `fields`, `datasets`, `subsets`, `dictionaries`, `producers`, `file_sources`, `triggers`, `toasts`, `sounds`, at lines 24-58) is a pure upsert-by-id that can only add/overwrite, never remove an id present in `existing` but absent from `incoming`; `window_priority`/`layout`/`glyphs` use an `incoming or existing` fallback that can't distinguish 'not sent' from 'intentionally cleared to empty'. This is fine for the documented single-window use case but would silently resurrect deleted items (or refuse to persist an intentional clear-to-empty) if any future caller ever sent a genuinely full-but-trimmed profile through the `merge=True` (default) path. Not observed to fire today since the only live caller opts out with merge=false; flagged as a latent footgun / stale doc, not an active bug.

### src/oc/profile/pretty.py

**Purpose:** Read/write the `<game>.pretty.yaml` sidecar: the 'Pretty Studio' design document (pages of freely-styled widgets bound to datasets/subsets/node inputs), kept deliberately loose (`extra="allow"`) so the front-end owns widget shape and a new widget field never needs a backend model change or 422s a save.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/pretty.py get_pretty/put_pretty (load_pretty/save_pretty), repoint (load_pretty/save_pretty)

- **[MEDIUM] error-handling** (line 58): load_pretty's exception guard doesn't cover pydantic ValidationError, so a schema-invalid (but YAML-syntax-valid) pretty file crashes the route instead of falling back to a default doc -- `load_pretty` (lines 49-66) catches only `(OSError, yaml.YAMLError)` around the YAML parse, then unconditionally calls `PrettyDoc.model_validate(raw)` outside any try/except. A file whose top-level shape doesn't match `PrettyDoc` (e.g. `pages: "not-a-list"` instead of a list) raises an uncaught `pydantic.ValidationError`. Confirmed by repro. This propagates straight through `GET /api/pretty/{game}` as an unhandled 500, contradicting the function's own docstring promise: 'or a fresh default if none exists (or the file is unreadable)' — a schema-invalid file is 'unreadable' in the sense the docstring means, but isn't handled the same way as a syntax-invalid one.

### src/oc/profile/pretty_repoint.py

**Purpose:** Server-side sweep that rewrites `{{kind:id}}` / `kind[id]` tokens embedded in a Pretty document's strings when a graph node (dataset/subset/window/field/item/trigger/producer/widget) is renamed, so references don't silently resolve to empty when Pretty wasn't open in the renaming session (a view switch reloads the doc fresh from disk).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/pretty.py POST /api/pretty/{game}/repoint (repoint_pretty); wired from the client via static/js/graph/model.js _emitRename -> static/js/api.js repointPretty -> the same endpoint

- **[LOW] correctness / doc-overclaim** (line 23): The module docstring's claim that 'plain prose never matches' isn't literally guaranteed by the regex boundary used -- `_HEAD_END = r"(?=$|[.\[|\s}])"` (line 23) only requires the matched id be followed by a token delimiter (a dot, `[`, `|`, whitespace, `}`, or end-of-string) — it does not require the surrounding text to actually be wrapped in `{{ }}` or otherwise be recognizable as a token. A free-typed widget `text`/`title` string that coincidentally contains the literal substring `dataset:<old-id>` (or `subset:`/`widget:`) followed by a space or period — plausible ordinary prose, e.g. 'check dataset:sales before shipping' when renaming a dataset id `sales` — would be silently rewritten as if it were a real token reference, contradicting the stated 'plain prose never matches' guarantee. Narrow/unlikely in practice (requires that specific substring shape in free text), so kept at low severity.

## Module: p18-enrich-jobs-notify-numbers  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/enrich/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package docstring for oc.enrich: explains the enrichment producers are post-capture only (kept out of the OCR loop so network latency/outages can't hurt collection robustness) and that the generic `http` producer is the one backend, authored entirely in the teach UI.

**Used by:** Package marker making oc.enrich.* importable; registry.py:61 imports 'oc.enrich.http_producer' by dotted path for discovery; every consumer below imports from this package.

- No issues found.

### src/oc/enrich/http_get.py

**Purpose:** Stdlib-only (urllib/http.client) JSON-over-HTTP transport shared by every network producer: per-thread keep-alive connection pooling (_conn/_drop_conn), the generic http_get_json() request+parse, and the pure helpers slugify/key_transform/json_path used to build fetch keys and walk arbitrary JSON response shapes taught per game in the profile.

**Used by:** src/oc/enrich/http_producer.py (imports http_get_json, json_path, key_transform, slugify); src/oc/enrich/sweep_engine.py:24 (imports NET_ERRORS to classify a fetch failure); tests/test_market_slug.py (slugify), tests/test_http_mapping.py (json_path, key_transform), tests/test_prices_live.py (NET_ERRORS, http_get_json)

- **[LOW] correctness**: Per-thread connection cache ignores a differing timeout on reuse -- _conn(scheme, host, port, timeout) (lines ~103-111) keys the thread-local connection cache purely by (scheme, host, port); the `timeout` argument is only applied when the connection object is first constructed. If two calls target the same host from the same worker thread with different `timeout` values (e.g. two HttpSpec nodes pointed at the same API with different configured timeouts), the second call silently keeps using the first call's timeout until the connection is dropped by a transport error. Low impact since a node's timeout is normally constant across its own sweep.

### src/oc/enrich/http_producer.py

**Purpose:** The one generic network producer backend (`@register_producer("http")`): fetches a taught URL per source item (or once for list/explode mode) and maps the JSON response to dataset columns via a pure mapping engine (map_response/map_rows), a name->key catalogue resolver, and shared preview/probe helpers used by the teach UI. Zero API-specific knowledge lives here — everything is driven by the profile's HttpSpec.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py:61,104,174 (registered via @register_producer("http") at http_producer.py:401-402, discovered through registry._IMPL_MODULES); src/oc/web/routes/prices.py:69,80 (resolved_inputs, probe_item for the teach UI preview); src/oc/collect/templating.py:168, src/oc/collect/triggers.py:346 (compute_view_rows re-exported via subset, but gather_source_names is this module's own); tests/test_http_producer.py, tests/test_http_mapping.py, tests/test_triggers.py (gather_source_names)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 415): Producer with no `sources` silently fetches nothing, contradicting the documented "whole catalogue" fallback -- gather_source_names() (lines 280-297) returns [] when `sources` is empty, and HttpProducer.run() (lines 406-449) only ever fetches names from ctx.items or gather_source_names(sources) — there is no code path that falls back to sweeping the whole resolved catalogue when a node has zero wired sources. Yet enrich/price_runner.py's module docstring (line 15: "...else the node's sources datasets/views, else the whole market catalogue"), web/routes/triggers.py:51 ("whole catalogue if none"), and static/js/graph/model.js:383 ("empty = whole catalogue") all describe that fallback as supported. A user who authors a price/producer node with an intentionally empty sources list (expecting it to price everything) gets a silent 0-item sweep instead.

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 444): A mapped field named the same as the item's resolved source column can silently override it -- write_one() builds the stored row as {"name": name, **row} (line 444); if the taught HttpSpec.fields include an out_field literally called "name", its mapped value overwrites the resolved source item's own name key (dict literal: later keys win). Likely intentional (lets an API's canonical name replace an OCR'd one) but worth a maintainer's awareness since it's easy to trip on when authoring field mappings.

### src/oc/enrich/price_runner.py

**Purpose:** The type-agnostic, cross-process producer-sweep supervisor: owns the per-game in-process lock + filesystem lock + cancel flag + status sidecar, spawns the actual fetch as a `python -m oc _sweep-job` child process (so a multi-minute sweep never freezes the single-worker uvicorn event loop / GIL), mirrors the child's progress, and reaps/re-announces results on this process's buses when it exits. Despite its legacy name (from when this only handled pricing), it now supervises any ProducerDef.type.

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/sweep_job.py:43 (imports the job-file helpers to run the actual child sweep); src/oc/cli/collect.py:40 (sweep_status); src/oc/collect/triggers.py:42 (start_sweep, sweep_status — on_change trigger firing); src/oc/web/app.py:160,206,253 (clear_stale_locks at startup, sweep_status, shutdown_sweeps at teardown); src/oc/web/routes/activity.py:14 (active_sweeps, recent_blocked); src/oc/web/trigger_sched.py:24 (sweep_status); src/oc/web/routes/triggers.py:14 (start_sweep, sweep_status); src/oc/web/routes/prices.py:13 (cancel_sweep, producer_for, start_sweep, sweep_status); tests/test_eventlog.py, tests/test_prices.py, tests/test_prices_live.py, tests/test_sweep_status.py

- **[HIGH] resource-leak** (line 440): An exception while writing the sweep job file permanently strands the per-game sweep gate -- start_sweep() (lines 414-473) acquires the in-process per-game threading.Lock (line 429-430) and the cross-process filesystem lock (line 432-435), then calls _write_job_file(job_file, profile, price_node, items) at line 440 — BEFORE the try/except block (lines 460-472) that is the only code path releasing those resources on failure (it only wraps runner.proc.start()). _write_job_file() does path.parent.mkdir(...) and path.write_text(json.dumps(...)) (lines 240-246), and profile.model_dump(mode="json")/node.model_dump(mode="json") beforehand — any OSError (disk full, permission denied, AV lock — all plausible on the project's target Windows platform) or serialization error there propagates straight out of start_sweep, leaving `gate` (this game's in-process threading.Lock) acquired forever for the rest of the process's life (nothing else ever releases it) and the cross-process `.price_sweep.lock` file on disk until its 30-minute staleness timeout (_LOCK_STALE). Concretely: one transient I/O hiccup while writing `.sweep_job.json` wedges every future producer refresh for that game in the running server/collect process, reporting "blocked: another sweep running" indefinitely (until process restart), even though nothing is actually running.

- **[MEDIUM] hard-rule** (line 10): Module docstring names "warframe.market" and a literal price-store filename as if this generic sweep supervisor is Warframe-specific -- Lines 10-11: "...two concurrent sweeps would blow past warframe.market's rate ceiling and clobber the shared price_store.json." No executable code here references any game name or that filename (confirmed by grep — only the docstring), so this is not a functional violation, just documentation drift left over from before the module was generalized to any producer type (mirrored by its own filename, price_runner.py, which is now a generic name). Low functional risk, flagged only because CLAUDE.md's own "zero game knowledge" principle is stated as a Python-file-wide rule and the project treats this kind of drift as worth surfacing; downgraded from a hard violation since CLAUDE.md's own docs use Warframe as the sanctioned illustrative example throughout. [Hard rule: Core principle: everything is teachable, nothing is game-specific]

- **[LOW] simplification** (line 414): start_sweep()'s `key` parameter is accepted but never used -- start_sweep(data_dir, game, price_node, *, profile=None, key=None, ...) (line 414) never references the local `key` anywhere in its body — _write_job_file() only serializes profile/node/items (lines 234-246), not key. The docstring explains why (line 422: "key is recomputed by the child from profile" since a KeySpec/callable can't cross a process boundary), which is a sound reason, but every call site (collect/triggers.py:416, web/routes/triggers.py:73, web/routes/prices.py:40) already omits `key` and relies on the default. The parameter is dead — harmless today, but a maintainer could reasonably (and wrongly) assume passing `key=...` has an effect.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 310): _foreign_sweep's "same pid" filter can never match this process's own spawned child -- _foreign_sweep() (lines 305-313) drops a status-sidecar entry when `d.get("pid") == os.getpid()`, with the comment "our own child's progress is mirrored via the in-memory runner instead, so it isn't double-counted here." But the sidecar's `pid` is written by the CHILD process (_write_sweep_status, called from the spawned `_sweep-job` subprocess) while this check runs in the PARENT — the two pids are never equal, so this comparison is effectively dead logic; it never actually filters out the current process's own sweep. It's harmless in practice only because active_sweeps() (lines 522-540) separately de-dupes by dataset name against the in-memory runners, which is what actually prevents a visible duplicate. The comment attributes the safety to the wrong mechanism.

### src/oc/enrich/subset.py

**Purpose:** Pure computation engine for a SubsetDef (a joined/filtered/derived view over one or more datasets): per-source join on independently normalised keys with configurable required/optional semantics (full outer by default, narrows toward inner as sources are marked required), derived `{column}`/`={expr}` template columns (a restricted, injection-safe AST arithmetic evaluator), multi-column stable sort, and the recursive compute_view_rows() that resolves a subset's own subset inputs with cycle protection — the one place this join/derivation logic lives for the web flow view, price/producer sourcing, and trigger change-gating alike.

**Used by:** src/oc/enrich/http_producer.py:284,292 (gather_source_names uses compute_view_rows for a subset source); src/oc/collect/templating.py:168,176 (live readout token resolution over a subset); src/oc/collect/triggers.py:346,352 (on_change trigger's changed-row computation); src/oc/web/routes/flow.py:195,215,222,237,270 (the Pretty/graph flow view endpoint); src/oc/numfmt.py:5 (docstring cross-reference for shared |round: directive syntax); tests/test_subset.py (compute_subset, compute_view)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 329): compute_view_rows' per-(input, aggregate) memo cache is not stack-aware, so a genuinely cyclic subset graph can poison a non-cyclic evaluation of the same subset reached via a different (diamond) path in the same query -- input_rows() (lines 329-343) checks `if ck in cache: return cache[ck]` BEFORE `elif input_id in stack: rows = []` (the cycle guard), and the cache key `ck = (input_id, aggregate)` deliberately excludes `stack`. If a subset X is genuinely self-/mutually-cyclic through one dependency chain (correctly resolving to `[]` via the cycle guard and caching that under (X, agg)), and the SAME subset X is ALSO reachable — with the same aggregate — via a second, non-cyclic path elsewhere in the same top-level compute_view_rows() call (a diamond), the second path's lookup hits the FIRST path's now-cached (and, for that second context, wrong) result instead of recomputing correctly, or vice versa (a real value computed on one branch masking what should have been an empty cyclic result on another). This produces silently wrong joined/derived data rather than a crash or an obviously-broken cycle guard. Requires an actually-cyclic subset configuration (a misconfiguration the code otherwise explicitly defends against) combined with reuse elsewhere to trigger, so real-world likelihood is low, but the mechanism is a genuine gap in the memoization contract as designed.

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 124): Inline `{=expr}` derived-column arithmetic can misparse a hyphenated column name as subtraction -- The inline math form ({=count*price_median} inside a text template, lines 124-150/164) evaluates its expression body as bare (unbraced) Python identifiers via ast.Name lookups against the row dict (_eval_arith, lines 59-73). A column literally named e.g. "price-median" referenced inside an inline block would parse as `price - median` (two separate names) rather than one identifier, silently producing wrong/empty math instead of an error. The leading `=col` form is unaffected since it substitutes `{col}` textually to a numeric literal before parsing. Minor, edge-case column-naming footgun inherent to the chosen syntax, not a code defect.

### src/oc/enrich/sweep_engine.py

**Purpose:** The generic, throttled, cancellable fan-out engine every producer sweep runs on: a shared rate limiter across N worker threads, single-consumer-thread writes (stores aren't thread-safe), periodic time-based checkpoint saves, prompt cooperative cancellation, an always-flush guarantee in `finally`, and the one summary log line per sweep — extracted so the `http` producer (and any future network backend) is a caller of one throttle/cancel/logging model, not a copy.

**Used by:** src/oc/enrich/http_producer.py:33,446 (run_sweep is the concurrency engine behind HttpProducer.run); tests/test_prices.py:7 (unique_items — test-only, see finding)

- **[LOW] reuse** (line 61): unique_items() has no production caller — the one consumer that could use it reimplements equivalent dedup logic inline instead -- unique_items(records, name_field, resolve) (lines 61-78) is exported for exactly the "dedupe display names to (key, name) pairs, one fetch per key" job, but grep across the whole repo shows it is only ever imported by tests/test_prices.py. The one real caller, HttpProducer.run() in http_producer.py (lines 419-426), instead hand-rolls the same resolve->dedup-by-key loop inline (working from a plain list[str] of names rather than raw records, so it isn't a byte-for-byte duplicate, but the dedup logic and intent are the same). Worth either wiring HttpProducer through unique_items or removing/retiring it if a future backend won't need the records-shaped entry point — as-is it's dead in production and a second near-duplicate implementation exists next to it, the pattern rule 7 warns against.

### src/oc/jobs/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package docstring + re-export point for the out-of-process job primitive: explains why heavy work must run in a separate process rather than a thread (single-worker uvicorn, one GIL) and exposes SubprocessJob as the one thing new heavy endpoints should reuse.

**Used by:** src/oc/enrich/price_runner.py:40 (from ..jobs import SubprocessJob)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/jobs/subprocess_job.py

**Purpose:** Generic supervised-child-process primitive: spawns `python -m oc <args>`, drains its stdout on a daemon reader thread (so a chatty child never blocks and EOF is always detected even with nobody polling), and fires on_exit exactly once when the child's stdout closes. The one place any future heavy endpoint gets "run out-of-process" for free.

**Used by:** src/oc/enrich/price_runner.py:40,79,458 (SubprocessJob spawns/supervises the `_sweep-job` child)

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 79): A TimeoutExpired on the post-EOF proc.wait() is silently swallowed and on_exit still fires -- _read_loop()'s finally block (lines 79-93) treats stdout closing as "the child exited" and always fires on_exit exactly once, even if the subsequent `proc.wait(timeout=5.0)` raises subprocess.TimeoutExpired (caught by the broad `except Exception: pass` at lines 85-86) — i.e. even in the rare case a process closes its stdout but doesn't actually terminate within 5s (e.g. it forked/kept a grandchild holding the write end of the pipe). alive()/returncode would still report the process as (technically) not-yet-exited while on_exit has already run gate/lock cleanup. Very low likelihood for this project's actual child (a straightforward `python -m oc _sweep-job` with no forking), flagged for completeness only.

### src/oc/notify/__init__.py
