### src/oc/notify/null.py

**Purpose:** No-op Notifier backend (@register_notifier("null")): the fallback used off-Windows or whenever the `toasted` library isn't installed, so a toast-node fire is always safe to call regardless of platform.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py:67,112,174 (registered; build_notifier falls back to it); src/oc/web/deps.py:34-38 (build_notifier(...) used by the FastAPI dependency for toast-node triggers); src/oc/engine.py:74 (Engine.build() wires the configured notifier, default settings likely point here off-Windows)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/notify/windows_toast.py

**Purpose:** Windows OS-toast Notifier backend (@register_notifier("windows")) built on the `toasted` WinRT wrapper: registers an AppUserModelID once per source label, builds the toast XML (styled text blocks, hero/inline/logo images) via `toasted`, then posts it directly with the raw winsdk ToastNotifier — deliberately bypassing toasted's own Toast.show() coroutine (which awaits an activation/dismissal callback that may never arrive from an unpackaged process and would otherwise hang the whole notify queue). The module-load `find_spec` gate (not a real import) keeps the WinRT DLLs from loading before onnxruntime's native extension does, which would otherwise break every OCR read.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py:68,112,174 (registered; discovered only when `toasted` is installed on Windows); src/oc/web/deps.py:35-38 / src/oc/engine.py:74 (selected by settings.notifier.name == "windows"); src/oc/collect/triggers.py (fires a Notifier for a toast-node target)

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 175): Muting a toast depends on a private/undocumented attribute of the third-party `toasted` library -- `toast._xml_mute_sound = spec.muted` (line 175) reaches into an attribute of toasted.Toast that isn't part of its documented public API, purely so its to_xml_string() emits `<audio silent=...>` (per the adjacent comment). If a future `toasted` release renames/removes that attribute, muted toasts would silently stop being muted with no error at all — the entire _show() body is wrapped in a broad `except Exception: pass` (deliberately, so a toast bug can never crash a trigger fire), so this kind of drift would be invisible. Documented and clearly a deliberate tradeoff, flagged only for awareness on a `toasted` version bump.

**Purpose:** Renders a ToastImageDef (solid/gradient background + positioned, coloured, aligned, wrapped-or-ellipsized text lines with {{token}} interpolation) to PNG bytes via PIL, for use as a toast's hero/inline image — the only way to get styled, dynamic-data text into a Windows toast popup, since the native toast XML text elements can't be positioned/coloured/backgrounded like this.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/triggers.py:464,485,493 (_render_toast_images renders hero/inline images before a live fire, via render_to_file); src/oc/web/routes/toasts.py:58,61 (render_png for the teach-UI live preview)

### src/oc/numbers/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package composition/re-export point for the fast floating-number pipeline (detect -> track -> process), documented in its own docstring as paired with oc.capture.fastcap for reading transient combat-damage/score-popup numbers as fast as possible; explicitly "Not wired into the collector."

**DEAD CODE CLAIM (unverified):** No import of oc.numbers anywhere outside the package itself — not from cli/, web/, collect/, or any test file (verified by repo-wide grep). This is not orphaned/leftover code, though: the package's own docstring explicitly states it is standalone tooling ("Pair it with oc.capture.fastcap... Not wired into the collector"), consistent with the project's own notes describing this as a deliberately separate damage/bar-number tool built alongside (not yet integrated into) the main collection pipeline. Flagged as unreachable-from-shipped-app for visibility, not as a candidate for deletion.

- **[INFO] dead-code**: Whole numbers/ package is currently unreferenced by any shipped entry point -- Confirmed via repo-wide search: no cli subcommand, web route, collect pipeline, or test file imports oc.numbers. It exists purely as a ready-to-use library for a not-yet-wired feature (manual/future combat-number reading), matching its own docstring's stated scope.

### src/oc/numbers/detect.py

**Purpose:** OCR-free blob detector (NumberDetector/DetectConfig/Blob): thresholds a frame (otsu/fixed/adaptive, or one/several BGR colour ranges), horizontally closes digits of one number into a single blob, then connected-components + size/aspect/fill gating to produce candidate number boxes cheaply, before the expensive OCR step runs on just those crops.

**DEAD CODE CLAIM (unverified):** Same as the package: no caller outside oc.numbers itself; deliberately standalone tooling per its own module docstring, not orphaned.

- No issues found.

### src/oc/numbers/process.py

**Purpose:** Orchestrates detect -> track -> OCR into deduplicated ReadNumber emissions: OCRs each track exactly once when it becomes "ripe" (enough matched frames), batches OCR across all ripe tracks in a frame via read_lines, parses/validates the numeric value (format guard against stray-glyph/CJK OCR junk, k/m/b suffix expansion, min/max bounds), and applies a spatial+temporal dedup guard against a re-tracked flicker double-emitting the same number.

**DEAD CODE CLAIM (unverified):** Same as the package: no external caller; standalone tooling per its own docstring.

- No issues found.

### src/oc/numbers/track.py

**Purpose:** Pure-Python (no cv2) greedy nearest-prediction motion tracker (NumberTracker/Track/TrackConfig): links a frame's blobs to existing tracks by predicted position + EMA velocity, ages/finalizes unmatched tracks after max_misses, and enforces the "read a track's value exactly once over its life" contract the processor relies on for the no-double-count guarantee.

**DEAD CODE CLAIM (unverified):** Same as the package: no external caller; standalone tooling per its own docstring, fully unit-testable (pure Python/numpy) even though nothing currently exercises it via tests either.

- No issues found.


_Module notes: Zero hardcoded game-specific logic found anywhere in this module (a couple of docstrings use "warframe.market pricing" purely as the illustrative example CLAUDE.md itself sanctions — no executable branch tests for a game/item name). No mutable-default-argument bugs (dataclasses correctly use field(default_factory=...) throughout). No bare `except:` clauses; broad `except Exception` catches are consistently narrow-purpose and commented `# noqa: BLE001` as deliberate best-effort/never-crash boundaries (network fetch, toast render/post, cache I/O) rather than bugs hiding real errors — except for the one gap flagged below in price_runner.start_sweep. The numbers/ package is fully self-contained, well-tested-by-design pure logic (detect/track pure Python+numpy, no cv2 import at module load) but has zero call sites anywhere in the shipped app (cli/web/collect/tests) — its own docstring says "Not wired into the collector," matching the user's own memory note describing it as standalone tooling paired manually with capture/fastcap.py, so it reads as deliberate, not orphaned. The two substantive findings worth a maintainer's attention are (1) a real gate/file-lock leak in enrich/price_runner.py's start_sweep when _write_job_file raises before the try/except that owns cleanup, and (2) a memoization-vs-cycle-detection ordering bug in enrich/subset.py's compute_view_rows that can silently return stale/wrong rows for a subset reached both cyclically and non-cyclically in the same query (narrow trigger condition, requires an actually-cyclic subset graph)._

## Module: p19-source  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/source/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package docstring for the file-source producer (locate -> parse -> write to dataset). Its existence as an __init__.py is what makes `oc.source` importable as a package at all; every submodule (extract/locate/reader/runner/parsers) depends on the package existing. Deleting it would break every `from oc.source... import ...` in the repo.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/triggers.py (imports oc.source.runner); src/oc/web/routes/sources.py; src/oc/web/source_sched.py; tests/test_sources.py; tests/test_sources_routes.py

- No issues found.

### src/oc/source/extract.py

**Purpose:** Declarative extraction primitives shared by every parser backend: line-match clauses (line_matches), per-line field pulls (after/between/column/whole), document dotted-path lookup (dig/path_parts), the required-field validation + type cast (cell/finalize/cast), record assembly for line-oriented and whole-document parsers (line_record/doc_record), and the DISMISSED/SOURCE_LINE markers the runner and preview route key off of. If deleted, every parser in oc/source/parsers/* loses its extraction logic and the runner loses the DISMISSED-row filter.

**Used by:** src/oc/source/parsers/ini.py (doc_record, path_fields); src/oc/source/parsers/json.py (dig, doc_record, leaf_paths, path_fields); src/oc/source/parsers/xml.py (doc_record, path_fields); src/oc/source/parsers/yaml.py (dig, doc_record, leaf_paths, path_fields); src/oc/source/parsers/log_lines.py (cast, line_matches, line_record); src/oc/source/runner.py (DISMISSED, line 18/48); src/oc/web/routes/sources.py (DISMISSED, SOURCE_LINE, line 19/149-156); tests/test_sources.py (dig, extract_line_field, line_matches)

- **[MEDIUM] correctness** (line 200): path_parts() crashes with an unhandled ValueError on a dotted path segment with multiple leading dashes, reachable from the live '/preview' route with no exception guard. -- path_parts checks `seg.lstrip("-").isdigit()` but then does `int(seg)` on the ORIGINAL (unstripped) segment. A segment like "--5" or "---7" passes the `.lstrip("-").isdigit()` test (lstrip removes ALL leading dashes before checking) but `int("--5")` raises ValueError: invalid literal for int() with base 10: '--5'. Verified by direct execution: path_parts('a.--5.b') raises. Reachable any time a user types such a path into a json/yaml SourceField.path box while authoring a source (e.g. items.--1.qty, a plausible fat-finger while editing an array index), and POST /{game}/preview (src/oc/web/routes/sources.py preview()) calls parser.parse(...)/parser.parse_indexed(...) with NO try/except around it -> dig() -> path_parts() -> unhandled 500 instead of the graceful 'invalid path, no rows' the preview UI expects. Other call sites (source_sched.py _watch_tick, routes/sources.py read_now's background thread) DO wrap read_source in broad try/except so they degrade silently instead of crashing, but the preview route does not.

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 99): cell()/cast() number-sniff a stringified non-scalar value (a dict/list dig() returned because the path pointed at a container, not a leaf) and can silently pull an unrelated digit out of its repr instead of treating the field as missing. -- If a json/yaml SourceField.path resolves to a nested dict/list (user pointed the path at an object instead of a leaf), cell() falls through to cast(raw, "number") for an optional numeric field, which does _NUM.search(str(value)) on the Python repr of the dict/list (e.g. str({'level': 3}) == "{'level': 3}") and would happily extract '3' as the field's value even though that's an artifact of repr formatting, not intended data. A required field would instead be dismissed via _STRICT_NUM.match failing, which is correct; only the optional-field lenient path has this hole. Narrow (requires a misconfigured path).

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 231): doc_record() silently ignores any field whose method isn't 'path' (or whose path is empty) rather than dismissing it, so a misconfigured REQUIRED field on a document-format source never drops the row. -- `for f in fields or []: if getattr(f, "method", "") != "path" or not getattr(f, "path", ""): continue` skips the field entirely without ever calling cell(), so ok=False/dismissal never triggers for it. In practice mitigated: the web UI (model.js addSourceField) always defaults method: isDoc ? "path" : "after" when a field is added to a document-format source, so a normally-authored profile won't hit this. Flagged as info since the Python layer itself doesn't enforce the contract its own docstring implies.

- **[INFO] dead_code** (line 164): extract_line_field()/finalize() are not called from any production code path — only from tests. -- extract_line_field (and the finalize helper it uses) is only referenced by tests/test_sources.py. All real parsing goes through line_record/_extract_raw directly. The docstring is explicit that this is intentional ('keeps the simple cast contract callers/tests rely on'), so not treated as a defect, just noted for the used_by audit.

### src/oc/source/locate.py

**Purpose:** Finds the concrete file a file-source producer should read: resolves an explicit path, or scans generic OS locations (%LOCALAPPDATA%, Documents/My Games, Saved Games, Steam library folders parsed from libraryfolders.vdf, ProgramData) for a profile-supplied filename glob. Keeps all game-specific data (the glob) in profile YAML while the scanned roots stay generic, per the project's zero-game-knowledge-in-Python rule.

**Used by:** src/oc/source/runner.py (resolve_path, line 19/28); src/oc/web/routes/sources.py (expand, find_candidates, resolve_path); src/oc/web/source_sched.py (resolve_path); tests/test_sources.py (find_candidates, resolve_path); tests/test_sources_routes.py (monkeypatches oc.source.locate.default_roots)

- **[INFO] robustness**: _steam_libraries()/find_candidates() are best-effort scans (silently skip on any OSError/ImportError); no logic bug was found, only noting the design is deliberately lossy rather than a defect. -- Confirmed no game-specific names are hardcoded (the 'Warframe EE.log/EE.cfg' mentions are docstring examples only, not branches). Path matching lowercases both the glob and filenames (fnmatch.fnmatch(fn.lower(), glob) with glob = filename_glob.lower()), consistent with the project's Windows-case-insensitivity convention. Depth pruning (dirnames[:] = [] at _MAX_DEPTH) and the triple-nested limit break are correct (each break level is followed by a limit check that also breaks its enclosing loop). No bug found; recorded as info per instructions to state findings even when none are notable.

### src/oc/source/reader.py

**Purpose:** Process-wide cache of file reads keyed by (path, mtime, size) so multiple file-source nodes pointed at the same file share one disk read; also implements windowed ('last_lines') and legacy incremental ('tail=True', per-caller consumed-offset) reads plus a line-aware variant that reports the absolute file line number of a chunk's first line.

**Used by:** src/oc/source/runner.py (default_reader, read/read_lined, lines 20/31/40/44); src/oc/web/routes/sources.py (default_reader); src/oc/web/source_sched.py (default_reader, line 24-25/35); tests/test_sources.py (SourceReader, tail=True path)

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 46): Cache invalidation keys on (mtime_ns, size); a same-tick, same-size edit on a coarse-resolution filesystem could theoretically be missed and serve stale cached text. -- _load() treats the cache as valid whenever (cached[0], cached[1]) == sig where sig = (st.st_mtime_ns, st.st_size). On filesystems/timers with coarse mtime resolution, an edit that happens to preserve file size within the same mtime tick would not be detected, serving stale content until the next real change. Standard mtime-cache caveat rather than a concrete bug this codebase is likely to hit (NTFS mtime resolution is ~100ns), kept low.

- **[INFO] dead_code** (line 57): The tail=True incremental (consumed-offset) mode is only exercised by tests; every production caller (runner.py, source_sched.py) uses last_lines-based windowed reads instead and never passes tail=True. -- runner.py's read_source calls reader.read(path, last_lines=last_lines, key=source.id) and reader.read_lined(path, last_lines=last_lines, key=source.id) without ever setting tail=True; the reader's own docstring for the tail parameter explicitly labels it '(legacy)'. Not a bug — the _consumed/_lines bookkeeping this mode maintains is dead weight in production but kept for the tested API/back-compat, exactly as documented.

### src/oc/source/runner.py

**Purpose:** The single funnel that reads one file-source: locate its file, parse it via the registered format backend, drop DISMISSED rows, and bulk-write survivors to the dataset store (one transaction, one change-bus announce) — used identically by the manual 'read now' route, the on_change watcher daemon, and trigger-fired reads, so behaviour can't drift between callers.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/sources.py (read_source, line 22/223); src/oc/web/source_sched.py (read_source, line 26/62); src/oc/collect/triggers.py (imports read_source inside read_source_target, line 526/528); tests/test_sources.py (read_source)

- **[LOW] dead_code** (line 80): The SourceRunner class (and its read/read_id methods) has no callers anywhere in the repo — every real caller uses the module-level read_source() function directly instead. -- Grepped the whole repo (src + tests) for SourceRunner and .read_id( — the only occurrence of the class name is its own definition. web/routes/sources.py, web/source_sched.py, and collect/triggers.py all call read_source(...) directly rather than instantiating SourceRunner. The docstring frames it as 'kept symmetric with TriggerRunner', but unlike TriggerRunner (constructed in 6+ places), this wrapper was never wired up anywhere. Harmless (small, self-contained), but genuinely dead per the used_by audit.

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 23): No exception handling around the parse/record_many path in read_source() itself — relies entirely on callers to guard against a bad parse (e.g. a malformed dotted path, see extract.py finding) crashing. -- read_source calls parser.parse_indexed(...) with no try/except. Two of its three call sites (source_sched.py's watcher, routes/sources.py's read_now background thread) wrap the call in broad except Exception, so they degrade silently; this is fine and by design, but is the reason the /preview route's lack of a guard (see extract.py finding) stands out as the one path that surfaces the crash to a user.

### src/oc/source/parsers/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package docstring only, explaining the registered-parser-per-format convention (@register_parser, listed in registry._IMPL_MODULES). Its existence makes oc.source.parsers an importable package so oc.source.parsers.log_lines etc. resolve for importlib-based registration.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES imports oc.source.parsers.log_lines/ini/json/xml/yaml, lines 62-66)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/source/parsers/ini.py

**Purpose:** Registers the 'ini' SourceParser backend for INI/cfg game config files (e.g. Warframe's EE.cfg, mentioned only as a docstring example). Whole-document parser: reads Section.Key paths via configparser (case-preserved, tolerant of duplicate keys) into one record, and offers a suggest() that proposes a path field per Section.Key found.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES, line 63; selected at runtime via build_parser('ini')); src/oc/web/routes/sources.py (build_parser); tests/test_sources.py (build_parser('ini'))

- No issues found.

### src/oc/source/parsers/json.py

**Purpose:** Registers the 'json' SourceParser backend. Whole-document parser: json.loads + dotted-path dig() into one record, plus a suggest() that enumerates scalar leaves as candidate fields.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES, line 64); src/oc/web/routes/sources.py (build_parser); tests/test_sources.py (build_parser('json'))

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 22): Inherits extract.py's path_parts() crash on malformed numeric-looking path segments (e.g. 'a.--5.b') since parse()/suggest() call dig() with no exception guard of their own. -- Neither parse() nor suggest() wraps dig()/leaf_paths() in a try/except; only json.loads itself is guarded (except (ValueError, TypeError)). See the primary finding recorded against src/oc/source/extract.py (path_parts, line 200) — this file is one of the two call sites (with yaml.py) that actually reach it via a user-supplied field.path.

### src/oc/source/parsers/log_lines.py

**Purpose:** Registers the 'log_lines' SourceParser backend (stream=True) for append-only text logs (e.g. Warframe's EE.log): filters lines via match clauses, extracts one record per kept line, and reports each record's true 1-based source line number so tailed reads still carry correct file positions. suggest() proposes whitespace-column fields off the widest matched line.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES, line 62); src/oc/source/runner.py (relies on parser.stream=True to enable tail_lines/line_position windowing); src/oc/web/routes/sources.py (build_parser, stream-aware preview logic); tests/test_sources.py (build_parser('log_lines'))

- No issues found.

### src/oc/source/parsers/xml.py

**Purpose:** Registers the 'xml' SourceParser backend. Whole-document parser: ElementTree, root/child/@attr-style dotted-slash paths (root tag prefix optional) into one record, plus a suggest() that walks the tree collecting attributes and leaf text as candidate paths.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES, line 65); src/oc/web/routes/sources.py (build_parser); tests/test_sources.py (build_parser('xml'))

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 25): _xml_get() has no exception guard around ElementTree's find(), so a malformed path segment (e.g. an unbalanced XPath-like predicate such as 'item[') raises an unhandled exception instead of returning None. -- Verified: root.find('item[') raises TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not callable (an ElementTree internal quirk when its XPath-lite parser chokes on unbalanced brackets). _xml_get/parse()/suggest() catch ET.ParseError for the document itself but nothing around the per-field node.find(p) calls, so a user typing a stray '[' in a path field while live-previewing (POST /{game}/preview has no guard either, see extract.py's finding for the same class of issue) would get a 500 instead of a graceful 'no value'. Narrow — requires literal bracket syntax in a path — kept low.

### src/oc/source/parsers/yaml.py

**Purpose:** Registers the 'yaml' SourceParser backend. Whole-document parser: yaml.safe_load + dotted-path dig() into one record, plus suggest() via leaf_paths(), identical shape to json.py but for YAML config files.

**Used by:** src/oc/registry.py (_IMPL_MODULES, line 66); src/oc/web/routes/sources.py (build_parser); tests/test_sources.py (build_parser('yaml'))

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 22): Same shared path_parts() crash as json.py (see src/oc/source/extract.py finding) — parse()/suggest() call dig() with no guard beyond yaml.YAMLError around the initial safe_load. -- Identical structure to json.py: except yaml.YAMLError only protects the parse step, not the subsequent dig(data, f.path) calls, so a malformed numeric-looking path segment reaches extract.py's path_parts() bug unguarded.


_Module notes: Module is clean overall: no hardcoded game knowledge (only docstring examples like 'Warframe EE.log'), no mutable default arguments, no bare excepts, all five parser backends properly self-register via registry._IMPL_MODULES, and the KeyMap/dataset-key selection in runner.py correctly respects a dataset's no-dedup override over a source's pinned key (matches the project's known read/write-key-mismatch bug class, and does NOT reproduce it). The one verified, reproducible bug is extract.py's path_parts() crashing on a dotted path segment with multiple leading dashes (e.g. 'a.--5.b'), which is reachable unguarded from the live preview route. Also flagged: the SourceRunner wrapper class in runner.py is entirely unused (real callers use the read_source function directly), and a couple of low/info robustness notes in xml.py and extract.py's cell()/doc_record(). No hard-rule (CLAUDE.md) violations found in this module._

## Module: p20-cli  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/cli/__init__.py

**Purpose:** The CLI's argparse wiring: build_parser() creates the top-level `data-occultist`/`occ` parser, registers every subcommand module's parser via each module's register(sub), and main() parses argv then dispatches to args.func(args). Deleting it would remove the console-script/`python -m oc` entry point entirely.

**Used by:** pyproject.toml: `data-occultist = "oc.cli:main"` and `occ = "oc.cli:main"` console-script entry points; src/oc/__main__.py: `from oc.cli import main` (enables `python -m oc ...`); src/oc/jobs/subprocess_job.py: spawns `[sys.executable, "-m", "oc", *args]`, which resolves through this module; src/oc/enrich/price_runner.py: start_sweep() builds argv beginning with "_sweep-job", dispatched through build_parser()

- **[INFO] style** (line 36): `return args.func(args) or 0` silently coerces any falsy non-None return (e.g. an accidental `False`) to 0 -- Every current subcommand's run() explicitly returns an int (0/1), so this is not observed today; noting only because a future subcommand returning `None` implicitly (falling off the end of a function) would silently look like success. Not a real bug given current call sites.

### src/oc/cli/app.py

**Purpose:** Implements `data-occultist app`: the terminal-runnable twin of the install.ps1 desktop shortcut. Delegates straight to desktop_main.run_release(), which boots the server in-process, opens the native window, and tears the server down when the window closes. Removing it would remove the one-shot "run everything, clean up on exit" release-mode entry point from the CLI (the .ps1 shortcut path would still work since it calls desktop_main directly).

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/__init__.py: `app_cmd.register(sub)`; invoked by users as `data-occultist app` / `occ app`

- No issues found.

### src/oc/cli/bench.py

**Purpose:** Implements `data-occultist bench <game>`: hammers grab_window() as fast as possible for N seconds (no OCR/classify/save) and reports grabs/s and, for backends exposing frame_seq (WGC), distinct frames/s. Used to A/B capture backends (printwindow vs wgc) without editing settings.yaml. Deleting it removes the only CLI capture-throughput diagnostic.

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/__init__.py: `bench_cmd.register(sub)`; invoked by users as `data-occultist bench <game> [--capture ...]`

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 53): The entire benchmark loop (warmup, frame_seq-property detection, grab loop, elapsed/rate math, size>3 empty-frame filter) is copy-pasted almost verbatim into src/oc/web/routes/bench.py instead of being extracted into one shared primitive. -- web/routes/bench.py's own docstring says it "Drives the same code path the CLI bench command does", yet there is no shared module (bench_core/run_benchmark/etc.) - grep for such a helper across src/oc turns up nothing. The two copies have already started to diverge exactly as CLAUDE.md hard rule 7 warns: this file detects a frame_seq counter via `seq_of = getattr(type(capture), "frame_seq", None)` (truthy for ANY non-None class attribute) while the web route uses the stricter `isinstance(getattr(type(backend), "frame_seq", None), property)`; this file also has no bounds validation on --seconds/--warmup while the web route's Query(..., ge=..., le=...) does. Per rule 7, the fix is to extract the shared loop (warmup, has_seq, grab loop, rate computation) into one parameterized function that both the CLI command and the FastAPI route call. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[LOW] input-validation** (line 35): --seconds and --warmup accept any float (including 0 or negative) with no bounds check, unlike the web route's Query(ge=..., le=...). -- A negative --seconds makes `end = t0 + args.seconds` less than t0, so the timing loop runs zero iterations; the command then just prints 0 grabs/s rather than erroring. Not a crash and not data-corrupting, just a silently useless invocation instead of a clear argparse error.

### src/oc/cli/capture.py

**Purpose:** Implements `data-occultist capture <game> --out f.png`: locates the game window once, grabs a single frame, and writes it as a PNG via cv2.imwrite. This is the one-shot screenshot tool used for authoring profile regions offline and for quick manual checks. Deleting it removes that single-capture entry point (the web UI's live preview capture endpoint is a separate code path in web/routes/preview.py).

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/__init__.py: `capture_cmd.register(sub)`; invoked by users as `data-occultist capture <game> --out <path>`

- No issues found.

### src/oc/cli/collect.py

**Purpose:** Implements `data-occultist collect <game> [--once] [--interval]`: builds the Engine + Collector and runs the capture/OCR/save loop (or a single tick), printing each TickResult. Also wires an OnChangeFirer onto the dataset change bus so any profile-defined on_change triggers (price sweeps, toasts, etc.) fire when this process's writes change a dataset. Deleting it removes the only long-running unattended-collection entry point outside the web UI's collector supervisor.

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/__init__.py: `collect_cmd.register(sub)`; invoked by users as `data-occultist collect <game>`

- **[INFO] style** (line 43): The subscribe(OnChangeFirer(...)) registration is never unsubscribed. -- For a single-shot CLI process this is harmless (process exits and the subscriber list is discarded), but it's worth noting the pattern relies entirely on process lifetime rather than an explicit teardown - fine here, would not be fine if this function were ever called more than once in a long-lived process.

### src/oc/cli/detect.py

**Purpose:** Implements `data-occultist detect`: lists every profile on disk and reports whether its process/window is currently running (with title + client-rect if so). Pure diagnostic command used to sanity-check that a profile's `detect` config actually finds the game. Deleting it removes the only quick "is my profile wired up" check.

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/__init__.py: `detect_cmd.register(sub)`; invoked by users as `data-occultist detect` / `occ detect`

- No issues found.

### src/oc/cli/profiles.py

**Purpose:** Implements `data-occultist profiles`: lists the names of every game profile YAML found in the configured profiles dir. A minimal listing command distinct from `detect` (which also probes whether each is running). Deleting it removes that plain listing shortcut (the web UI has its own profile listing via routes/profiles.py).

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/__init__.py: `profiles_cmd.register(sub)`; invoked by users as `data-occultist profiles`

- No issues found.

### src/oc/cli/serve.py

**Purpose:** Implements `data-occultist serve [--port] [--reload]`: launches the FastAPI web UI via uvicorn.run("oc.web.app:app", ...). Also propagates --verbose-access into an env var so the app's access-log filter (installed in its startup lifespan) knows whether to show static-asset GET spam, including across the --reload worker subprocess. Deleting it removes the primary way to start the teaching UI / server from the terminal.

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/__init__.py: `serve_cmd.register(sub)`; invoked by users as `data-occultist serve` / `occ serve`; src/oc/web/app.py:137-141 reads OCC_VERBOSE_ACCESS env var this module sets, confirming the hand-off

- No issues found.

### src/oc/cli/sweep_job.py

**Purpose:** Implements the hidden `oc _sweep-job` subcommand: the child-process half of the price/producer sweep supervisor. Reads a hand-off job file (resolved profile + ProducerDef + optional item list) written by price_runner.start_sweep(), runs build_producer(node.type).run(ctx) to actually fetch/write, publishes throttled progress to the cross-process status sidecar, polls a cross-process cancel flag, records timing stats, and prints one OCC_SWEEP_SUMMARY <json> line for the parent to parse on reap. Running this heavy work in its own process keeps re-encoding/scanning bursts off the single-worker server's event loop/GIL. Deleting it would break every price-sweep trigger and the web UI's price-sweep button (both go through this child process).

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/__init__.py: `sweep_job_cmd.register(sub)`; src/oc/enrich/price_runner.py:414-472 start_sweep() builds args=["_sweep-job", game, "--dataset", ..., "--job-file", ..., "--timeout", ..., "--limit", ..., "--workers", ...] and spawns it via SubprocessJob (src/oc/jobs/subprocess_job.py), which execs `python -m oc <args>` - all flags/positional args match this file's argparse definitions exactly; src/oc/collect/triggers.py:416 calls start_sweep(...) (indirect trigger path); src/oc/web/routes/prices.py:40 and src/oc/web/routes/triggers.py:73 call start_sweep(...) (web UI trigger path)

- **[INFO] correctness**: No incorrectness found; hand-off contract (job-file keys, ProducerCtx fields, SweepState fields, cancel-file semantics) was cross-checked against price_runner.py and interfaces.ProducerCtx and matches exactly. -- doc["node"] is accessed with plain indexing (would KeyError if missing) but price_runner._write_job_file() always writes profile/node/items, so the job file this process ever reads is always well-formed; not reachable as a real failure. Broad except Exception: pass around stats_store.flush_all() and the cancel/job-file unlink cleanup are deliberate best-effort teardown (documented via noqa: BLE001), consistent with project conventions for teardown code that must not mask the summary line being printed.

### src/oc/cli/view.py

**Purpose:** Implements `data-occultist view`: opens a native desktop window pointed at an ALREADY-RUNNING server (default http://127.0.0.1:8000), without starting or owning that server's lifecycle. Distinct from `app`, which owns and kills its own server. Deleting it removes the lightweight "just open a window onto dev-server" workflow used during development.

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/__init__.py: `view_cmd.register(sub)`; invoked by users as `data-occultist view` (typically against a `serve --reload` instance during development)

- No issues found.


_Module notes: No hardcoded game/item names or window-title logic found anywhere in this module (satisfies the zero-game-knowledge rule) - every file is purely orchestration (argparse wiring + delegating to Engine/profile/collector/producer machinery). No mutable default arguments, no bare `except:`, no locale-default date/time formatting, no blocking dialogs (none of these files are JS/UI), and no .ps1 files in this set. All ten files are reachable (none dead): cli/__init__.py is the console-script + `python -m oc` entry point; every other file registers itself as a subcommand from cli/__init__.py's build_parser() and several are additionally invoked programmatically as subprocess argv (sweep_job.py from price_runner.start_sweep, app.py/view.py as user-facing desktop launchers). The one substantive finding is a hard-rule-7 (no-copy-paste) violation: src/oc/cli/bench.py's benchmark loop is duplicated near-verbatim in src/oc/web/routes/bench.py (outside this module's file list, so not itself audited here) with no shared primitive, and the two copies have already diverged in their frame_seq-detection check and bounds validation - flagged on bench.py since that's the file in scope._

## Module: p21-web-core  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package marker + one-line docstring for the oc.web package. Nothing else lives here; its only job is to make `oc.web` importable and give the package a description.

**Used by:** Implicitly by every `from oc.web...` / `from .web...` import across the codebase (app.py, cli/serve.py, desktop_main.py, all tests importing oc.web.*).

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/app.py

**Purpose:** The FastAPI application factory: builds `create_app()` (localhost-only middleware, global exception handler, mounts every route router, serves the static SPA at `/`) and the `lifespan` context manager that wires up all background subsystems (warmup thread, shutdown-signal chaining, access-log filtering, stray-session cleanup, trigger/source schedulers, GPU watchdog, dataset-change subscribers for on_change triggers/db backup/dictionary feeds). Deleting it removes the entire web UI — there would be no ASGI app for uvicorn to serve.

**Used by:** uvicorn.Config/uvicorn.run("oc.web.app:app", ...) in src/oc/cli/serve.py and src/oc/web/desktop.py:44; tests/test_flow_routes.py:20 and tests/test_sources_routes.py:32 (`from oc.web.app import create_app`); scripts/debug.ps1 and scripts/start_server.ps1 (`uvicorn oc.web.app:app`)

- **[INFO] maintainability** (line 309): Router registration is a manually-kept-in-sync two-list pattern (import block + include_router calls) -- The routers imported at lines 16-40 and the app.include_router(...) calls at lines 309-330 are two separate lists that must be kept 1:1 by hand. Currently they match (23/23), but adding a new route module to only one list would silently fail to mount it with no error — worth a comment or a loop-driven registration if this list keeps growing, but not a present bug.

### src/oc/web/captures_store.py

**Purpose:** Filesystem persistence for everything the teaching UI stashes: window captures (captures/<game>/*.jpg, plus a `live/` subfolder), frozen item cutouts (items/), taught glyph crops (glyphs/), and the window->stash binding map (_bindings.json). Deleting it removes the ability to reload a past capture for box tuning, freeze item templates, teach glyphs, or remember which stash a window is bound to — the whole 'teach against a stored screenshot' workflow breaks.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/capture.py (save/listing/stats/clear/path_for/get_bindings/as_list/set_binding/set_bindings/save_cutout/cutout_path/save_glyph/glyph_path — every capture/binding endpoint); src/oc/web/routes/preview.py (path_for, get_bindings, first, cutout_loader, glyph_loader, cutout_path); src/oc/collect/collector.py (cutout_loader, glyph_loader for item-template/glyph matching); src/oc/collect/live.py (save, LIVE — stashes live-mode frames); src/oc/collect/precapture.py (cutout_loader, glyph_loader); scripts/bench_preprocess.py (path_for, cutout_loader, get_bindings)

- **[MEDIUM] path-traversal** (line 76): path_for/cutout_path/glyph_path's traversal guard misses a Windows drive-relative filename, letting the resolved path escape captures_dir -- Each guard only rejects `name` containing `/`, `\`, or `..`. A name of the form `C:evil.png` (a drive-relative Windows path — no root slash, so `os.path.isabs`/`in name` slash checks don't catch it) contains none of those. Joining it onto the base with pathlib silently discards every preceding component: verified directly — `Path('captures')/'game'/'C:evil.png'` evaluates to `Path('C:evil.png')`, not a path under `captures/game/`. Since `game`/`name`/`capture` are FastAPI path params taken straight from the URL in routes/capture.py (e.g. `GET /captures/{game}/{name}` at capture.py:127-130) and routes/preview.py, a request for `/captures/anygame/C:secrets.png` (or any file reachable relative to the process's per-drive current directory) is served instead of a 404, directly contradicting the function's own docstring/comment 'Guard against traversal: only a bare filename in the game's folder.'

- **[MEDIUM] reliability** (line 156): _bindings.json is read with no corruption handling and written non-atomically, unlike the sibling OcrCache in the same package -- get_bindings() does `json.loads(p.read_text(...))` with no try/except; a truncated/corrupt file (e.g. process killed mid-write, or disk full) raises JSONDecodeError straight out of every route that touches bindings (capture.py's binding endpoints, preview.py's window-binding lookup) until someone manually deletes the file. _write_bindings() also writes directly via `p.write_text(...)` rather than the temp-file + os.replace atomic-swap pattern OcrCache.save() already uses a few files over in this same module (ocr_cache.py:65-82) specifically to survive a crash mid-write. The corruption-prone write plus the un-guarded read compound into a real, if low-probability, permanent-breakage scenario.

- **[LOW] duplication** (line 77): The traversal guard (`"/" in name or "\\" in name or ".." in name`) is copy-pasted verbatim in path_for, cutout_path, and glyph_path -- All three currently agree, but any future fix (e.g. patching the drive-letter gap above) has to be applied in three places by hand; a natural follow-up to extracting a single `_safe_name(name) -> bool` helper.

- **[LOW] concurrency** (line 185): set_binding/set_bindings do a read-modify-write on _bindings.json with no lock -- Two concurrent requests (e.g. two browser tabs) rebinding different windows for the same game race: both read the same on-disk dict, and whichever writes last wins, silently dropping the other's change. Low impact for a single local user, but worth noting given ocr_cache.py in the same module already uses a threading.Lock for exactly this class of hazard.

### src/oc/web/deps.py

**Purpose:** Process-wide singleton accessors (`lru_cache`-memoized) for Settings, Engine, WindowLocator, the OS-notifier, and per-game OcrCache instances — the one place the rest of the web layer gets these expensive-to-build objects without re-constructing them per request.

**Used by:** Nearly every route module (activity, bench, capture, capture_backend, preview, ocr, pretty, triggers, live, precapture, flow, profiles, prices, capture, sources, video, dbschema, dbbackup, dictionaries) imports get_settings/get_engine/get_locator/get_notifier/get_ocr_cache; src/oc/web/app.py (get_settings, get_locator in _warm/lifespan); src/oc/web/gpu_watch.py (get_engine); src/oc/web/source_sched.py, trigger_sched.py (get_notifier)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/desktop.py

**Purpose:** Desktop-mode helpers: run uvicorn on a background thread (`serve_in_thread`), open a native frameless pywebview window pointed at the running app (`open_window`), and the JS-callable `_WindowChrome` bridge (minimize/maximize/close/move-resize) the front-end's custom title bar drives since the window has no OS chrome. Deleting it removes `data-occultist app`/`data-occultist view` desktop mode entirely (falls back to browser-only `serve`).

### src/oc/web/encode.py

Not urgent since frame_to_jpeg's signature takes a Frame specifically, but a natural candidate to generalize into one shared encode-with-quality helper rather than two independent call sites.

### src/oc/web/gpu_watch.py

**Purpose:** Background watchdog that auto-releases the resident GPU OCR session (freeing VRAM back to the game) once no browser tab is connected AND OCR has been idle for a grace period AND no precapture/live worker is mid-session. Deleting it means the OCR model stays GPU-resident forever once loaded, permanently holding VRAM even after the teaching UI is closed.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py:198-199 (`from .gpu_watch import start as _start_gpu_watch` in lifespan); src/oc/web/routes/events.py:75,112 (client_connected/client_disconnected — the one SSE socket per page is the liveness signal); tests/test_gpu_watch.py (frontend_gone_for, _maybe_release, _clients/_last_client monkeypatch targets)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/logfilter.py

**Purpose:** Drops successful (<400) GET access-log lines for static assets/HTML pages from uvicorn's access logger, so a page load's dozens of asset requests don't bury the `/api` calls and errors that actually matter. Deleting it makes the console log noisy on every browser refresh but changes no behavior.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py:140-141 (`from .logfilter import install as _install_access_filter` in lifespan, called on every launch path)

- **[INFO] documentation** (line 8): Docstring says the filter is 'wired into the access logger via uvicorn's log_config (see oc.cli.serve)', but it is actually installed directly by app.py's lifespan, not via a log_config passed from cli/serve.py -- src/oc/cli/serve.py never constructs or passes a `log_config` to uvicorn.run — it just calls uvicorn.run("oc.web.app:app", ...). The actual wiring is app.py's lifespan calling `install()` unconditionally on every launch path (serve, --reload worker, desktop, bare uvicorn), which is a strictly more general mechanism than the docstring describes. No functional impact, just a stale cross-reference.

### src/oc/web/ocr_cache.py

**Purpose:** Per-game persisted cache (data/<game>/ocr_cache.json) of OCR results keyed on an immutable image id + a hash of the OCR-affecting config (window def, fields, engine signature) so reopening the graph doesn't re-run OCR on unchanged stashed images/cutouts every boot. Deleting it means every boot re-OCRs every stashed image and item/glyph cutout from scratch (the exact multi-second cold-boot variance this module exists to eliminate, per the boot-perf memory note).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/deps.py:19-22 (get_ocr_cache, lru_cache'd per game); src/oc/web/routes/preview.py:31-32,44-48 (cache_key, get_ocr_cache used in detect/preview/item-read caching); tests/test_ocr_cache.py (OcrCache, cache_key directly)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/shutdown.py

**Purpose:** The one process-wide 'server is shutting down' signal (sync Event + asyncio Event pair), set from a chained SIGINT/SIGTERM handler and again as a lifespan-shutdown backstop, so every long-lived SSE stream and background scheduler loop notices instantly and self-closes instead of forcing uvicorn's connection-drain to time out. Deleting it would reintroduce a graceful-shutdown timeout on every Ctrl+C/reload (the exact problem it was built to avoid).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (bind_loop, _install_shutdown_signals->signal_shutdown, signal_shutdown at shutdown backstop); src/oc/web/sse.py:23 (is_shutting_down, wait_shutdown — the shared SSE loop primitive); src/oc/web/gpu_watch.py:85 (is_shutting_down, wait_shutdown in _run); src/oc/web/source_sched.py:73, trigger_sched.py:66 (is_shutting_down in the scheduler loops)

- **[LOW] test-isolation** (line 19): The shutdown flag is a module-level threading.Event with no reset hook, so once signal_shutdown() has fired it stays fired for the rest of the process -- This is fine for the real server (a process that signals shutdown is about to exit), but there is no `_reset()`/`clear()` exported. If a future test suite constructs `with TestClient(create_app()) as client:` (which triggers the real lifespan, including the unconditional `signal_shutdown()` call in the shutdown block), every subsequent test in the same pytest process that builds another app would see `is_shutting_down()` already True the moment `bind_loop()` reflects the pre-set flag onto the new event — any SSE stream or scheduler loop in that later test would end/never-run immediately. Currently no test in the repo uses the `with TestClient(...)` context-manager form (verified: none use lifespan), so this does not manifest today, but it's a latent trap for whoever writes the first lifespan-covering test.

### src/oc/web/source_sched.py

**Purpose:** Background daemon thread that watches every enabled on_change file source (stat + trailing-throttle read via the shared SourceReader) and fires app-start/capture/live-start/live-stop lifecycle triggers — the file-source equivalent of the collector loop, running purely in the web process since file sources never run inside the OCR capture loop. Deleting it means on_change file sources never get read unless a live collector happens to also be running, and lifecycle triggers (on_app_start etc.) never fire from the teach UI alone.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py:192-193 (`from .source_sched import start as _start_sources` in lifespan); src/oc/web/routes/activity.py:16 (`sources as source_status` — feeds the Activity panel); src/oc/web/routes/live.py:117-118 (`from .. import source_sched; getattr(source_sched, fn_name)(...)` — fire_capture/fire_live_start/fire_live_stop); src/oc/web/routes/precapture.py:86 (`from ..source_sched import fire_capture`)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/sse.py

**Purpose:** The single shared Server-Sent-Events streaming loop (emit ready -> optional backfill -> forward bus items until disconnect/window-expiry/shutdown) that every SSE endpoint in the app is built from, so per-event wire formatting is the only thing a route needs to supply. Deleting it would force every SSE-like endpoint (dataset changes, flow hops, log lines, activity) to hand-roll its own disconnect/shutdown-racing loop — exactly the kind of duplicated-primitive situation the project's hard rule 7 warns against, except here there's only ONE caller because this module already IS the shared primitive.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/events.py:22,142 (sse_response — the one endpoint multiplexing dataset/flow/log/activity events)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/trigger_sched.py

**Purpose:** Background daemon thread that keeps interval triggers firing (and feeds the Activity panel's live countdown + on_change/on_any_change visibility) while only the teach UI is up, without a real collector running. Reuses TriggerRunner so firing logic matches the collector exactly. Confirmed current on-disk content already reflects the on_any_change scheduling-visibility addition from commit e3f2537 (docstring + `schedule()`'s `elif t.kind in ("on_change", "on_any_change")` branch) — no stale state from an earlier audit pass. Deleting it means interval-trigger sweeps and their countdowns stop the moment no real `oc collect` process is running.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py:186-187 (`from .trigger_sched import start as _start_triggers` in lifespan); src/oc/web/routes/activity.py:17,47 (`schedule as trigger_schedule` — feeds the Activity panel's trigger list)

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 43): Any change to any single trigger's config resets _last (and thus the countdown) for every interval trigger in the profile, not just the one that changed -- _runner_for rebuilds the whole TriggerRunner (reseeding all `_last` fire times to 'now') whenever `_sig(profile)` — a repr of every trigger's (id, kind, interval_s, targets, enabled) — differs from the cached signature. Editing trigger B's targets in the UI therefore also resets trigger A's in-flight countdown, even though A's config didn't change. This matches the docstring's own stated behavior ('rebuilt only when the profile's trigger config changes' — config as a whole, not per-trigger), so it's a documented coarse-invalidation tradeoff rather than an unnoticed bug, but worth flagging as a minor UX surprise (a countdown restarts more often than a user might expect).

### src/oc/web/video_source.py

**Purpose:** A singleton VideoSource that plays a recorded screen-capture clip frame-by-frame as a stand-in for the live game window, so live-mode detect/OCR can be exercised end-to-end in the testing harness without the actual game running. Deleting it removes the testing/video-playback panel entirely (live mode would only ever be able to read the real window).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/routes/preview.py (_frame_for reads get_video_source().current_image() when enabled); src/oc/web/routes/video.py (the load/seek/step/enable/close/status endpoints wrapping VideoSource); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/testing.js (the front-end panel driving those endpoints)

- No issues found.


_Module notes: Scope: the process-wide infrastructure layer under src/oc/web (NOT the routes/ or static/ subpackages, which import these modules but were audited elsewhere). Zero game-specific knowledge found in any of the 14 files — all logic is generic (capture stashing, SSE plumbing, trigger scheduling, GPU lifecycle, desktop chrome). No hardcoded item/window/game names, matching the project's core rule. No bare `except:`, no mutable default args, and every `except Exception` carries a `# noqa: BLE001` + a comment explaining the best-effort rationale (consistent with the codebase's documented "best-effort startup/shutdown" convention) — these are not flagged as swallowed-exception bugs since the surrounding docstrings make the intent explicit and each still logs (or is inherently non-critical, e.g. warmup/cache).

Two genuine, concrete findings worth prioritizing, both in captures_store.py:
1. The three traversal guards (`path_for`/`cutout_path`/`glyph_path`) block `/`, `\` and `..` in a user-supplied filename but not a bare drive spec like `C:foo.png` — pathlib silently discards the preceding `captures_dir/game` components when joined with a drive-relative segment (verified empirically: `Path('a')/'b'/'C:evil.png' == Path('C:evil.png')`), so the file actually served resolves outside the intended captures folder, contradicting the function's own "Guard against traversal: only a bare filename in the game's folder" comment. `name`/`capture` in these calls come straight from FastAPI path params in routes/capture.py and routes/preview.py.
2. `get_bindings()` calls `json.loads(p.read_text())` with no try/except (unlike the sibling `OcrCache._load`, which explicitly catches `JSONDecodeError`/`OSError` and self-heals to `{}`), and `_write_bindings()` writes directly with `p.write_text(...)` rather than the atomic temp-file+`os.replace` pattern `OcrCache.save()` already established in the same module. A crash/power-loss mid-write leaves a truncated `_bindings.json` that then throws on every subsequent binding-related request for that game until someone manually deletes the file.

Everything else reviewed line-by-line with no bugs found: the SSE loop (sse.py), shutdown-flag plumbing (shutdown.py), GPU auto-release watchdog (gpu_watch.py), OCR result cache (ocr_cache.py), and both background schedulers (trigger_sched.py — confirmed the on_any_change addition is correctly wired into the current on-disk content per the cache-bust note; source_sched.py) are all sound, race-safe under their own locks, and match their documented contracts. app.py's router wiring was cross-checked (23 imported == 23 included, no orphan). desktop.py's JS-bridge methods (minimize/toggle_maximize/close/set_bounds) were all confirmed called from static/js/titlebar.js and desktop_resize.js. No dead code found anywhere in this module — every public function has at least one real call site, either in-repo Python (including registry-adjacent CLI entry points oc.desktop_main / oc.cli.view / oc.cli.serve) or a front-end JS reference for the desktop chrome bridge._

## Module: p22-routes-a  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/routes/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Package marker docstring for the routes package; makes `oc.web.routes` an importable package so app.py's `from .routes import (...)` works. Deleting it would break every route import.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (imports the oc.web.routes package)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/routes/activity.py

**Purpose:** Aggregates one read-only snapshot of all live background work for a game (price sweeps, precapture worker status, live collector status, enabled triggers with countdown, sources, OCR device state) so the front-end can poll/subscribe to a single endpoint instead of many. build_activity() is also the payload builder reused by the pushed SSE 'activity' channel in events.py.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py:318 app.include_router(activity.router); src/oc/web/routes/events.py:23 `from .activity import build_activity` and its pump_activity() coroutine; src/oc/web/static/js/api.js:603 GET /api/activity/{game}; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/activity.js (Activity floating panel); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/dsevents.js (comment referencing /api/activity as the poll this SSE channel replaces)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/routes/bench.py

**Purpose:** Drives a raw capture-throughput benchmark (no OCR) against the web app's warmed window locator, reusing the same grab loop the CLI `bench` command uses, so the Testing panel can A/B capture backends live.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py:327 app.include_router(bench.router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js:407-409 GET /api/bench/backends, GET /api/bench; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/testing.js (backend A/B panel)

- **[MEDIUM] concurrency** (line 56): bench() caches `engine.capture` once, then keeps calling it for up to 20s while a concurrent backend swap could close it out from under the running loop -- `backend = engine.capture` is read once before the warmup+measure loops (lines 55-57) and reused for up to `seconds` (max 20s) of repeated `grab_window()` calls. If, during that window, another request hits `POST /api/capture/backend` (capture_backend.py's `_swap()`), the live `engine.capture` is replaced and the OLD object is closed. `bench()`'s local `backend` variable still points at that now-closed object and keeps calling `grab_window()` on it for the rest of the run. `src/oc/collect/precapture.py:616-617` explicitly documents and accepts this exact tradeoff for its own local caching ('grab off the SAME object'), so this is a known accepted pattern in the codebase rather than a totally novel bug, but bench.py does it without that same acknowledgement/comment and over a much longer window (up to 20s vs one tick). Concrete scenario: a user has the Testing panel bench running with capture=None (uses live engine.capture) while, in another browser tab or shortly after, flips the foreground/background grabber in the same panel — the in-flight bench keeps hammering a closed backend for the remainder of its run.

### src/oc/web/routes/capture.py

**Purpose:** Serves the live game-window screenshot the teaching UI overlays boxes on (capture coordinates are exact window fractions of this image), lists/serves/binds stashed captures, and provides item/glyph cutout freezing plus an OCR-assisted auto-glypher for teaching the glyph atlas.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py:309 app.include_router(capture.router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js:299-323 item/cutout, glyph/cutout, glyph/auto; front-end capture/teaching panels consuming /api/capture, /api/captures/*, /api/games

- **[HIGH] hard-rule** (line 136): item_cutout, glyph_cutout, and glyph_auto copy-paste the identical 'load stashed capture -> cv2.imread -> fraction box to pixel crop' block three times instead of sharing one helper (hard rule 7) -- Lines 136-159 (`item_cutout`) and 171-194 (`glyph_cutout`) are near-verbatim duplicates: same `path_for` lookup + 404, same `cv2.imread` + 500 guard, same `px, py = max(0, int(x*W)), max(0, int(y*H))` / `pw, ph = max(1, int(w*W)), max(1, int(h*H))` pixel-box math, same crop/encode/save/return shape, differing only in the `item`/`glyph` naming and the `captures_store.save_*` call. `glyph_auto` (lines 206-254) repeats the same load+crop boilerplate a THIRD time (lines 220-230) before doing its own OCR-driven segmentation. CLAUDE.md hard rule 7 explicitly calls this exact anti-pattern out as 'the most expensive recurring mistake' and requires the SECOND instance to be built by extracting the first into a shared parameterized primitive (e.g. `_load_and_crop(game, capture, x, y, w, h) -> (path, crop)` returning the right HTTPException on miss) — instead all three routes independently reimplement it, so a future fix (e.g. correcting the ad hoc rounding vs. `FractionBox.to_pixels`) would have to be applied in three places and will likely drift. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 152): Cutout pixel-box math clamps only the origin and minimum size, not the far edge, so an out-of-range fraction box silently crops smaller than requested instead of erroring -- `px, py = max(0, int(x*W)), max(0, int(y*H))` and `pw, ph = max(1, int(w*W)), max(1, int(h*H))` never check that `px+pw <= W` / `py+ph <= H`. If the UI ever sends a box that runs past the image edge (e.g. from a stale box drawn against a different capture size), numpy's slice `img[py:py+ph, px:px+pw]` silently clips instead of raising, producing a smaller-than-requested cutout/glyph with no error surfaced to the user. Low severity since the UI is expected to only ever send boxes within [0,1] against the same image it fetched.

### src/oc/web/routes/capture_backend.py

**Purpose:** Lets the UI independently pick the foreground (game-focused) and background (occluded) capture grabber, persists the choice in sidecar files under data_dir so it survives restarts without touching settings.yaml, and rebuilds the live 'adaptive' composite capture backend in place, closing the outgoing backend first (documented as required to join WGC's native capture thread before it's dropped).

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py:310 app.include_router(capture_backend.router); src/oc/web/app.py:79 `from .routes.capture_backend import apply_persisted as apply_capture` called from the startup `_warm()`; src/oc/web/static/js/api.js:376-381 GET/POST /api/capture/backend; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/testing.js (foreground/background grabber picker)

- **[HIGH] correctness** (line 84): _swap()'s 'close the outgoing backend first' safety net is a no-op in practice because the outgoing backend is always the AdaptiveCapture composite, which has no close() to forward to its wrapped sub-backends -- `_build()` (line 76) always constructs the live capture as `build_capture(_COMPOSITE, ...)` i.e. an `AdaptiveCapture` (src/oc/capture/adaptive_backend.py), and `config/settings.yaml`'s `capture.name` is itself `adaptive`, so `engine.capture` is the composite from the very first boot onward — every subsequent `_swap()` call has `old` pointing at a composite. `AdaptiveCapture` defines no `close()` method (only `grab_window`/`grab`/`_is_foreground`) and `CaptureBackend` (interfaces.py) provides no default `close()` either, so `getattr(old, "close", None)` at line 86 resolves to `None` and `callable(close)` is `False` — the `close()` call this function exists to make (per its own module docstring: 'the WGC backend runs a free-threaded native capture thread that MUST be joined (close()) or interpreter exit crashes') never actually reaches the composite's inner `self._bg`/`self._fg` sub-backends. Concrete failure: a user opens the Testing panel, sets the background grabber to `wgc`, uses it a while, then switches it back to `printwindow` — the outgoing composite (holding a live `WgcCaptureBackend` with an open native capture session) is simply dropped with no `close()` called on the wgc instance, leaking its native capture thread and DWM/GPU stream for as long as the process keeps running (only `wgc_backend.py`'s own `atexit.register(self.close)` eventually joins it at interpreter exit, which is a crash-prevention last resort, not the intended prompt release). Each further backend flip constructs and orphans another live wgc session the same way. Fix belongs in AdaptiveCapture: add a close() that closes self._fg and self._bg.

### src/oc/web/routes/dbschema.py

**Purpose:** Reports a game's SQLite store structure (tables/columns/indexes/row counts, per-dataset event/present/batch stats) for the DB-structure panel, and provides the destructive whole-store-drop and single-table-clear operations (each auto-snapshotting first).

- **[INFO] sql-injection** (line 40): Table names interpolated into raw SQL (`DELETE FROM {t}` / `PRAGMA table_info({name})`) are always allow-listed against sqlite_master or a fixed literal tuple first, so this is not exploitable -- `clear_table` (dataset_store.py, called from clear_db_table) validates `table` against a live `SELECT name FROM sqlite_master` allow-list before interpolating it into `DELETE FROM {table}` — confirmed safe by its own docstring/comment. `drop_database`'s `DELETE FROM {t}` iterates a fixed literal tuple `("events","current","datasets","positions")`, not user input. `dbschema()`'s `PRAGMA table_info({name})`/`COUNT(*) FROM {name}` uses `name` values that came from the same `sqlite_master` query, never from the request. Noting only because table-name interpolation is normally a red flag worth double-checking; no actual vulnerability found.

### src/oc/web/routes/flow.py

**Purpose:** The dashboard/graph backend: reports each window's dataset wiring, exposes per-dataset detail/batches/history/observations, dataset lifecycle ops (rename/clear/delete/revert/remove/edit down to single events), computes derived 'subset' views (outer-joined datasets with filter/derive/sort), and a single batched /details endpoint so the graph's boot fetches every dataset+subset detail in one round trip.

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 66): _detail() calls store.ensure_loaded() under a comment describing behavior that no longer exists — DatasetStore.ensure_loaded() is now a hard no-op -- `src/oc/store/dataset_store.py:500-502` defines `ensure_loaded(self) -> None: """No-op: the DB is always the live source of truth (no snapshot cache to heal)."""; return None`. But `_detail()` here (line 65-66) still says: '# Parse the ledger up front so `records` (keyed state) can't lag the `batches`/`history` we show from that same ledger — and so a stale state cache self-heals before we read it.' followed by `store.ensure_loaded()`. Since the store dropped its JSON snapshot cache in favor of SQLite-as-source-of-truth, this call does nothing and the comment is stale/actively misleading about what guarantees `_detail()` has (it doesn't fail closed, it's just harmless dead code — the '870ms cost' the sibling `_flow_fetch()` docstring at line 201 explicitly warns about and deliberately avoids no longer applies to this call either, since it's already a no-op). Not a functional bug (no behavior changes if removed) but worth cleaning up: either delete the call+comment, or if some future re-introduction of a cache makes ensure_loaded meaningful again, this is the one place still depending on it.

### src/oc/web/routes/live.py

**Purpose:** Starts/stops/polls the real collection pipeline for a game in a background LiveSession (one per game, process-lifetime cached), mirrors its persisted frame-limiter interval, folds live status into the activity heartbeat, exposes readouts for toast/trigger token interpolation, and fires on_capture/on-lifecycle triggers around start/stop.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py:315 app.include_router(live.router); src/oc/web/routes/activity.py:18-20,40-44 `from .live import _sessions as _live_sessions` folded into build_activity(); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js:445-455 /api/live/{game}/start|stop|status|debug, /api/live/interval; src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/livewin.js (Live collection panel + debug log poll)

- **[INFO] correctness** (line 129): stop() fires the on-lifecycle 'fire_live_stop' trigger even if the session was never actually running (no is_running() gate before calling s.stop()) -- `stop()` (line 123-130) calls `s.stop()` and unconditionally `_fire_lifecycle(game, "fire_live_stop")` whenever a cached session object exists for the game, regardless of whether it was running at the time. Pressing Stop twice, or stopping a session that already finished on its own, re-fires the lifecycle trigger. `start()` has the same unconditional-fire shape for symmetry. This may be intentional (each user action is its own event, not gated on a state transition) — flagging as info only since I can't point to a concrete harmful outcome, just a plausible surprise if a trigger is authored assuming 'stop' means 'was running and got stopped'.


_Module notes: All 11 files are reachable in production: every router is included in src/oc/web/app.py, and every endpoint has a confirmed caller in src/oc/web/static/js (api.js + graph panels) or is called cross-module (build_activity from events.py, dbschema() from dbbackup.py, _persisted/read_mode from live.py). No hardcoded game-specific knowledge found in any of these files (clean per the zero-game-knowledge rule). Two notable cross-cutting findings: (1) a hard-rule-7 (copy-paste) violation in capture.py where item_cutout/glyph_cutout/glyph_auto reimplement the same box-to-pixel-crop-and-save logic three times instead of one shared helper; (2) a real resource-leak bug in the capture-backend hot-swap path: AdaptiveCapture (the composite that settings.yaml now makes the ALWAYS-active engine.capture) has no close() method, so capture_backend._swap()'s "close the outgoing backend first" safety net (there specifically to join WGC's native capture thread) silently no-ops on every live swap — confirmed by reading src/oc/capture/adaptive_backend.py (no close()) and src/oc/capture/wgc_backend.py (close() does real work, only reached via its own atexit hook as a last-resort net, not via the intentional swap-time close). This means toggling foreground/background backends live via the Testing panel while a wgc sub-backend is active leaks its native capture thread/GPU stream until process exit, contradicting the module's own docstring guarantee. Flagged on capture_backend.py since that's the file in this module driving the swap; root cause lives in capture/adaptive_backend.py (not in this module's file list) — worth a follow-up task to add AdaptiveCapture.close() that forwards to self._fg/self._bg._
