## Module: p23-routes-b  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/web/routes/ocr.py

**Purpose:** Runtime OCR device/knob control: persists device mode (cpu/gpu/auto), GPU downscale, per-burst yield, thread cap, CUDA arena size, and inference-engine choice to per-name dotfiles under data_dir, and exposes GET/POST endpoints plus apply_persisted() (called at server startup) so choices survive restarts. If deleted, the web UI's OCR device panel would have nothing to call and persisted knobs would reset to settings.yaml defaults every restart.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router, and calls routes.ocr.apply_persisted in _warm()); src/oc/web/routes/activity.py:19 imports ocr_state for the heartbeat; src/oc/web/routes/live.py:16 imports _persisted, read_mode; src/oc/web/routes/precapture.py:23 imports read_mode; src/oc/web/static/js/api.js (device/scale/yield/threads/gpumem/engine endpoints)

- **[INFO] style**: _persisted() is a nicely generalized single primitive already following rule 7 (one helper backs six knobs). -- No action needed; noting it as correctly-applied convention, not a finding against the code.

### src/oc/web/routes/precapture.py

**Purpose:** Drives the PrecaptureSession worker (record frames -> batch OCR -> review -> save) per game, and serves session listing, per-frame thumbnails, per-step timing log, and frame capture-times straight off disk for the frame-review UI. If deleted, the whole precapture/record-then-process workflow (and its UI) would have no backend.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router; kill_all_sessions/any_running called from lifespan + gpu_watch.py:62-63); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js (precapture.* calls: frame url builder, sessions, status, frameTimes, reclog, kill-all)

- **[MEDIUM] security** (line 110): game path segment is not checked for '..'/path-separators before being used to build a filesystem path, unlike sid which is explicitly checked. -- session_frame (line 110), session_reclog (line 148) and session_frametimes (line 171) each guard `sid` with `if "/" in sid or "\\" in sid or ".." in sid` before joining it into `base = Path(captures_dir) / _safe(game) / "precapture" / _safe(sid)`, but apply no equivalent check to `game` (nor do they validate game against list_profiles the way _session()/`_profile_or_404`-style helpers elsewhere in the router do). `_safe()` (captures_store.py) only replaces characters outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` with `_` -- a bare `..` segment (no slash) passes through UNCHANGED because '.' is in the allowed set. A request like `GET /api/precapture/../frame/0` (game='..') therefore resolves one directory above captures_dir before appending `precapture/<sid>/<idx>.jpg`, serving whatever matches there instead of 404ing. The fix is the same one-line guard already used for sid, applied to game too.

- **[LOW] concurrency** (line 52): _session()'s check-then-create on the module-level _sessions dict is not lock-protected. -- Two concurrent first-ever requests for the same never-seen game can both see `s is None` and both construct a PrecaptureSession; the dict assignment race means one is silently discarded. PrecaptureSession.__init__ starts no threads, so the practical impact is just a discarded, GC'd object -- but it's worth noting since every other shared mutable dict in this module family (e.g. sources.py's `_reading`) is lock-guarded and this one isn't.

### src/oc/web/routes/pretty.py

**Purpose:** Backs Pretty Studio: CRUD for the <game>.pretty.yaml design sidecar, {{token}} repointing after a graph-node rename, transient runtime overrides (set/list/clear/commit-to-YAML), and a manual one-record write into a dataset from a Pretty data-entry form.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/pretty/api.js (design get/put, repoint, overrides CRUD, dataset record write)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/routes/preview.py

**Purpose:** The teaching UI's live-read surface: /detect evaluates window/state detectors against a frame, /preview OCRs the current (unsaved) region layout and shows raw+resolved values per cell with dedup keys, /preview/commit re-reads and actually writes through the same gates the live collector uses, /rule_trace dumps every field's rule-pipeline trace in one OCR pass, /item/read tests one item's frozen cutout, and /detect/collisions/{game} cross-checks every window's detectors against every other window's reference capture to catch ambiguous/misclassifying detectors. This is the core 'see exactly what the boxes read' feedback loop the whole authoring UI depends on.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js, graph/imaging.js, keys.js (detect/preview/preview/commit/rule_trace/item/read/scroll/pos/detect/collisions calls)

- **[MEDIUM] duplication** (line 112): The scrollbar-thumb-position read block is copy-pasted verbatim between detect() and preview() instead of being one shared helper. -- Lines 112-125 (inside detect()) and lines 320-330 (inside preview()) both do: check `sc = window.scroll` / `sc.scrollbar`, import scroll_detail, convert the scrollbar FractionBox to pixels, crop the frame, call scroll_detail, and build the same `{pos, px, conf}` dict -- differing only in the local variable name it's assigned to (`scrollbar` vs `out["scrollbar"]`). Per the project's rule on a second instance of something (extract-then-both-call, never copy-paste), this should be one function e.g. `_scrollbar_reading(window, frame) -> dict | None` called from both endpoints, so a future fix/tweak to scrollbar reading can't land in only one of the two call sites. [Hard rule: 7]

- **[LOW] style** (line 354): rule_trace() reaches into RegionReader's protected _resolver attribute from outside the class. -- `run_rules(field, raw, dict_hook=reader._resolver.apply_dictionary, ...)` at line 354 uses a leading-underscore attribute across a module boundary. Works today (no functional bug), but couples this route to RegionReader's internals; a public accessor (e.g. reader.dict_hook or similar) would be more robust to refactors of RegionReader.

### src/oc/web/routes/prices.py

**Purpose:** Thin orchestration layer over enrich.price_runner: start/cancel/poll a background pricing sweep for a producer's dataset, plus preview (resolved inputs, no fetch) and probe (one live test-fetch) for debugging a producer's HTTP mapping. If deleted, the price/producer node's refresh/preview/probe buttons in the UI would have nothing to call.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js (prices.* calls)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/routes/profiles.py

**Purpose:** Profile CRUD for the teaching UI: list/get/put a game's profile (with merge-by-default so windows accumulate one at a time), a gitignored per-device graph viewport sidecar, and versioned-backup listing/fetch/restore.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js (profiles get/put/graphlocal/backups calls)

- **[INFO] error-handling**: apply_feeds() failures are swallowed with a bare `except Exception`. -- Deliberate and documented ("never block a save"), consistent with the rest of the codebase's best-effort side-effect pattern -- flagging only as a note that a real bug in dictionary-feed application would fail silently on every save with no server-side trace, not as a violation.

### src/oc/web/routes/screenshot.py

**Purpose:** Accepts a client-rendered PNG (node-canvas or viewport screenshot from the graph editor's toolbox) and writes it to a timestamped file under .trash/ for throwaway visual debugging -- not tracked data, deliberately outside captures/ and data/.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js:342 (screenshot endpoint call)

- **[INFO] consistency**: _TRASH = Path(".trash") resolves relative to the process's current working directory, not an anchored repo root. -- If `data-occultist serve` is launched from a directory other than the repo root, screenshots land in a `.trash` under that CWD rather than the intended repo-root scratch dir. This mirrors the rest of the app's convention though: settings.yaml's own `profiles_dir: config/games` and `data_dir: data` are equally CWD-relative, so this isn't a deviation from how the app already expects to be launched -- noting only as a latent footgun, not a new bug.

### src/oc/web/routes/sounds.py

**Purpose:** Lists audio filenames under static/sounds/ so the trigger UI's sound-picker <select> can be populated; the files themselves are already served for free by the existing static mount at /sounds/<file>.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js:617 (sounds.list)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/routes/sources.py

**Purpose:** File-source node backend: registered-format listing, auto-find candidate files on disk, peek at a candidate's head bytes, live preview of what the current (unsaved) extraction rules would produce (rows + dismissed rows), auto-resolve suggested extraction fields from the file's own structure, and kick an on-demand background read of a saved source into its dataset.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js (sources.read/preview/resolve/find/peek calls)

- **[INFO] error-handling**: read_now()'s background thread swallows any read_source exception with a bare except. -- Documented and deliberate ("a read failure must not wedge the in-flight guard") -- the finally-block correctly discards the (game, source_id) key either way so a later retry isn't blocked forever; just noting a failed read is otherwise invisible server-side beyond whatever read_source itself logs.

### src/oc/web/routes/stats.py

**Purpose:** Per-node execution-duration stats for the graph's stats panel: a live rollup (count/last/avg/min/max ms per node+op), on-demand history samples for a node's trend chart, and rename/remove hooks that carry a node's stat history across a graph edit.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/graph/panels/stats.js (stats/history/rename/remove calls)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/routes/toasts.py

**Purpose:** Toast-node test endpoints: fire one toast immediately with its current config (bypassing the trigger wiring and the enabled flag, since a test should always pop), and render a hero/inline toast-image spec to a live PNG for the node editor's design preview.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js (toasts.test / toasts preview calls)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/web/routes/triggers.py

**Purpose:** Lists a game's triggers with each producer target's live sweep status, and fires one trigger on demand through the exact same dispatch funnels the collector loop uses (fire_target for producers, read_source_target for file sources, fire_toast for toasts, fire_dataset_target for dataset actions) -- the teach UI's manual test-fire button.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js (triggers.list / triggers.fire)

- **[INFO] correctness**: The fire() lambda passed to fire_target correctly avoids the classic late-binding closure bug. -- `lambda p, items: start_sweep(data_dir, game, p, profile=profile, items=items)` takes `p` as a parameter rather than closing over the loop variable `pn`, so it can't accidentally fire the LAST iteration's producer for every target the way a `lambda: start_sweep(..., pn, ...)` would have. No bug, noting it as correctly written since it's exactly the shape that's easy to get wrong.

### src/oc/web/routes/video.py

**Purpose:** Testing-harness endpoints: upload a screen-capture clip, then play/seek/step/enable it so live-mode and the preview/detect endpoints read decoded video frames instead of the actual game window -- lets the whole detect+OCR pipeline be exercised with no game running.

**Used by:** src/oc/web/app.py (include_router); src/oc/web/static/js/api.js (video.upload/status/seek/step/enable/close)

- **[INFO] correctness**: upload() correctly strips directory components from the client-supplied filename before using it as a disk path. -- `Path(file.filename or "video").name` takes only the final path component, so a filename like `../../evil` can't escape `_videos_dir()`. No finding; noting it as the right pattern (contrast with precapture.py's session_frame gap on `game`).


_Module notes: All 13 files are wired into src/oc/web/app.py via app.include_router() and confirmed reachable from the front end (grepped src/oc/web/static for each route prefix) -- no dead code in this module. No hardcoded game-specific knowledge found in any file (all operate on generic profile/window/field/dataset/source/producer/trigger abstractions per the project's zero-game-knowledge-in-Python rule). No 12-hour/locale-default date formatting found (screenshot.py's one strftime call uses the required dd-mm-yy + 24h format). No mutable-default-argument bugs (sources.py's `Body(default={})` is FastAPI's field-default sentinel and the dict is never mutated in the handler, so it's not exploitable even if the same object were reused). Two real findings worth prioritizing: (1) src/oc/web/routes/precapture.py's session_frame/session_reclog/session_frametimes validate `sid` against '..'/slashes but never apply the same check to `game`, and `_safe()` does not strip dots, so a literal `game=\"..\"` escapes one directory level from captures_dir -- an easy one-line fix mirroring the existing sid guard. (2) src/oc/web/routes/preview.py's scrollbar-thumb-read block is duplicated verbatim between detect() and preview() (hard rule 7) and should be hoisted into one shared helper._

## Module: p24-toplevel  _audit (UNVERIFIED)_

### src/oc/backup.py

**Purpose:** Format-agnostic snapshot machinery shared by every versioned backup in the app: mints collision-free sortable UTC stamps (new_stamp_path), lists/prunes snapshot files by extension, and implements two retention policies (retention_keep: dense-recent/daily/weekly thinning; gfs_keep: grandfather-father-son for large snapshots) plus a flat keep_last_n. If deleted, both oc.profile.loader's YAML profile backups and oc.store.db_backup's SQLite backups would lose their stamp minting, listing, pruning and retention-policy logic (each would have to re-implement the stamp/list/prune dance CLAUDE.md explicitly says this module exists to avoid).

**Used by:** src/oc/store/db_backup.py (via `from .. import backup`: backup.gfs_keep, backup.list_snapshots, backup.prune, backup.new_stamp_path, backup.parse_stamp); src/oc/profile/loader.py (via `from .. import backup`: backup.retention_keep, backup.STAMP_FMT, backup.list_snapshots, backup.prune, backup.new_stamp_path); tests/test_db_backup.py (backup.keep_last_n)

- **[LOW] dead-code** (line 95): keep_last_n has no production call site -- gfs_keep (not keep_last_n) is what src/oc/store/db_backup.py actually uses for its GFS retention policy (db_backup.py:94), and src/oc/profile/loader.py uses retention_keep for YAML backups. keep_last_n is only exercised by tests/test_db_backup.py::test_keep_last_n_keeps_newest. It is plausibly a superseded/earlier policy kept as public API, or reserved for a future simpler backend; not clearly wrong, just currently unused outside tests.

### src/oc/desktop_main.py

**Purpose:** Release-mode entry point that starts the web server on a daemon thread and opens the native pywebview window in one process, tearing the server down when the window closes (or under a headless smoke-test env var). This is exactly what `pythonw -m oc.desktop_main` (the install.ps1/app.ps1 desktop shortcut) and `data-occultist app` (via oc/cli/app.py) run; deleting it would break both the installed desktop shortcut and the `app` CLI subcommand.

**Used by:** src/oc/cli/app.py:16 (`from ..desktop_main import run_release`, the `data-occultist app` subcommand); scripts/install.ps1:139 (desktop shortcut target: `pythonw.exe -m oc.desktop_main`); scripts/app.ps1 (launches `pythonw -m oc.desktop_main` detached); scripts/make_shortcut.ps1 (shortcut target comment/logic); README.md (documents both launch paths)

- **[INFO] test-coverage** (line 42): OC_DESKTOP_SMOKE headless self-test path is not invoked by any test or CI workflow in the repo -- The env-var-gated smoke branch (lines 42-51) is designed for manual/CI verification of the release entry point without opening a GUI window, per its comment. No test file, script, or (nonexistent) CI workflow in the repo currently sets OC_DESKTOP_SMOKE=1, so this path is presently only reachable by a developer running it by hand. Not a bug, just unexercised automation.

### src/oc/eventlog.py

**Purpose:** In-process, thread-safe pub/sub activity bus: backend code (collector, sweep/price workers, trigger effects) calls publish() to announce one-line human-facing events; the web layer subscribes and streams them to the browser log bar over SSE, with a bounded ring buffer (recent()) so a reconnecting client can backfill. slog() additionally prints a 24h-timestamped line to the server console. Deleting it would break the browser activity log bar, terminal trigger-fire logging, and blocked-sweep surfacing across collect/triggers.py, web/routes/events.py, gpu_watch.py, price_runner.py and sweep_engine.py.

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/triggers.py (publish/slog for trigger fires); src/oc/web/routes/events.py (SSE endpoint subscribing via eventlog.subscribe/recent); src/oc/web/gpu_watch.py; src/oc/enrich/price_runner.py; src/oc/enrich/sweep_engine.py; src/oc/store/db_backup.py; src/oc/web/static/js/logstream.js (browser-side SSE consumer of the stream this module feeds); tests/test_eventlog.py, tests/test_triggers.py

- **[LOW] correctness** (line 41): publish()'s subscriber dispatch swallows all subscriber exceptions with zero trace -- `except Exception: pass` (line 41-42) is deliberately broad and commented ("one bad subscriber must never break a publisher"), which is a reasonable design given publish() can be called from arbitrary backend threads. But it means a subscriber bug (e.g. a broken SSE encoder) fails completely silently with no log line anywhere, which could hide a real regression indefinitely. Flagging as low since the broad-except itself is intentional and documented, not accidental.

### src/oc/numfmt.py

**Purpose:** One shared parser for the trailing `|round:N` / `|dp:N` / `|fixed:N` / `|.Nf` decimal-format directive used in `{{token}}`/`{column}` templates, so the toast/readout templating engine and the subset derived-math engine don't each re-implement (and drift on) the same little grammar. Deleting it would break decimal formatting in both toast/notification text (collect/templating.py) and computed subset columns (enrich/subset.py).

**Used by:** src/oc/collect/templating.py:26,220 (`from ..numfmt import split_dp`, used in render()'s token substitution); src/oc/enrich/subset.py:30,111,129 (`from ..numfmt import split_dp`, used in render_template() and _eval_math())

- No issues found.

### src/oc/__main__.py

**Purpose:** Enables `python -m oc` as a venv-name-independent equivalent of the `data-occultist`/`occ` console scripts. Used wherever the code needs to re-invoke itself as a subprocess without assuming the console-script shim is on PATH (job supervision, sweep workers) and as a documented fallback for scripts/serve.ps1 when the installed console exe can't be found.

**Used by:** scripts/serve.ps1:52 (`& $py -m oc serve --host ... --port ...`); src/oc/jobs/subprocess_job.py (spawns `python -m oc <args...>` child processes); src/oc/enrich/price_runner.py (spawns `python -m oc _sweep-job ...` as its own-GIL process); src/oc/cli/sweep_job.py (docstring documents being invoked this way)

- No issues found.

### src/oc/__init__.py

**Purpose:** Top-level package marker/docstring for `oc`, plus the `__version__` string. It is implicitly imported the instant anything under `oc.*` is imported (every module in the codebase, every test, the console-script entry point), so by definition it is never dead.

**Used by:** Implicitly by every `import oc...` / `from oc...` statement across src/oc, tests, and the installed console scripts (data-occultist, occ)

- **[LOW] maintainability** (line 3): __version__ is a second, disconnected source of truth for the package version -- `__version__ = "0.1.0"` is not referenced anywhere else in the repo (no `--version` CLI flag, no UI display, and pyproject.toml declares its own literal `version = "0.1.0"` rather than `dynamic = ["version"]` reading this constant). The two currently agree (0.1.0 in both) but nothing enforces that; a future bump to one without the other would silently desync them.


_Module notes: All six files are small, single-purpose, well-documented utility/entry-point modules. No hardcoded game/item names or window-title logic found in any of them (compliant with the zero-game-knowledge rule). No mutable default arguments, no dangerous bare excepts (the two broad `except Exception` sites are both commented/justified: eventlog.publish isolating one bad subscriber, db_backup's backup-on-write path not covered here). No hard-rule (CLAUDE.md items 1-9) violations found - eventlog.slog already uses the correct 24h HH:MM:SS format. Cross-checked every import against pyproject.toml, cli/app.py, scripts/*.ps1, and the modules that consume oc.backup/oc.numfmt to confirm none of these six files are dead._
